# The Wempe Einheits-Chronometer model

An interactive 3D model, in progress, of the German *Deutsches Einheits-Chronometer*: the standard marine chronometer designed by Gerhard D. Wempe's chronometer works in Hamburg in the early 1940s and made by Wempe and A. Lange & Söhne. The model is the spring-detent version (*Feder-Chronometer*, Kaliber 05) as its works drawings draw it, each sheet **as last revised** (about 1946-48). Where a sheet was revised the model is built as last revised; the parts the revisions replaced are built as well, shown under Variants, The drawings: As first drawn (below, As first drawn). The lever chronometer's sheets (05 04_20-23, 05 08_30-33) are used only to cross-check the holes it shares.

Phase P1a (14 October 2026) built the frame: the two plates with every hole of their tables, the three pillars and their screws, the movement ring, the barrel bridge with its click, the fusee bush and dust bush, the two balance cocks and the intermediate bridge, each with its screws and steady pins. Holes for the parts of later phases (the train's arbors, the detent block's screw, the balance holding work, the escape wheel's settings) are cut now, as the tables give them; the parts themselves come later.

Phase P1b (14 October 2026) built the going train: the centre (minute), intermediate and seconds arbors, each a brass wheel riveted on its steel pinion and arbor, in their bearings: the brass bushes WN-301 and WN-300 in the top plate, WN-302 in the pillar plate and WN-300 in the intermediate bridge, and the seconds arbor's jewel in its setting in the bridge; and the pillar plate's two stud bushes (05 08 02-2) with the studs the motion work turns on. The escape wheel (05 04) turns the train: until it is built, P0's stand-in escapement steps it a tooth a beat.

Phase P1c (14 October 2026) built the fusee, its wheel and Harrison's maintaining work (ratchet, clicks and their springs, the maintaining spring, the maintaining detent on its arbor at hole 4), the Vorreiber, cover plate, dust plate and the stop work (the bar across the fusee's small end and the stop pin in the top plate's hole 7), the barrel with its lid, arbor, hook and set-up ratchet, the mainspring, and both the chain and the band, chosen under Variants (the chain by default). Winding (the panel) sets the hours since full wind or winds it through the key, the maintaining spring keeping the train going; the hash keeps both (`wind=`, `conn=band`).

Phase P1d (14 October 2026) built the balance and the spring detent escapement (drawing groups 05 03 and 05 04): the balance with its rim, masses, timing weights and staff; the helical hairspring with its collet and stud; the impulse and discharge rollers; the escape wheel with its pinion and collet; the detent with its gold spring and locking stone, on its block; and the balance's and escape wheel's jewels, settings and endstones. They move: the balance swings twice a second and the escapement unlocks, impulses and locks as 05 04_1 lays it out (kinematically, at the swing phase 2a solves: Physics).

Phase P1e (14 October 2026) built the dial and what is under and over it (drawing group 05 02): the cannon tube with its pinion on the centre arbor, the change wheel and the hour wheel, the up-and-down wheel and its pinion on the fusee arbor, the four hands, and the dial with its printing, screws and pin. The hands show the time (the page's clock is local time, as the Hamilton's), the up-and-down hand the fusee's turns from full wind. The hand setting (05 33), the balance holding device (05 36) and the seconds contact (05 38) are not built (below).

Phase P1f (15 October 2026) built the case and the box (drawing groups 05 01 and 51 00): the cast brass bowl with its closing plate, brackets and locking bracket, the bezel and its glass, the gimbal ring with its bracket, pivots and lock nuts, the transit lock, the mahogany box with its glazed lid, hinges, handles, maker's plate and latch tongue, and the one-way winding key of 1943, standing in its holder in the box's back right corner (since 15 October 2026: until then it lay on the floor); and the movement ring's radial pin, which sets the movement's turn in the case. Two views show them: Box (shut, from high enough to read the dial through the lid's glass) and Case (open), and the Dial view now looks down on the dial in its case; the other views lift the movement out and turn it train side up, as the Hamilton's do. The gimbals stood still until 15 October 2026, when they were given their swing ("The page's time, ship motion and reports", below); their axes are as drawn.

Phase 2a (15 October 2026) made the escapement run physically, as the Hamilton's does: the balance's swing comes from the drive, the fusee's force as 05 06_20 measured it, through the train, and the rate from the escapement (the shared `makeEsc` with the Wempe's settings, `js/escphys.js`); run down, the train stops and the balance swings down to rest, and once wound it needs a twist to start (below, Physics).

Phase 2c (15 October 2026) settled the hairspring's length (the whole strip 13 turns less 3.5 mm, 05 03_7: the period 0.468 s with the weighed balance), checked its terminal curves against Phillips' conditions and the force they leave on the pivots, gave the model the drawn spring's own isochronism, and worked the cut bimetallic balance and steel spring in heat and cold against the 6MX's TU (below, Physics: hairspring and temperature).

Readings of 15 October 2026 settled four open figures from the sheets and photographs (each with its evidence and sureness under How the balance and escapement were measured and Physics: energy): the balance's masses stand from the rim's lower face to 6.46 (they were built from 1.1, the hidden pair's outline), 30° from the cuts' line (32 scaled), which makes the balance 10 % heavier (2,501 g·mm²; 2,561 since the shape batch below, and the tool weighing one revision: the period 0.497 s) and the hairspring's period 0.491 s; the detent's horn is 4.4 long (3.5 built); the detent's banking is the block's cross screw, bearing on the pipe at the stone (not drawn yet); and the mainspring's "0,37" is not overwritten. The physics was run again on them.

Phase E1 (15 October 2026) built the escapement as first drawn, the parts the later sheets replaced, beside those they replaced, chosen under Variants (The drawings: As last revised, As first drawn; `rev=first` in the link): 05 04_1's layout of 1940-41 with the escape wheel and rollers just under the top plate, the 1941 staff with its brass collar (05 04_5), the 1941 escape arbor and collet (05 04_7.1), the detent at 1436's first sizes and the block of 1941 with its long arm and cone. Wempe 3955 is made so (below, As first drawn). Phase E2 added the barrel of 1941 in three pieces, its lid on the pillar plate's side, and the 1941 maintaining spring (020605), widening round its turn; phase E3 the winding key of 1940, with its flat pear-shaped handle, and on the cards the figures the revisions changed by too little to show.

## Files

- `index.html`: the page. It loads three.js and the fonts from `../../vendor/`, the Hamilton's `core.js` (materials, geometry helpers, sections, the ink drawing) and stylesheet by relative path, then `../shared/kit.js`, `../shared/viewer.js` and its own scripts.
- `js/dims.js`: `WD`, the shared dimensions: the plates, the pillars and their screws, and the hole table `WD.H` (every hole by its number on the sheets, in the movement frame). Each number names its sheet and how sure the reading is. The pillars' fits keep their limits beside the middles the model draws (`PIL.sF`, `PIL_HF`, `PIL.LT`, `PIL_SEAT_T`), which the essay works the fit from.
- `js/frame.js`: `WFRAME.build(mv, M, K)`, the frame's parts. Each part's own sizes sit in the comment beside the code that draws them, with the sheet, the figure and how sure.
- `js/fusee.js`: `WFUSEE.build(mv, M, K, FR, TR)`, the fusee, maintaining work, stop work, barrel, mainspring, chain and band, and `update(s, c)`, which sets them from the centre arbor's angle `c` (the fusee wheel turns with it by 14/90) and `s.n`, the fusee's turns from full wind (the Hamilton's MoveState: 0 fully wound, `NRUN` 8.70 run down), `s.winding` (the key on the square); it returns the fusee arbor's angle, which `update()` publishes as `mv.userData.ang.f` for the up-and-down work. `setConn('chain'|'band')` chooses the connector.
- `js/train.js`: `WTRAIN.build(mv, M, K)`, the going train and its bearings, and `update(s)`, which turns the arbors from the escape wheel's place `s.E` and returns their angles (`c` the centre arbor's, for the motion work and the fusee; `s` the seconds arbor's, for the seconds hand). `mv.userData.R.tr` holds the arbors' turning groups, each with its `wheel` and `pin` meshes, for the fusee wheel and the escape pinion to phase against (`gearLash`); `mv.userData.ang` the last angles.
- `js/movement.js`: `buildWempe(M)`, which calls the groups' builders (the frame so far), and `update()`. Later groups each get a file of their own (`train.js`, `escapement.js`…), loaded before `movement.js`, each declaring one global.
- `js/escapement.js`: `WESC`, the balance and escapement: `WESC.build(mv, M, K)` builds them and returns `update(s)`; `WESC.ESC` is the escapement's motion (`ESC` in movement.js, which the page and the tools drive, `ESC.state(p, amp, drive)`, at the swing `ESC.A` by default): since 15 October 2026 makeEsc's solved state as the model draws it (`WESC.adapt`, called by escphys.js; The page's escapement, below), the layout's kinematic motion kept as `ESC.layout`; `WESC.HOLES` the holes it needs in the frame's parts (frame.js cuts them); `WESC.SET` the escapement's settings for makeEsc, from its layout (`MK` puts a plan point in makeEsc's frame; `toKin` turns makeEsc's lift into the detent's turn); `WESC.HSL` the hairspring's working length and its other readings, `WESC.HSP` its plan (the path pin to pin or end to end, which the page draws and `tools/wempe_hairspring.js` checks).
- `js/escphys.js`: `WPHYS`, the escapement in time: makeEsc (`../shared/escapement.js`, loaded before it) built with `WESC.SET` and the drive (05 06_20's force, the train's ratio and efficiency), its reference driven at the escape wheel's torque 12 h from full wind (`TQ`); `drv(n)`, the drive n fusee turns from full wind, times the reference's; `ampStep`, the swing stepped as the Hamilton's; the hairspring's stiffness and the period it gives (`HS`); the maintaining spring (`MSK`, `MSK2`: its push against the ratchet's lead over the fusee wheel; `MSB`, the lead where it banks; `runPhi`, `susPhi`, `susDrv`: the lead running and while the key turns, and the drive then; `SUS`, the key's time to the push spent: `tools/wempe_sustaining.js`). It hands its makeEsc to `WESC.adapt`, so the page's escapement (`WESC.ESC.state`) moves as it solves it, at the reference's solved swing by default. `app.js`'s tick keeps the swing (`H`), stops the train when it is too small or run down, and starts it again (a twist, when the balance has stopped). `tools/wempe_escapement.js` prints and checks it; `tools/wempe_load.js` runs these scripts in Node.
- `js/dial.js`: `WDIAL`, the dial, motion work, hands and up-and-down work: `WDIAL.build(mv, M, K)` builds them and returns `update(s, ang)` (from the train's angles `ang.c`, `ang.s`, the fusee's `ang.f` once the fusee group gives it, else the wind `s.n`, fusee turns from full wind as the Hamilton's MoveState); `WDIAL.face()` draws the dial's printing on a canvas: the geometric parts from the numbers measured on Wempe 3955's dial, the glyphs from `js/dialface.js`.
- `js/dialface.js`: `WDIALFACE`, generated by `tools/dialtrace.py --write` (don't edit): every numeral, figure and letter of the dial's printing, the serial and the anchor, traced off Wempe 3955's dial as contours in 0.01 mm, and the two tracks' divisions' measured angles. Loaded before `dial.js`.
- `js/dialface_lange.js`: `WDIALFACE_LANGE`, the same for A. Lange & Söhne No. 5177's dial, generated by `tools/dialtrace.py --dial lange --write` (don't edit); the page's Variants show it (Dial, `#dial=lange`). Loaded before `dial.js`.
- `dials/`: the three dials as vector drawings, generated by `tools/dialsvg.py --write` (don't edit): `wempe-3955.svg`, `kirov-247-6mx.svg` (the Soviet 6MX's, its serial in red) and `lange-5177.svg`, in mm at 1:1 (Ø 103.55, 12 at the top, the centre arbor at 0, 0), layered paper (the disc with the printed face's holes), ink, red and drill (hidden: every hole through the disc). They are the page's printing, not traced again: `tools/dialsvg.js` runs `dial.js`'s own `face()` on a canvas that records its calls in mm, and `dialsvg.py` resolves its white-over-black paint order into ink alone (shapely). `dialsvg.py --verify` rasterises each against the page's canvas at 4× (24 October 2026: no shape differs at 12.6 µm a pixel, the ink's area within 0.13 %); `dialtrace.py --dial D --compare --canvas r_dialsvg_D.png` (after `dialsvg.py --png`) lays one on its photograph as the page's canvas is laid (Wempe 3955: ink only in the photo 243.7 mm², mostly its hands, only in the SVG 49.4; the page's canvas 241.9 and 51.3). Rerun `--write` after changing the dial's printing (`ci.py` fails on a stale one).
- `js/movement.js`: `buildWempe(M)`, which calls the groups' builders (the frame, the escapement), and `update()`. Later groups each get a file of their own (`train.js`, `escapement.js`…), loaded before `movement.js`, each declaring one global.
- `js/case.js`: `WCASE.build(M)`, the case and the box, outside the movement: the box is the scene's group, the gimbal ring's group in it turns about x (the outer pivots, at `YP`), the bowl's group in that about z (the inner pivots), and the page puts the movement into the bowl's group, lifting it out (600 mm) and turning it over in the movement's views (`app.js`'s tick, from each view's `case`: the lid's opening and the lift). `?qa` gives `window.__BX` (root, ring, bowl, lid), which `tools/audit.py box --model wempe` audits (`checks.py`'s `FLOAT_BOX`, `audit-expected.json`'s `box`). The outer box (`over`, its lid `overLid`, turning about x at its hinges) is built in the box's group and shown only in the Transport box view (its `case.over`); audit it there with `--eval "new Promise(r=>{location.hash='view=transport';setTimeout(r,3000);})"` (the tick sets its visibility each frame).
- `js/app.js`: the parts' cards (`PARTS`, `PIECES`), views, sections and labels, given to the shared viewer, with its Moving parts only (`cfg.drive`) and walkthrough (`WTOUR`, `cfg.TOUR`), and the panels ported from the Hamilton's (tick sound, Swing and isochronism, Rate and timing weights, the 35-day trial, Escapement in plan: below, The page's capabilities against the Hamilton's); it passes the viewer the essay (`essay: ESSAY`) and binds the essay to the model once built (its time, wind and swing).
- `js/essay.js`: `ESSAY`, the Its drawings tab (the page's Essay tab is the essay shared by both models, on its own page, `../essay/`, since 21 October 2026), *The Einheits-Chronometer* (its text and figure markup the `<article id="essay">` in `index.html`; open `index.html#drawings`, or `#drawings=detent`: its hash key since 21 October 2026, essaykit's `key`; `?from=wempe#essay=detent`, the page's old address's links through worker.js, opens it too): what the drawings and the makers' own measurement tell us and where they disagree, phase A (15 October 2026): the dial keeping the model's time (`WDIAL.face` and the hands' outlines, `WDIAL.HANDS`), the sheets' dates, the top plate's hole table and plan (`WD.H`) and the pillar's fit (`WD.PIL`'s limits), 05 06_20's force against the solved mainspring, the escape wheel's chase against the layout's 21°, and the 6MX's TU against GOST 8916-58; phase B (15 October 2026): the hairspring (05 03_7's curve drawn from `WESC.HSP` with its compass arcs and its centroid against Phillips' point, and the force on the pivots against the helix's turns), heat (the balance cloned from the model with its masses moved along the rim, the coefficient against their place, and the 6MX's 35-day trial for that place, its figures worked as `wempe_errorbudget.js` works them), the escapement in plan (the model's own pieces flattened onto the plan in the frame of what moves them, moved by `WESC.ESC.state`: `drawEscPlan` draws the Hamilton's parts, not these), the box and gimbals (`WCASE.build`), and what the drawings leave open. Its facts come from `References/wempe/` and this README only; its numbers are live (`fillLive`: `data-live` spans from `WPHYS`, `WESC`, `WD`) or generated (`data-wphys` spans and the figures' data from `js/essaydata.js`). No drawing scan or photograph is shown: every figure is drawn from the model's numbers, and the text links to dg-chrono.de for the sheets. The machinery is the shared kit, `../shared/essaykit.js` (`ESSAYKIT`), the look the Hamilton's `css/essay.css` (with a few rules in `css/wempe.css`). While the essay shows the viewer doesn't draw the model (it keeps its time), leaves the keys to it and writes `#essay[=section]` (`../shared/viewer.js`'s `cfg.essay`).
- `js/essaydata.js`: `WEDATA`, generated by `../chronometer-working-model/tools/wempe_essaydata.js --write` (don't edit): the sheets' catalogue from `References/wempe/README.md`, the fusee's measured and solved curves and the losses' range (`wempe_mainspring.json`, `wempe_physics.js`), the detent's fall after it lets a tooth go (makeEsc's state), the rate a °C against the masses' place (`wempe_thermal.js`'s `sweep`), one seeded TU trial's days less the balance's linear term (`wempe_errorbudget.js`'s `essay()`), the force on the pivots against the helix's turns (`wempe_hairspring.js`'s spring, `HSPR.lateral`), the swing and the escapement's rate against the drive (makeEsc's `ampAt` and `rateAt`, the drive 0.2 to 1.8 times the reference's: the page's Swing and isochronism panel reads between them), the gimbals' bodies (`gimbals.js`'s, the Wempe's and the Hamilton's), and the figures the text quotes from them (`data-wphys`, which the tool also writes into `index.html`). Without `--write` the tool fails if this file or the spans are stale (`ci.py`'s `wempe-essaydata`, `selfcontained.py`): rerun it after a change to `WESC.SET`, `WPHYS`, the mainspring's solve, the balance or hairspring, the case or the catalogue (about 2 minutes: the trial with its summary, the rate over the wind and the page's swing table run in child processes at once). It also carries what the page's Swing, Rate and trial panels read: `trial.airK`, `trial.Sint` and `trial.sum`, the TU's figures over 2,000 trials compensated and 500 at the masses' drawn place (`wempe_errorbudget.js`'s `summary()`).
- `js/makers.js`: `MAKERS`, generated by `../chronometer-working-model/tools/wempe_makers.py` (don't edit): the maker's sheets' data, each line of the parts list with its measured fit, its drawing's material and treatment and how it is made, the 1950s build's deviations and the bench's tests (Making it, below). `BOM.md`, beside this file, is `bom.py --model wempe --md`'s parts list against the model, the fits it reads.
- `../shared/kit.js` (`KIT`): the part and screw makers and the plan helpers copied from the Hamilton's `movement.js`, and, added for this frame, `solidGeo` (a closed solid from plan cells in depth bands, with holes, counterbores, pockets and coned walls) and `bool` (two outlines crossed, sharing their crossing points). `KIT.screw` takes a material.
- `../shared/contacts.js` (`CONTACTS`): the Contacts debug view, through the viewer (below, The page's capabilities against the Hamilton's), loaded after `viewer.js`; `js/contactdata.js`: `WCONTACT`, generated by `../chronometer-working-model/tools/contacts.py --model wempe --write` from `checks.py` (don't edit): `fine.py`'s expected overlaps and states, which the view calls expected and sweeps through.
- `checks.py`, `bom.json`, `audit-expected.json`: the tools' tables for `--model wempe` (`../chronometer-working-model/tools/model.py`).
- `verification/`: the report *The Wempe Einheits-Chronometer: does the model hold to its drawings, and would it keep time?* (`index.html`, open the file; About and the essay's last section link to it): every Wempe check's method and results, from the tools' own outputs. `node reportdata.js` runs every check (the full runs of dynamics.js, tripping.js, gearing.js and gimbals.js with `--model wempe`, the build checks wempe_tolerances.js, wempe_bench.js, wempe_montecarlo.js and keystone.js --model wempe, at once) and works the figures it charts from the tools' modules and JSON (`assets/data.json`, about 6 minutes); `python geometry.py` runs the browser checks (solids, fine, audit, exploded, bom) one after another (`assets/geometry.json`, about 6 minutes); `python build_report.py` writes `index.html` from `template.html`, those two files, `checks.py`, `bom.json` and this README's Sources, Estimated, Readings corrected and Unresolved sections (a second). The root build copies it to `site/wempe/verification/`. No drawing scan or photograph is in it: its charts are drawn from the model's numbers, the videos linked at their moments. Rebuild it after changing what it reports.

## Coordinates and units

Millimetres. The movement frame is the Hamilton's: dial side +y, 12 o'clock −z, 3 o'clock +x. The pillar plate's inner face is at y 0 and its dial face at +3.35; the top plate's inner face at −19 and its outer face at −22.35.

The sheets' hole tables give every hole as N/S and O/W (Ost, east, and West) from hole 1, the centre wheel's arbor, which is the plates' centre. Every plan of the frame is drawn with N up and O to the right, seen from above with the movement lying dial down: the top plate from its outer face (its pillar seats, in its inner face, are dashed; its sections mark that face "oben") and the pillar plate from its inner face. N is 12 o'clock: the seconds wheel is at S 23 and the up-and-down at N 23. O is the dial's 9 o'clock: Wempe 3955's dial side (`photos/dg-chrono-dec/3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`) has the Wechselrad (hole 19, O 10.9) left of centre, and the movement ring's sheet, drawn from the dial side, puts its screws 20° clockwise from 12, where Wempe 3955's top plate has them at 339° from N toward O. So x = W and z = S (`WD.NO`).

## Sources

- **The works drawings**, `References/wempe/drawings/` (© Johannes Altmeppen, by courtesy of Gerhard D. Wempe KG, 2017), as transcribed in `References/wempe/transcriptions/05-08-frame.md` and checked on the sheets themselves, cropped and enlarged wherever a number mattered. The frame's sheets are 05 08_1a/1b (Oberplatte, 05 09 01), _2 (Oberplatte kompl., 05 08 01), _3a/3b (Unterplatte, 05 08 02-1), _32.2 (Unterplatte kompl., 05 08 02), _4 (Unruhkloben unten, 05 08 00-2), _5 and _6 (Unruhkloben oben, 05 08 03-1, and the kompl. 127-556.04 U30), _7 (Werkring, 05 08 00-3), _8 (Schneckenfutter, 05 08 01-2), _9 (Staubfutter, 05 08 01-3), _10 (Zwischenbrücke kompl., 05 08 04), _11 (Pfeiler, 05 08 00-1), _12 to _14 (Federhausbrücke kompl., 05 08 05; Federhausbrücke, 05 08 05-1; Sperrkegel, 05 08 05-2), and sheet 001 (the movement in plan at 2:1).
- **The coloured section** (`drawings/Wempe_Einheitschronometer.jpg`): the whole movement in one unrolled section, with the parts' numbers, including the Wempe standard parts (WN) that have no sheet in the set. Measured on it where no sheet gives a size, scaled on the pillar's 8.1 and the 19 between the plates (28.3 px/mm in a 3× crop, the same both ways where it was checked).
- **Photographs of genuine German movements**: Wempe 3955 above all (`photos/dg-chrono-dec/`): `2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg`, the top plate square-on on millimetre paper (8.82 px/mm at the plate, from the pillar screws' 62.41 and 58.7 mm apart); `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`, the dial side; `12_` and `13_Wempe_3955_obere_Platte_*.jpg`, the top plate off, both faces; the side views `4-7_Wempe_3955_*.jpg`. ALS 5177 and the Kirow movements likewise. The 6MX differs (see `References/wempe/README.md`) and isn't evidence for the frame.

- **The 6MX's test cards** (`photos/dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/bilder-zum-6mx/`: eight passports and attestats of Soviet 6MXs, 1953-1993, transcribed in `References/wempe/documents/6MX-figures.md`, section 8): the scatter and drift and the masses' setting the trial is run with, and the check on its secondary error.
- **National Bureau of Standards, Circular 58**, *Invar and related nickel steels* (2nd ed., 1923), Tables 5 (p. 86) and 13 (p. 90): Guillaume's nickel steels' expansion, a + 2bt, by their nickel; the rim's 42-44.4 % "anibal" quadratic −3.4 to −2.51 × 10⁻⁹ a °C² (`tools/wempe_thermal.js`).
## How each frame part was measured

- **Plates.** Diameter, thickness and every hole from the hole tables (05 09 01, 05 08 02-1) at their drilled, reamed or tapped sizes, drawn at the middle of each fit. The sections give the rest: the pillar seats Ø 8.6 × 0.3 in the inner faces (L-M, E-F); the steady pins' holes reamed 1.6025 from the side their part sits on and opened Ø 2.5 × 1.6 from the other (A-B, C-D); the lower cock's screw sunk Ø 6 H7 × 1.8 (G-H); the escape wheel's setting seats (P-Q, G-H: Ø 5.2 H7 with a 0.5 lip of Ø 4.5 or 4.59 H7, two M1.2 3.5 either side); the recess Ø 22 × 0.7 with its 45° wall round the fusee's hole in the top plate's inner face (J-K); the pocket for the balance holding work in the same face, 1.35 −0.05 deep, from the balance opening to hole 51 and round it at r 4, its edges the common tangents of the opening's r 5 and that r 4, so 10 wide at the opening narrowing to 8 at 51 (N-O, with Wempe 3955's inner face, `13_…unten`, which shows it). The top plate is a crescent: the cut round the barrel milled Ø 42.5 about hole 45 runs out through the rim. The pillar plate's opening for the intermediate wheel is milled Ø 24.5, joined by the seconds arbor's hole (punched 6.0) on its rim. The three small windows beside the pillars (Altmeppen's list of German features) were read as the auxiliary holes 3, 12 and 40; the 6MX, which he says lacks them, has those holes (The Soviet 6MX, X1a), so what he meant is unresolved.
- **The pillar plate's hand-setting cut and holes** (16 October 2026). 05 08 02-1 strikes holes 14-18 through ("nur wenn Zeigerstellung eingebaut wird"), but Wempe 3955's plate is cut and drilled for the hand-setting with nothing of it fitted but a bush in 18 (`3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`, `6_Wempe_3955_S.jpg`, `8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg`), and the plate's assembly 05 08 02 draws them so: built so. The holes at the table's places, 14 and 15 drilled 1.45 (steady pins), 16 and 17 drilled 2.8 (screws), 18 reamed 3.50 +0.012 with its bush. The cut lies on the line from the centre through 18, 30.0° below O toward S, the four holes symmetric about it: a window 6.5 square on the dial face (05 08_3b's two "6,5"), its outer edge 8.3 −0.1 from the rim, widened along the cut to 12 +0.2 on the inner face (05 08_3a's Schnitt C-D, which runs along it, and 05 08 02's E-F), each end a Ø 14 arc from one face's edge to the other's, hollow (traced on C-D: each bows 2-3 px into the metal at 9.74 px/mm); 05 08_3a's "9,3 −0,1" is from the inner face's end to the dial face's far edge. Wempe 3955's dial side, fitted on the plate's holes 1, 12, 26, 30, 40 (8.52 px/mm, 0.3 mm rms), puts 14-18 within 0.15-0.6 mm of the table and the window 6.4 × 6.5 at 27.7-33.9 from the centre, its edges at 30.2° (the model's 27.74-34.24): sure. Its bush in 18 there: rim Ø 3.46, bore 1.29 with a bright sink to 1.64 (likely). Not built: C-D's bore Ø 2 at mid-thickness (1.68 from the dial face) from the window's outer wall out through the rim, which a solid of plan cells can't cut across the plate (its mouth shows on the rim's notch on `8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg`). Wempe 3955's notch in the rim where it leaves and the groove in its movement ring under it, on no sheet, are built in its version (below, "Wempe 3955 as photographed"). The Soviet 6MX's plate has none of it (Kirov 247's dial side, `3_Kirow_247_uZ.jpg`, is plain there; Altmeppen lists no external hand-setting): the cut, the holes and the bush in 18 are the drawings' versions' only.
- **Pillars.** Every figure of 05 08 00-1. Its countersinks and the half-round relief grooves in the shoulders are drawn without figures and scaled at 5:1.
- **Movement ring.** Its diameters, heights, screw holes and pin holes from 05 08 00-3 as revised 6.12.46. The sheet's heights fix the profile: the pillar plate's seat Ø 85 H9 is 3 +0.1 deep, the dial's recess Ø 104 +0.1 is 0.7 +0.1 deep, 7 −0.1 lies between their floors, 10.7 −0.1 overall; the flange is 4.7 high. The section in the coloured drawing shows the order: the seat at the movement side, a lip Ø 83 under the plate, a 45° cone opening from it to Ø 93 at the recess. Its three M2.6 on Ø 89 at 340°, 100° and 220° from N toward O (the sheet's 20°, 140°, 260° from 12 seen from the dial side; Wempe 3955's three brass heads at 339°, 99°, 218°). The three run on through the flange until they open in its 45° cone, where Wempe 3955's dial side shows them as dark holes (3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg; the sheet's plan, seen from the dial side, draws them whole): cut with fusee.js's `bore` from a 0.6 start in the flange's face (frame.js, FLC; 25 October 2026, until then stopped at the flange).
- **Barrel bridge.** 05 08 05-1: its chord is 7 from hole 45 and 7.7 from hole 42 (both figures square to it), which puts it 18.493 from the centre, the greatest width 24.002 (the sheet's 24): three figures to 0.002; the sheet's 2:1 view draws it so (45 7.10, 42 7.59, 48 6.84 from the drawn chord, measured at 4.87 px/mm by the holes' spacing). The short edge at the top at 150° to it, 5 from hole 48 (Wempe 3955: 4.95, 150.2°), which leaves the 73.5 from the point to the top corner 73.31. Its outer edge is the plate's rim. Wempe 3955's chord (`2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg`, fitted to the edge and carried onto the sheet's frame by similarities on its screws) lies between this and the chord square to 45's line built until 15 October 2026 (its normal 14.35-14.46° N of W, against 14.94 and 13.61): the sheet decides.
- **Click.** 05 08 05-2 at 5:1: the hole, length, beak, nose and the hollow r 6 from its figures; its back an arc r 12.5, its centre fitted to 37 points traced along it (0.05 mm rms). Its turn on the bridge traced on Wempe 3955 (its nose's centre lands where the photograph has it).
- **Fusee bush and dust bush.** Every figure of 05 08 01-2 and 05 08 01-3. Which way the dust bush's flat faces follows from the upper cock's place: it passes 7.0 from the fusee's axis, where the collar would otherwise stand 0.55 into the cock's web.
- **The cork ring** (Korkring 05 08 01-4, 15 October 2026). No sheet: 05 08 01 names it on the dust bush and the coloured section draws it in section, its label "Korkring 05 08 01-4" (the case-and-box transcription's list reads "05 06 01-4": the section's crop at 8× reads 08). On the section (`Wempe_Einheitschronometer.jpg`, enlarged 8×) the dust bush's own figures give the scale: its body Ø 13.1 is 122 px and its neck Ø 9.7 90.5 px, 9.32 px/mm. The ring is square-cut, hatched as cork: x 720-870.5 px, Ø 16.1 outside, its bore on the neck; y 135-171, 3.86 high, from the collar's top to 0.95 over the neck's top (y 144; the neck's 27 px is its drawn 3.0 to 0.1). Lange No. 5829 side-on (`stahl_113510_lange_4.jpg`) shows it on a real movement: 1.23 times the bush's body across (the section's 16.1/13.1 is 1.23) and about a quarter as high as wide (sure). Its colour from that photograph's lit face (about #d2a25f under warm light; drawn #c8a070, matte).
- **Balance cocks.** Every figure and hole of 05 08 03-1 and 05 08 00-2, their places from their own tables (the same as the plate's). The upper cock's arm is relieved 0.8 underneath within r 11.2 of the setting, a 45° step out to r 12 (05 08 03-1's 0.8, 45° and 12 r; drawn on 127-556.04 U30); the boss under it turned, face Ø 6, its flank a 45° cone to Ø 7.6. The lower cock hangs under the top plate: its holes are all in the top plate's table, its screw comes down from the plate's outer face (G-H), and its lug, at the block's far face, holds the balance's lower setting about 10 under the plate, as the coloured section draws it.
- **Intermediate bridge.** Its holes' places from the pillar plate's table (all six on one line, to 0.001). Its own sheet (05 08 04-1) isn't in the set: its outline and thickness are scaled on the assembly sheet 05 08 04 at 2:1, calibrated on the screws' 34.13 and the pins' 41.13 apart (7.03 px/mm); it lies on the pillar plate's dial side, as 05 08 02 draws it dashed and Wempe 3955's dial side shows it.
- **Screws and steady pins.** Places from the tables; threads from the tables (M2.6, M2, M1.7, M1.4, M1.2). The WN standard parts have no sheets in the set: their heads and lengths are measured on the coloured section and on Wempe 3955's top plate (below). The steady pins are Ø 1.6, pressed into 1.585 −0.005 holes 2.5 deep in their part and a slide fit in the plate's reamed 1.6025, standing 1.3 out (05 08 04, 05 08 05, U30).

## How the going train was measured (P1b)

The sheets: 05 05_1 (Laufwerk, the train in section, 1941: plates drawn 3.0, superseded by the plates' own 3.35), 05 05_2 to _4 (Sekundenrad kompl., the wheel, the pinion revised 24.3.48), _5 to _7 (Zwischenrad), _8 to _10 (Minutenrad); 05 08_2 (Oberplatte kompl.: the bushes WN-300, WN-301), 05 08_10 (Zwischenbrücke kompl.: the bush WN-300 and the jewel), 05 08_32.2 (Unterplatte kompl.: WN-302, the stud bush 05 08 02-2 and the studs); the coloured section for the bearings' names and order; Wempe 3955's photographs for the bushes' oil sinks and the wheels' look (`8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg`, `12_…obere_Platte_oben.jpg`, `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`). Each number is in `js/train.js` beside the code, with its sheet and how sure.

- **Places.** Each arbor exactly at its hole of `WD.H` (1, 38, 35); the centre distances 18.12 and 12.63 (05 05_1) are the tables' to 0.001.
- **Teeth.** Each wheel's count, module (its pitch diameter over its teeth), tip and root diameters and tooth thickness from its table. The form is core.js's cycloidal one (gearOutline's wheel tooth: radial flanks below the pitch circle, the epicycloid of its pinion's rolling circle above), run up to the table's tip: the epicycloid would meet the tooth's middle at 1.60-1.69 m and the tables' tips are at 1.56-1.58 m, so the teeth are the sheets' rounded ogives, just topped. The pinions are gearOutline's leaves (radial flanks, a round end) with their radii put on the sheet's tip and root, long and round-ended as 05 05 03-2's end view draws them. The centre pinion's 14 leaves, tip 6.79 and root 4.24 are on its sheet. **The intermediate pinion's 12 and the seconds pinion's 10 leaves are on no sheet**: (2 × 18.12 − 31.976)/0.35529 = 12.0 and (2 × 12.63 − 22.453)/0.28066 = 10.0, and no other count fits the centres (confirmed; centre 90/12 × 80/10 = 60 turns of the seconds arbor an hour).
- **Heights.** Every shoulder, seat, pinion and collar from the pinion sheets as last revised, the wheels' thicknesses from their sheets. The arbors' train-side shoulders rest on bushes flush with the top plate's inner face; the sheets' lengths then put the dial-side shoulders on: the centre arbor's riveting collar (Ø 3.0 at its foot) on WN-302, set 0.1 under the pillar plate's inner face (05 08 02), 19 (+0.05, framed) from its other shoulder, so 0.07 endshake; the intermediate's on the bridge's bush set 0.7 +0.02 in (05 08 04), 23.06 between the bearings against 23.05 −0.1, 0.06; the seconds' on its jewel 1.7 in (05 08 04), 24.1 against 24.1 −0.1, 0.05 (bom.py measures 0.070, 0.060, 0.050). The centre wheel's rim then stands 0.49 above the pillar plate (05 05_1's "0,5"), the seconds wheel 13.7 under the top plate (05 05_1's 13.8, of 1941), and the seconds wheel's rim clears the intermediate pinion's end by 0.055: its hub, left 0.2 thicker than the rim (the 1.2 sheet, the rim turned to 1.0), stands proud toward the pinion (05 05 01's "0,2"), which is what lets it clear.
- **Wheels.** Five straight spokes, hub, rim and hole from each wheel's sheet; each hub left the sheet metal's thickness, 0.2 proud of the rim on one side (the kompl. sheets' "0,2" and the "10∅", "7∅", "6∅" they draw there): the centre and intermediate wheels' on the dial side, the seconds wheel's toward its pinion.
- **Pivots.** Diameters from the pinion sheets as last revised (the intermediate's 0.85 −0.05 of 1946 over the kompl.'s 0.82 −0.02 of 1940; the seconds' 0.82 −0.02 and 0.76 −0.02); lengths from the kompl. sheets (3): the pinion sheets' 4.5-5.1 are the turned blanks', each with its 60° centre, which the kompl. sheets show cut to 3 and rounded. Tapered as the sheets write ("Kegel 1:90" on the seconds pinion, read on the intermediate's as the same; 1:30 on the centre arbor's).
- **The centre arbor's dial end.** The riveting collar Ø 3.0 at its foot, flared to Ø 3.2 at its top, its flank running on into a V groove 0.99 deep in the wheel's seat under it, the groove's outer wall rising at 34° to the seat's edge, which the riveting spreads over the wheel (05 05 03-2 at 5:1, 141.7 px/mm by the journal's 2.2 and the seat's 5.4; 05 05 03 draws it riveted). The journal "C" Ø 2.2 e8 (05 05 03; 05 05 03-2's fit letter is unreadable) on a 1:30 taper ("Kegel 1:30" along it on both sheets), Ø 2.2 at the collar, to 23 +0.05, and WN-302 bored to it; then the shank Ø 1.8 tapering 1:80, 14.3 long to the cannon tube's top (05 05 03), with a groove under the tube's punch mark (05 05 03, 1943: its square shoulder 6.07 over C, a cone 0.24 deep rising to 6.73), its end crowned with the tube's on one sphere (r 3.1: the arc 05 05 03 draws across both rises 0.35). The tube (Minutenrohr 05 05 03-3, with the cannon pinion turned on its foot) is the motion work's.
- **Bearings.** The top plate's three bushes in its holes reamed 4.59, flush both sides, an oil sink in the outer face as Wempe 3955's plate shows them (`12_…oben`, a cup nearly the bush's width); WN-302 in the pillar plate as 05 08 02's Schnitt A-B draws it (0.1 +0.02 under the inner face, a spherical cup on the dial side, Ø 3.8 × 0.55 scaled against the bush's Ø 4.59 in a schematic section); the bridge's WN-300 at 38 (0.7 +0.02 in, a sink on the dial side, Ø 3.7 on `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`) and the seconds jewel's setting at 35 (its mouth Ø 3.8 on the dial side, the same photograph).
- **Motion.** E, the escape wheel's place in teeth, turns the seconds arbor once in 120 teeth (15 teeth × 80/10), the intermediate once in 960, the centre once in 7,200 (an hour at a tooth a beat); each mesh turns the other way, so the centre and seconds arbors turn clockwise from the dial (rotation.y falling) and the intermediate anticlockwise. The meshes are phased under load as the Hamilton's (`gearLash`): the centre wheel drives the intermediate pinion, the intermediate wheel the seconds pinion.

## How the balance and escapement were measured (P1d)

Sheets as last revised, from `References/wempe/transcriptions/05-03-05-04-balance-escapement.md`, each figure checked on the sheet where it mattered (crops at 2-5×). Which sheets are the detent chronometer's: the 05 03 and 05 04 numbers and the 02 04 series (Kaliber 020); 05 03_1, _5, _7 and 05 04_20, _21 carry the lever chronometer's 127-556.04 numbers but 05 03_5 and _7 draw the same spring (05 03_6's strip, 15 turns) and are used for the spring's heights and curve.

- **Layout** (05 04_1, Hemmung für Feder-Chronometer, 10:1). The balance at hole 23, the escape wheel at 26: the tables put them 10.000 apart, the sheet's "Gangrad–Unruh 10,00". The sheet is drawn from above (as the frame's plans): the wheel turns clockwise there, counterclockwise from the dial, as the train must turn it. The locking stone's centre 7.14 ±0.04 from the wheel's, 37.0 ±0.3° on from the line of centres (scaled against the wheel's tips Ø 14.00 at 24.47 px/mm, checked by the impulse roller's 6.77 against its written 6.78: likely), so the tips lock 0.235 deep, where Belyakov gives Lange's MX 0.22 and the Navy's 1958 repair book sets 0.20-0.25 on the stone; the roller's shake to the teeth either side of the line of centres is then 0.011. Until 19 October 2026 the stone stood 7.2 and 36.5° (24.4 px/mm, the angle inferred from the teeth's clearance to the impulse roller, 0.05 and 0.12), locking 0.175 deep. The roller's "24°" is read as where the balance stands as drawn: the impulse stone 24° before the line of centres, the discharge stone leaving the gold spring. That is the balance at rest here.
- **Balance staff** (05 04 02, Unruhwelle kompl., 29.5.46). Its datum is the hub's flange top: the arm's seat Ø 2 h6 1.1 above it, the hairspring collet's seat 1:70 from Ø 1.58 to Ø 1.65 s6 to 6.1 above, the brass hub (05 04 02-2) Ø 6.3 −0.1 × 1.8 with its two M1 and its neck Ø 2.63 −0.06 to 4.1 +0.05 below and its bore's foot Ø 2.14 +0.01, the impulse roller's seat Ø 1.65 s6 from the hub's end to 5.6 below and the discharge roller's Ø 1.2 r6 under it (the sheet's widths there, 28 and 21 px at 17.6 px/mm; read until 27 October 2026 as a collar Ø 2.14 to 5.6 with the impulse roller's seat under it: Readings corrected), the lower end 14.03 −0.06 below. It is drawn as a blank between centres, its ends (Ø 1.1): the pivots, Ø 0.21 × 0.4 on cones 2 long, are 05 03_3.2's, each end 0.02 off its endstone. 05 03_3.2 (13.3.65, "050401" in pencil, 25.6 overall, the flange in one with the staff) is a later production staff with the same 6.1, 4.1 and 5.6-5.7 (its Ø 1.65 seat 1.6 long from 4.1 to 5.7, the impulse roller's 1.6): 05 04 02 is built because it is of the drawings' time and the coloured section numbers the staff and hub 05 04 02-1 and -2; 05 04_5 (040401-1, 1941, a staff with a brass collar 4.7 to the collar) is superseded by it.
- **Heights.** The staff's place along its axis follows from the hairspring: the stud's bar on the cock's top, the spring's top coil 3.15 and its lower end 13.4 under the bar's underside (05 03_5), the collet's underside 0.75 under the spring's lower end. That puts the arm's top 0.05 under the collet, the rim's lower face 0.45 over the top plate (the coloured section draws 0.53) and the impulse roller's top 0.32 under the plate's inner face, the discharge roller right under it, 1.92-3.52 under it (the coloured section: the impulse roller's top about 0.3 under, both rollers 1.5-1.6 thick one under the other, at 9.4 px/mm; 05 04_1's "4,1 bis Unruhauflage"). The escape wheel is level with the impulse roller (its rim 1.4 inside the roller's 1.6, its teeth 0.4-1.8 under the plate), just under the plate as Wempe No. 9166 of 1972 is made (Wempe 9166 below). Its arbor's place between the plates isn't printed (05 04_7.2 has 2.9 of room between their outer faces): set by the wheel, its pivot ends lie 1.98 into the top plate and 1.55 into the pillar plate from its dial face (05 04_7.1's 2.0 and 1.39), 22.78 apart (05 04_7.2: 22.8 −0.02), the wheel's pinion-side face 19 from the pinion end (so its other face 2.38 from the top plate's end; the sheet's "2" runs from that end to the arbor's shoulder, 0.4 under the wheel), the pinion's face 5.3 from the pinion end (all sure). Until 27 October 2026 the rollers hung 1.5 lower, under the misread collar, the impulse roller 1.8-3.4 under the plate, the wheel's teeth 1.89-3.29, its arbor's ends 0.5 and 0.06 inside the plates (until 15 October 2026 the wheel 2 from the end, its teeth 1.51-2.91 under the plate, the "2" read to the wheel).
- **Balance** (05 03 01-1, Unruhreifen: nickel steel and brass). Every figure of its sheet: Ø 32.32 −0.04, the joint Ø 31.29 −0.04, inside Ø 30.48 +0.04, 3.8 high; the arm in one with the steel, across the rim's lower face, 1.15 thick, 3.2 wide at the rim, 4 where it swells in two hollow arcs r 20, their centres outside the arm (marked on the sheet 7.2 along and 21.95 across from the centre), to a point 6.5 across at the hub ("6,5"; traced: sure; Wempe 3955's arm shows the hollow flanks; until 15 October 2026 convex arcs to 6.17, "6,5" read as where the arm is 4 wide); the hub's hole 2 H7 and the screws' holes Ø 1 at 2.14 either side; the cuts 1.5 +0.2 at 90° from the arm (built 1.6, the fit's middle, since 25 October 2026; 1.5 before); the timing screws' holes Ø 0.9 × 0.16 2.15 over the rim's lower face. The arm lies along 12-6 at rest (05 03_1's note on the timing screws). Wempe 3955's balance (`9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`) shows the same: the steel arm, four brass masses across the rim, two cylindrical timing weights at the arm's ends, the rim cut beside the masses.
- **Masses and timing weights** (05 03 01-4, 05 03 01-2/-3; 05 03_2, a poor copy). The masses Ø 6.3 (scaled at 5:1), their centres on the rim's middle, held by a screw and nut across the rim, the screw 2.45 over the rim's lower face (05 03_2's slotted head 2.83, Wempe 3955's 2.55, Lange 5829's 2.0); the screw's slotted head, Ø 1.6, sunk flush in the mass's outer face and nothing standing out of the inner (Wempe 3955, `9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`: sure), the nut sunk the same way (estimated; until 15 October 2026 head and nut stood 0.55 out). The timing screws' slotted heads inside the rim on its inner face (05 03_2's plan, Ø 1.65 × 1.3 scaled), the weights on them outside, their tips 42.1 over both (likely); the weights split by two saw cuts across them, 1.3 deep from the outer end and 1.7 from the inner, at right angles (05 03_2 and Wempe 3955's weights, the outer cut across the whole end face with the screw's tip in it: likely), Ø 3.8 × 3.3, 0.8 short of the tip (the sheet scales Ø 3.5-4.3 × 3.1-3.3, the photographs Ø 3.0-3.9: unsure, ±0.4; until 15 October 2026 Ø 3.0 × 4.0, uncut, the head outside the weight). The coloured section's labels make 05 03 01-2 the screw and -3 the weight.
  - *Their height* (15 October 2026, likely): **from the rim's lower face to 6.46 over it**. 05 03_2's side view (12.18 px/mm by the rim's 3.8 between its two faces' lines) draws the near pair, whole, from the rim's lower face to 6.40 (the end's chamfer, about 0.4, is a second line to 6.0), and the far pair from 1.24: that is the arm's cut section (1.15 thick, hatched) hiding their foot, so the "5.4 from 1.1" P1d built was the hidden pair's outline. Wempe 3955's side view (`5_Wempe_3955_O.jpg`, about 25° above the plate; each mass's height against its own width, and the hairspring's Ø 12.8 for the scale, 10.1 px/mm) gives height/diameter 0.98 and 1.00 for its two front masses, 6.2-6.3 high (5.9-6.1 with the scale corrected for their nearer place); Lange 5829's (`stahl_113510_lange_4.jpg`, nearly side-on) 0.92, 5.8. Wempe 3955's balance off the movement (`9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`, seen from the arm's side) shows each mass's face flush with the rim's face within about 0.3 (no wall of the rim shows over it) on its far mass, 0.6-0.7 on its near ones (in the slot's shadow, unsure). Built 0-6.46, the far pair's top kept (both outlines end there); the chamfer at the top is left out (about 1 % of a mass).
  - *Their place* (15 October 2026, likely): **30° from the 3-9 line** (the cuts'). Wempe 3955's balance (`9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`) rectified by the affine map that takes its rim's two cuts (at r 15.7) and the arm's two ends (at the rim's inside, 15.24) to their plan places, all on the rim's lower face where the masses' faces lie: the four masses at 28.2°, 32.0°, 30.0° and 29.6° from the cuts' line (each ±1-2°: their faces' centres read by eye to 2-3 px at 15.5 px/mm), at r 15.0-16.5 (15.7 built), two either side of each cut. The 6MX's book draws 30° (Fig. 5.62); 05 03_2 scales 32.5 (P1d built 32). The rim's graduation ("8 Körner in 60 Teilung") isn't resolved in any photograph (a division is 1.6 mm, the punch marks under a pixel).
- **Hairspring** (05 03 02-1, Spiralfaden 02 03 10): the strip 0.52 × 0.285, Ø 12.8 outside, 0.21 between coils, 15 turns left-hand as wound, the whole strip cut to 13 turns less 3.5 mm before the curves are bent (05 03_7; phase 2c: 11.80 turns of helix between them, 505.4 mm pin to pin; phase 2a and P1d read it as 12.911 turns of helix, the curves added: below, Physics: hairspring and temperature). Both ends follow 05 03_7's table of radii to the strip's inside every 10°, from 6.12 at 0° (the coil's inside, 6.115) to 3.37 at 230-250°, pinned at 220°. The inner end ends 3.51 from the staff, where the collet's hole is (05 03 02-2: 3.5 −0.02). The outer end follows the same table into the stud, 3.55 from the staff, where the stud's leaning upright brings its slot (05 03 02-3; until 15 October 2026 drawn in proportion to end 3.87 out). Going up the coils turn counterclockwise seen from the dial ("links"); the collet's turn on the staff is set by the 4,691° from one pinning point to the other, which leaves its pin end 10.8° from under the stud (05 03_1 and 05 03_5 draw them together). The spring's top curve stands 3.99 under the stud's bar, in the stud's slot (05 03_5 draws 3.15 with its 9.5 high helix; the stud's pin hole is drilled at assembly, 05 03 02-3, and 05 03_1's stud height is "6≈ ... übereinstimmend mit ... Schwingungen", set by the spring as vibrated).
- **Collet** (05 03 02-2, Spiralrolle): every figure; traced (34.5 px/mm), both ends arcs about the bore (sure), the bore in the middle of its length, 4.55 from each end (4.58 and 4.52 traced: likely), the slit stopping 0.1 +0.05 short of the bore (until 15 October 2026 the ends straight, the bore 4.25 from the wide end, the slit into the bore). **Stud** (05 03 02-3, Klötzchen): every figure; the bar 1.6 thick, 2.5 wide at its square end and 3.1 at its hole (5 from that end), its point two flat facets from the plan's V (apex 6.6 on from the hole) to a knife edge, meeting along the middle (the section; Wempe 3955 shows them); its upright, 1.8 g6, leaning 8° toward the axis (98°), slides in the cock's slot (05 08 03-1: 1.8 H7, across the arm on the setting's line, to 3.4 from the axis on the top, its inner wall at 82°, Schnitt C-D), the bar on the cock's top along the arm toward the foot (Wempe 3955: along the cock's axis, its square end 1.1 past the setting's line, its middle 4.1 off the axis), the spring's end in the upright's slot with its pin. The plan's Ø 0.8 round at the point is left out (the facets leave it no room).
- **Rollers.** The impulse roller (02 04 02, Heberolle, revised 10.9.41: beryllium copper): Ø 6.78 ±0.01 × 1.6 +0.1, bored 1.627/1.65 on 1:70; the hollow's bottom r 3.5 between corners 3.5 apart, the stone's face 2 from one of them; the stone 0.5 × 1.8 from 1.55, 1.7 wide, its end bevelled 45° to 1.3 at its back (r 2.85; traced: sure; until 15 October 2026 to r 3.2); the hole Ø 1.3. The discharge roller (05 04 04-1) has no sheet: scaled on 05 04_1's dashed outline, 2.77 × 2.15, 1.6 thick (the elevation's lower "1,6"), its stone 1.7 wide; brass, as Wempe 3955's block beyond its impulse roller (likely; steel until 15 October 2026).
- **Escape wheel** (05 04_7.2, 05 04 05-1, 19.7.43; tooth form 05 04_23, 02 04 08a, 20.1.42). 15 teeth, tips Ø 14 +0.03; the gaps' floor Ø 11.7, the locking face straight down to it, leaning 25° back from the radius through the tip (05 04_23's "25°"), its root 5.38° behind the tip; the tip flat 0.1834 (1½°); the back hollow, the ∅ 6 cutter's arc (traced r 3.02) to Ø 12.1 15° behind the tip ("12,1", "15°"), where a step 0.2 falls to the floor, the tooth 1 wide there. The sheet's lines traced and circles fitted at 82.8 px/mm, rms 0.25 px (sure); until 15 October 2026 the floor was read as Ø 12.1, the face leaning 15° (root 2.43° behind), the back to 11.9° and a step 0.15 up to 12.4. 1.4 thick at the teeth and 0.8 in the web, the recess on the plate's side Ø 11.62 (05 04_7.2's dashed recess scales 11.65; drawn 0.04 inside the floor), flush on the pinion's side: 05 04_1's elevation draws the teeth standing 0.6 over the web toward the plate, 0.5 under it. Three openings, each bounded by the rim's inside and one arc r 7.9 centred 9.43 out on its middle line, coming within 1.53 of the centre (05 04_23's crossing traced: its flanks r 7.93 and 7.92, centres 9.43 out; 05 04_1 1.54: sure), the crossings 0.6 wide at the rim and 0.53 at their narrowest (until 15 October 2026 two arcs meeting at a point 1.4 from the centre). Its pinion (05 04 05-2): 10 leaves of m 0.263, tips Ø 2.97, 4.0 long (05 04_7.2 scaled: 71 px at 17.76 px/mm by its 22.8, the 5.3 and the wheel's 1.4 agreeing; Wempe 3955's 4.3 ±0.3 and the 6MX's 3.9: likely; until 25 October 2026 05 04_7.1's 3, which left 0.3 of the seconds wheel's 1.0 rim past the leaves); pivots Ø 0.24 × 0.5; the arbor Ø 1.0 under the wheel to its shoulder 2 from the end, then a cone to the pivot; the collet (05 04 05-3, 05 04 10 on 05 04_7.2), brass, 4.7 long: Ø 2.8 for 1.6 over the wheel, then Ø 1.7, each step chamfered 0.15 (scaled at 17.6 px/mm; until 15 October 2026 a cone Ø 2.6 to 1.5).
- **Detent** (1436, Gangfeder, Rud. Grießbach 17.2.43). Each figure is followed by a second in brackets, written later in another hand outside the dimension lines: 0,25 (0,22), 0,33 (0,28), 0,55 (0,50), 0,65 (0,60), 0,8 (0,75), 2 (1,9), 1 (0,9), 5,4 (4,9). Every bracketed value is the smaller, by 0.03-0.1 (0.5 on the 5.4). They are read as the detent as revised, a lighter detent, and built: foot 0.60 × 1.9 × 6.6, spring 1.9 × 5, 0.09 thick at each end and 0.07 at its middle, straight between (1436's left view: sure; built so since 25 October 2026, until then 0.07 throughout; makeEsc and the frame take the uniform strip as stiff, 0.0792, the moment along so short a spring nearly even), square 1.9 × 2, blade 0.50 then 0.28, 0.9 wide, flush with the lower edge of the foot, spring and square (the right view), pipe Ø 1.15, horn 0.22 × 0.75 at the blade's middle (until 25 October 2026 the foot 2, the blade 1 and the horn 0.8 tall, the unbracketed figures). The lengths 1436 doesn't figure are scaled on it (17.3 px/mm): the spring 5.0, the blade's thicker part 2.9, pipe to square 8.4 (8.07 here, where the block puts it).
- **Gold spring** (05 04 01-2, Goldblech 333 federhart): every figure (0.15 thick, the arm 1 wide flush with one edge of its head 2 × 2.4, the hole Ø 0.7 1.2 from the head's end, 15.4 overall; about 15 here). Its tip 1.54 from the staff is traced on 05 04_1; its free part is one smooth bow round the pipe to the tip, which alone rests on the horn's, traced on Wempe 3955's detent seen square-on along its pipe (`10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg`, flattened into the detent's frame at 80 px/mm: the strip's middle between its edges, traced by hand in `tools/tracer.html` (`tools/traces/wempe_detent10.mm.json`) and by its yellow, CIELAB b*, the two 0.02-0.04 apart), pinned at its bending point and its tip by taking off a line (distances across that photograph run 0.3-0.4 large where the parts stand off the steel: its arm reads 0.4 off the square's face it is screwed to), so it closes onto the horn with the gap between them narrowing to the tip, as the photograph shows it (likely, ±0.05 in shape); the 6MX's beside it the same. Until 18 October 2026 a polyline lying 3 along the horn and cranked out by the pipe; then, that day, the bow traced once from a pipe centre about 0.3 off the blade's line, the same round the pipe to 0.03 but meeting the traced horn before its tip.
- **Detent block** (05 04 01-4, Gangfederkloben, 8.7.43). Every figure (tolerance ±0.1): the base 14.6 × 7.5 (25 overall less 17.5), 6.5 deep for 6.4 from its end ("6,5", drawn 6.4) and 3 deep beyond ("3"), the thin part on the plate (sure; until 15 October 2026 6.5 deep over its whole length), the upright 3 × 3 from 1.4 +0.1 in from the base's end, its far side stepped out 0.35 at 2.5 over the base and joined to it by a cove r 3 to 2.0 out and 0.4 over the base, then straight down, flush with the 6.5-deep part's end (the front view's arc fitted: r 2.96, its centre in the air; a cove r 3 tangent to both until 27 October 2026), its end slit 0.3 for 7 with an M1.2 across at 18.8 ±0.03 from the base's hole row, the fixing M1.7 at 7.3 +0.04, the hole Ø 1.3 × 3 and the M1 into it from the end, the steady pins Ø 0.9 at 2.3 and 12.3, 2 either side of the row. Screwed up through hole 13, the detent's foot clamped to the base's end. Wempe 3955's block (`10_`, `11_Wempe_3955_*`) is the earlier one of 05 04_1 (a plate with a long arm and a brass cone at the stone): not built.
- **The banking, the horn, the gold spring's tip and the preload** (15 October 2026, from the photographs of detents on their blocks: `10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg`, Wempe 3955's beside a 6MX's; `8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg`, a 6MX's; `11_Wempe_3955_Gangfeder_plus.jpg`; crops in the session's scratchpad):
  - *The banking is the block's cross screw* (likely). The 6MX's block, which is 05 04 01-4's form (the base with its fixing screw between two steady pins at opposite corners, the upright with its fillet, the foot clamped to the base's end by a screw along it), carries a screw through its upright with a domed brass head on the detent's far side, about Ø 3.7 and 1.7 deep against the upright's 3 (31 px/mm), its thread standing out behind; the detent's pipe rests on the head's flat face. Wempe 3955's earlier block has the same: a domed brass head beside the stone, its pointed tail (05 04_1's "pointed piece" on the pipe, P1d's "brass cone") through a slit in the block's arm. The 1954 instruction (п. 11: "Винтом-ограничителем … устанавливается глубина сцепления камня покоя") and the 6MX's book (p. 168, screw 12) say a banking screw sets the depth of lock. So 05 04 01-4's M1.2 across the slit upright, 18.8 from the base's hole row, where the stone is, is that screw, the slit gripping its thread: it bears on the pipe at the stone, on the wheel's side. Drawn whole since 27 October 2026 in every version but the first drawn (the escapement of 1943's: its domed slotted head Ø 3.4, the pipe's side on it, the slit gripping its thread), the block read in first-angle projection (DIN's), the upright at the free face and the screw 5.0-5.15 under the plate, below the wheel; until then the last revised block was read in third angle, the upright flush with the plate, and its screw a plain shank bearing on nothing (Readings corrected: the 1946 staff). The physics takes it: the frame's banking (tools/wempe_tripping.js) is now one contact, the pipe's side on the head at the stone (until now under the blade 0.7-1.4 from it, where makeEsc puts the Hamilton's stop button). makeEsc and dynamics.js take the detent rigid, so where its banking is doesn't change them.
  - *The horn* is **4.4 long from the stone** (likely): 1436's figure (its drawing scales 5.2, not to scale there). Wempe 3955's detent seen along its pipe (1436's left view: the horn curving with the gold spring, the "Anschrägen") measures 4.4-4.55 from the pipe's centre to the horn's and the gold spring's tips (21.7 px/mm by the pipe's Ø 1.15, ±8 %; by the pipe-to-square 8.4 scaled on 1436, 4.2). On both photographed detents the gold spring's tip ends just past the horn's (under 0.3). Its line in plan (18 October 2026): on the blade's line at the pipe, bending steadily toward the block, 0.06 off at 2 from the stone, 0.20 at 3, 0.37 at 3.7, to its end where the gold spring's tip rests (0.55 off, its face on GNB as before): traced twice on the same photograph flattened, by hand (its centre line) and by the steel's edge against the gap beside the gold spring, each from the blade's line beside it, the two 0.05-0.1 apart, fitted as one curve (rms 0.05 and 0.02). 1436's left view draws the bend later, over its last quarter (0.06 at 4.0 of its 5.2 drawn, 0.45-0.5 at the top), not to scale along there. Until that day a step at the pipe and a straight bar 0.54 off the blade's line (its end's place). At 4.4 the horn's end is 1.598 from the staff, 0.038 outside the discharge stone's reach (1.560): the stone clears it on the return swing, and the gold spring's tip (1.54) stands 0.056 past it. P1d drew 3.5, 1 short of the tip, reading 4.4 as reaching the stone's path. The 6MX's horn measures about 3.5 (its detent is shorter: 6.4 from the pipe to the square against 1436's 8.4).
  - *The gold spring's tip*: square, as 05 04 01-2 cuts it (sure for the blank). Wempe 3955's tip, the only one seen, looks square or slightly oblique at 21.7 px/mm: a rounding under 0.1 can't be seen. Kept square (makeEsc's `TSQ`).
  - *The preload* stays an estimate (0.8 g at the stone since 19 October 2026, just over the margin's floor with the deeper lock: The physics pass; 0.63 g before). The 6MX's detent on its block rests with its blade about 2° (±1°) out of line with its foot, toward its banking: so the spring is set (bent) to press the pipe onto the head, as a straight detent couldn't rest there; its free line isn't shown, so the preload can't be read. 05 04 01-4's end face, where the foot is clamped, is "90° +0.5°": the foot may be set up to 0.5° out of square, which would be about a quarter of the estimate's bend (2.05°); taken as a tolerance, not a set.
- **Jewels.** The coloured section labels each bearing's hole jewel (Lagerstein 05 04 06-1, the balance's 05 04 07-1) in its setting (Fassung 05 04 06-2) and the endstone (Deckstein 05 04 08-1) in its own (05 04 08-2); none has a sheet in the set. The settings are sized to their seats (Ø 5.2 H7 in the cocks and the top plate; the pillar plate's hole 26, reamed 2.50) and drawn as the coloured section draws them. The stones themselves are traced on the section's red (18 October 2026, row by row, at 9.4 px/mm, which gives the cocks' seats Ø 5.25 for their 5.2 H7, so the section is to scale there; ±1 px, 0.11), the form each end the same: the hole jewel Ø 2.9, 0.8 thick, its face to the endstone domed 0.3 and an oil sink opening into its face toward the arbor, a cone from the hole to Ø 1.6 0.55 deep, the olive hole 0.25 long at the crown, 0.03 off the endstone; the endstone Ø 2.9, flat to the pivot, its edge 0.5 and its outside domed 0.4. Wempe 3955's top plate (`2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg`, enlarged 10×) shows both upper endstones whole, the balance's Ø 2.95-3.1 in a dark bevel Ø 4.4-4.5, the escape wheel's Ø 2.95-3.2 in one Ø 3.7: the endstones' settings open in a cone from a lip over the stone's edge, as the section draws the cock's; the balance's stone colourless, the pivot's hole dark through it (Kirov 247's faceted and colourless; both sure: drawn clear, the ruby hole jewel under it showing through, since 18 October 2026; opaque steel before) (the lower balance endstone's setting a bore of the stone's size, Ø 3.0, as the section draws it). Where the sheets leave less room the form is scaled: the escape wheel's lower hole jewel to the pillar plate's 2.50 hole (Ø 1.6, 0.45 thick: the section draws that end as the upper, Ø 2.9 in a setting Ø 5.1, which the sheet's hole can't take), its upper as last revised to the 0.45 its arbor's end leaves under the inner face (0.4). The seconds jewel (05 05 04-1) is flat on the arbor's side and sunk on the dial side, a cone to Ø 1.7 0.6 deep.
- **Motion** (`WESC.ESC.state(p, amp)`, p the phase of the balance's 0.5 s period, as the Hamilton's: th = −A cos 2πp, the active swing for p < ½). The swing A is solved from the drive (Physics); until phase 2a it was set at 225° each side. The discharge stone's end meets the gold spring's tip 12° before rest, pushing the gold spring against the horn and turning the detent about the middle of its spring. The tooth is free when the stone has lifted 0.185 (0.175 of lock and 0.01), 3° before rest. The stone is fully lifted, 0.245, as the discharge stone leaves the gold spring at rest (the drawn instant). The wheel then drops onto the impulse stone at 21° before the line of centres and follows it, the tip sliding on the stone's face, until the tip leaves the stone's end 21.2° past. It runs on to the next lock in 3° more of the balance. The escaping tooth's back holds the stone up while it passes under it. On the return swing the discharge stone bends the gold spring away from the horn, from about 7° to 20° before rest, and the detent stays down. The escape wheel's E (teeth) is the Hamilton's: E = beats − 1 + progress. Its turn, 24° a beat, drives the train through `update()` (the train phase).

## How the fusee and barrel were measured (P1c)

The sheets, each as last revised: 05 06_1 (Schnecke kompl., 1941: the order along the arbor and its fits), 05 06_2.2 (the body 05 06 04-2, revised 27.2.48), 05 06_3.2 (Schnecke 05 06 04, revised 2.3.48), 05 06_4 (the partial views: the maintaining work, Schnitt A-B, and the stop work, Schnitt C-D), 05 06_5 to _8 (dust plate, Vorreiber, fusee wheel, cover plate), 05 06_9, _10 (the maintaining spring), _11 to _14 (the maintaining ratchet, its clicks and their springs), _15 (the arbor 05 06 04-1), _16 (the click wheel 05 06 04-3, 6.8.47), _20 (the fusee's curve and its force, Herb. Müller 24.7.41); 05 05_11, _12 (the maintaining detent 05 05 05); 05 07_1 to _14 (the barrel 05 07 01 as revised 20.4.48, its drum and lid, the arbor 05 07 02 and 020704-1, the set-up ratchet, the band 05 07 03 and its hooks, the chain 05 07 04). Every number is in `js/fusee.js` beside the code, with its sheet and how sure; the coloured section gives the order of the parts along each arbor where no sheet does.

- **Places.** The fusee at hole 10, the barrel at 45, the maintaining detent at 4 (both plates drill it 0.9), the stop pin at 7: `WD.H`.
- **The stack along the fusee arbor.** The arbor's 19.7 +0.05 between its shoulders (05 06 04-1) fills the 19.75 from the pillar plate to the fusee bush's end (below), so it has 0.05 endshake. Its cross hole, 2 from the dial-side shoulder (19.7 less the 17.7 −0.05 to the far shoulder), takes the Vorreiber's pin, whose centre is 1.1 ±0.1 from the Vorreiber's hub face (05 06 00-2; the coloured section draws it so). The fusee body's large face, re-turned on the arbor at assembly, lies 13.6 from the end of the arbor's Ø 6.1 step (05 06 04's "13,7 −0,1", below: Readings corrected), so the face is 6.1 from the dial-side shoulder. Face a, under the fusee wheel, is to be re-turned at the fusee's assembly (05 06 00-2's note) to fit what lies between it and the fusee's face: 2.76 from the hub face (the flange 0.86 of its 1.2 −0.2), which puts the fusee wheel's underside 2.385 off the plate. Upward from there each part rests on the one below with 0.05 between (none given): the maintaining spring (1.3) in the wheel's 32.3 recess on its web, the ratchet on the wheel's rim, the fusee body's large end on the ratchet's plate, the click wheel on the floor of the body's recess (Ø 26.3 × 2.7 as turned, 2.5 under the re-turned face), the cover plate on the body's small end. The maintaining detent, at 6.2 of its 6.3 ±0.1 (05 05 05), lays its tip on the ratchet's teeth over 0.85 of their 1.02, and the cover plate's top comes 0.425 under the J-K recess's floor. The coloured section agrees (wheel 2.4 off the plate, the ratchet 4.8 to 6.1, the detent 4.7 to 6.0 by its scale, and an empty 0.5 of the J-K recess over the cover plate). Until 25 October 2026 face a was 2.9, the wheel 2.5 off the plate, the fusee's face 13.46 from the step and not re-turned, the detent at 6.3 and the cover plate 0.085 under the recess's floor.
- **The fusee body.** 05 06 04-2's figures: the flange Ø 35.6 with its two ribs and the slot between them (2 ±0.02 over both: the first rib 0.76, its "0,8" the nominal, the slot 0.87 +0.01, the second rib 0.37; the slot's floor r 17.25), the 45° chamfer from Ø 29.8 into the large end's recess Ø 26.3, 2.7 deep (12.6 less 9.9), the bore 7 H7 (straight on this issue), the small end's recess Ø 15 × 1.6, its holes (cut 25 October 2026; until then left out, the screws and the chain's pin lying in the metal, as "a turned body can't be holed across"): the three M2 for the click wheel's screws on r 5.75, 6 +0.5 deep from the large end's recess floor, at 74°, 194° and 314° from the chain's pin A (Schnitt C-D, seen from the small end, draws them at 16°, 137° and 259° with A at 90°: likely ±2°; until then 47° from there, which would have broken the cover plate's M1.7 into the nearest), the M1.7 on C-D's "6 r" circle 6 deep from the small end's recess floor (A-B), the chain's pin's hole Ø 0.55 +0.01 axially through both flange ribs, and the stop bar's spring's pin's hole 0.5 H7 along the slot from the recess out through the end; drawn at their threads' sizes and 0.02 (the frame's rule), flat at the drill's depth. The body is three closed pieces holed as solids (fusee.js `bore`, a facePocket that splits only what lies near the hole: the flange's lathe, the rest swept ray by ray, and the tube between the bore and r 7.5 as plan cells), 12.6 −0.1 overall (drawn 12.55; the assembly's bracketed 12.3 is a reference). The groove: the nine "Grund" radii (14.75, 13.32, 12.17, 11.24, 10.55, 10.07, 9.71, 9.47, 9.30 from the first turn to the ninth, the same on 05 06_20 but its 10.04 at the sixth) at whole turns, 05 06_20's curve between them (scaled at quarter turns, 0.035 high there against the written values, taken off); r 17.25 at turn 0, the flange's slot. A square-cut helix: the pitch 1.2 ±0.01 (02-2's "1,2" from the second rib's outer face to the first groove rib's far face: a land and its rib), each land 0.87 wide (1.2 less the rib) with a rib 0.33 thick on its small-end side standing 0.6 over it ("alle Maße: +0,6"); the lands the slot's width, so the chain (0.82 ±0.02) runs in a 0.87 channel on the whole fusee, 0.03-0.07 clear. Turn 0 lies in the slot, turn 1 on the first land (2.0 to 2.87, the section's "1,2"), and the groove's last turn, 8.75, ends 12.17 from the large end, 0.08 short of "Gangende Punkt N" 0.3 from the small end (the pitch's ±0.01 is ±0.09 over the turns); full wind, where the stop holds the fusee, falls 0.112 turn short of it (the stop work, below). The chain's first 0.15 turn leaves the slot's middle level, where its hook lies on the pin, and rises into the helix at its pitch. Sure of the figures; the 1,2 as every turn's pitch likely: the only reading in which the slot, the 1,2 and point N agree (16 October 2026; until then the pitch was 02-1's pencilled 1.15 and the lands 0.82, which cut the second rib to 0.14 and needed the slot narrowed to 0.8). Under the chain's pin (Ø 0.55, 1 under the ribs' tops, across both ribs) the slot is cut deeper by a cutter Ø 13.5, 2.9 deep, its arc centred on the pin in view C-D: section A-B, through the pin, draws the ribs whole with the slot about 2.8 deep between them (scaled), so it is a slitting cutter in the slot, where the hook wraps the pin, not a flat across the ribs. Built ray by ray on that radius field, 216 rays a turn, each ray's outer line flat along the axis but at the groove's walls, where it has a corner pair at the wall's own depth, its foot and its top, rays joined wall to wall as a thread's flanks are (fusee.js `helixSolid`, 27 October 2026): the walls square and on the helix. Until then a lathe pushed out to the field, rows 0.04 apart, as the Hamilton's fusee is, each wall a 0.04 slope between rows whose edge jumped 0.04 as the helix crossed a row, every rib's edge stair-stepped (the drawings check, batch 5); the floor is the same (`tools/wempe_fusee_profile.py`: within 0.001 of 05 06_20 over the working turns, rms 0.0004, the pitch 1.2000; within 0.025 before). The groove is cut only to N (turn 8.817, its land's edge 0.3 from the small end); past N, and over the last land's rib, the end is the uncut blank at the hub's Ø 21, so the hub's inner face follows the helix: Schnitt A-B draws the last land against a block at Ø 21, 0.3 thick at D and about 1.3 at C (scaled at 12.1 px/mm: likely), no rib between them (until 17 October 2026 the hub was 0.3 all round with the groove cut on under it, and the stop bar's slot cut a loose flange: RESOLVED.md).
- **The arbor.** Every figure of 05 06 04-1: the stub Ø 1.6 +0.1/+0.05 for the up-and-down pinion (the motion work's, P1e), the pivot Ø 2.8 ±0.02 to 2.7 (1:70, 6.5) in the pillar plate, the Vorreiber's seat 4.38 to 4.5 (1:70), the collar Ø 7.04 −0.01 × 7.9 pressed in the body, the step Ø 6.1 −0.05 × 3.1 for the cover plate, the journal 4.5 to 4.39 (1:70, 8) in the fusee bush, the square 3.17 r6 tapering 1:100. Its 60.6 overall counts two lathe centres: the dial end's is cut off (its end 0.25 under the dial), the square's end is that centre's point, a cone cut through the square so its flats end in curves, as the sheet draws them (15 October 2026; a 1 mm pyramid until then): scaled at 7.0 px/mm (the 60.6 over 424.6 px; the 28.3 gives 6.9), the tip at x 585, the flats' ends 2.0 and the corners' 2.43 from it, a cone of half-angle 38.7° fitting both to 0.2 (likely: a pixel is 0.14). Kirow 247's square ends so (`4_Kirow_247_N.jpg`, sure); the coloured section's dome is pictorial. Its cross hole "1,1 bohren + konisch reiben" 2 from the dial-side shoulder, at the Vorreiber's pin's turn, is cut (25 October 2026), drawn straight at the drill's Ø 1.1 (the taper the reamer leaves at assembly isn't given); the Vorreiber's own Ø 0.8 hole across its hub (05 06 00-2, 1.1 from the hub face) is cut too; its pin stays drawn as its two ends in the hub (whole, it would bind the arbor and the Vorreiber, which the Exploded view parts along the arbor).
- **The fusee wheel.** Its table (z 90, D 40.57, d 39.21, dg 37.84, l 0.64, m 0.4356) on core.js's cycloidal form with the centre pinion's 14 leaves as its mate (train.js's `teeth`, copied), phased against the centre pinion with `gearLash`; the rim 2.62, the ledge 0.2 under it out of Ø 33.8, the web 1.22 inside Ø 32.3, the hub Ø 16.6 as thick as the rim, the underside recess Ø 14 × 1.3; the tube Ø 6.5 g6 to 5 from the underside and the bore 4.55 from the 1941 assembly's fits (4.45 H7/g6, 6.5 H7/g6), where 05 06 00-3's own figures are overwritten or can't pass the arbor (README, below).
- **The maintaining work.** The ratchet 05 06 02-1 (120 teeth Ø 37.25, 0.7 deep, the plate 1.02 at the teeth and 1.3 inside the cup, the cup Ø 26.1/24.45, 3.1 overall, its outer top edge 0.3 × 45°), its clicks' screws on 19.5 (M1.7 tapped, the screws' shanks threaded and matched to it, 27 October 2026), its click springs' pins at 42° and 60°, its pin hole at r 14.2; the clicks (05 06 02-3) traced on their 10:1 sheet between its figures (10.05 long, the hole 5.2 from nose and tail, the back r 8, the hollow r 3 about 2.78, 3.54, whose chord is the base line 1.52 under the hole; the nose 2 high, its face at 85° leaning back toward the pivot); the click springs (05 06 02-2: arcs r 12.0 and 10.4, the S round two 2.2 mandrels) laid along the cup's wall as 05 06 02 draws them, each with its two holes Ø 0.6 +0.02 on the cup's two pins (05 06 02-2: one 5 from the end, the end's "0,6∅" the other's, 18° from it; 27 October 2026); the maintaining spring (05 06 01-1: Ø 32.3, 2 wide, its eye r 1.8, its holes on r 14.2 at 0° and 65°, its inner edge three arcs traced on the sheet: r 10.41 from the eye, tangent to the inner circle 50° on, so the band widens toward the eye (3.16 at 10°, where it was 2 until 27 October 2026), the swell's r 6 about the small circle on the 9 o'clock line, and r 13.24 from its corner to the square end's 4; the outer edge running into the eye's round by an arc tangent to the ring 60° from the eye (`tools/wempe_maintspring.py`; Readings corrected); its eye's pin up into the ratchet and the other down into the wheel, 05 06_4 A-B: Readings corrected); the click wheel (05 06 04-3: 35 teeth Ø 16.0/14.5, undercut 8°, each tip overhanging its gap, on Ø 11.5's three screws, its bore's 0.3 +0.2 × 45° chamfer on the free face, the face the screw holes open 1:8 toward: drawn 0.4, since 25 October 2026). The clicks' pivot holes sunk 45° to Ø 1.7 on one face and opened to Ø 2.2 on the other (05 06 02-3's edge view: the sink 0.24 deep, the cone 0.42; read in first angle, the 2.2 on the free face toward the small end and the 1.7 on the cup's floor: likely; 25 October 2026). The click springs (05 06 02-2) as drawn: from their end 5 along r 12.0 to the pin hole (on the cup's 60° pin), 5 more to the S, the S in to r 10.4 over 8.5° (05 06 02's assembly), and the long arm to its tip on the click's back 110° round from the hole (the part's "110°"; the assembly draws the tip there): developed 26.6 against the sheet's "gestreckt 27,0" (until 25 October 2026 the S 3.6 past the pin and the tip a third of the way along the back from the nose, 99° from the hole: 24.4). The drawings of this work are seen from the fusee's small end, the movement's back (the top plate's side): mirrored here. The two clicks stand half a tooth apart on the 35-tooth wheel, so one bears while the other rides; both are seated each frame on the click wheel's outline (`seat()`), as the detent is on the ratchet's.
- **The maintaining detent's wire spring** (Feder 05 05 06-1, the coloured section's; no sheet). Its hole is the top plate's 5 (05 08_1a: "Drahtfeder", N 38.507 O 10.684, M1.7, 4.73 from the detent's hole 4; 05 06_4's "Drahtfedermitte"), empty on Wempe 3955's plate off (13_…obere_Platte_unten.jpg). The stud and wire were measured on three movements from the north (4_Wempe_3955_N, 4_Kirow_247_N, 4_Lange_5177_N) and from above (2_*_A), each scaled by the hole table's own spacing of holes 3, 4, 5 and 49 on the same photograph (8.8 px/mm from above, 11-12 from the north), the wire's line traced row by row (its centroid on each row, fitted as a line, 1 px rms) and its lean taken against the detent's arbor's on the same frame; the coloured section (9.37 px/mm by the plates' 19) draws the stud's head, its shank standing out under the plate and the wire leaving its end, and the wire's end over the pillar plate. The sizes and how sure each is are under Estimated. The wire is drawn upright, its axis on the stud's, and bent as a cantilever by the detent's back, which stands 0.32 over that axis, so it bears on the arm's upper corner alone; `fusee.js` rebuilds it as the detent rides the ratchet's teeth.
- **The maintaining detent.** 05 05 05-1's outline from its figures (the boss Ø 5, the back r 18, the hollow r 9 about 6.273, 9.645, tangent to the boss, the tip's lower corner on r 12 about the hole and its end 2.6 tall at 80°, leaning back toward the hole), 2 thick with its tip 1 thick at its upper face, which reaches the ratchet's teeth; 6.3 from its arbor's lower shoulder (05 05 05). It reaches over the fusee wheel's teeth to the ratchet: its thick part is cut back to where it keeps 0.25 clear of their tips as it rides (the step drawn 7.55 from the hole, the sheet's 8.6).
- **The stop work.** 05 06_4's Schnitt C-D, traced at 12.2 px/mm about its centre (its Ø 21 hub, Ø 15 recess and Ø 6 step come out so) and scaled 0.989 to its 22.5, and the body's 05 06 04-2.
