C-D is seen from the fusee's small end, as A-B on the same sheet (the maintaining work's): mirrored here, which puts the stop pin against the bar's far side at full wind, where C-D draws it ("Stellung im aufgezogenen Zustand"); unmirrored (until 15 October 2026) the bar met the pin with its near side. **The bar** (Stellungssteg 05 06 03-1, the coloured section's; no sheet): 22.5 × 2 (drawn 1.98, a sliding fit in the slot's 2 H8, estimated: drawn 2.00 until 18 October 2026, its sides in the slot's walls, they flickered through each other), its middle line 5 from the axis, its ends 11.52 and 10.98 from the foot of the axis's perpendicular, retracted; its lower end cut obliquely, the point on the axis's side (r 0.39) and the far side 0.94 shorter; its upper end square on the far side, the near side rounded r 2.47; a hole across it 6.0 from the foot for the spring's end. 1.45 thick (the coloured section), on the cover plate (dial up, the small end faces down), so at its lower end it reaches through the uncut end (0.62 there) into the top turn's groove (its land 0.87, to 1.49) and no further; thinned to 1.05 from 3.4 along toward its upper end (below). Its travel 3.6, between C-D's two positions as drawn (C-D's figures read from the hub: Readings corrected). **The slot**, cut in the body (05 06 04-2): 2 H8 wide, its near edge 4 from the axis, right across the small end, "1,5+0,05 tief" along the bar's lower part and "1,1+0,05 tief" toward its upper end, from u 8.0, where 02-2's hidden arc r 9.45 crosses it: there the rib between the top turn and the next is left, and the bar's thinner upper end, standing out over the groove, passes under it, clear of the chain on the turn below (fine.py found the full-thickness bar 0.26 into that chain); the coloured section draws the bar's end standing out 9.5 px thick against its middle's 13.5 (0.70 ±0.07; the model's 1.05/1.45, 0.72), flush on the cover plate's side, and its lower end at full thickness. The swept body stops at the slot's floor; the small end's last 1.5 is swept the same way in the two pieces the slot leaves, and the shallow part. **The spring** (Stegfeder 05 06 03-3, the coloured section's; no sheet): C-D draws it in both positions, a strip along the recess wall from its end at the slot round the bar's upper side and the recess to a free span that comes in to the bar's hole; traced on C-D (each point at the line's darkest pixel; the dashed, wound one searched along rays through its gaps): running, it leaves the wall 152° round from the bar's upper end; wound, at 95°, looping in to 3.15 from the axis. The model draws it between the two as the bar slides (the two drawn lengths differ by 4 %, 32.1 and 30.8). Its thickness from its bending: between the two shapes its curvature changes by up to 0.064-0.073 /mm along the free span (the end in the bar's hole carries no moment), so a hardened spring-steel strip t thick and 1.0 high is stressed 6,500-7,500 × t MPa (t in mm) wound and pushes the bar out with 0.06-0.08 N half way and 0.11-0.16 N wound (its strain energy over the 3.6). At 0.10: half the steel's yield, once a wind, and 20-30 times the 0.5 g bar's weight against a knock along it; C-D's double line, 0.15, would be stressed to 0.7 of yield. **Its pin** (Stift 05 06 04-4): in the body's hole "0,5 H7" along the slot, 1.5 from the line through the axis on the bar's side (02-2's "15"), standing into the recess to 6.4 from the axis (C-D) through the spring. **The slide** is worked out from the contact: the spring holds the bar out until the chain (or band) on the top turn, or coming down onto it, reaches its lower end, which then slides in as far as keeps it 0.02 off the connector's inner side (the chain's plates 1.0 from its pitch line, the band's 0.11): it starts 0.195 turn before full wind and has 3.37 at full wind (the band 0.185 and 3.38); the drawing's 3.6 is its limit. **The stop and full wind**: the slot runs along the line from the axis to the chain's pin A, its upper end toward A (02-2 dimensions it from that line: "4", "2 H8", no angle). The stop pin (hole 7, 14.0 from the fusee's axis, Ø 1.6) bears on the bar's far side 2.3 below its end, 0.02 off, and that turn of the fusee is full wind: 0.112 turn short of the chain lying to the groove's end N, so 8.638 turns of chain on the fusee, and from there 8.70 turns and 55.9 h to run down (05 06_20's test: 8.75 working turns; the up-and-down dial: 56). Until 15 October 2026 the model took full wind at N and set the bar's turn from the stop, 40° off A. The groove's last tenth of a turn stays empty, as a fusee's should: the chain never reaches its end. 02-2 draws N a further 0.07 turn on (55° from the bar's upper end, where the groove at its 1.15 pitch puts it 80°; N's angle isn't dimensioned, and 0.07 turn is 0.08 along the axis). Before full wind the bar comes no nearer the pin than 0.69 a turn earlier, and closes on it steadily over the last 0.05 turn (`tools/wempe_stopwork.py`: every 0.005 turn over the last 1.2 turns, chain and band, the bar's faces sampled against the pin's cylinder; ci.py runs it). **The stop pin** (05 08 01-5, no sheet): Ø 1.6 in hole 7, flush with the plate's outer face (estimated); its length off the coloured section, where the "Anschlagstift" leader, followed across the barrel's wall, the band and the maintaining detent's arbor (inked blue where it crosses them), ends on a stub standing under the top plate (px 628-642 × 357-375): on the plate's drawn 31 px for its 3.35, straight sides 15 px (1.62) under the inner face, then a domed end 2.5 px (0.27), ±1 px each (±0.1); 14 px wide (Ø 1.5, for its 1.6). Built 1.6 straight with a 0.25 dome, 1.85 in all (likely; estimated 1.5 until 15 October 2026); the bar's thin end, 1.235 under the face, bears on its straight part. The section draws the bar's protruding end 10 px (1.05) thick, the model's thin end, but 0.69 under the plate's face where the sheets' stack has 0.185 (it draws the whole small end so: the cover plate's top level with the plate's face, under 0.5 of the J-K recess left empty, where the sheets leave 0.085), so there the dome reaches just past the bar's lower face. Wempe 3955's train photograph (`8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg`) shows the cover plate over the small end whole and the bar's two ends out past its rim, the plate's screw toward the bar's upper end on the side away from it, as the model has them.
- **The barrel.** 05 07 01 (revised 20.4.48) and its parts: the drum Ø 41.6 × 18.1, inside 38.5, the bottom 1.25 with its boss (Ø 12 land, 45° to Ø 8, 3.1 from the outside), the bore 4.5 +0.1, the lid's seat Ø 39.2 × 1.2; the lid (1.3, its boss 3.3 from its face, recessed 0.6 under the rim); the hook block 05 07 01-3 0.5 deep off the bore (the wall at it 2.05 against 1.55 on 05 07 01; 0207's step 0.5) and 6 long round it (0207's flat, dimensioned), 3.05 high, riveted through the M2.6 hole 9.05 from the bottom 1.4 from its far end (0207's plan, scaled: likely ±0.5; the hole bored through the wall and the block at its thread's size and 0.02, 25 October 2026); the Steg 05 07 00-1 (the coloured section's #6; 0207's part 106), a steel bar 2 × 0.5 standing inside the wall from the bottom to the lid at r 18.3-18.8, 45° back from the rivet, its ends through the bottom's slot (05 07 01-1: 2 +0.05 wide, r 18.3-19.1, the section drawing it through the bottom just inside the bore and the plan its outline there) and the lid's 9 o'clock notch (to 18.3 ±0.02, its seat), the mainspring's outer coil threaded between it and the wall (built 18 October 2026: until then the hook block 0.35 × 3, no Steg, and the 9 o'clock slot cut through the drum's wall from the rim, read as a slot for prising the lid out). **The lid is on the barrel bridge's side**: 05 07 01 draws the arbor's square through the lid, and the coloured section draws the lid at the top (the 1941 assembly 0207 had it on the plate's side). Sure from the sheets; neither photograph shows it.
- **The barrel arbor.** The arbor 05 07 02 (the kompl. of 1943: 40.2 overall, the ball end 5, the square 10) with 020704-1's diameters (1941), each drawn to the later parts it runs in (its hook pin's hole Ø 2 × 6 in the core, 0207's section, cut 25 October 2026, the pin pressed to its foot): its pivots 2.88 and 3.88 in the plate's 2.90 and the bridge's 3.9 (the 1941 sheet's 3 and 4 can't enter them), its journals 4.48 in the drum's 4.55 (its 5 can't), its core 10.98 between the drum's bosses (its 12 can't fit the 1948 drum's 11.1), its shoulders 22.29 apart (22.2 +0.1, framed), 0.03 off the plate and the bridge. The hook (05 07 02-2): a pin Ø 2 in the core, its end a ramp across it from flush to 0.5 −0.05 at a radial face (the end view), the face on the side the spring pulls.
- **The set-up ratchet.** 05 07 00-3: 16 teeth Ø 14.1/11.1, each face on a line 1.2 off the radius, the back an arc Ø 12.5 (r 6.25) centred on the Ø 6.4 circle, 2.65 thick, its square hole 2.7 H7 (1:100); turned until a face meets the frame's click (P1a, traced on Wempe 3955).
- **The chain.** 05 07 04: the pitch 2.05 (framed), the plates 4.05 × 2 (framed), 0.82 ±0.02 across (framed: drawn 0.80, three plates of 0.26), 780 +3 over its hooks; drawn 375 pitches (781.25 over all), so both ends are outer links with the hooks riveted between their plates. Its pitch line runs 1.02 out from the groove's floor and the drum. Its top hook (5 to its crown, 3.5 wide, its seat 3.5 from its rivet, its nose turned back toward the chain) takes the pin across the fusee's flange slot (the "0,55∅ +0,01" hole through both ribs) in its seat, the chain's first rivet set along the path where the seat reaches the pin; its bottom hook (its barb's root 3.5 from its last rivet, the barb 1.4 past the shank, pointing back toward the rivet, the tip 7.5) a slot in the drum's wall cut at the fusee's assembly (0207: "Schlitz für Kette erst bei Schneckenmontage einsetzen", 2.5 wide round the drum, its section's 0.85 along it, through the wall), cut where the hook's barb comes to the drum (fusee.js `DRUM`, 25 October 2026: the chain once laid, its barb's points inside the drum's outside put in the drum's frame, the slot's middle at -19.6° and the barb 1.03 into the wall; the band's own drum slotted at -37.5° for its hook; until then not cut, the barbs in the metal). Laid every update: along the groove from its first rivet to where it leaves the fusee, straight to the barrel, round the barrel to its hook, each piece of chain at the height it left the fusee at (the drums' heights so match, as on Wempe 3955's barrel, where its turns lie about 2 apart: 7_Wempe_3955_W.jpg). The barrel's turn keeps the chain's length; the mainspring's turns follow the barrel's.
- **The chain runs across between the drums.** It leaves the fusee's south side and comes onto the barrel's north side (their inner common tangent), so the barrel turns against the fusee. The model first ran it along their south side, as an open chain, the barrel turning with the fusee; that run passed through the lower balance cock (fine.py), and a run along their north side would turn the fusee, and so the centre arbor, the wrong way. The photographs agree with the crossed run: the set-up click and ratchet on Wempe 3955 and Lange 5177 (2_…A.jpg) hold the barrel arbor against turning anticlockwise from the dial, which is how a barrel turning clockwise pulls it, and sheet 001 draws the chain on the barrel's far side from the fusee. Likely, not sure: no photograph shows the run itself (Unresolved).
- **The band.** 05 07 03: 0.22 +0.03 thick, 730 ±3 between its hooks; its width isn't given: drawn 0.78, inside the 0.87 land and its hooks' 10 × 0.8 strip (estimated). Its hooks 05 07 03-2 (round, on the fusee's pin) and -3 (barbed, into the drum) built from their 10:1 sheets: each figure as written, the arcs and lines the sheets don't figure traced on the scans (`tools/wempe_bandhooks.py`, then `mfgfit.py`; fusee.js gives each primitive and its source). Its pitch line 0.12 out from the floor.
- **The mainspring.** 15 × 0.37 (05 06_20), drawn 14.8 high (below) and 0.36 thick so packed coils lie at 0.37; its length fitted to 05 06_20's force, 1,240 mm (Physics: energy). Drawn as two packs, on the core and on the wall, joined by one turn, its turns from the barrel's: 12.69 at run down (lying all on the wall, 11.94, and the ¾ turn of set-up, 05 06_20's "3/4 Umgang gespannt"), 17.80 fully wound (5.11 barrel turns with the chain), 1.1 short of coil-bound (18.9).
- **Motion.** The fusee wheel turns with the centre arbor by 14/90 the other way (anticlockwise from the dial running); the maintaining ratchet turns with it, ahead of it by the maintaining spring's closing (`s.ms`: 14.4-15.6° running, its ends nearly meeting; while the key turns the ratchet, fallen back to its detent's tooth (fusee.js `fall()`, 0 to 3°), held there and the wheel running on, the lead falling tooth by tooth with the train; run down, held by the detent), the spring drawn bent to it (an elastica: Physics: energy, The maintaining spring). The fusee turns by `n`, its turns from full wind (6.43 h a turn, 55.9 h from full to run down, 8.70 turns: 05 06_20's 24 h in 3.75 turns), and running is carried forward onto the click that bears (its click wheel's face against that click's nose); winding (the key on the square, 1.5 turns a second) it turns back, the clicks riding over the click wheel and the detent holding the ratchet. Run down, the train stops. The chain's leaving point sets where on the groove the fusee's turn puts it (`m0`), the barrel's turn keeps the chain's length, and the mainspring follows the barrel.
- **The variants.** The chain is the page's default: its sheet sizes it completely (the band's width isn't given), the groove's 0.87 lands take its framed 0.82 ±0.02, and Wempe 3955 and Lange 5177, the genuine German movements photographed, run with chains (5_Wempe_3955_O.jpg, 7_Wempe_3955_W.jpg, 7_Lange_5177_W.jpg). The band, drawn in 1943, is on Greenwich's wartime Wempe and came with Wempe 3955 (14_Wempe_3955_Band_Kette.jpg).

## How the dial, motion work and hands were measured (P1e)

The sheets: 05 02_1 (Zeigerwerk, Auf u. Ab gesamt, 28.3.41, 5:1: the arrangement, drawn with plates 3.9 thick), 05 02_2 to _5 (the hands), _6 and _7 (Wechselrad kompl. and the wheel), _8 and _9 (Stundenrad), _10 and _11 (Auf u. Abrad), _12 (Zifferblatt 05 02 00-4), with 05 05_8 (Minutenrad kompl., 22.7.43: the cannon tube 05 05 03-3) and the coloured section for the parts' names and order (Auf u. Abtrieb 05 02 00-5, Zeigerfutter 05 02 01-2). Photographs: Wempe 3955's dial (`1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg`, 9.14 px/mm by the minute track's Ø 90) and its dial side with the dial off (`3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`, 8.41 px/mm by the plates' Ø 85). Every number is in `js/dial.js` beside the code, with its sheet and how sure.

- **Places.** The change wheel on the short stud at hole 19 and the up-and-down wheel on the long stud at hole 2 (train.js built both studs and their bushes); the sheet's centre distances 12.6 and 18.4 (hole 2 to the fusee's hole 10) are the tables' to 0.001.
- **Teeth.** The wheels' counts, modules, tips, roots and tooth thicknesses from their tables, the pencil corrections taken (the change wheel's tip 21.05 for the struck 21.30, its tooth 0.555; the hour wheel's tip 19.7 for the struck 20.0); cycloidal (train.js's forms). The pinions' counts are on no sheet and follow from the centres: the cannon pinion 14 (the tube's block on 05 05 03 scales Ø 5.86, a 14-leaf tip for m 0.36), the change pinion 18, the up-and-down pinion 12; 56/14 × 54/18 = 12. The change pinion's and the up-and-down pinion's tips and roots measured on Wempe 3955's dial side (6.9/5.4: a brass pinion cut like a wheel; 4.2/2.6: a steel pinion with narrow leaves, drawn 4.0/2.4, which is all the wheel's teeth leave); the cannon pinion's root as the centre pinion's sheet gives its. Every mesh is phased under load (gearLash); bom.py measures the centres to 0.0006 and the ratios exactly (−4, −3, −10).
- **Heights.** The dial on the movement ring's recess floor, 7 over the pillar plate's dial face (05 08 00-3 and 05 02_1). The cannon tube's foot on the centre arbor's journal "C" (05 05 03: its pinion block to 25.4 from the arbor's shoulder, its square to 14.3 over C, flush with the shank's end). The change wheel's and the up-and-down wheel's pipes stand on the stud bushes, flush with the plate's dial face: the change wheel's pipe 6.9 (7 −0.1) ends 0.1 under the dial, the wheel 1.0 over the plate on the cannon pinion's level and its pinion on the hour wheel's (05 02 02); the up-and-down pipe's 9.0 (05 02 04: 5.2 + 1 + 2.8) ends 0.05 over the long stud's end, as 05 08 02's 12.3 puts it, the wheel 0.8 under the dial (05 02_1's "0,8"), the hand's flange 0.2 over it (05 02_1's "0,2"). The hour wheel over the cannon pinion, drawn in the middle of its 0.3 of play to the dial; the up-and-down pinion 3 long, 0.3 under the dial (05 02_1).
- **The hands' heights.** The seconds arbor's post (05 05 01-2, 1946) ends 9.2 over its shoulder, 2.6 over the dial, 2 mm higher than the 1940 sheet's 7.2 that 05 02_1 and the coloured section draw (with the seconds hand just over the dial, then the hour hand, then the minute hand). With the 1946 post the order stays the same, raised: the seconds hand at the post's end (1.9 over the dial), the hour hand's blade 0.125 over the post's end (its pipe, 05 02 03's 8.2, reaches there), the minute hand 0.5 over the hour hand's boss (05 02_1 scales 0.49 there) on the tube's square, 0.665 under the tube's crown. The minute hand's top is then 2.65 under the glass where 05 02_1's pencil "17,0" puts it (the case, 05 01, is a later phase).
- **Hands.** Each outline is a run of half-widths along the hand: the sheet's widths at its stations (the minute hand 1.2 at the boss, 2 at 13, 0.7 at 25, 1.7 at 36, 0.4 at 46; the hour hand 1.7, 2.4 at 9, 0.8 at 19.5, the ball Ø 5.2 at 22.5, 0.6 at 30, 0.5 at 35; the seconds hand 0.6 and 0.2, its drop 1.7 from 6.5 to 10; the up-and-down hand 0.9 at the boss and 0.25 at its tip) and between them as traced on the sheets' plans (the outermost of the outline's lines in each column, less its stroke), the thicknesses from the side views. Each blade's underside is flush with its flange's underside, as the side views draw them all: the hour hand's blade (0.5) 0.3 under its flange's top (0.8), the minute hand's (0.7) 0.5 under its (1.2). The minute hand's flange rounded r 0.4 on its top edge (its side view); the minute hand's square hole and the hour hand's round one on 1:70 ("Kegel 1:70"), as is the hour wheel's seat for it (05 02 03). Isolated beside their sheets they match: the minute hand's two lobes and waist, the hour hand's spade, the seconds hand's teardrop. The minute and hour hands Goldin (gold-coloured brass, polished), the seconds and up-and-down hands blued steel, as the title blocks and Wempe 3955's.
- **Dial.** Ø 103.6 −0.1 × 1.3 and its holes (centre 6.0, up-and-down 3.2, seconds 1.7) from 05 02 00-4. Etched and filled black on white (Wempe 3955's: the sheet's "weiß lackiert" is struck through). Its five holes on Ø 101: three sunk Ø 1.8 × 1.0 for the screws at 354°, 120° and 240° (the sheet's 6° and scaled 120.6°, 240.2°; Altmeppen: German dials are screwed at the 20, 40 and 59 minute marks), the pin's Ø 1 at 6°, where the movement ring's sheet drills the dial's pin hole, and a Ø 1 at 185.7° (scaled), these two under the printing, which no photographed dial shows pierced (18 October 2026). Beside Wempe 3955's dial the layout matches mark for mark (`1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg`).
- **The dial's printing** (15 October 2026) is Wempe 3955's, as made, measured and traced on its photograph (`1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg`) by `tools/dialtrace.py` (its docstring says how; about 7 s). The photograph is put on the dial in millimetres by one homography that makes its six printed circles circles (rms 12-17 µm), with the two sub-dials' centres on their arbors at (0, ±23): the scale is the arbors' 46 mm apart, so no printed size is assumed. The sheet's layout holds on it: the minute track's lines centred on Ø 85.44 and 89.94 (the sheet's 85 and 90, scaled 85.4 there), the sub-dials' circles Ø 26.08, 15.19/18.02, 37.08/33.06, 23.01 (the sheet's 26, 15/18, 37/33, 23). Where the sheet and the dial as made differ, the dial is drawn: its lines 0.27-0.40 wide (the sheet 0.3), the minute divisions 0.42 and hour blocks 1.68 (the sheet 0.3 and 1.5), the seconds divisions 0.39 and 0.67 at each five (the sheet 0.2 and 0.5), the seconds sub-dial's lines at 5, 15 … 55 s 0.61; the tracks' white boxes with rounded corners (r 0.17-0.19, measured along their diagonals less the photo's blur), the up-and-down band's open ends rounded r 0.2; the minute track centred 0.07 under the arbors' midpoint, and each division at its measured angle (0.06-0.08° off the nominal on the mean, sd 0.1°): the printing's own scatter. The serial is the photograph's 3955 (the sheet draws 2367); the sheet's "Made in Germany" along the track at 7-5 isn't on this dial and isn't drawn. Drawn as geometry in `dial.js` from those numbers: the tracks (a black band with white boxes cut in it), the circles, the band, the lines; traced (`js/dialface.js`): every numeral, figure and letter, the serial and the anchor, each glyph's contours with their holes, simplified to 0.03 mm. The hours are a condensed heavy grotesque, its 1 a plain bar except in 12; each sits where the dial has it: the 12 standing on the up-and-down sub-dial's circle, the 7 and the 5 running under the seconds track, whose white boxes are printed over them (drawn last). CHRONOMETERWERKE is in outline capitals of a geometric face, WEMPE and HAMBURG solid in the same face; the up-and-down and seconds figures are set radially, each where the dial has it (the 0 and the 56 set off their divisions toward the band's gap). Two glyphs are partly under hands in the photograph: the seconds hand runs through the 0 of 20, which is the 0 of 50 (the same series, at the same angle, fitted on what shows: 1.2 % of its visible pixels differ); the minute hand's shadow lies over the 8's top (5.2 mm² of it), completed by an ellipse through the upper loop's visible edge. Elsewhere the thin up-and-down and seconds hands are filled in from round them (the 56's 6). Cleaned: white flecks in the black where it has chipped (under 0.1 mm² in the hours and serial, 0.022 elsewhere) and specks under 0.02 mm² dropped. `dialtrace.py --compare` lays the page's own canvas on the rectified photograph (`r_dial_side.png`, `r_dial_diff.png`): mark for mark they agree to the line's edge, the hands and the dirt outside the track left over.
- **The Lange dial** (15 October 2026): the Variants' Dial shows the printing of A. Lange & Söhne's No. 5177 (Glashütte made the Einheits-Chronometer to the same drawings) in place of Wempe 3955's; the link keeps it (`#dial=lange`), the dial's card says which is shown. Measured and traced the same way on `1_Lange_5177_Z.jpg` (the same photographer's square-on view, 8.9 px/mm, sharper: blur sigma 0.024 mm) by `tools/dialtrace.py --dial lange` (about 12 s): its six circles made circles by one homography with the sub-dials on the arbors at (0, ±23), rms 14-20 µm. The layout is the sheet's and Wempe's to 0.1 mm (the minute track's lines centred on Ø 85.34 and 89.82, centred 0.068 under the arbors' midpoint as Wempe's 0.067; the sub-dials' circles Ø 26.06, 15.16/18.00, 37.01/32.99, 22.98), but Lange printed it finer and in its own style: every line 0.19-0.25 wide (Wempe's 0.27-0.40); the minute divisions 0.245 and hour blocks 1.437 (0.42 and 1.68); the seconds divisions 0.238, 0.420 each five (0.39, 0.67); the lines at 5, 15 … 55 s 0.417 (0.61); box corners r 0.13-0.17; the divisions at their measured angles (0.05° and 0.13° off the nominal on the mean, sd 0.1°). The up-and-down sub-dial differs most: its circle open at the top from 337.4° to 23.0° (square ends, as they show), Ab (down) over its left end and Auf (up) over its right; its band two thin lines, not a black band, the divisions 0.25, the end strokes at 0 and 56 h 0.21 wide and running on inward past the inner line to r 5.71; the figures 0 … 56 at the same places, the lower half's, as Wempe's, tops inward. The hours are a narrow face with serifs (the 1 with a flag and foot, the 4 closed), A. LANGE & SÖHNE in geometric capitals over GLASHÜTTE and B/DRESDEN in narrower ones, the serif figures of the sub-dials, the serial 5177; no anchor. Drawn on the model's white ground (#f2f0ea, Wempe 3955's): the photograph's cream is the lacquer yellowed with age, as its rust spots are wear; the spots are left out. The disc stays the Wempe's (05 02 00-4: Ø 103.55 × 1.3, the seconds hole Ø 1.7): the variant swaps only the printing. Lange's own dial sheet, 1453/A ("B-Chronometer Zifferblatt", 9.6.44, 2:1, Werkstoff Eisen: the rust on 5177's), draws its disc Ø 103, 1,5 thick, the seconds hole Ø 2,2 (1,5 struck), the centre Ø 6,0 (4,5 struck), the up-and-down Ø 3,2, both 23 from the centre (±0,05), and no screw holes; 5177's dial is screwed at about 354°, 120° and 240° on r about 49.8 (1_Lange_5177_Z.jpg, 8.8 px/mm by the arbors' 23 apart: ±1-2°, sure), the Wempe's three places, so the sheet leaves off holes drilled at assembly, as 05 08 00-3 leaves off the ring's tapped holes for them. Kept (27 October 2026, the drawings check's open item): a 1.5 disc in the drawn ring's 0.7 recess stands 0.2 higher, level with the up-and-down hand's flange (0.2 over the 1.3 disc: 05 02_1's "0,2") and 0.2 into the bezel's step, which bears on the dial (05 01 01); Lange's heights for its hands and bezel are on no sheet in the set, and the photograph, square on, can't show a thickness, so a Lange disc would take two estimates (the hand pressed 0.2 less far onto its seat, the bezel screwed 0.2 less far down) for a difference of 0.2 in thickness, 0.55 in diameter and 0.5 in a hole the seconds hand's boss covers. Until 27 October 2026 this README said 05 02 00-4 was one dial for both makers. `--compare` lays the page's canvas on the rectified photograph: line for line and glyph for glyph they agree to the edge, the hands, the rust and the boss left in the photograph only (389 mm² of its ink; 48 mm² in the canvas only, mostly the worn black of the 9 and the 4, solid in the canvas). The hands of 5177 are the model's forms (the minute hand's two swellings, the hour hand's pear spade, the seconds hand's teardrop counterpoise, the tapered up-and-down hand), its minute and hour hands tarnished to a dark brown; they are left as drawn.
- **Motion.** The cannon tube turns with the centre arbor (an hour a turn), the change wheel back at a quarter of it, the hour wheel on at a twelfth; the seconds hand with the seconds arbor. The page's clock is local time, so the hands show the time. The up-and-down pinion turns with the fusee, the wheel and hand back at a tenth: the hand at 0 (22.5° right of 12) at full wind and at 56 after 8.75 turns of the fusee (05 06_20: 3.75 turns in 24 hours; the model runs down after 8.70, at 55.9), 7/8 of a turn (Lange's "7/8 Teilung"); until the fusee is built the page stands it at 3.75 turns (24 hours).
- **Frame.** The movement ring's flange is tapped M1 2.5 deep for the dial's three screws (not on its sheet; drilled at assembly, as its dial pin hole: frame.js).

## How the case and box were measured (P1f)

The sheets: 05 01_1 (Gehäuse Kardan, 05 01, 1:1, 30.10.40, reissued 28.6.46 and 31.3.47, scanned at 2.45 px/mm by its 160 and the bowl's 106: its elevation, Schnitte A-B and C-D and its 15-line list), _2 (Gehäusetopf R-05 01 00-1, 6.3.42), _3 (Verschlußplatte 05 01 00-2, rev. a 14.4.48), _4 (Glasrand kompl. 05 01 01, 27.11.41, 4.78 px/mm by its title block), _5 (Glasrand R-05 01 01-2, 6.3.42), _7 to _9 (the key 51 00 11 of 16.7.43 and its body and tube); 51 00_1 (Boden-Chronometer, 1:2), _2 (the handle, Änderung von 51 00 10), _3 (Firmenschild), _4 (Zunge). Every number is in `js/case.js` beside the code, with its sheet and how sure. Photographs: Wempe 3955's Bakelite bowl and gimbal ring (`16_Wempe_3955_Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`), Kirow 422's brass bowl (`11_Kirow_DEC_422_Gehäuse.jpg`), the boxes of Greenwich's wartime Wempe (`rmg_L5393_*`), Lange 1947 (`drouot_33810142_*`), Wempe 6161 and 5377 and the Kirow movements.

- **The bowl.** R-05 01 00-1 is the casting ("R-", Ms-Sandguß, Kokillenguß), the only bowl sheet in the set: every figure built (flange Ø 118 × 12, band Ø 108 to 23.5, body Ø 106, the 40° cone over its last 14.5 to the foot Ø 72.5, 65 overall; the bore Ø 98 × 53; the recess under the bottom Ø 64 × 5.5); the inner corner r 7, scaled. Machined where it must be: the recess opened to Ø 68.2 for the closing plate, the winding hole Ø 8.2 through the bottom over hole 10 (22.65 from the centre: the closing plate's Ø 8 at 22.6 is that radius), the closing plate's screw's M3. 05 01's elevation draws the 1940 bowl with the same outline (106 measured, the cone a little shorter). Since 24 October 2026 the casting stands 4.7 higher on the movement than its top face did: the top 4.7 of the flange turned down to a fine thread outside (the bezel screws onto it: The bezel and glass, below), a rebate in it Ø 106.06 to the seat on which the movement ring's flange lies, flush with the rim's top, and a notch through the threaded rim at 12 o'clock, 1.8 wide, 3.9 deep, for the ring's radial pin, as the 6MX's bowls are made (Kirov-Poljot 24020's, `dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/leben-des-6mx/13_KirPol_24020_E-Kontakt3.jpg`: the thread and the notch through it; Kirov 14917's, rAw2zmulpk8 0:29, 2:52). So the inside bottom lies 48.3 under the seat as cast and is faced to 49: 05 06_01's "49 von Werkring-auflage" (sure), where the cork ring on the dust bush meets it (drawn 3.70, squeezed 0.16 from the section's 3.86). Until then the casting's top was the seat, the bottom 53 under it with a raised ring round the winding hole (estimated) to meet the cork. The bowl weighs 1,195 g in brass (the movement in it and the bezel, 2,516 g), its centre of gravity 14.0 under the pivots (16.0 before). The casting turned from its profile as one solid with the thread on it up to the notch's floor (`lathT()`), the rim above it less the notch another (`thread()`).
- **The movement in the bowl.** The movement ring's flange (Ø 105.95 × 4.7) lies on the seat in the bowl's rim (until 24 October 2026 the bowl's top face), its body (Ø 96 −0.3) in the bore (Ø 98); the bezel screws onto the rim round the flange, its step 5.0 up bearing on the dial (05 01 01's section; its casting's lower bore Ø 106 is the flange's figure). With the bezel there, its glass's underside is 15.3 over the pillar plate's dial face on the 1941 bezel (05 01 01's 12.3 from its underside), against 05 02_1's original 15.8: the two drawings of 1941 agree within 0.5. The ring's radial pin (WN-202, Stellstift 3 in the coloured section: Ø 1.6 at 2.8 from the dial's face at 12 o'clock, 05 08 00-3's plan drawing its hole as a dashed pair across the ring at 12 figured "1,6", its section the hole end on; Wempe 3955's dial side shows its end on the ring's cone at 12; until 15 October 2026 Ø 1.1 at 6 o'clock) stands 1.78 out of the flange (the coloured section reads 2.0, 2 px more; 2.0 until 24 October 2026) in the bowl's notch, its end inside the bezel's thread's crests, which pass over it as the bezel is screwed on: it turns the movement to the bowl and holds it while the bezel is tightened (05 01 01 draws no notch in the bezel; one was cut there until 21 October 2026).
- **The bezel and glass.** The ring finished as Glasrand kompl. (05 01 01) draws it in section, inside its casting R-05 01 01-2 (Ø 125 for 8.2, the 30° chamfer to Ø 116, 16 high, bores Ø 92 and Ø 106 × 4.5: every figure; the "R-" a casting's sheet, as the bowl's R-05 01 00-1). 05 01 01 has no figures: its section traced at the sheet's 4.78 px/mm (`tools/wempe_bezel_trace.py`, which prints the points and draws them over the sheet with the model's profile), its height 16.0, the casting's, and every line inside the casting: a sight cone from the casting's Ø 92 at the step (5.0 over its underside) opening to Ø 110.5 at the glass's seat 12.3 up; the lower bore Ø 109.8, 5.0 deep, threaded: the sheet marks a thread in it, its symbol a hidden zig-zag up the bore at its left end (x 202-215 px, 22 px from the underside to the step: 4.6 high at 4.78 px/mm; sure that it is a thread, likely its height). Since 24 October 2026 the thread is cut (until then the bore plain Ø 110.5, nothing on the bowl to take it): a fine right-hand thread, pitch 0.75 (Kirov-Poljot 24020's rim: 7.0 px a groove over its 50-51 px from the rim's top face to the collar, the 6MX's 5.55: 0.77), the ISO basic form (60°, 0.406 deep), its crests at the traced Ø 109.8 (r 54.9), its roots Ø 110.61, run out 0.5 at each end, on the bowl's rim's thread (crests Ø 110.55, the 6MX's rim measuring Ø 110.5; roots Ø 109.74), the two on one helix 0.03 apart radially all round, 0.015 along the flanks, which bear when the bezel is screwed down; the bezel screwed down until its step bears on the dial; outside, Ø 113.2 at the underside, the widest band Ø 121.2 from 7.5 to 9.8 up (05 01 scales the bezel about 120), knurled, then a bevel in to the glass's edge at the top. The glass (05 01 01-1) Ø 110.5 × 3.7 in the seat, flush with the ring's edge: its underside 15.6 over the pillar plate's dial face (05 02_1: 15.8, corrected in pencil to 17.0), 1.25 over the minute hand. Its step bears on the dial's rim: the dial 1.3 thick (05 02 00-4) in the movement ring's recess Ø 104 × 0.7 (05 08 00-3) stands 0.6 proud of the flange, so the bezel's underside is 0.3 over the bowl's shoulder outside the thread (until 21 October 2026 the step was relieved over the dial, estimated). Until 21 October 2026 the ring was drawn as its casting, flat-topped with the glass Ø 112 × 2.3 flush in it.
- **The closing plate.** 05 01 00-2, every figure. It is a shutter: it lies in the bowl's foot turning on its screw (7, WN-71) in the bottom, a knob (WN-72, brass on Wempe 3955) in its M2.6 to turn it; its Ø 8 comes over the winding hole. Wempe 3955's bowl, upside down, shows it: the plate in the foot, the boss Ø 28 with the screw, the hole and the brass knob. The Ø 1.2 at 12.4 is drilled blind from the groove's floor, its point just short of the boss's face, and a round r 1.3 joins the rim's underside to the boss (both drawn, not figured; until 15 October 2026 the hole through and the corner sharp).
- **The gimbals.** 05 01, measured on the scan's pixel profiles (line centres, 2.45 px/mm, 25 October 2026; scaled by eye before: 3.3 thick, 23.2 high, until then): the ring Ø 140 inside (circles fitted to Schnitt A-B: the lines' inner edge Ø 139.2, outer edge 146.8), 4.0 thick (A-B's radial profiles every 3°, the median line centres 9.6 px apart; the elevation 3.9, Schnitt C-D 4.1: likely ±0.2), thickened outward on its inside Ø 140, 24.0 high (the elevation 24.1, C-D 23.9: ±0.3), its top 6.7 over the bowl's (6.2 until 25 October 2026); both pivot axes in one plane at its mid height, 12.0 under its top (the elevation's line centres 12.0/11.8 at 9 o'clock, 12.2/11.9 at 3; 11.5 until 25 October 2026, the "11,5" read as the ring's: Readings corrected) and 5.3 under the seat where the movement ring's flange lies (the bowl's top face until 24 October 2026, when the bowl's rim was cut round the flange: The bowl, above; the elevation 4.6 over axis A, Schnitt C-D 4.75 over axis b, scaled on the "11,5", ±0.5, for the 1940 bowl it draws; 5.3 for the cast bowl of 1942, 65 high, whose foot's edge (Ø 72.5) clears the ring's Ø 140 turning to be wound only with the pivots over 5.11 under its top: estimated; the glass's top 15.8 over the seat, the bezel's 16: so the bowl, the movement in it, the bezel and the glass hang 6.2 lower in the gimbals and the box than until 21 October 2026, when the ring's top was taken level with the bowl's), the outer on the 3-9 line (A-B), the inner on 12-6. The outer pivots are M6 screws through the box's sides 93.5 over its bottom (51 00 10's M6, 11.5 under the line 105 over the bottom, the lid's parting line, and 05 01's "11,5" the same: the ring's top 0.5 over it), so the two drawings agree. The case brackets (05 01 00-3) at 12 and 6, the ring bracket (05 01 00-6) at 3 o'clock, the locking bracket (05 01 00-4) at 50° from 6 toward 3 ("50°"), the transit lock's knob and lever in the front right corner (A-B): their places and sizes scaled, their forms from the sheet's views and Kirow 422's bowl. Re-measured on 05 01 on 25 October 2026 (line centres on pixel profiles, ±0.4 a reading: likely; `case.js` has every number): the ring bracket 3.3 thick, 15.4 high at the ring with its outer edges bevelled 2 × 2 (16.7 and square before); the case brackets cranked as Schnitt C-D draws them, a foot on the bowl 20.3 to 8.3 under the pivots (its inside stepped to the 1942 casting's band and body, r 54 and 53), its outside leaning out to a boss whose face, 5.8 inside the ring (r 64.3), stands to 4.8 over the pivots, the boss 4.0 deep and 9.0 wide (the elevation; A-B draws 12.0: Readings corrected), its screws (WN-78, heads Ø 7.0, so M4) 15.0 either side and 14.3 under (a plate 18.7-27.7 under and a block 9 wide to r 66.5 before); the inner pivot screws (WN-79) head Ø 9.2 × 2.8, M5, ending 0.1 off the boss, a plain tip Ø 3.0 × 4.6 through its hole, the knurled lock nuts (WN-400) Ø 22 × 2.4 against the ring's inside (M4, head Ø 7 and nuts Ø 19 × 2.5, from Wempe 3955's Bakelite case, before); the outer pivots (WN-80) M6 from the wall to the ring at 9 o'clock and to the bracket at 3, then a tip Ø 3.1 through the ring to its inside at 9 and through the bracket 1.7 into the ring at 3, heads Ø 12 (11.8-12.2), the bearing discs Ø 22.7 × 1.9 (a plain M6 to the ring's inside, heads Ø 12.5 and discs Ø 23 × 2.4, before). The holes for the pivots' tips, the inner pivot screws and the ring bracket's screws are cut along the radius through the ring, its bracket and the case brackets (`sweepS` in `case.js`: until then the case's holes across turned parts weren't cut).
- **The locking bracket and the transit lock** (Feststellkloben 05 01 00-4 and Feststeller 05 01 01, no sheets of their own; re-measured 24 October 2026). How they lock, from 05 01's section and the 6MX's book (Romanov 1975, p. 204, with its Fig. 95): the lever on its bracket on the box's wall, turned in clockwise, "passes through the slot of the outer ring" and stops "against the wall of bracket 6" on the bowl, the knob's screw clamping it. Schnitt A-B is cut at the pivots: its ring's band is left unhatched between radial lines at 20.6° and 52.4° from 6 o'clock toward 3 (±1°), the slot (Wempe 6161's ring shows it beside the knob, `antiquorum_208-4_wempe6161.jpg`, a little higher in its band: a later case or the band's foreshortened edges, likely); and the bracket, resampled along its radius about the ring's fitted centre (274.3, 491.0 px, 2.46 px/mm), is 15.7 wide, its upright's faces at r 59.0 and 63.2, its foot's at 55.1, cut only over its 3 o'clock 6.1 at r 59-63.2: a neck, the rest of the width open, a slot across the upright, open toward 6 o'clock. The elevation (heights from the pivots' line; the bracket seen 50° round, its outer faces 0.64 of their 15.7, its sides 0.77 of their depth): the foot's face 16.5 to 25.8 under the pivots, its screw at 20.5, its side 4.2 deep; the crank's face leaning out at 46°, from r 55.1 at 16.5 under to 63.0 at 8.7 under (drawn as steps until 24 October 2026); the upright to 2.6 under, the neck 2.6 under to 1.4 over (the slot 4.2 high), the head 1.6 to 5.9 over. Built on the model's bowl (likely, each reading ±0.4, a pixel): the upright and head r 59.6-63.2 (0.6 off the 1942 casting's flange, Ø 118 to 2.0 under the pivots, which 05 01's 1940 bowl doesn't draw), the foot r 53-57.2, the back's step under the band (Ø 108, to 13.5 under) estimated, the sides parallel and the bracket centred on its 50° line. Until 24 October 2026 it carried a pad 2.5 short of the ring, which the lever pressed the ring onto from over the ring's top: the box at the head's right on the elevation is the bowl's flange behind it. The lever is so at the pivots' height, 1.7 under to 0.8 over them in the bracket's slot (Wempe 3044 from straight above shows its lever's end running in under the ring's band; Wempe 5377 and Greenwich's wartime Wempe, which draw it in front of the band, show nothing of its height: turned off it lies outside the ring, 79 from the centre), its end's centre 27.0 from the knob's axis where 05 01 scales 28.3 (at 28.3 it would swing 0.8 into the 1942 bowl's flange: shortened to clear it by 0.5, estimated; `tools/wempe_lock.py`); the ring's slot 0.5 over and under it (estimated). Turned 69.95° its end meets the neck (worked on the outlines); its sweep crosses the band from 27.5° to 50.7° (27.8° on the ring's 3.3 before 25 October 2026), inside the sheet's slot; the lever's pin, at 0.52 of its length from the knob's axis (0.55 until 25 October 2026, which would stand 0.33 in the ring's 4.0), clears the ring's outside by 0.48 over the turn (`wempe_lock.py sweep`). The page turns it in (Display, Motion: Transit lock) once the gimbals are level, and out before they are let go or the bowl is turned over to wind. Kirow 422's bowl (`11_Kirow_DEC_422_Gehäuse.jpg`, upside down) shows the same kind of bracket: an L screwed to the body, cranked out to a pad at the case pivot's height (its pivot hole), though with two screws in a column where 05 01 draws one (another maker's: the sheet's one is built).
- **The box.** 185 × 185 × 180 outside (05 01's note), walls 12.5 (51 00 10's section), 160 inside (05 01's 160), the handles every figure of 51 00 10, 75 over the bottom (their ends 6 off the side and the middle's outside 12.5, the section's "6" and "12,5", the strip 3; the bends S-curves traced on the bottom view: 27 October 2026, until then the ends 3, the middle 10 and straight kinks), each over a finger recess in its side wall (25 October 2026: the sheet's oval 70 × 30, dashed behind the strap and solid below it, is the section's unhatched hollow in the hatched 12.5 panel, not a pressing of the strap as it was drawn until then): 10 deep at its middle, the section's figure "10" (scaled 10.5; built so until 27 October 2026), leaving 2.5 of wood, rounded top and bottom about as deep as it is (2.5 over its foot it is 6.8 from the face), its middle 8.5 under the strap's (measured on the scan at 2.38 px/mm: the section 8.7, the front view 8.1), built as a cutter of radius 10 following the oval, its cove in 40 rings of quads (10 rings until 27 October 2026, terraced); and the outer pivot's M6 through each wall (51 00 10's pencil "Verbohren 4,8 mm, Gew. M 6"). The lid 75 high (180 less the 105 to the line across 51 00 10's side). The height kept at 05 01's 180, where 51 00 10 draws the side panel 185 high (below, Unresolved, the case). Over each outer pivot a small round-headed pin stands out of the side wall's parting face, as 05 01's elevation draws it (measured on its pixel profiles, 2.45 px/mm: Ø 2.5, 2.3 proud, 3.3 into the wall, on the wall's middle line; likely), the closed lid bored Ø 2.7 × 2.6 for it: its purpose inferred (Estimated). The maker's plate every figure and its lettering (00 01 01-9), "Type 3371". The tongue every figure (P. 51 00 02-2), its hole's 0.5 × 45° sink and its last 7 thinned 0.2 on the section's upper face (built 27 October 2026).
- **The outer box** (Überkasten 127-556.01, renumbered 52 00 16, 28.2.42, 1:2; 15 October 2026), built so the Boden-Chronometer arrangement (127-556. A-1, 4.3.42) can be seen as drawn: the wooden box shut in it, its lid open (the Transport box view; the other views leave it out, as the box is lifted out to be used). Bleached birch, dovetailed and glued. Its figures: the body 157 high with the base plate, the lid 88 (88 + 157 against a pencil 240); 225 inside across at the rim (Schnitt A-B), the walls and base plate 12.5 (likely), the plate 270 across and 10 past the walls; in plan 370 over the plate's ends, the body 270 long outside (370 less the plan's 50 each end; it scales 271), so 245 inside. The scan's scale is 1.195 px/mm by the 370, and its 225 and 270 measure to it. The plate's ends are traced on the plan (the outline every 6 px): arcs of R 367 about the long axis (sagitta 24 over the 270, the intermediate points within a pixel), the corners r 8 (scaled). The rim strip (17, 18) 13 × 5, 4 over the walls (likely). The cushions are lenses 20 thick at their middle (the sides' and the lid's "20", sure; the ends' 23 and the floor's 21 scaled, built 20), and the figures then hold the wooden box exactly: 225 = 20 + 185 + 20 across, 245 = 20 + 10 + 185 + 10 + 20 along (its handles against the end cushions, as 51 00_1's plan lays it), 220 = 20 + 180 + 20 high inside. A-1's section scales the wooden box standing about 49 above the body's rim; built, 55.5 (180 + 20 − 144.5). The hinges (7) 32 long, 68-100 off the middle, 2.5 proud; the strap screws 80 apart, the lower 60 over the floor; the eyes 25 out from the end walls. Checked: `solids.py --model wempe` (closed), `audit.py box --model wempe` with the Transport box view (nothing new); isolated beside A-1's section and plan.
- **The key.** The 1943 key (51 00 11), the later of the two: its body (05 01 02-2) and tube (05 01 02-3) every figure, the tube's square socket cut (3.1 +0.05, 19 deep, its corners as the fusee arbor's square has them), its mouth sunk 60° 1.1 deep to the square's flats (05 01 02-3's "60°", "1 +0,2": 27 October 2026); its handle a flat round paddle Ø 34.5 × 3 in the body's slot, scaled, its rim on the slot's floor (05 01 02-2: the Ø 35 arc, 6.3 under the top where it meets the body's outside, so 7.40 at the bore's edge, where the paddle rests: its centre 9.37 over the body, as 51 00 11 scales it; 10.95 until 17 October 2026, the 6.3 taken at the axis); the grooved pin across the slot through the paddle's thickness, 4 under the body's top (05 01 02-2's cross hole, which shows end-on where the paddle shows its face), Ø 2.5 × 15.3, its rounded ends 0.4 proud of the body (51 00 11's section); the tube's head 8.0 under the body's top, just under the paddle (51 00 11's section; 6.8 until 17 October 2026); the coil spring as 51 00 11's section draws it, about 12 open coils of wire about 0.6 at a pitch of 0.95, wound on the tube from its head down to the bore's floor, its end turned down into the body's Ø 0.9 hole and along it to the bottom face (until 17 October 2026 a 0.9 wire standing clear of the tube, its end not drawn), which grips the tube one way ("Linksaufzug b. Rechtsdrehung entspannt sich die Feder": followed up from its end its coils turn clockwise seen from the paddle, so the body, turned counterclockwise seen from the paddle to wind, drags the end the way that adds turns and closes them on the tube; inferred). A 1943 key taken apart (*The Mariner's Chronometer*, Fig. 2.35, a photograph of a page the user sent, 17 October 2026, not kept) shows the same pieces: the flat round handle, its lower part where the slot covered it unpolished with the pin's hole through it, the grooved pin, the spring wound on the tube under its head over about a fifth of its length (about 12 coils at about 0.8-0.95), and the body's hole for the spring's end beside its bore in its bottom face. The body is cut as 05 01 02-2 draws it (17 October 2026; until then a plain turned body, the paddle, pin and spring's end in its metal): the slot 3 H11 down to its Ø 35 floor, the cross hole Ø 2.5 +0.05 through both jaws, the spring's Ø 0.9 hole 3.8 out in the bottom face and 5° in toward the axis up to the bore's drill point, the paddle holed for the pin. The cell builder cuts upright holes in flat faces only, so the body is four solids joined into one closed surface: the bottom ring with the slanted hole, the drill point and the walls to the jaws' foot, and the two jaws built along the pin's axis, where the cross hole is upright and the slot's walls and floor are faces; it also shows a hole through the handle's middle, which 51 00 11 doesn't draw (not drawn), and a body of bare brass where 05 01 02-2 has it sprayed black (kept black, as the sheet). It lies in the box; while the Wind button winds with the movement out of its case (the movement's views), it is on the fusee's square, its tube's mouth on the dust plate (where the square is 3.1 across), turning with the fusee, and back in the box when it lets go (as the Hamilton's key goes on its square). In the case (the Dial, Box and Case views) it is wound as the 6MX's user instruction has it (pp. 3-4: "turned slowly and carefully onto its side (in the gimbals), the key hole in the case opened, the key put in" and turned, then taken out and the chronometer turned back) and two videos of 6MXs show it (`References/wempe/VIDEOS.md`: DIxob2Rl4dc 7:05-7:22, the bowl turned about its 12-6 pivots, the dial toward 3 o'clock, until its bottom faces up, the key put in from above; Uy8A88_j-cA 5:16-5:26): the lid opens, the bowl turns over in the ring about its inner pivots, the closing plate opens, the key comes down onto the square, and when it is wound all goes back. The bowl clears the ring and box all the way over (fine-interference.js over the box's root at 0°, 45°, 90°, 135° and 180°, and with the key on the square through the open plate: nothing new between the case's parts). Checked on the square through `fine.py --model wempe --eval` (the key put on the square, turning with the fusee through every state): nothing new.
- **Checked.** The case with the movement in it, open and shut, through `fine-interference.js` run over the box's root: nothing of the case meets the movement; the overlaps are the radial screws and pivots in holes not cut, the hinges' leaves on their knuckles and the latch's screw in the lid.

## Physics (phase 2a)

The escapement runs as the Hamilton's: `makeEsc` (`../shared/escapement.js`) solves it from its parts' shapes and bodies, and the page steps the balance's swing by its averaged equation of motion (`js/escphys.js`, `app.js`'s tick). `tools/wempe_escapement.js` prints every number below and checks them (about 20 s).

- **The settings** (`WESC.SET`, js/escapement.js) are the layout's own numbers put in makeEsc's frame: makeEsc sees the escapement as the sheets draw it, from above (the wheel turning clockwise), this file's plan from the dial, so each plan point is mirrored about the line of centres (`MK`). The wheel's 15 teeth, Ø 14, 10.00 from the balance; the lock 37.0° on, 0.235 deep (36.5° and 0.175 until 19 October 2026: The physics pass), the stone Ø 0.75 half round, its flat on the wheel's radius (it draws −3.7° against the detent's line); the detent's flexure at its spring's middle, 12.83 from the locked tip (12.87 before); the gold spring's square end 1.54 from the staff, free 11.25 from its bending point (11.18); the discharge stone's acting corner, its bevelled end (0.3 back over its 0.4) and its place at rest; the impulse stone's face on its radius to 3.35, 28°10' before the line of centres at rest; the tooth (05 04_23: the 1.5° land, the back the Ø 6 cutter's arc to Ø 12.1 15° behind the tip, makeEsc's root circle, the face leaning 25°, 4.27° behind the tip there; until 15 October 2026 the face's root 2.43° behind the tip on Ø 12.1 and the back to 11.9°). makeEsc's lift is the gold spring's tip's travel; the drawing's (`WESC.ESC.state`) is the detent's turn: `toKin` divides by the tip's distance from the flexure, and the tool checks that the stone, the locked tip and the stone carried by the detent's turn agree in both frames (to 1e-15 of the wheel's radius). makeEsc took three settings for it (15 October 2026), each 0 or as before by default, so the Hamilton's results are bit for bit what they were (`tools/escgolden.js`, and tools/escapement.js, physics.js, keystone.js, tolerances.js, dynamics.js --quick and tripping.js --quick print the same): `BEV`, the discharge jewel's end bevelled back from its acting corner; `TSQ`, the passing spring's tip the square end of its strip, not a disc (a disc's round end reaches 0.075 nearer the staff, and carried the detent 17° too long); `TH1`, the contact tables carried past the Wempe's impulse, which ends 45° past rest (the Hamilton's at 21°). Its swing solver now starts from the least swing that sees an impulse through, and the stone rides an escaping tooth whenever the jewel lets the detent down onto it (both reached only by the Wempe). Checked in time (phase 2d, below), makeEsc took two more for it, each 0 for the Hamilton: `DEP`, the detent's work counted only as far as it rides the stone (flung off the stone's square corner, it never rides its bevel back down), and `RW`, the wheel's chase run with the stone riding the tooth it has let go; and `depart` takes the detent's speed off a square-ended spring as the slope it came up (`TSQ`).
- **The balance**, weighed on the model's solids (`tools/wempe_inertia.py`, about 30 s; `wempe_inertia.json`): 2,561 g·mm² about the staff (the rim, arm and staff 2,545, of it the four masses 1,523, the rim 750, the timing weights 200; the rollers 2.4, the collet 5.8, a third of the spring's 22.6, weighed drawn at rest: phase 2c), 12.7 g with its rollers and collet (since 15 October 2026's shape batch: the hollow arm, the screws flush in the masses, the timing weights Ø 3.8 × 3.3 with their heads inside the rim, and `wempe-inertia.js` weighing the shown revision only, where it had counted both revisions' rollers, staff and hub; 2,501 before, and 2,269 g·mm² and 11.7 g until the masses' height was read: How the balance and escapement were measured). Densities: brass 8.45, steel 7.85, the rim's nickel steel 8.1, beryllium copper 8.25, the gold spring 11.6 (333-333: likely). The 1963 Soviet book puts marine chronometers' balances at 0.2-0.3 gf·mm·s², 1,961-2,942 g·mm²: inside. The escape arbor 8.7 g·mm² and the seconds arbor and hand reflected (÷ 8²) 1.25: JW 9.96 (12.4 until the tool weighed one revision: the arbor's two collets counted); the detent's moving part about its flexure 8.49 (ID, since 19 October 2026's gold spring at 11.6 and the stone at 7.14; 8.35 before them, 8.46 before the gold spring's bow; 8.60 with both revisions' pieces, 8.25 with the horn 3.5 long).
- **The hairspring and the period.** The strip 0.285 thick (the coil bends it radially) and 0.52 high, steel hardened and tempered blue (05 03_6): k = E b h³ / 12 L, E 206 GPa (200-210), L its working length pin to pin, 505.4 mm (phase 2c: the whole strip 13 turns less 3.5 mm, below): 409 µN·m a radian (397-417). With the weighed balance that is a period of **0.494 s, 1.2 % short of the train's 0.5 s** (its seconds arbor turns once in 120 teeth; 0.497 s until 18 October 2026, with a nut on each mass); 0.5 s wants a balance of 2,589 g·mm², 2.5 % more (below, Physics: hairspring and temperature; 0.491 s with 2,501 g·mm² until 15 October 2026's shape batch, 0.468 s, 6.4 % short, until the masses' height was read: it was most of the gap). Phase 2a read the length as 12.911 turns of helix with the curves added, 549.1 mm, 0.488 s. makeEsc runs the balance at 0.5 s (its stiffness I (2π/0.5)²): the timing a maker sets.
- **The drive.** 05 06_20's force (g on a lever 262 mm long, running down, the mean of two measurements, scaled off its curve at 24 places, ±0.7 g) is on the fusee arbor: the sheet's own sum, 131.5 g × 262 / 19.6 = 1.758 kg at the fusee wheel's pitch circle, takes no fusee radius, and so even a pull (126.5-138.4 g) over the barrel's five turns is what a fusee gives its arbor. The mainspring's torque it implies (the pull on the chain at the fusee's radius and pitch line, times the barrel's) rises 1.9:1 to 749 mN·m fully wound, 2.2 GPa in the 15 × 0.37 strip, a tempered steel mainspring's: so the reading holds. Measured running down, the spring's, chain's and fusee's losses are in it. The fusee arbor's 325-356 mN·m reach the escape wheel through the train, 90/14 · 90/12 · 80/10 · 80/10 = 3,085.7:1, at its efficiency: each stage from its parts as the Hamilton's (physics.js A6: the pivots' friction on the tooth loads, mu 0.10-0.15, and the mesh's, mu 0.10-0.20), the meshes worked tooth by tooth (gearing.js), 0.730-0.833 for the four, the model taking their geometric mean, 0.780 (0.749 from the meshes' rule of thumb, 0.661-0.848, until 19 October 2026: The physics pass). The escape wheel's torque is 82.1-89.8 µN·m over the wind, 86.1 at 12 h from full wind, the reference (`TQ`; 78.9-86.3 and 82.8 before).
- **The damping** (no Wempe source gives it): each loss from the parts, as the Hamilton's (physics.js A6), at the swing: air on the masses (Ø 6.3 × 6.46 across the rim), the timing weights, the screws' heads and nuts, the arm edgewise and the rim's cut ends, a pressure drag at Cd 0.8-1.4 (Re about 300 on the masses), Q 1,060-1,855; the endstone's constant friction, the Hamilton's measured effective mu (0.008-0.012) under this balance's weight on its Ø 0.21 pivot, Q 10,580-15,870 (the 6MX has a diamond endstone for "the heavy balance", 6MX-figures.md); the spring's own, Q 1,000-5,000. Together Q 510-1,317 (at 197°), a free run-down TF 81-210 s. The model takes **TF 111 s** (Q 697; 108 s, Q 679, until 19 October 2026's physics pass, which lowered the swing the video's fit is taken at), where a running 6MX on video, fitted with this escapement's work at the model's own swing, gives it back (below; 15 October 2026, the user's choice: until then the parts' middle, TF 125 s, Q 785, which the video's fit then left outside its range once the shape batch moved the escapement's work and the balance's weight). That swings the balance **194.3° at 12 h** (197.2° until 19 October 2026's physics pass: the lock 0.235, the train's 0.780, the preload 0.8 g; 199.4° before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles and the discharge stone's place; 207.3° with TF 125 s; 214.0° until the masses' height made the balance 10 % heavier; 219° until phase 2d took up the detent's flight off the stone and the stone's ride on the escaping tooth), 176-252° over the parts' range. The sources' band, 225-270° (the 1954 instruction for chronometers in general; the Hamilton manual's 1⅜-1½ turns), wants TF about 165 s for 225° (153 s, and 200 s for 247.5°, before the physics pass): inside the damping's range, so the balance as built can swing the band on the drive as measured, and the model, as the video gives it, falls 12 % under it. A running 6MX's top plate is filmed (References/wempe/VIDEOS.md: moMvyx8eXfo 17:53-19:52, gAczA7-a7TY 4:47-5:52); `tools/swing.py` reads a swing off such a video from the balance's screws passing the plate's cutouts and could be pointed at them with the Wempe's masses and weights in place of the Hamilton's screws (not run): that would settle TF. **A running 6MX on video bears it out** (References/wempe/VIDEOS.md, "Measured", 15 October 2026): its balance, seen whole from above at 30 frames a second (moMvyx8eXfo 17:53-19:50), read frame by frame by its arm (`tools/wempe_swing.py`), relaxes after the movement is set down (110° rising to 137°) and after a twist (185° falling to 157°); fitted with this escapement's work and the damping split as above (`tools/wempe_startup.js`), **TF 111 s** at the model's swing, 102-125 s over the stretches' relative scale (the work held flat, 80 s), and the model is set to it (108 s, 96-121, at 199.4° until the physics pass) (TF 114 s at 207°, 99-129, before the shape batch: the model's 125 s then inside it); the measurement narrows the parts' 81-209 s to about 102-125 s. Since then the escapement's reconciliation (26 October 2026: TF 133 s, 118-149) and the detent's blade (27 October: 141 s, 133-158, drive 0.55, rms 0.67°) have moved the fit, and the model keeps 111 s outside it (the owner's choice, 27 October 2026), which `wempe_escapement.js` reports; the fit read 332 s on the blade's day until `wempe_startup.js` stopped counting a swing where makeEsc gives no impulse (a weak drive, the jewel gone before the tooth) as a free run-down: the fit had taken the drive to 0.45 and let the stretch after the twist coast down without an impulse. It is a 6MX's balance, the same kind (a cut bimetallic rim, four masses, two timing nuts on the arm) but not the Wempe's drawing; out of its bowl, dial down, part wound (it settles at 149° on 53 % of the model's drive), so its swing says nothing of the Wempe's at 12 h. `wempe_escapement.js` checks the model's TF against it (`wempe_startup.json`).
- **The swing and the rate across the wind**: 199.5° and +0.20 s a day fully wound, 191.3° and −0.13 at 24 h, 193.5° and −0.04 near run-down (54 h); 188.5-199.5° and −0.26 to +0.20 s a day over the whole 56 h, against the reference at 12 h (since 19 October 2026's physics pass; 191.9-202.0° and −0.15 to +0.12 since 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles; 194.1-204.2° and −0.06 to +0.03 before it, since 15 October 2026's shape batch and TF 108 s; 201.5-212.1° and −0.08 to +0.08 before it, 208.0-219.0° and −0.12 to +0.13 before the masses' height, 212.7-223.6° and −0.16 to +0.17 before phase 2d). That is the escapement's isochronism with the drawn hairspring's (phase 2c: it loses 0.46 s a day for each 10° the swing grows, `WESC.SET.HS`; 0.48-0.49 before the physics pass), which partly cancel. Wound daily, as the 6MX's instruction asks, the rate changes 0.33 s a day between full wind and 24 h (0.19 before the physics pass). The tools' band for the model, the sources' 225-270° less the damping's estimate, is 180-270° (wempe_escapement.js, gearing.js, wempe_tolerances.js, the page's swing panel: its floor 14° under the reference's swing, 194.3°, since 19 October 2026's physics pass; 185°, 14° under 199.4°, until 16 October 2026 and 12° under 197.2° until the pass, as 193° was under 207° and 200° under 214° before).
- **The escapement's figures** since 19 October 2026's physics pass (the lock 0.235): the gold spring met at −10.3° of the balance, the wheel released at +2.6° (lock 12.9°), the detent off the stone at +4.1° (let-off 1.5°), the return swing past the gold spring at −18.8° (overall 22.9°); roller shake 0.011, the stone's lift at release 0.257; the wheel chases the stone 10.3° of the balance and strikes it at 34.8 rad/s, the strike losing 10.4 % of the impulse, the unlocking 26.1 % of its work; the stone falls back onto the tooth it let go at 5.2° and is on its banking at 13.6°, 0.9° after the strike (dynamics.js 13.5°). Before it (makeEsc's measure(); from 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles: the impulse face 28°10' before the line of centres at rest, the discharge stone 5° later on the staff, Closed): the gold spring met at −7.3° of the balance, the wheel released at +2.6° (lock 9.9°), the detent off the stone at +4.5° (let-off 1.9°), the return swing past the gold spring at −12.6° (overall 17.1°); roller shake 0.046 mm, the teeth 0.39 into the roller, the stone's lift at release 0.196 mm. The impulse stone comes into the teeth's path at +6.1°, 3.8° after the release (the sheet's impulse begins at 22°10', 6° after rest). Released from rest, the wheel, with the seconds arbor behind it, gathers speed slowly on 83 µN·m, chases the stone 12.3° of the balance and strikes it at 14.9°, its face 13.3° before the line of centres, where the layout's tooth falls on it at once, 22°10' before; the impulse ends 21.8° past the line (the layout's 21.2°), the wheel driven 16.1° of its 24°, the strike losing 14.9 % of the impulse; the unlocking takes 16.2 % of its work. The least swing that works is 50.0° (the layout's 53.4°). The detent leaves the discharge stone's acting corner at +3.2°, flung up off it, falls back onto the tooth it has just let go at 5.6° and rides it down its back onto its banking at 13.1° (dynamics.js 13.8°), long before the next lock. Until 16 October 2026 (the face at 24°, the discharge stone leaving the gold spring at rest): met −12.3°, released −2.4°, off −0.5°, the stone in the path 4.6° after the release, the strike 16.1° later at 10.3° before the line losing 20 %, the least swing 45.8°. The page draws makeEsc's solved motion (`WESC.ESC.state`). The locking stone is never inside a tooth (stoneClash, as the Hamilton's: 0.03 µm at most over 11 swings from 50.0° to 340°).
- **Stopping and starting.** Run down (the fusee at the end of its chain), the train runs on what the maintaining spring keeps, the ratchet held by its detent as while the key turns, the swing falling as the spring opens (from 193°, the train stops after about 12 minutes; until 18 October 2026 at once, the spring left closed); a set of the wind to run down (the slider, the link) leaves the spring spent. Stopped, the train stands at a locked beat and the balance swings down (makeEsc's freeStep: the gold spring and the detent still take their work): from 201° it falls under the 45.8° it needs in about 100 s and is at rest in about 11 minutes. Wound before then, the train goes on at once; once it has stopped it needs a twist (the Twist button: about 130°, the 6MX's "light jerk ... by turning the chronometer gently about the hands' axis", estimated), as a detent escapement doesn't start itself (the 1954 instruction, п. 7). While the key turns the maintaining spring drives the train with what it keeps (Physics: energy, The maintaining spring): closed by the running drive until its ends nearly meet, it pushes 89 % of the drive at once (the detent half a tooth late), falling as the fusee wheel runs it out; the balance keeps going 10.7-13.9 minutes, its swing under 185° after 31 s to 2.5 min (the 1954 instruction gives 1-2 minutes for chronometers in general; until 16 October 2026 70 s, from 05 06_9's "x = 0,27 mm" read as its deflection); a wind takes about 6 s, so the swing hardly moves. Wound from run down, it carried nothing and the train stays stopped until twisted. Model time runs no faster than real while the key turns.

## Physics: hairspring and temperature (phase 2c)

`tools/wempe_hairspring.js` (about 20 s) and `tools/wempe_thermal.js` (under a second) print every number below and check them; `ci.py` runs both.

- **Which sheets are the Feder-Chronometer's spring.** 05 03_5 (Spiralfeder kompl.) and 05 03_7 (Spiralkurve) carry the lever chronometer's numbers (127-556.04, 04-series), 05 03_6 (Spiralfaden) both the lever's Sach-Nr. and the detent caliber's 020310. But the spring they draw is this one's: 05 03_5's collet and stud are 05 03 02-2 and -3 (the tapered slit bar; the pointed stud with its hole 5 from the end), and the lever's balance on 05 03_1 is this balance, measured on the sheet (crops at 2-4×): the rim scales 33.4 against its masses' boxed Ø 6.35 (the detent's 32.32 against 6.3, within 3 %), cut at 3 and 9, the four masses 30° from the cuts' line at r 16.2, the timing screws at 12 and 6. So the spring, its curve and its length are shared (likely).
- **The length.** 05 03_7: "vorm biegen der Kurve auf 13 Umgänge −3,5 mm kürzen", before bending the curve shorten to 13 turns less 3.5 mm. Read as the words read, the whole strip is cut to 13 turns of the coil as wound (Ø 12.515 at its middle) less 3.5 mm, 507.6 mm, and both curves are bent from its ends: **11.80 turns of helix between them, 505.4 mm pin to pin** (each curve 20.5-20.9 mm from the coil to its pin, 2.25 past the two pins, estimated). Read so, the two pinning points come out 7° apart in plan, where 05 03_1 draws them 5° apart (its "unterer" and "oberer Ansteckungspunkt" at 15° and 20°) and 05 03_5 draws the collet's pin end under the stud: the 3.5 mm is what brings them in line, for any whole number of turns. Phase 2a's reading (13 turns less 3.5 mm of helix, the curves added) leaves them 48° apart. Against it, the drawn coil counts lean a turn longer: 05 03_5's helix stands 9.5 between the curves (to the strip's middle, as its 0.75 to the collet's pin hole: 13.0 pitches, where this reading's 11.80 turns stand 8.61) and is drawn with about 12.4 turns; the coloured section draws 13 pitches between its top and bottom bands; Wempe 3955's spring (`5_Wempe_3955_O.jpg`, side-on: 12 front coils at 6.7 px a pitch, the pitch 0.73 ± 0.03 against the coil's Ø, the stud at the front so the top curve's junction is 0.4-0.6 turn above the first) has about 12 turns of helix: 11.5-12.8 (by its count, the top curve's junction 0.4-0.6 turn above the first front coil; by its height, the front coils' 79 px against the coil's Ø, 120-128 px, 8.1 ± 0.4 mm, where this reading's helix rises 8.61 and phase 2a's 9.43). Side views draw coils schematically; the pins' place is a construction. Settled as the whole strip (likely: the text sure, the pins agree; the coil counts don't).
- **The period.** 505.4 mm gives k 409 µN·m a radian and, with the weighed balance (2,526 g·mm²), **0.494 s**, 1.2 % short of 0.5 s (0.497 s with 2,561 until 18 October 2026, 0.491 s with 2,501 until 15 October 2026's shape batch); 14 turns less 3.5 mm would give 0.510 s, 15 turns less 3.5 mm (05 03_6's coil as wound, cut) 0.528 s, phase 2a's reading 0.512 s. 0.5 s wants 2,589 g·mm² (1.1 % more), or the strip 0.4 % thinner, or the modulus 1.1 % lower: inside the modulus's and the strip's ranges. Until 15 October 2026 the masses were built 5.4 high from 1.1 over the rim's lower face (05 03_2's far pair, its foot hidden by the arm's section), the balance 2,269 g·mm² and the period 0.468 s, 6.4 % short: their height, now read from the rim's face to 6.46 (05 03_2's near pair, Wempe 3955's side view: How the balance and escapement were measured), was the larger part of the gap, as this section expected.
- **The curve and Phillips' conditions.** 05 03_7's table (radii to the strip's inside from the coil's centre every 10°, pinned at 220°) is two compass arcs: 0-90° on a circle r 5.94 about a point 0.33 mm off the centre, 110-220° on one r 4.35 about a point 1.26 mm toward 90°, both within 22 µm; the sheet marks small circles at those two points (0.23 mm from the second): the compass's points, not another origin for the radii (the drawn curve scales to the table from the coil's centre within 0.07-0.12, where from the off-centre mark it would miss by 1.3). Phillips' conditions on the curve from the coil to its pin (E. Phillips, 1861, as HSPR): its centroid lies on the perpendicular to the junction's radius (1.7° off: the first condition holds), but 3.01 from the staff where the second wants R²/l = 1.91: its first moment l·OG is 61.7 mm² against R² 39.2, **1.58 times**. The outer curve, drawn in proportion to the stud, the same (1.60). The curves that meet both conditions to the same pins and headings (HSPR.design) are 28.4 and 26.0 mm long, leaving the coil 108° and 81° earlier.
- **The force on the pivots** under a couple, × R / couple (HSPR.lateral; 0 for Phillips' ends, about 1 for plain ones): **0.018** as drawn. Each curve's error lies along its junction's tangent, so the two cancel when the helix between them is a whole number of turns (11 turns 0.0010, 12 0.0009) and add at a half (0.030); the pins' place sets the helix's 0.80. Both ends designed to the drawn pins: 2.5e-5. The Hamilton's spring as its model draws it leaves 0.017 (hairspring.js), a designed one under 0.001: the Wempe's drawn curve is no better than plain ramps drawn with care; the tool fails only past 0.1. At large swings (an elastica, HSPR.large, ±300°) the force is 0.004 at 90°, 0.024 at 180°, 0.030 at 270°, and the beam elements (hairspring-fe.js's solver on 1,684 elements, the strip's 0.285) agree to 1 %.
- **The spring as the page draws it breathes** (15 October 2026; `WESC.HSP.bend`, as the Hamilton's `R.spPath`): its pull on the balance taken as a pure couple, the bending moment is the same all along the strip, so each part of it changes its curvature by θ/L and the heading at s from the collet turns θ(1 − s/L). The coils open and close concentrically, 4.9 % of their radius (0.31 mm) at the model's 207° swing, the terminal curves curling by as much, and the strip keeps its length. Against the strip solved as an elastica on the same path end to end (HSPR.large; HSPR.shape gives its nodes) it lies within **0.010 mm** from −300° to 300° (`wempe_hairspring.js` fails past 0.03). Until then every coil was turned rigidly by θ(1 − u) at its radius (u its height up the helix): 1.2 mm off the elastica at the model's swing (1.7 at 300°), the strip 4.5 % longer or shorter, and the coils never opening or closing.
- **Its isochronism.** The drawn spring stiffens a little as it winds (its couple over θ EI/L 1.0006 at 180°), so the balance gains 3.33 s a day at 188.5° and 2.82 at 199.5° against small swings: it **loses 0.46 s a day for each 10°** about the model's swing, 194.3° (since 19 October 2026's physics pass; 0.48 at 197.2° before it, 0.50 at 199.4° before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles; −2.5 s a day over the 1954 instruction's 225-270°), the elastica and the elements agreeing to 0.01 s a day. The model runs with it (`WESC.SET.HS` −0.464 since the physics pass, makeEsc's rate; −0.491 before it, −0.499 earlier; −0.543 about 207° with both curves the table's, −0.659 with the outer curve drawn in proportion to 3.87 until 15 October 2026, −0.700 about 214° before that, −0.721 about 218.6° before phase 2d): over the wind it counters the escapement's own isochronism, the rate **−0.26 to +0.20** s a day since the physics pass (−0.53 to +0.44 for the escapement alone; −0.06 to +0.03 before it) (−0.08 to +0.08 with the old outer curve).
- **Temperature: the linear term.** The rim's quarters are cantilevers from the arm's edge (5.8°) to the cuts (87.3°), 22.3 mm each; the nickel steel 0.405 inside the brass's 0.515. Their curl a °C by Timoshenko's bimetal (1925), each element's radius moving by the layers' mean growth, the root carried by the arm (nickel steel) and the curl integrated from the root, the masses clamping the rim over their slot (estimated); every part weighed on the model's solids. The steel spring alone loses 9.5 s a day a °C (its thermoelastic coefficient −240 to −270 × 10⁻⁶, physics.js's −250 and a steel spring on a steel balance's 11 s a day a °C, VintageWatchstraps; its expansion 11-12.5 × 10⁻⁶). With Guillaume's 42-44 % nickel steel inside (7-8.5 × 10⁻⁶, E 145-160; the sheet's "Nickelstahl Sonderlegierung" read so, estimated) and brass (18.5-20 × 10⁻⁶, E 95-110) the balance gives back 7.0 at the masses' drawn place, 30° from the cuts' line (Wempe 3955's and the 6MX's; 05 03_2 scales 32): **−2.5 s a day a °C** (the TU's C +2.5, against ±0.10; −2.3 before the bodies were weighed again on 15-16 October 2026); at 32°, −3.0. The masses null it at **19.5° from the cuts' line** (63-75° from the arm over the constants' 32 corners, 28 of them on the free arc; 20.5° before), a degree along the rim moving it 0.23 s a day a °C: the TU's ±0.10 is ±0.4° of their place, which the adjuster sets on trial (the 6MX's book: the masses are moved along the rim for compensation). The drawn place would compensate with a nickel steel of 4.7 × 10⁻⁶ (an invar-like 36-38 % alloy) or a spring of −201 × 10⁻⁶; with a plain nickel steel (11-12.5) no place on the arc does (−2.8 at its end). So the drawn place is a starting point, 10° short of compensation with the middle constants. Wempe 3955's masses, at 30° on an adjusted chronometer, stand where its adjuster left them, so its own constants compensated there: the alloy and the spring's coefficient are the open part, not the masses' place.
- **Temperature: the secondary error.** A steel spring's modulus falls faster as it warms: Dent's error, a few seconds a day gained at 15 °C with the rate right at 0 and 30 (Guillaume's Nobel lecture, 1920), about 2.5 s in the middle of a 30 °C range (VintageWatchstraps, "Temperature Compensation by Nickel Steels", after the Neuchâtel trials): 2-3 s, a quadratic of −0.009 to −0.013 s a day a °C². Over the TU's 4/20/36 °C that alone is **S +2.3 to +3.4 s**, and a rim of brass on a plain nickel steel (their quadratics 5.5 and 5.2 × 10⁻⁹ a °C²) leaves it so: outside the TU's ±1.20. The integral balance cancels it with a nickel steel whose expansion's quadratic is −8.5 to −15 × 10⁻⁹ a °C², negative as Guillaume's 44.4 % alloy's is (1899; the 6MX's book calls its balance "integral", Fig. 5.62); Guillaume's 42-44.4 % nickel steel as the National Bureau of Standards tabulates it (Circular 58, 2nd ed., 1923, Tables 5 and 13: its quadratic −3.4 to −2.51 × 10⁻⁹ a °C², its linear 6.85-8.41 × 10⁻⁶ agreeing with the rim's) cancels part of it: with Dent's error at 2 s, **S +0.74 to +0.89 s**, inside the TU's ±1.20; at 3 s, +1.9 to +2.0, outside it. The 6MX's passports state S +0.80 ±0.17 (five of the TU's trial, 1955-1993: `documents/6MX-figures.md`, "The test cards"), so Dent's error is taken at 2 s (likely; until 18 October 2026 S was taken as a fifth of the spring's own, Guillaume's balances at Neuchâtel in 1902, +0.5 to +0.7 s).
- **The build checks** (`tolerances.js` and `keystone.js` for the Wempe, with the bench and the Monte Carlo): below, Building it: tolerances, the bench and the Monte Carlo.

## Physics: energy (phase 2b)

Where the drive's energy goes, from the mainspring through the fusee and chain, the train and the escapement to the balance's damping, each from the Wempe's own parts, and whether the sheets' own figures agree with each other. The tools (no browser): `tools/wempe_mainspring.py` (the mainspring solved by the Hamilton's `mainspring.py` as a variant and fitted to 05 06_20; `--check` a second), `mainspring_friction.py` on it (`wempe_mainspring.py --friction`, about a minute), `tools/wempe_physics.js` (the budget, a second), `tools/gearing.js --model wempe` (the meshes tooth by tooth, about 2 min; `--quick` 30 s) and `tools/wempe_strength.js` (a second). The Hamilton's runs of the shared tools are unchanged (mainspring.py's constants and solve bit for bit, gearing.js's, strength.js's and physics.js's output byte for byte).

- **The mainspring, against its own drawing.** The strip 15 × 0.37 (05 06_20) in the drum's Ø 38.5 (05 07 01-1: its outer coil on the wall, threaded behind the Steg 05 07 00-1, its last 5.7 on the hook block 0.5 deep: tools/mainspring.py's MC_MS_STEG and MC_MS_BLOCK since 18 October 2026, a plain wall until then) on the arbor's core Ø 13.3 (05 07 02), solved as the Hamilton's is (its shape of least bending energy at each quarter turn of the wind, the coils pressing on each other, the inner end on the hook; the torque the energy's slope). No sheet gives its length or its free shape: the free curve is the Hamilton's measured (KLUwI2UUCMQ 32:18), scaled to the length, with its turns scaled by a set (×1 the Hamilton's), the eye's place the Hamilton's (9.4 mm), the modulus 207 GPa. The test: set up ¾ turn past its slack (05 06_20's "3/4 Umgang gespannt") and wound through the drawn fusee (the groove's radii, fusee.js; the chain's pitch line 1.02 out on the fusee and on the drum Ø 41.6), its torque on the fusee arbor at each of the sheet's 23 readings from turn 0.29 (the first 0.29 turn left out: the chain rises from the flange's slot there) against the measured force, each scaled to its mean. The drawn fusee and drum take 5.08 barrel turns over the fusee's 8.75, and the sheet's barrel marks (5, 4, 3, 2, 1 at fusee turns 0, 1.35, 2.87, 4.62, 6.59) fall at 5.08, 4.06, 3.07, 2.07, 1.05: the sheet's fusee and barrel agree to 0.08 turn.
- **The fit.** Thirteen lengths and sets were solved (`mainspring_wempe_L*.js`, about 12-25 min each): 1,300 mm at the Hamilton's set fits 6.3 % rms (its torque rises 2.2:1, the fusee's levers only 1.7:1), more set worse (13 % at ×1.3, 21 % at ×1.6), less set better: at ×0.7, 1,180 / 1,240 / 1,300 / 1,360 mm fit 2.3 / 1.2 / 1.6 / 2.1 %; at 1,240, ×0.6 / 0.7 / 0.75 / 0.8 fit 1.07 / 1.19 / 2.10 / 2.3 % (the sheet's own reading ±0.7 g, 0.5 %; an even pull would be 2.17 %).
