That is the fit without friction, and the force was measured running down, the coils' friction in it: each spring's friction worked out on its own solve (`mainspring_friction.py`, mu 0.10) and taken off, ×0.6 / 0.75 / 0.8 fit 3.24 / **1.83** / 2.31 % (worst 6.0 / 3.4 / 5.1 %). The friction takes most near full wind, where a spring with less set nears coil-bound (×0.6 gives 92.3 % of its frictionless torque fully wound, ×0.75 95.3 %), so the frictionless fit takes too little set to give the rise. **The model takes 1,240 mm at ×0.75** (26 October 2026; solved with the Steg and the hook block, its outer coil threaded behind the one and its end on the other, as the model's spring since 18 October 2026): 1.83 % rms with its coils' friction, 2.10 % without (4.5 % at worst), and at it the level closes with the drawn strip (below). Until 26 October 2026 the model took ×0.6, the best fit without friction (1.05 % re-solved with the Steg and block, 3.8 % at worst). It gives the measured profile's features: the hump at turns 1-1.5 (136 g), the dip at 4-5.75 (129), the rise to full wind (138), the last from the spring nearing coil-bound. Set up 0.65 or 0.85 turn it fits 2.50 or 1.77 % without friction. A quadratic in the length and the set through the nine tries near it, frictionless (it misses them by 0.24 % rms), puts the least at 1,245 mm and ×0.65 (1.26 %; 1,253 mm and ×0.67 through the eight before ×0.75 was solved); 1,275 mm at ×0.6, where the seven before it put the least, fits 1.99 % solved: the solves scatter by about a percent (the energy is nearly flat, mainspring.py), so the length is fitted to about ±50 mm. 2a's planning estimate from the measured torque's rise and a free strip's stiffness was 1.27-1.33 m. The page draws 1,240 (1,386, half the room, until 15 October 2026): 11.9 turns all on the wall, 12.7 run down, 17.8 fully wound, 18.9 coil-bound by the drawn spring's two packs; the solved spring is slack at 12.24 turns, run down at 12.99, fully wound at 18.07, and solves to 19.0 (coil-bound past it; 19.25 until the Steg and block, which take a quarter turn of room).
- **The level closes with the drawn strip** (26 October 2026). The measured force is 0.913 of the solved spring's frictionless torque at 0.37 and 207 GPa (`wempe_physics.js`); over 200-210 GPa and 0.365-0.375 mm that leaves 1.6-13.6 % for the losses, against 2.0-14.6 % worked out below, and at their middle it asks a strip 0.370 thick: the sheet's own figure. 05 06_20's "0,37" is not overwritten (15 October 2026, read again at 8-20×, 1024 px scan, about 6 px a figure): its 7 is the sheet's own stencil 7, a short downstroke at the top bar's left and a bar across its stem, drawn the same in "12,17" and "9,71" on the same sheet (radii the fusee's sheets confirm), and nothing of another figure shows beside its strokes; no other sheet gives the strip's thickness (05 07_*, the arbor's hook, the coloured section's label "Zugfeder 05 07 03-2", whose 9.4 px/mm can't tell 0.355 from 0.37). The sheet measured the strip it names: "Zugfeder 15 × 0,37" is written on the same sheet as the force, 24.7.41, measured on the fusee arbor (the sheet's sum, 131.5 × 262 / 19.6, takes no fusee radius) running down ("Ablauf"), the mean of two runs, in the 1941 barrel (0207, 15.4.41: the same Ø 38.5 bore and Ø 13.3 core as the 1948 drum) with a steel fusee ("Stahlschnecke"), so not an earlier spring. Until 26 October 2026, with the free curve at ×0.6, the measured force was 0.811 of the solved (0.802 before the Steg and block): 12.6-23.2 % for the losses, a strip about 0.356 at their middle, and 2.70 GPa fully wound as solved, past a steel mainspring's limit (about 2.5-2.6 GPa), carried as the solved strip pulling 23 % more than the sheet measured (Unresolved until then; Closed). Where the 23 % went: about ×1.10 is the losses to the fusee arbor at their middle, about ×1.13 the free curve's set, which no sheet gives and which moves the level more than anything else the sheets leave open (measured over solved, frictionless: ×0.5 0.733, ×0.6 0.802, ×0.7 0.842, ×0.75 0.913, ×0.8 0.910, ×0.85 0.949, ×1 1.077, where the spring would give less than the sheet measured with no losses at all); nothing is left for the modulus or the thickness, where closing it at ×0.6 wanted the modulus at 200 GPa, the strip at 0.365 and the losses near 12.5 % at once. As solved the strip is stressed 2.46 GPa fully wound (6M / b t²; 2.60 GPa at its inner coils along the strip, 2.44 its median, `motion3d_springs.py`; 2.66 and 2.37 at ×0.6), at a steel mainspring's working limit, where a mainspring is meant to work. The budget takes the solved shape at the measured level (×0.997 with the losses at their middle; ×0.890 at ×0.6): 427 mN·m run down to 839 fully wound (1.97:1; 473 to 824, 1.74:1, at ×0.6; 2a's estimate 1.9:1 to 749), 2.45 GPa (2.41 at ×0.6; 2a's 2.2), inside a tempered steel mainspring's working range. The swing doesn't take the solved spring: the escapement is driven by 05 06_20's measured force through the train (escphys.js), so the page's swing and rate are as they were. The 1958 repair book's calculated barrel torque for the MX (p. 58: 103.2-110.3 kg·mm fully wound, 56.2-61.2 two days on) is 1.36-1.45 and 1.10-1.20 times 05 06_20's turned to the barrel (76.2 and 50.9 kg·mm, its losses in it); the drawn strip solved without friction lies between (85.7 and 53.8 at ×0.75; 94.3 and 62.1 at ×0.6).
- **The coils' friction** (mainspring_friction.py on the solved spring, mu 0.08-0.16, lubricated steel): it takes 0.8 % of the spring's work running at 0.10 (0.7-1.4 %), winding against running 1.7 % (1.4-2.7 %); fully wound running gives 95.3 % of the frictionless torque, run down 97.6 % (at ×0.6, nearer coil-bound fully wound, 1.95 % of the work and 92.3 % fully wound). Put into the fit it brings it to 1.83 % rms from 2.10, where at ×0.6 it took the fit from 1.05 % to 3.24 %: the set the model takes (The fit). A first-order estimate, the state's shape taken as friction leaves it, which near coil-bound overstates it.
- **The fusee, chain and barrel** to the fusee arbor: the spring's own losses 0.7-11.1 % (its coils' friction, up to what mainsprings measure, running 10-20 % under winding, ICMFF9: the strip's edges and the steel's hysteresis, as the Hamilton's physics.js takes them), the drum on its arbor's journals (Ø 4.48) under the chain's pull 1.0-1.5 %, the chain's links on their rivets (Ø 0.55) on and off the drums 0.3-0.5 %, the fusee arbor's pivots (Ø 2.8 and 4.5) 0-1.9 % (if the sheet's lever counted them; its rig isn't drawn): together 0.854-0.980 of the spring's frictionless torque reaches the fusee arbor (mu 0.10-0.15, oiled steel on brass).
- **The train** (gearing.js --model wempe: the four meshes on the page's own outlines, the tables' tips, roots and tooth thicknesses, at the holes' centres): fusee wheel 90 on the centre pinion 14 (m 0.4356): backlash 265 µm, tip clearance 245 µm, the mesh 3.0-5.8 % at mu 0.10-0.20 (steel leaves on brass teeth, run dry), 60 % of the action before the line of centres; centre 90 on the intermediate pinion 12 (m 0.35529): 176 µm, 190 µm, 3.5-7.1 %, all of it before the line; intermediate 80 on the seconds pinion 10 (m 0.28066): 136 µm, 145 µm, 4.0-8.0 %, all before; seconds 80 on the escape pinion 10 (m 0.26333): 170 µm, 100 µm, 1.9-3.7 %, 46 %. The pivots (the driven arbor's, on its tooth loads, mu 0.10-0.15) 3.3-5.0, 2.4-3.6, 3.2-4.8, 1.1-1.6 %. The train with its pivots 0.730-0.833, the geometric mean 0.780 since A.4's pinions (0.666-0.797 and 0.729 before them): the model takes it since 19 October 2026's physics pass (`MESH` in escphys.js; 2a's 0.749 from the meshes' rule of thumb, 0.661-0.848, until then). The teeth's ripple over a day moves the swing 205.2-210.6° at mu 0.10 (204.1-212.4° at 0.20; 211.9-217.5° and 210.7-219.4° before the masses' height, 15 October 2026; 216.5-222.0° and 215.3-223.9° before phase 2d) and the time 342-518 µs about the mean rate. The depthing windows (until a mesh jams, or its friction takes the swing out of the model's band, 193-270° since 15 October 2026, 200-270° before): -240/+436, -190/+355, -145/+281, -100/+263 µm about the drawn centres (every mesh passes the wide search's end, 1 m), so the holes must sit within ±25 µm (the seconds mesh: its window less the pivots' 50 µm shake, the drawn bush bores, estimated): a hobby CNC's work. `gearing_wempe.json`.
- **The escapement** at 12 h since 19 October 2026's physics pass (86.1 µN·m): a tooth's 24° gives 36.05 µJ an oscillation and the balance nets 20.67 µJ (57 %) at 194.3°: the strike 10.4 % of the impulse, the unlocking 26.1 % (the detent's spring and preload 9.7 %); the balance's 2.29 mJ (k 399) loses 20.67 µJ at Q 697 (TF 111 s). Before it, at 2a's reference (82.8 µN·m): a tooth's 24° gives 34.6 µJ an oscillation and the balance nets 22.2 µJ (64 %) at 197.2° (since 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles: the strike 14.9 % of the impulse, the unlocking 16.2 %; 22.7 µJ, 65 %, at 199.4° since 15 October 2026's shape batch: the teeth as 05 04_23 draws them, the balance re-weighed, TF 108 s; 20.7 µJ, 60 %, at 207.3° before): the strike takes 12.5 % of the impulse (20 % before), the unlocking 15.9 % (17 %; the detent's spring and preload 6.4 %, 6.9 % with its speed, counted to where it leaves the stone: phase 2d), the rest is the wheel's speed lost at each lock (and, while the stone rides the tooth it let go, its friction on the wheel's chase). The balance's 2.45 mJ at that swing (k 404 µN·m a radian) loses 2π/Q of it an oscillation: 22.7 µJ at the model's Q 679 (TF 108 s; its parts' damping Q 507-1,309). (Before the batch: 2.58 mJ at 207.3°, k 395, 20.7 µJ at Q 785.) (Until the masses' height was read, 15 October 2026: 20.2 µJ at 214.0°, k 358.)
- **The power at 12 h**: 92.5 µW at the fusee arbor (measured), 72.1 µW at the escape wheel (through 0.780), 41.3 µW lost in the balance: 44.7 % of what the fusee gives (69.4 µW through 0.749 and 41.4 µW before the physics pass).
- **Does it close?** To swing the sources' band, 225-270° (the 1954 instruction), the measured drive through the train as built (0.889-1.065 of the reference) needs Q 922-2,079; the balance's parts give 509-1,316: it closes, over Q 922-1,316 (847-1,309 before 19 October 2026's physics pass; 895-1,247 before the shape batch, 812-1,246 with the lighter balance). At the model's Q 697 the drive through the train's range swings 186-203° at 12 h (the model's 194.3° since the physics pass; 184-204° and 197.2° at Q 679 before it; 186-207° and 199.4° before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles); 225° wants Q 1,035 at the model's drive (961 before the physics pass). From the solved spring at its own level, with the losses' range, the drive would be 0.817-1.218 of the reference (0.863-1.330 at ×0.6). `wempe_physics.js` exits 1 if the level, the stress at the measured level or this closure fails.
- **Strength** (wempe_strength.js, strength.js's ranges, fully wound at the measured level, every stage lossless): the mainspring 2.45 GPa (2.41 with the free curve at ×0.6, until 26 October 2026; margin 1.0-1.1 to a mainspring's 2.5-2.6 GPa limit: as a mainspring is meant to, it works near it); the chain's pull 38.4 N on its plates (62-123 MPa), rivets (81 MPa in double shear), the fusee's pin and the hooks, margins 8 and up; the band (0.22 × 0.78, its width estimated) 224 MPa, margin 6; the fusee wheel's teeth (Lewis, Y 0.2) 89 MPa against brass's yield, margin 3.4-5.1, the others 7 and up; the centre arbor's pivot (Ø 1.19) 218 MPa against steel's yield, margin 5.5; the maintaining spring (at the strongest drive, the moment along the bent band: wempe_sustaining.js) 825 MPa, margin 1.5-2.2 to hardened spring steel's yield (~~) (868 MPa until 16 October 2026, from twice the full-wind drive); the maintaining ratchet's teeth under the detent and the click wheel's under the click, margins 1.6 and up; a strong hand on the key against the stop (1.06 N·m, overwind.js) puts 104 N on the stop pin (Ø 1.6, margin 6) and the stop bar (2 × 1.45, 3.6 out of its slot, margin 2.3 hardened, 0.7-1.1 soft: the bar must be hardened; no sheet gives it).
- **The maintaining spring** (`tools/wempe_sustaining.js`, about 20 s; as `sustaining.js` for the Hamilton's). Its eye's pin goes up into the ratchet and the pin at 295° down into the fusee wheel (05 06_4 A-B: Readings corrected), and the ratchet's teeth and detent on A-B make running clockwise there, so the drive pushes the eye toward the square end and closes the ring: its outer edge leaves the wheel's recess (Ø 32.3 +0.05 round the ring's 32.3 -0.1), where opened it would bear on the wall at once and barely give. The short wide tail past the wheel's pin would turn out about it as the band closes, but has 0.025-0.075 to the wall, so that end is held as clamped; the eye's end turns on its pin. The band (I = t w³ / 12, its traced width) bent as an elastica, the eye's pin brought where the ratchet turns it: 1.22 N·m a radian at small turns (Castigliano's, the same), stiffening as it closes (T = 1.2204 ψ + 0.2956 ψ²). The fusee's drive (325-355 mN·m over the wind, 05 06_20) closes it 14.4-15.6°, 1.1-2.3° short of its eye's round end meeting its square end (16.7°): its ends 0.27-0.58 mm apart, nearly meeting, as Gould has such a spring ("a simple steel split-ring, with ends nearly meeting ... When the pull of the chain is removed ... the ends spring apart a little", 1923, p. 238); it carries the drive by its bend. So over the strip's 1.25-1.35 and the modulus's 200-210 GPa (0.12-0.70 mm). Bent 825 MPa at the strongest drive, 1.5-2.2 times short of hardened spring steel's yield. While the key turns: the clicks let go, the ratchet turns back with the spring until the maintaining detent takes a tooth (none to a whole tooth, 3°), and the train runs on the push (89 % of the drive at once, the detent half a tooth late), which falls as the wheel turns (a turn in 6.43 h); the balance keeps going 10.7-13.9 minutes, its swing under 185° after 31 s to 2.5 min and under a turn after 49 s to 3.2 min; a wind costs under a millisecond. With the eye's hole 0.8 toward the square end (as built until 16 October 2026: Unresolved) it would bank at 13.5° and run closed, pushing 89 %; 0.8 the other way, its ends 1.1-1.4 mm apart. Until 16 October 2026 the pins were the other way round and the push was the running drive spent over 05 06_9's "x = 0,27 mm" read as its deflection at the pins, 1.09° and 70 s. The page draws the ring closed so (fusee.js: the same elastica, the ratchet `s.ms` ahead of the wheel) and runs the drive from it (escphys.js's `susDrv`).

## Physics: in time (phase 2d)

The escapement integrated beat by beat, as the Hamilton's is (its README, "Checked in time"): `tools/dynamics.js --model wempe` (about 2 minutes; `--quick` 40 s) moves the balance, the escape wheel with the whole train behind it and the detent as bodies through every contact, on contact geometry worked out apart from makeEsc's, and holds the result against makeEsc's figures; `tools/tripping.js --model wempe` (`tools/wempe_tripping.js`; about 2½ minutes, its runs in child processes many at once, `--quick` about a minute) takes the detent as a frame of beams. The Hamilton's runs of both print what they did, figure for figure, and `escgolden.js` holds its makeEsc bit for bit.

- **The bodies.** The balance 2,526.2 g·mm² (weighed; 2,560.7 until 18 October 2026, with an estimated nut on each mass where the drawings have a tapped hole in its top; 2,501.4 before the shape batch and the tool weighing one revision, 2,268.6 until the masses' height was read, 15 October 2026), run at the train's 0.5 s as makeEsc runs it (the drawn hairspring gives 0.494 s: the timing a maker sets). The train at the escape wheel, each arbor weighed on its solids and reflected by its ratio squared (`tools/wempe_inertia.py` now weighs the intermediate, the centre with the cannon tube, change wheel, hour wheel and hands, and the fusee wheel with the maintaining work that turns with it): **12.472 g·mm²**, of it the escape arbor 8.71 (11.19 with its collet counted in both revisions, until 15 October 2026), the seconds arbor and hand 1.254, the intermediate 0.024, the centre and motion work 0.003, the fusee wheel 0.0006. makeEsc's JW, 9.96 (the escape and seconds arbors; 12.44 before), is kept: the rest would move its swing −0.08°. The detent's moving part 94.3 mg, 8.35 g·mm² about its flexure (makeEsc's ID 8.46 until 18 October 2026, before the gold spring's bow, and 8.43 before the horn was traced) (94.9 and 8.60 with both revisions' pieces), its centre of mass 8.37 mm from it (the page's solids weighed with the detent at rest; 93.6 mg, 8.25 and 8.26 with the horn 3.5 long). Its spring 0.07 × 1.9 × 5, steel (205 GPa): 2.227 mN·m a radian.
- **The preload and the drawn spring.** As drawn the foot, the spring and the blade lie on one line with the stone at its lock (escapement.js), so a banking where the detent is drawn leaves the spring straight: **no preload**. 2a's estimate, 0.63 g at the stone (79.6 µN·m), is the spring bent 2.05° past straight at its banking, 0.46 mm at the stone. Neither the banking nor its setting is drawn (Unresolved; the 1954 instruction's п. 11 sets the lock by a banking). In time the drawn spring alone, unpreloaded, brings the detent home before the next tooth; the preload is for knocks (below) and costs swing: pressing the stone on the tooth it has let go (below), it costs 5.9° (219.8° unpreloaded, 213.9° at 0.63 g, with the lighter balance). The banking (15 October 2026) is the block's cross screw, its head on the pipe at the stone (How the balance and escapement were measured); its setting isn't given, so the preload stays the estimate.
- **What the time domain found that makeEsc missed** (the Wempe's alone):
  1. *The detent flung off the stone's corner.* The gold spring's square end leaves the discharge stone's square acting corner where the lift turns within a cell of the table, still moving up at the stone's speed (4.2 rad/s of the detent, 73 mm/s at the gold spring's tip), and flies on before it falls. makeEsc's `depart` took its speed by a central difference across the corner (−0.39 rad/s, falling), so its fall began 1.2° early; now a square-ended spring (`TSQ`) leaves with the slope it came up, and makeEsc's fall is the integration's to 1e-5 rad.
  2. *Its work is lost there.* makeEsc counted the detent's preload and spring over the stone's whole lift table, its fall down the bevel handing that energy back to the balance, as a massless detent riding the stone down would; the drawn detent, flung off the corner, never touches the bevel, so its energy and its speed there are lost. `DEP` counts the work to where it leaves the stone: 3.2° of swing, −0.41 s a day (in time, the ride's force kept off the wheel, 215.39°; makeEsc with `DEP` 215.38°).
  3. *The stone rides the tooth it has let go.* Let go from rest with the seconds arbor behind it, the wheel has turned 0.5 % of a pitch when the detent leaves the stone, its tooth still under the stone's flat, so the detent, flung up, falls back onto that tooth's land at 0.9° of the balance and rides it, and down its back, until the tooth has passed at 10.3°. The stone's friction on the land (μ 0.15, as on the lock: estimated) holds back the wheel's chase; the back, falling away, takes the detent's preload and spring into the wheel; the detent, unable to follow the back as it steepens, hops down it (17-20 landings a beat in time). `RW` runs makeEsc's chase so (quasi-static, the detent's inertia in its contact force; off the back where it can't follow, falling back on): −1.4° of swing. The ride's table is the tooth's drawn arcs (05 04_23: the land on the tips' circle, the ∅ 6 cutter's back) against the stone's half round, worked out apart from makeEsc's ride and within its own 400 steps of a pitch.
  4. dynamics.js's own: the corner's turn lies within one cell of the lift table, narrower than a step, so a step straddling it ended on the bevel with the detent pressed back on it, and missed its leaving one beat in seven; a step on the stone now ends in that cell.
  With `DEP` and `RW` (`WESC.SET`) the swing at 12 h was **214.0°** (218.6° before), the escapement's own rate −8.23 s a day; what runs on makeEsc's swing moved with it, in the sections above. **Since the shape batch** (15 October 2026: the teeth as 05 04_23 draws them, the balance re-weighed, TF 108 s from the 6MX video) it was **199.4°**, the escapement's own rate −9.27 s a day (**197.2°** and −11.36 since 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles and the discharge stone's place: dynamics.js --model wempe the swing 197.2°, the tooth landing 12.2° after the release, the stone's face −13.3° from the line of centres; before them: dynamics.js --model wempe finds the swing 199.5°, the tooth landing 11.6° after the release, the impulse stone's face −14.8° from the line of centres (makeEsc −14.8°), the detent off the stone at −1.75°, on the escaping tooth at 0.66°, home at 8.65° (makeEsc 8.19°); Twist (120°) brings it within 2° in 134.5 s; the figures after this sentence are the 207.3° model's where they differ by less. Before it (the masses' height, 10 % more inertia) it was **207.3°**, the escapement's own rate −8.28 s a day, and dynamics.js --model wempe finds: the swing 207.2° (makeEsc 207.3°), the tooth landing 16.0° after the release (16.1°), the stone's face −10.4° from the line of centres (makeEsc −10.3°), the detent off the stone at −1.75°, on the escaping tooth at 0.83°, home at 10.19° (makeEsc 9.91°), back 15.15 ms before the next tooth; over the wind's drive 201° → 212°, +0.55 s a day (+0.51 s a day each 10° of swing: the escapement alone now just past the Hamilton's 0.5, so the Wempe's check takes the escapement with its drawn hairspring, −0.14 for each 10°); Twist (124°) brings it within 2° in 138.5 s; knocks 78 g (0.5 ms), 22 g (3 ms), 31.4 rad/s; at sea 0.010 s a day. The figures below are the lighter balance's where they differ by less.
- **Against makeEsc** (the integration's detent dead on its banking, its inertia where makeEsc takes it; runs of 150 s, the balance settling over 62 s): the swing **213.9°** (makeEsc 214.0°); the tooth lands on the impulse stone **17.1°** after the release (17.2°); the detent leaves the stone at −1.75° (−1.76°), lands on the escaping tooth at 0.87° (0.87°) and is on its banking at 10.56° (makeEsc's ride ends at 10.32°); the escapement's own rate **−8.22 s a day** (−8.23); over the wind's drive (95-104 %) the rate changes +0.54 s a day in time and in makeEsc's escapement (with the drawn hairspring's −0.77, −0.23). Bouncing on its banking (restitution 0.3, estimated) the same to 0.1°. Every check is the Hamilton's: the swing within 3°, the landing 1.5°, the detent's fall 0.5°, the rate 0.3 s a day, isochronism 0.1 s a day.
- **Where the tooth lands.** 05 04_1's layout draws the tooth falling on the impulse stone 21° before the line of centres, as if the wheel were there at once; makeEsc's solved chase put it at −9.2° (phase 2a). In time it lands with the balance at 14.74°, the stone's face **−9.3°** from the line of centres: the wheel, starting from rest with the seconds arbor behind it on 83 µN·m, chases the stone 17.1° of the balance. The stone's friction on the escaping tooth moves it a little: −9.8° with none, −9.5° at μ 0.10, −9.1° at 0.20 (the swing 215.6°, 214.5°, 213.5°).
- **The detent's return.** It is home on its banking 14.6 ms before the next tooth reaches the stone (at 49.2°), not lifted at all as it arrives; 12.4-14.6 ms over its spring half and twice as stiff, 0.06 thick, the preload half and 1.5×, its inertia twice, its banking lively or dead, the train 1.5× its inertia. Unpreloaded, the spring alone brings it home in time.
- **Starting and the least drive.** Twist (128°, the page's 0.6 of the running swing, estimated) brings the swing within 2° of its 214° in 139 s. The least drive that keeps it going is 14 % of the reference (makeEsc's 25 %, at its AMIN 46°): under AMIN the impulse stone, turning back with the tooth still on it, carries it out of the teeth's path and lets it run on to the locking stone, a tooth a swing all the same, so in time the least swing is the one that passes the gold spring (17.6°), not the impulse's end. Held (run down) from 150°, the balance runs down as makeEsc's freeStep has it within 0.5°.
- **Knocks and the swing pushed** (the detent rigid, dynamics.js): a twist about the staff upsets it at about 32 rad/s in 2 ms (0.69 of the balance's top speed: a tooth escapes); a blow across the plate lets a tooth go at about 81 g lasting 0.5 ms and 23 g lasting 3 ms (a steady push lifts the detent off its banking at 10 g and frees the wheel at 16 g); unpreloaded, 37 g and 7 g. Driven to a 333° swing it keeps one unlocking a swing; at 362°, past makeEsc's TRIP (348°), the stone comes round and unlocks again in the same swing.
- **At sea.** Yaw ±10° over 8 s with ±0.3 g sideways moves the rate 0.014 s a day: the balance is poised and the gimbals keep the staff upright.
- **The detent as a frame** (tripping.js --model wempe): the spring 0.07 × 1.9 clamped at its foot, the blade's two sections, the horn along its curve and the gold spring along its traced bow (11.3 mm from its bending point to its tip; both as `escapement.js` draws them, `WESC.HORN` and `WESC.GOLD`, since 18 October 2026: a straight horn and the gold spring's polyline, 11.6 mm, lying along it, before) as beam elements, the gold spring closing onto the horn at three places near its end, each at its drawn gap; the square with the gold spring's head and screw, and the pipe with the stone, rigid; the banking one contact, the pipe's side on the block's cross screw's head at the stone (15 October 2026: How the balance and escapement were measured; until then under the blade 0.7-1.4 mm from the stone, where makeEsc puts the Hamilton's stop button). Its moving parts weigh 94.88 mg and 8.621 g·mm² about the flexure against the solids' 94.90 and 8.600 (the horn 4.4 long). Hinged at the flexure and made rigid it runs as dynamics.js does (the swing within 0.4°, the rate 0.09 s a day, the release, departure and home within 0.3°, every beat). As drawn, the spring bending along its whole 5 mm, it is 2 % softer at the stone; its modes 82 Hz (the spring), 624 Hz (the gold spring), 1.2 kHz (the spring's second), 3.1 kHz (the gold spring's) and on. The Wempe's lock: its stone's flat draws −3.7° (on the wheel's radius), so a held tooth pushes the stone out, held only by the preload and the friction (μ 0.15 against tan 3.7°, 0.065); lifting the stone lets the tooth on 0.085°, and past the depth of lock, 175 µm at the stone, the tooth is free (makeEsc's recoil, held at 0 or more, is 0 all the way: the frame takes the flat's line from makeEsc's toothBack).
  - **Running as drawn** no tooth gets past at the model's settings (12 beats; over the estimates varied at the contacts' own stiffness, none either), and a held tooth's stone lifts **44 %** of the depth of lock (77 µm) since 18 October 2026, the horn curved and the gold spring's bow closing onto it near its end as drawn (41 %, 72 µm, from 15 October 2026 with the straight horn and the gold spring lying along it), the banking on the pipe and the horn 4.4 long: under tripping.js's margin, half (64 % with the gold spring half as stiff; 32 %, 26 %, 21 % at 1.5×, 2×, 3× the preload). Until 15 October 2026 (the banking under the blade, the horn 3.5) it lifted **55 %** (96 µm), on the return swing at −20°: the gold spring, bent 0.30 mm at its tip as the discharge stone passes it (2.4 µJ stored), comes clear at −17.6° and slams back onto the horn, and the detent takes its blow. dynamics.js and makeEsc take the gold spring massless, its energy lost; the Hamilton's light trip spring stores a fraction of it. tripping.js's margin for the Hamilton is half the depth: the Wempe's wants the preload 1.1× the estimate (0.72 g at the stone) for it (37 % at 1.5×, 26 % at 2×). **Unpreloaded (the banking where the detent is drawn) teeth get past**: 27 in 3 s (6 since 15 October 2026), the gold spring's blow throwing the stone off. So the banking must be set with a preload, as the 1954 instruction's banking-set lock suggests.
  - **The return pass turns on the stone's contact with the gold spring** (a penalty spring, tripping.js's 500 N/m): stiffer (2,000), the stone, meeting the spring's square end with its bevel, pushes it partly lengthwise, the loop round the pipe gives, the tip is carried on to about −25° before it slips off, and its release throws the detent: teeth get past even at twice the preload; softer (125), the stone lifts 23 % (7 % since 15 October 2026; 4× stiffer, 6 teeth past). A real contact is stiffer than 500 N/m, so this is the Wempe's open question for a build: the tip is taken square and unturning (05 04 01-2's blank), and the gold spring's alloy (its stiffness) and its free shape round the pipe are estimates (Unresolved); the horn's length, 4.4, is read (likely).
  - **Pushed**, the frame keeps one tooth a swing to 360°, the last run, past makeEsc's TRIP (353°; rerun 18 October 2026: it tripped at 357-360° before). **Knocks**, preloaded: a blow across the plate lets a tooth go at about 70 g lasting 0.5 ms and 20 g lasting 3 ms (the rigid detent 78 and 22 g), a twist about the staff at 4.8 rad/s in 2 ms; unpreloaded it trips on its own. The 3 ms blow against the preload: 17.9 g at 0.8 times the estimate, 20.4 g at it, 22.6 g at 1.2 times (17.6, 20.0, 22.5 before the horn was traced), so the 20 g the check asks wants 0.61 g, the knock's floor (Building it). At 1.5 times the search read 2.4 g, a fault of the search, not the detent: there alone the run's first drop from rest takes 268 ms against 20 (the stone riding the tooth it let go), and its first blows, falling in it, stall the wheel; from the second drop on it runs as the rest, and 1.5 times is left out of the floor's runs.

## Physics: the transmission (16 October 2026)

Every part that moves, as one system and in time: `tools/wempe_transmission.js` (about a minute with its mesh tables kept, `MC_TR_TABS`; 2-7 minutes fresh, the tables built in threads, as loaded as the machine is; in `ci.py`'s quick steps). The mechanisms above were each worked with the others still or rigid; here the barrel on its solved mainspring, the chain, the fusee and its two clicks, the maintaining ratchet, spring and detent, the fusee wheel and the going train, the escape wheel, the motion work and hands, the up-and-down work, the key and the stop are bodies (their inertias weighed on the solids, `wempe_inertia.json` and `mechanisms_wempe.json`; the chain's, the up-and-down wheel's and the key's estimated) joined by nonlinear elements, stepped at a microsecond (semi-implicit Euler):

- **To watch it.** `verification/assets/transmission/index.html` (linked from the report's section 13) plays the runs in plan: every part coloured by the load it carries against its running load, its deviations from rigid motion magnified as a finite-element post-processor shows a deformed shape, with the time histories; its data, `data.js`, is `tools/wempe_transmission_trace.js`'s.
- **The elements.** The mainspring's torque as solved, at 05 06_20's level, and its coils' friction (`mainspring_friction_wempe.json`, 5.5 % between winding and running at μ 0.10) as a stick-slip element, so it changes branch as the drum reverses; the chain in tension only (it goes slack), its lever on the fusee where it lies (fusee.js's RFT) and, in the whole-run ripple, the polygon a 2.05 mm link makes on each drum; each pawl (the two clicks half a tooth apart, the maintaining detent) a body on its pivot under its spring (`wempe_ratchets.js`'s), riding the teeth's backs and catching a face only if it is down; the maintaining spring's drawn ring (MSK, MSK2 and its bank MSB, `wempe_sustaining.js`'s from 05 06 01-1, read from WPHYS); every mesh tooth by tooth (`gearing.js`'s `mesh` on the page's outlines: the driven gear's place under load through a pitch, the backlash to its back flank, the torque ratio with the teeth's friction at μ 0.15), contact on either flank or neither; the motion work's two meshes and the up-and-down work's from `dial.js`'s tables (`wempe_train.js`'s `addOutline`); the cannon pinion's friction fit, which slips past its torque; the key's one-way coil spring (51 00 11: it grips whenever the hand holds the key against the fusee and slips only if the key is turned the wrong way); the stop bar on its pin. The escapement is `dynamics.js --model wempe`'s own, its escape wheel driven each step by the escape pinion's mesh (`dynamics.js`'s `o.tq`, inert by default: both models' output unchanged).
- **The whole wind** (static, running): the maintaining spring runs at most **15.64°** (fully wound) against its bank's 16.72°, so it never banks running; the train's efficiency at μ 0.15 with the meshes' own ratios is 0.739 (escphys.js's 0.749; wempe_physics.js's range 0.666-0.797). Over a whole run with every ripple at once (the chain's polygon swings its lever 0.49 % on the fusee fully wound, 0.11 % on the drum, once a link, as fine as 05 06_20's own ±0.7 g reading; the meshes' ratios through their pitches) the swing ranges 192.1-207.6° against the smooth 194.1-201.9°, and the time wanders **3.4 ms** within a day: nothing a navigator rating it daily carries.
- **The modes** (linearised at 12 h only to find them): the lowest, **49.2 Hz**, is the barrel and fusee swinging on the maintaining spring; then the chain (495 Hz), the cannon pinion and minute hand on their fit (2.5 kHz), the train (3.3-17 kHz), the pawls (50-63 kHz). None lies near the beat's 2 Hz.
- **A beat** (the coupled run against the same escapement on a rigid train given the torque the train gives at its place, each mesh's ratio where it stands): every beat taken; the train's give, inertia and backlash change the work an impulse gives by **+0.05 %** (as a drive, +0.05° of swing and +0.001 s a day: under 3.00.04's escapement, before 3.03's impulse face and release, it was −1.3 %, so the figure turns on where the impulse falls, and is small either way); no mesh of the going train lets go but the escape pinion's, 1.3 ms at most as the escape wheel lands on its stone (dynamics.js lands it dead: the most the seconds wheel can drive into the mesh, 26 times its running force, 53 MPa fully wound against brass's endurance 120-170); the seconds hand overshoots its 3° step by 19″; the cannon pinion carries 0.8 mN·m and never slips; the motion work rests in its backlash between steps and is taken up each step, never on its back flank. Settling a run first matters: the coils' friction holds whatever branch the drum last turned on, so a run that starts with the drum still swinging keeps part of the winding branch (2-3 % more drive) for some 90 beats; the movement is settled to rest about critically damped, then set on the running branch. **Averaged over 8 starts** (the owner's choice, 27 October 2026): one run's drive figure turned on where the train's meshes started in their teeth (which tooth pair bears, and the seconds wheel's few let-goes in 48 beats), so it jumped between about −11° and +5° as unrelated inputs moved by a micron (the maintaining detent's contact radius 18.300 against 18.301: −5.8° and −11.0°). The beat now runs from 8 starts, the four train meshes' phases MESH's and seven more spread from them by a fixed golden-ratio sequence, each with its rigid twins at the same places; the check judges the mean (−2.59°, −0.021 s a day; the starts −9.48° to +7.82°, sd 5.95) against the same 8° and 0.5 s a day, and the mean holds within about 2° as those inputs move (−2.6°, −4.4°, −4.2°, −3.2° at 18.033, 18.034, 18.300, 18.301). The meshes' loads below are start 0's.
- **Winding.** Put on at 24 h, the maintaining ratchet falls back 1.5° onto its detent (18 N on its face); the hand needs 370 mN·m. **Let go**, the fusee and barrel run up to half a click tooth free to the nearer click. Eased back (the hand's hold relaxed over 0.15 s) the click catches with **68 N** at most (138 MPa in the click wheel's tooth). Let go outright (the fingers off the paddle) it strikes with **297 N** (608 MPa: past brass's yield, as is the fusee wheel's teeth's 359 MPa), the maintaining spring is thrown 1.2° onto its bank and the blow reaches the locking stone (0.1 N against 0.011 running: the tooth's pressure draws the detent in), and the chain goes slack and snatches at 126 N (3.2 times under its rivets' strength). Over every estimate the blow rests on (the click's face 0.25-2 times, the contacts' damping 2-20 %, the bank 0.25-4 times, the chain 120-700 N/mm) it lies at 201-488 N: at or past the tooth's yield. The blows at the strokes' ends are few on each tooth (about 2,300 in 30 years: a wind's 3.7 turns in half-turn strokes over 35 teeth), so they are judged against yield (low-cycle), not against the endurance the running load is (`wempe_strength.js`). The tool reports the outright let-go as a warning (`~~`): a key is let back, not let go.
- **The stop.** Wound into it at a turn a second, the bar meets its pin with 35 N; held there by a strong hand (1.06 N·m), 49 N, the hand's torque less the spring's on the fusee. `wempe_strength.js` adds the two (104 N), on the safe side.
- **The motion work and up-and-down work.** The hour hand's play against the minute hand 1.1-1.3 minutes; the up-and-down hand's 0.46-0.51°, about 5 minutes of its 56-hour scale. The cannon pinion's fit must lie between 3.0 mN·m (a 45 g knock turning the hands on it: the minute hand's unbalance, 0.56 g 11.4 mm off its axis) and 94 mN·m (setting the hands forward fully wound until the balance swings to makeEsc's TRIP); the model takes the middle, 16.9.

## The physics pass (19 October 2026)

PLAN-wempe-estimates.md's "one physics pass", built in one go after the hook block and the mainspring's re-solve (3.17.00), each change the user's choice; every figure in the sections above that moved with it is updated, the old one kept beside it.

- **The lock re-laid (D).** 05 04_1 scaled again against the wheel's tips Ø 14.00 (24.47 px/mm, the impulse roller's 6.77 against its written 6.78) puts the locking stone 7.14 ±0.04 from the wheel's centre and 37.0 ±0.3° on from the line of centres (7.2 and 36.5° before, 24.4 px/mm): the tips lock **0.235 deep** (0.175), where Belyakov gives Lange's MX 0.22 and the Navy's 1958 repair book sets 0.20-0.25. The lock 12.9° of the balance (9.9°), let-off 1.5° (1.9°), overall 22.9° (17.1°), roller shake 0.011 mm (0.046); the discharge stone's reach follows (1.563 from the staff, lifting the stone 0.305: 0.07 past the lock). The deeper lock costs about 8° of swing. Isolated beside 05 04_1 the locked tip reaches about a third of the stone's width into it on both (moderately sure). The drawn detent's turn against makeEsc's lift is now judged as a lead in the balance's angle, under 0.25° (0.19° here, 0.10° at 0.175): where makeEsc meets the gold spring is 3° earlier, and the lead is largest there.
- **The train's efficiency (B.5): 0.780** (0.730-0.833), the pivots by the rule as before and each mesh's tooth-by-tooth torque ratio from gearing.js at mu 0.10 and 0.20 (`MESH` in escphys.js; `wempe_physics.js` fails if it is stale), against the rule of thumb's 0.749 (0.661-0.848): about +2° of swing.
- **The gold spring (B.4): 98 GPa and 11.6 g/cm³**, Au/Ag/Cu 333-333, the alloy Poljot 20027's passport gives the 6MX's (05 04 01-2 says only "Goldblech 333": likely; 90 GPa and 11.0 estimated before). The detent about its flexure weighs 8.49 g·mm² (8.35), the 6MX's 8.28 (8.13).
- **The preload: 0.8 g at the stone** (0.63 g, the Hamilton's after Op. 78, before). With the deeper lock the discharge stone's longer reach bends the gold spring 0.39 mm on the return, and at 0.63 g a held tooth's stone rang 132 µm, 56 % of the depth of lock, past the margin of half; it falls under half from 0.78 g (`wempe_tripping.js`). At 0.9 g (tried first) the stone, falling home, catches the escaping tooth's back during the impulse in 7 beats of 12 (from about 0.85 g), and dynamics.js's plastic landing passes that blow through the wheel into the balance (+0.47 µJ a beat), where makeEsc has no such term and the tripping frame's elastic stone, hopping on the tooth, never catches it: the three parted by 1.4° of swing. At 0.8 g it never happens and they agree (the swing within 0.1°). A held tooth's stone rings 116 µm, 49.3 % of the lock; a 3 ms blow of 25.6 g lets a tooth go; teeth get past at 0.2 times and under.
- **TF 111 s** (108 s): the deeper lock and the preload lowered the swing, and the 6MX video's fit (`wempe_startup.js`) at the model's own swing moved to 114 s; refitted to where they agree, as on 15 October 2026: 111 s (108-114) at 194.3°.
- **The result**: the swing **194.3° at 12 h**, **188.5-199.5° over the wind** (197.2° and 191.9-202.0° before), the rate −0.26 to +0.20 s a day against 12 h; the guard band 180-270°, 14° under the reference (185° under 199.4° before); the 6MX's own escapement 186.7-197.5° (`wempe_6mx.js`). The balance nets 20.67 µJ of the escape wheel's 36.05 an oscillation (57 %): the strike 10.4 % of the impulse, the unlocking 26.1 % (the detent's spring and preload 9.7 %). The sources' 225° wants Q 1,035 (TF 165 s) at the model's drive.
- **The lock's window, 0.232-0.247 mm** (0.154-0.188 before): it unlocks only to 0.247, and the margin's floor is twice the held tooth's 116 µm. The drawn 0.235 lies 3 µm over the floor, so the bench aims the banking at the window's middle, 0.239 (the drawn depth before), and sets it again until it reads inside (`wempe_bench.js`, `wempe_montecarlo.js`: 100 % work after the bench, 2.4 % as made). The preload's window 0.78-1.56 g (the margin's floor over the knock's 0.64 g).
- **Checks between the models, each widened by the user** (19 October 2026), with why: the tripping frame's detent home within 0.6° of dynamics.js's (0.5°; the frame's gold spring, flexible even as the check's rigid detent, slaps the horn as the stone lands and it hops 6.8 times on the tooth a beat, coming home 0.54° after dynamics.js's plastic, once-landed stone); dynamics.js's isochronism within 0.2 s a day of makeEsc's (0.1; 0.14 apart at TF 108 s, 0.00 at 111 s, from the last degree of the stone's ride overlapping the impulse); `wempe_transmission.js`'s coupled beat within 8° and 0.5 s a day as a drive (3° and 0.3): with the stone pressed harder on the tooth it let go through the chase, the train's meshes give back more than a rigid train would, an impulse's work +3.8 % (+2.1 % before), as a drive +4.6°, so **the rigid train makeEsc, dynamics.js and the page take reads the swing low by about that** (open: Unresolved). Two more, the owner's choice, 27 October 2026, when the detent's blade was built flush toward its block (Readings corrected): `dynamics.js --model wempe`'s isochronism within 0.3 s a day of makeEsc's (0.2; +0.29 since: makeEsc's swing at full wind held on a step of the stone's hops, 201° where in time 204°) and `wempe_tripping.js`'s frame's swing within 1.25° of dynamics.js's (1°; 1.07-1.22° over the estimates tried, 0.87° with the blade centred): the stone hops along the escaping tooth's back through the impulse, and whether a hop lands again flips with the swing, the work rough by about 3 % (`tools/wempe_cliff.js`).

## The build: a Wempe to navigate by (19 October 2026)

The user's objective: a working Wempe, made from the model, that a navigator could rely on: about 2 miles of longitude a month (8 s at 30 days). Each choice below was the user's, after a consultation on what it implies (PLAN-wempe-navigation.md). There are two builds, and the maker chooses: **the 1940s build** (`js/escapement.js`'s `BUILD40`; `MC_WREV=build40` for every Wempe tool) keeps to the Einheits-Chronometer's own decade, and **the 1950s build** (`BUILD`; `MC_WREV=build`) takes a mainspring alloy patented in 1948, as the Hamilton's build does (deviations.js). The drawn versions are unchanged.

- **What was wrong for a navigator, as drawn.** The balance swings 191-200° over the wind (Physics), under the sources' 225-270°: little margin as the oil ages. The depth of lock has a window of 15 µm (Building it), too fine to set and to keep as the stone and teeth wear. Set as the 6MX passports show adjusters set the masses (±0.45°), the chronometer carries 17 s (4.3 miles) after 30 days at sea, most of it the temperature's residual.
- **The gold spring, both builds: thinned to 0.06 over its first 3 mm from its bending point.** Belyakov (1957, Table 3) gives Lange's MX 0.06 at its bend; 05 04 01-2's 0.15 is taken as the blank (no photograph resolves the strip's thickness: every view of Wempe 3955's and the 6MX's detents shows part of its 1.0 height, at 21-31 px/mm). `tools/wempe_detent_study.js` worked the window against the gold spring, the preload (0.8, 1.0, 1.2 g) and the discharge stone's reach (as drawn, +0.03, +0.06) at both drives: the stiff strip, sprung back onto the horn after the discharge stone passes on the return, sets the held tooth's stone ringing (113-116 µm); thinned at its root it rings 44 µm and the window opens to 196 µm at the 1950s build's drive (0.089-0.285), 151 µm thinned over 1 mm, 219 µm over 4. A longer reach always narrows it (the spring bent further); more preload widens it a little, but past about 0.85 g the stone catches the escaping tooth's back (the physics pass), so the preload stays 0.8 g. makeEsc takes the thinned strip as the uniform one of the same tip stiffness, 0.0702; the tripping frame draws it thinned (`tsBend`, `WESC.GBEND`).
- **The drive.** The 1950s build: 1.26 times 05 06_20's (the Soviet Navy's 1958 MX repair book's mainspring, 1.01-1.08 N·m fully wound against the model's 0.83), from **Nivaflex 45/5, 0.393 × 15 × 1,240 mm, its free curve set ×0.8** (`wempe_mainspring.py --t 0.393 --set 0.8`: 536-1,074 mN·m, 1.257 times the drawn strip's solved at the fusee arbor, where 1.26 times the measured asks 1.256: the measured level is 0.997 of the drawn strip's solved since 26 October 2026, its free curve at ×0.75; 1.119 against 1.122 with it at ×0.6 until then). Its inner coils carry 2.88-2.96 GPa fully wound on its 3.0 (along the strip: `motion3d_springs.py`, the 3D viewer's reading, and `wempe_build_spring.py`, which smooth and take full wind a little differently). Until 19 October 2026 the build took 0.377 at set ×0.6, which gave the same drive and an even one (7.6 % over the wind against the drawn 7.4 %), but the 3D analysis found its inner coils at 3.25 GPa, past the alloy: its 2.94 GPa had been the strip's mean, 6M / b t² (`docs/motion-3d/`). No set of the 0.377 strip gives both the drive and the stress (×0.8: 0.99 of the drive; ×1.0: 0.84), so the strip is thicker. Its drive is less even than the drawn strip's frictionless solve (12.2 %): strong fully wound, weaker run down. The escapement takes it turn by turn against 05 06_20's measured drive (`tools/wempe_build_drive.json`, `wempe_load.js`: the strip over the drawn strip's solved at the measured level, 1.253-1.281 since 26 October 2026, the two solved at nearly the same set, against 1.13-1.31 with the drawn at ×0.6), and the swing runs 226.0-237.2° over the wind (214.8-239.5° until 26 October 2026; 227-236° with the even 0.377 strip), with the 30-day error unchanged (5.8 s). Fully wound it gives 1.6 % over the drive (3.5 % until 26 October 2026), inside the ±3.8 % its own thickness tolerance (±0.005 mm) moves its torque; `wempe_physics.js` allows 4 %. Its room past full wind to coil-bound is 0.48 turn (the even strip's 0.93). No carbon strip gives the drive: 0.385 gives the torque at 2.89 GPa, past steel's 2.5-2.6, and the drawn 0.37 as solved is at it already, 2.46 GPa (Physics: energy). The 1940s build keeps the drawings' drive and strip. At 12 h the 1950s build swings 230.3° (231.6° until 26 October 2026), the 1940s build 202.9° (the gold spring's lost work on the return mostly saved).
- **The maintaining spring, banked** (the 1950s build): from 1.07 times the drawings' drive its ring closes onto its banking while running, as the Hamilton's does by design; while the key turns it pushes 381 mN·m and keeps the train going 11.4 min (`wempe_sustaining.js`), stressed 884 MPa (margin 1.4-2.0).
- **Winding: let the key back**, both builds (the user's choice, 19 October 2026). Let go outright at the end of a stroke, the fusee runs forward onto its click; the blow dents the click's nose and the click wheel's tooth where they meet, and the maintaining detent's blow on the maintaining ratchet bends the ratchet's brass teeth (about 500 MPa by `wempe_ratchets.js`'s beam, past brass's 300-450). Let back, as a key is meant to be, every part stays within its limit (the 3D analysis, `docs/motion-3d/`; the Wempe report's section 13). No fix a maker could make keeps every part within its material let go outright (`wempe_letgo_fix.js`: more clicks, a brake, a cushioned face), so the builds keep the drawn brass ratchet and the instruction: turn the key to the end of each stroke, hold it, and let it back.
- **Endshake**, both builds and the bench: the 1958 repair book's figures (Building it).
- **The temperature adjustment**, both builds: the masses set to ±0.1° on further rounds of the trial (27 µm along the rim): 6.2 s (1.5 miles) after 30 days (`wempe_errorbudget.js`).
- **Checked** (`tools/wempe_build.js`, both builds: the swing over the wind, the lock's window, running in time, pushed, knocked, the energy, strength, the maintaining spring and the voyage). makeEsc, `dynamics.js` and the tripping frame agree on every build's swing within 0.3°. Three of the drawn version's cross-checks fail on the thinned gold spring, each on detail of the detent's motion they calibrate on the drawn detent: makeEsc's turn leads the drawn layout's by 1.49° of the balance (`wempe_escapement.js`), the detent's ride down the tooth's back is home 1.4° earlier in time than makeEsc has it (`dynamics.js`), and the hinged frame's first mode is 78.4 Hz against 81.8 (`tripping.js`, the floppy gold spring left flexible); the builds are judged by `wempe_build.js` instead.

## Navigating by it (25 October 2026)

`tools/wempe_navigation.js` follows the whole chain from a made chronometer to the ship's position: the verification report's section 21.

- **The made chronometer.** 2,000 a version, as `wempe_montecarlo.js` makes them after the bench: each one's swing is drawn from its file's 5-50-95 % after the bench, and it sets how much the oil's thickening moves that chronometer's rate (makeEsc's rate at its own swing). Each version is adjusted as the trial would adjust it. As last revised, the masses are set within ±0.45° of their compensating place (`wempe_errorbudget.js`: the 6MX passports' spread of C). The two builds are set within ±0.1° on further rounds of the trial (`wempe_bench.js`).
- **The voyage and the navigator.** Each one is rated over ten days in port and carried sixty days to sea, through `wempe_errorbudget.js`'s own voyage. The voyage runs from a winter port into the tropics over twenty days, with the scatter and drift calibrated on the 6MX's test cards, the barometer, the oil, and heavy weather one day in five. The navigator carries the rated rate, as `rating.py`'s backtest does. `wempe_errorbudget.js` exports that voyage (`nav()`), and its own output is unchanged.
- **The position.** 4 s of time are 1′ of longitude, cos(latitude) nautical miles. The route is estimated, from 45° N to 15° N by day 20. The fix's own error is Bowditch's probable error of a celestial fix, ±2 miles (*The American Practical Navigator*, Pub. No. 9, 2019, Vol. 2, Ch. 3, "Navigational Errors"), taken as a circular normal.
- **The criterion,** stated before the results: after 30 days, the chronometer's longitude error at the equator is under 2 miles in the median (the fix's probable error, and the user's aim of about two miles a month) and under the fix's own 95 % radius, 4.16 miles, in 19 voyages of 20. Each figure is a sample of 2,000 voyages, so each half is judged with its sampling error (the user's choice, 25 October 2026): 1,000 bootstrap resamples of the voyages (seeded) give each figure's 95 % interval, and a half fails only if that whole interval is over its limit; a figure over its limit whose interval still reaches it passes within sampling, and one whose interval misses it by under 1 % of the limit is on the line (`~~`, reported, not failed: the owner's choice, 26 October 2026). The limits are unchanged. The 1950s build must not fail; the tool exits 1 if it does, or if `tools/navigation_wempe.json` is stale.
- **An independent pass** (26 October 2026, the report's section 22): every build and navigation analysis read again against the per-part audit's changes, its criteria against their sources and what it assumes of a maker. Stale copies fixed: the bench's detent ring test and the Monte Carlo took the tapered detent spring as 0.07 mm, not its uniform equivalent 0.0792 (the bench's band 69-137 Hz, not 83-164); `wempe_train.js`'s escape pinion face 3, now 4.0; the video's damping refitted on today's escapement, TF 114.4 s (111-118), the model keeping 111 s (the owner's choice), its check then within the fit's range (the fit has since moved to 141 s: above, "A running 6MX on video"). The TU trial's limits and the 1958 book's endshake bands match their sources; the swing's guard band (180°) is looser than the sources' 225°, and says so; Bowditch's 2 miles is read as the fix's median radius, the lenient reading: read as its 95 % radius or along the parallel alone, every version fails (reported beside the verdict, the owner's choice). **Verdict:** every version can be made and put together; as drawn it fails as a navigator's clock (4.2 miles a month), mostly on its masses' setting; the 1950s build passes within sampling and the 1940s build is on the line at 95 % (since the error budget's calibration was steadied and makeEsc counts the stone's blow, 26 October 2026), both resting on the masses holding ±0.1° and on drift that a ten-day rating can't see.
- **Results** (2,000 voyages, the tool's seed; since 26 October 2026 the error budget's scatter and drift calibrated on common random numbers, 4,000 trials, the drift 11.6-11.95 ms a day over seeds: before, each bisection step drew fresh trials and the drift landed anywhere in 10.7-12.3, the 10.85 the figures below replaced being near its bottom), the longitude error at the equator after 30 days, median and 95 %:
  - as last revised: 4.12 (interval 3.96-4.33) and 8.91 (8.69-9.11) miles. It fails: the masses' setting on the trial leaves too much temperature error.
  - the 1940s build: 1.53 (1.47-1.61) and 4.330 (4.162-4.424) miles: on the line, its interval missing the limit by 0.004 mile.
  - the 1950s build: 1.55 (1.48-1.63) and 4.325 (4.155-4.441) miles: passes within sampling (on the line at 4.343, 4.160-4.447, before makeEsc counted the stone's blow on the escaping tooth's back, RWD, 26 October 2026; it passed at 4.150 and 4.159 on the error budget's old calibration's luckier draws; the drawings check's heavier balance itself moved neither figure).
  - as last revised with the builds' setting of the masses (±0.1°; reported, 26 October 2026): 1.56 (1.48-1.63) and 4.33 (4.17-4.49) miles, on the line: the median passes, the 95 % fails. So the drawn version fails mostly on its masses' setting, not its swing; the swing (193°) leaves the last 0.3 mile at 95 %.

  Both builds are well inside the median and on the line at 95 %: the 1950s build's interval reaches the limit (4.155 against 4.158), the 1940s build's misses it by 0.004 mile, under the 1 % the owner allows as on the line; neither has a margin there, both resting on the masses holding ±0.1° and on drift a ten-day rating can't see.
  With the fix's error added, the 1950s build's position a month out is within 2.54 miles in the median and 5.69 in 19 of 20; after 60 days the clock alone is 4.70 and 13.06.
  The two builds' figures nearly coincide: they differ in the drive and the swing, which the rating takes out, not in what moves the rate at sea (temperature, scatter, drift).

## The page's escapement (15 October 2026)

Until 15 October 2026 the 3D model moved its escapement as the layout draws it (`ESC.layout`, kinematic, timed from 05 04_1: the tooth on the impulse stone at once, 21° before the line of centres, the detent let fall the moment the gold spring slipped off). Now the page moves it as makeEsc solves it (`WPHYS.ESC`, the Physics sections above), as the Hamilton's page moves its own: `app.js` asks `ESC.state(phase, swing, drive)` each frame with the live swing (`H.amp`) and drive (`drv`: the fusee's torque at that wind, or the maintaining spring's while the key turns), and `WESC.adapt` (js/escapement.js) turns makeEsc's state into the drawing's terms:

- **The escape wheel** follows makeEsc's progress: locked until the release (+2.6° of the balance at 12 h; −2.4° before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles), let go from rest and chasing the impulse stone (12.3° of swing), striking it at 14.9° (the stone's face 13.3° before the line of centres, not the layout's 22°10'), driving it to 50.0°, then running on to the next tooth's lock at 49.2°.
- **The detent** turns about its spring's middle by makeEsc's lift (`toKin`: its gold spring's tip's travel over its length from the flexure; `tools/wempe_escapement.js` checks the frames to 1e-9): lifted by the discharge stone from −12.3°, flung up off the stone's square corner at −1.8°, falling back onto the tooth it has just let go at 0.8° and riding its land and back (the stone's least lift over the tooth, makeEsc's `ride`) until it is home on its banking at 9.9°.
- **The gold spring** is bent on the return swing by makeEsc's travel at its tip, turned about its bending point (its square end 11.2 from it; the stone reaches 0.02 past its tip, so it meets it there), from −5.9° to −17.6°.
- **The balance** swings through the beat as the solved work drives it: each push makeEsc solves (`pushes`: the strike and the drive, the stone's draw, the detent's springs and blows, the gold spring on the return) changes its energy where it acts, the air's and pivots' loss spread as a viscous one, scaled so the beat closes on itself. That scale comes out 1.003 times the free run-down's (makeEsc's swing is where its work and its losses balance, which it solves), the swing at the turn before the impulse 206.76° and after it 207.27°: the impulse raises the swing about 0.5 % (about 1°), its speed with it, and the balance departs from a sine by 0.5° at most. Every state is makeEsc's at the balance's angle (the sine's phase that gives it), so the parts stand exactly where makeEsc puts them for the balance as drawn; the chase's and the detent's fall's time is counted on that sine, 0.01 % from the beat's own length (0.50005 s).
- **Held** (run down, or a swing under makeEsc's AMIN, 45.8°): the wheel stays locked, the balance swings as a sine, the discharge stone still lifts the detent (which falls as makeEsc's) and bends the gold spring on the return.

Drawn, makeEsc's contacts are kept 0.01 apart as the layout kept them, for the tools: the wheel stands 0.01 behind makeEsc's at the tooth's tip (its locked tooth 0.01 short of the stone's flat, as built), the detent 0.01 higher (at the gold spring's tip) while the discharge stone carries it or the stone rides a tooth, the gold spring 0.01 further bent. The ride is taken where the tooth is drawn: makeEsc's own ride, with the wheel 0.01 behind, would put the stone 6.6 µm into the escaping tooth's back. `tools/wempe_escapement.js` checks all of it (the beat, the frames, the drawn detent against the layout's own geometry within 2 µm at the stone, the drawn stone never inside the drawn tooth over eight swings and drives, the train held); `fine.py --model wempe` checks the parts in 3D at the solved phases (`checks.py`'s `PH`: the unlocking, the release, the detent's fall and ride, the chase, the strike, the drive, the run on, the lock, the return pass) and, with `--dense`, at 101 more.



The case and its gimbals weighed and run in a seaway, and the chronometer as the model's numbers make it run through the 6MX's TU trial and carried to sea. The tools: `massprops.py --model wempe` (the browser, about 15 s; `--write` writes `tools/gimbals_wempe.json`), `gimbals.js --model wempe` (about 4 minutes; `--quick` 12 s: its report is `tools/wempe_gimbals.js`'s) and `tools/wempe_errorbudget.js` (about 10 s). The Hamilton's runs are unchanged (massprops.py finds its `gimbals.json` up to date, which isn't rewritten; gimbals.js --quick prints the same).

- **The bodies**, every closed solid at its material's density (as `wempe_inertia.py`'s: brass Ms 58 8.45, steel 7.85, the rim's nickel steel 8.1, the impulse roller's beryllium copper 8.25, the gold spring 11.6; the dial Messingblech, 05 02 00-4), with the movement in the bowl (the Case view): the bowl with the movement in it **2,516 g** (the bowl, brass as R-05 01 00-1's "Ms-Sandguß", 1,195 g; the bezel and glass 327; the closing plate 79; the movement with its ring and dial 916), the gimbal ring with its bracket, pivot screws and nuts **334 g**: 2.85 kg in the gimbals (the 6MX's TU allows its chronometer 2.2 kg). Figures of 24 October 2026, the bowl's rim threaded and cut round the movement ring's flange (How the case and box were measured, The bowl).
- **The sheet's "Gewicht 350 gr."** The bowl's drawn solid is 141.5 cm³: 1,195 g in brass; 350 g is 2.47 g/cm³, no brass (aluminium would weigh 382 g, Bakelite 194, hard Buna rubber 167). The drawn pivots don't decide the material: a light bowl hangs on them too, the case's centre of gravity 6.82 mm under the pivots with a Bakelite bowl (1,514 g, a period of 0.86 s), 6.49 with Buna (1,488 g, 0.87 s), 8.81 with aluminium (1,703 g, 0.78 s), 8.50 at the sheet's 350 g (1,671 g, 0.79 s). The model's bowl is brass, as the sheet's material says. (Until 21 October 2026, the pivots 11.5 under the bowl's top, a light bowl's centre of gravity came over them and it turned over.)
- **Levelled as 05 01 says.** As drawn the case's centre of gravity lies 13.99 mm under the gimbal centre and 0.93 mm across its pivots' line (it would hang 3.8° out of level), the ring and case together 0.49 mm along the ring's (2.3°). 05 01: "Kloben verstellen bis Gleichgewicht erreicht ist": the brackets are moved in their slots by those amounts until it hangs level, and the tool does so (the Hamilton's drawn pivots are 7.8 mm off theirs, levelled the same way by its manual).
- **The periods.** Levelled, the case swings on its pivots (12-6) with a period of **0.67 s** and the ring, carrying it, on its own (3-9) **0.75 s** (the Hamilton's 0.58 and 0.65, its centre of gravity 14.7 mm under its pivots against the Wempe's 14.0; 0.65, 0.75 and 10.4 until its pivots were raised, 25 October 2026); the case's radius of gyration about its pivots 40 mm. Both far below a ship's roll (10-30 s) and 34 % clear of the balance's beat. Driven by a regular roll the case leans from the apparent vertical 0.003 of the roll at 20 s, 0.008 at 8 s, 0.02 at 4 s, 0.6 at its own period, 0.10 at 0.5 s; the balance's beat, out of poise by 2 µm (estimated), swings the case 0.24 seconds of arc.
- **The stops.** The box stops neither gimbal within 60° either way: the ring and case reach 76.9 mm from the ring's axis in the box's 160 inside (76.2 until the ring was thickened to 05 01's 4.0, 25 October 2026), and the transit lock's lever, drawn turned off along the front wall, is clear (the stops' test confirms a point in an obstacle's bounding box inside the solid itself, `__mpExact`: the lever lies diagonally in its box, which the bounding box alone took for a stop at 3.5°). In its ring the case turns to any angle: no point of the bowl's own parts comes within the ring's band (the 1942 casting's foot passes 0.16 inside it, the case brackets' bosses end at r 64.3; the slot through the band isn't counted). From 24 to 25 October 2026 it met the band at 55.5° either way: the case brackets' blocks, scaled by eye to 27.7 under the pivots at r 66.5, swung into it, so the bowl couldn't be turned over to be wound (the locking bracket's pad, 2.5 short of the ring, until 24 October 2026). Until the locking bracket was measured (above, How the case and box were measured) its estimated pad, hanging 12.5 under the pivots' line, met the ring at 8.5° one way, which a moderate sea reached.
- **At sea** (gimbals.js's seas, periods and NORDFORSK's 0.12 g limit; in its installation, the case's pivots fore and aft so the case takes the roll, and turned a quarter): in the seas a ship works the case keeps within 0.40° of the apparent vertical (9.8° of the true) and meets neither its ring nor the box (at the NORDFORSK limit it turns up to 24.6° in its ring, 13.1° with the box turned); the escapement feels at most 9.4 mg across its staff (169 mg in a small vessel's rough sea). In a storm (rolls to 35-45°) the case turns to 57.4° in its ring and swings onto the locking bracket's pad once in the ten minutes (23.6° with the box turned a quarter); a case swung onto the pad passes nothing measurable to the balance. Every gimbals.js criterion holds, and ci.py runs the full run with --full.
- **What reaches the escapement**, through makeEsc's rate response alone (the Wempe has no escapement in time yet: phase 2d part 1, claude/wempe-dynamics, isn't merged): the specific force across the staff loads the balance's pivots (Ø 0.21, mu 0.15: estimated), 0.05 g steadily costing 5.1° of swing and 0.24 s a day; the case's turn about the staff sets the balance's centre of swing off its spring's rest by −a/ω² (a beat error: +0.32 s a day a degree, makeEsc with the rollers turned), averaging out to the first order. In the working seas the rate moves at most 0.006 s a day. The detent's trip: its preload (0.63 g at the stone, 12.87 mm from the flexure: the Hamilton's, estimated) against its own weight (94.9 mg, 8.37 mm from the flexure since 15 October 2026; 93.6 mg, 8.26 before) wants 10.5 g across it (static, the draw left out: a lower bound), 24 times what the roughest sea passes on.
- **The TU's trial** (wempe_errorbudget.js: 35 days at 36/20/4/20/36 °C, the first two days of each period not counted, wound daily to "8" and to "0" at each period's end, rates read to 0.01 s; the TU's E, R, D, C, S, each period's mean and N = 42E + 4.5R + 60C + 2.4S). Each day's rate: the temperature curve (wempe_thermal.js: the masses set by the adjuster at their compensating place, 20.5° from the cuts' line, to ±0.45°, so C within ±0.10, its spread the 6MX passports' adjusted C, sd 0.060; the integral balance's S +0.74 to +0.89 s), the air's density with temperature (it takes 57 % of the damping: 5.8 % denser at 4 °C, −0.12 s a day; the masses null its linear part with the rest), the swing over the wind (wound to "8" the spring runs 8 to 32 h down, 208.6-204.7°, −0.02 to +0.03 s a day: the same every day, and the full winds fall on uncounted days), the scatter and drift (calibrated on eight 6MX test cards, 1953-1993, `documents/6MX-figures.md`: the trial's median E made theirs, 0.225, and its median |R|, 0.26: 0.31 s a day and ±12 ms a day each day; the Hamilton's No. 3390 card's 0.042 and ±2.7 until 18 October 2026; the trial's median D 0.92, N 16 and largest period mean 1.79 then fall within the cards', 0.58-1.29, 12-25 and 0.81-1.90), the barometer and the oil (the Hamilton's estimates through makeEsc: −0.020 s a day for 1 % denser air, +0.035 for 1 % of the drive lost), the regulation at 20 °C ±0.5 s a day (estimated). **As adjusted it passes 95.3 % of its trials** (18 October 2026, with the 6MX cards' scatter: median E 0.224, R +0.01, D 0.92, C −0.003 (0.099 at 95 %), S +0.83, the largest period mean 1.79 s, N 16.0 (20.5 at 95 %), against ±0.35, ±2.00, ±2.30, ±0.10, ±1.20, ±3.50 and 30, as the cards' figures fall; its scatter and drift could be 1.5 times the cards' before it passed only half its trials. With the Hamilton's card it passed every trial, N 4.8: a fifth of the 6MX passports' 12-16). **At the masses' drawn place** (30° from the cuts' line, Wempe 3955's) it loses 2.3 s a day a °C: C +2.33, N 143, every trial failed (32° and 3.0 until 15 October 2026); the adjuster moves them 10° along the rim toward the cuts, about one and a half of the rim's 6° divisions ("8 Körner in 60 Teilung", read as eight punch marks of a 60-division).
- **At sea** (errorbudget.js's voyage: 60 days from a port at 12.8 °C into the tropics at 29.4 °C, rated over 10 days in port; heavy weather one day in five as the gimbals pass it on, a sea beyond NORDFORSK's limit: 2.3 mg across the staff, −0.011 s a day): as adjusted it carries **2.2 s after 10 days, 17.2 s (4.3 miles of longitude) after 30, 44.5 s (11.1 miles) after 60** (median; 95 % 6.0, 37.8, 101.7 s; 1.1, 9.5, 24.5 s with the Hamilton's scatter and the masses to ±0.25°, before 18 October 2026). Most of it is the masses' setting (±0.45°: the residual C over the voyage's 17 °C); ±0.1° would leave two fifths of it. The masses at their drawn place would carry 13 minutes in a month. **The build** (the user's aim, 19 October 2026: about 2 miles of longitude a month) sets the masses to ±0.1° on further 4 °C and 36 °C rounds after the trial (`wempe_bench.js`'s trial test: 27 µm along the rim at r 15.70, both of a pair alike; a round measures C to about ±0.005 s a day a °C, ±0.02° of the masses): it carries **6.2 s (1.5 miles) after 30 days, 19.4 s (4.9 miles) after 60** (median; 95 % 17.8 and 55.0 s), the temperature curve's share falling from 15.8 to 3.7 s at 30 days; drift (5.0 s) and scatter (2.3 s), the 6MX cards', are then the larger part, and only workmanship moves them. `wempe_errorbudget.js` fails if the build carries more than 8 s at 30 days.

## The page's time, ship motion and reports (15 October 2026)

For the full release (3.00.00) the page gained what the Hamilton's has for keeping and showing time at sea, each reusing the Hamilton's code where it applies:

- **Time** (`js/app.js`): Set the hands, Now, and Keep GMT or local time. GMT is the default, as a ship's chronometers are kept for navigation (until now the page kept local time). Setting never turns the train (RESOLVED.md, "Setting the time turned the train"): the hour and minute hands go to the minute by the cannon pinion's slip on the centre arbor (`slip`, seconds of time; `dial.js`'s `update` turns the motion work and both hands by it, not the centre arbor, the fusee or the train), leaving the dial fast by under a minute; the balance is then stopped and the train held until the time the hands should read comes round to the dial's, and a twist (the Twist button's) starts it. Run down, only the minutes are set. The link keeps `tz=local` and `t=HH:MM:SS` (a time in the link puts the train there before the model shows, as the Hamilton's does: the fusee and dial are stateless in the centre's angle). The Hamilton's With the key and Stop to set follow its manual's Sec. III; no Wempe source in the set gives its procedure, so the Wempe has the simpler setting alone.
- **Ship motion** (Display, Motion: Ship motion, Transit lock, Roll period): the ship rolls and pitches, the case hanging in its gimbals leans toward the apparent vertical. Drawn with the apparent vertical up (18 October 2026, as the Hamilton's), as a plumb line hung beside the box would show down (`gwStep`'s `ap`, `ar`): the bowl, hanging along it, stays put and the box rolls and pitches round it; locked, the bowl goes with the box. The export's At sea clip is drawn the same way. Until 16 October the box rolled in the true vertical's frame and the bowl leaned in it, so both seemed to move; then until 18 October the box stood still, the camera riding with the ship, and the movement swung in it. The equations are box.js's `GIM.sea` and `gimStep`, copied into app.js (`GW`, `gwStep`) with the Wempe's own figures from `tools/gimbals.js --model wempe` (the weighed bowl and ring, levelled): the bowl on its 12-6 pivots 0.654 s, the ring on its 3-9 pivots 0.721 s (0.958 and 1.046 until 21 October 2026, when the bowl hung 6.2 higher), and no stop within 60°. The transit lock turned in holds both gimbals level with the box, its lever turning in through the ring's slot into the bowl's bracket once they are level (since 24 October 2026; not turned until then); turned over to wind, the bowl is held the same way, the lever first turned out. The Hamilton's Jolt the box, which shows its detent setting or tripping, has no counterpart: `tripping.js --model wempe` gives the Wempe's figures in the report, not on the page.
- **Report a bug or inaccuracy**: the Hamilton's `report.js` and dialog, its subject and its Inaccuracy hint given by `REPORT.bind({name, inacc})` (the drawings first, then photographs and videos of real ones); from the panel, a part's card and the essay's end.
- **Link preview**: `site-assets/social-wempe.png` (`tools/social.py wempe`: the Case view at 10:09:36), the Wempe page's `og:image` (build.py).

## The page's capabilities against the Hamilton's (16 October 2026)

The Hamilton's page has controls the Wempe's lacked; these were ported, each from the Hamilton's `app.js`, the generic parts into `../shared/viewer.js` (which only the Wempe's page uses: the Hamilton keeps its own copy) and the Wempe's own figures into `js/app.js`:

- **Moving parts only** (View; `drive=1` in the link): the viewer's `cfg.drive` hides the frame (the plates, pillars, ring, cocks, bridges and bushes, the jewels' settings and endstones), the dial and the case, draws the barrel see-through so the mainspring shows, and shows the motion work and hands only with **Include motion work and hands**; the views in the case (Dial, Box, Case, Transport box) give way to Movement, as the Hamilton's Box and Dial do. See-through now ghosts the pillars, cocks, bridges and ring with the plates, as the Hamilton's ghosts its bridges and cock.
- **The Train, Fusee and Balance views**, the Hamilton's, aimed at the Wempe's arbors (`WD.H`, `WESC.B`).
- **Winding as the Hamilton's** (26 October 2026; the owner's request, both pages winding alike, the Hamilton's Wind now turning its case over as this page's does): under Winding, **Wind with the key** lifts the movement out (the Movement view), its plates see-through and the parts that wind picked out, and turns the key half a turn at a time (0.7 s, a 0.3 s pause to change grip), 17½ half turns from run down (**From run down**, on), the camera closing in on the fusee's small end as the stop bar meets its pin, the key kept on 3 s; **Load path in colour** tints the parts carrying the train's load while winding (the maintaining spring driving, rose; the maintaining ratchet and its detent holding, teal) and for 2 s after the key lets go (the mainspring's path: barrel, chain, fusee, click wheel, clicks, ratchet, spring, fusee wheel), through the viewer's `cfg.tint`; **Close-up of the maintaining spring** (the Hamilton's sustaining spring's close-up, `#cu`, drawn after each frame through the viewer's `cfg.overlay` on its own camera): the fusee wheel's maintaining work seen from the top plate's side, the ratchet see-through, with the time the spring has left (the ratchet's lead `H.ms` at the wheel's turn, `WPHYS.WFW`; about 16 minutes from the running lead), zoom, pan, enlarge, slow motion, keep open (**Open the close-up**) and how the spring takes the load in its three phases (`?`). The Hamilton's ×20 exaggeration of the spring is left out: the Wempe's opens 16.7° at most, drawn as it is.
- **Tick sound** (Time, Sound; on by default, as the Hamilton's): a click each time the escape wheel's place passes a half tooth, its tooth on the impulse stone, so once a beat, 0.5 s, at up to real time and none while the train is held; the audio starts with the first click, tap or key, as browsers ask.
- **Exploded: Movement or All** (25 October 2026; `exp=all` in the link): the Hamilton's Take apart switch, in `../shared/viewer.js` (`st.all`, its eased share `st.allK` for `cfg.tick`, a view's `all` camera): All takes the case apart too under the movement, up the bowl's axis (`case.js`'s `explode(e)`: the gimbal ring, the closing plate, the bowl with its brackets, the bezel with its glass; the transit lock and the key out to the right, the lid open and back off its hinges), the movement brought down to just over it. The outer transport box stays out (its own view). Its offsets are a view's layout, not measurements.
- **Swing and isochronism** (its own panel; `mset=` in the link): the Hamilton's three charts, the swing over the last minute, the escapement's rate against the swing at a steady drive and the swing over a wind, from makeEsc with the Wempe's settings: a solve takes about 0.3 s in the page, so `tools/wempe_essaydata.js` solves 81 drives in Node (`WEDATA.swing`) and the page reads between them; the drive over the wind is 05 06_20's measured force through the train (`WPHYS.drv`), less the mainspring's set (the Hamilton's slider: the model's drive scaled). Its band is the sources' 225-270° (the 1954 instruction for chronometers in general), the tools' guard band's floor 185° dashed; it gives the swing's and the rate's range over the wind (194.1-204.2°, −0.06 to +0.03 s a day) and the rate's change between full wind and 24 h, the daily wind the 6MX's instruction asks for. Left out of it: the Hamilton's going-barrel comparison (a what-if: it would need the solved spring's torque taken through a train the Wempe hasn't, left for later), its isochronism check (the U.S. Navy's, Sec. IX: the TU has no such figure) and its sustaining spring's minutes (here 05 06_9's maintaining spring, about 70 s, which a 6 s wind hardly feels). **The banking screw** (27 October 2026, `bank=239` in the link, µm): a slider turns the screw that sets the depth of lock (the M1.2 across the block's upright, 0.25 mm a turn, its head where the detent's pipe rests), 0.150 to 0.250 mm, and the screw turns on the model in every version (the detent itself isn't moved: the depth changes by microns). The escapement is rebuilt at each depth by `tools/wempe_banking.js` (makeEsc with `dL`, the drawings' and the 6MX's, 33 depths at 11 drives, into `js/bankdata.js`, WBANK; about a minute), and the page reads between them as an offset on the drawn escapement's swing, least swing and rate: the swing the balance settles at follows it live (and the chart over a wind, the drawn banking dashed), the lock and let-off are measure()'s, and the readout judges the depth against the bench's window (`tolerances_wempe.json`: 0.2317 to 0.2437, its middle the bench's aim): shallower, a held tooth's stone rings over half the depth; deeper, the swing falls away over the wind; from 0.247 it won't unlock and the train stops.
- **Rate and timing weights** (`bal=tw:8,pl:19.5,T:4` in the link): the timing weights, in eighths of a turn in (a turn of both 44.2 s a day; as drawn against their screws' heads, so only in, to 1.875 turns: Building it), drawn moving along their screws (0.16 a turn); the masses' place along the rim (as drawn, 30°, or Compensated, where their coefficient nulls the air's linear part, as `wempe_errorbudget.js`'s adjusted chronometer has it), which isn't drawn (the rim is drilled for them at their drawn place only); and the temperature, 0-40 °C. The rate against temperature is the tool's day's rate without its scatter: the cut bimetallic balance's coefficient at that place (`WEDATA.heat`, `wempe_thermal.js`), the integral balance's secondary error at the middle of its range (estimated) and the air's density against 20 °C (`WEDATA.trial.airK`), with the TU's C and S for the curve.
