The model's clock runs at the escapement's rate and these, as the Hamilton's runs at its weights' and screws'. The Hamilton's balance screws and washers, verniers and Table IV have no Wempe counterpart (its masses are moved along the rim instead).
- **35-day trial** (the Hamilton's 30-day performance test): the 6MX's TU's trial, 36/20/4/20/36 °C, as `wempe_errorbudget.js` runs it, on its seeded trial's days (`WEDATA.trial.days`) with the masses' place and the weights' regulation put back, each period's daily rates and mean and the TU's E, R, D, C, S, largest period mean and N against its limits; beside them the tool's medians over 2,000 trials compensated and 500 at the drawn place (`WEDATA.trial.sum`, `summary()`), and how many pass. The page opens with the masses at their drawn place, where the trial fails on C (and so on the period means and N); under the result it says so and why (the model's estimated materials compensate at 19.5°, where an adjuster would move them on this trial), with a button that moves the masses to their compensating place and the timing weights back as drawn, so that it passes, and one back to the drawn place.
- **Escapement in plan** (the Hamilton's adjuster's bench's plan): `shared/escplan.js`'s `drawEscPlan` draws the Hamilton's parts (its detent, trip spring and rollers' shapes), not the Wempe's, so the panel draws the Wempe's as the essay's escapement figure does: the model's own pieces flattened onto the plan in the frame of what moves them, moved by the state the model shows (running, held or stepping fast), seen as 05 04_1 draws it, with a caption of what the escapement is doing; under it makeEsc's measure() at the model's settings (`tools/wempe_escapement.js`'s figures) beside 05 04_1's where the README gives them. Left out: the **adjuster's bench** itself. Its sliders rebuild the Hamilton's detent, jewels and roller from makeEsc's settings; the Wempe's parts are built from 05 04_1's layout and the parts' sheets (`js/escapement.js`), and `WESC.SET` is taken from them, so a setting moved would leave the drawn parts where the sheets put them, and the model would no longer be the drawings'.
- **The walkthrough** (How it works; `tour=N` in the link): the viewer's `cfg.TOUR` (the Hamilton's tour, lifted into `../shared/viewer.js` as the drive mode was), eight steps from the case to the dial: the case and gimbals (the ship's motion on), the frame, the going train (its ratios' table), the fusee and chain (05 06_20's pull over the fusee's turns, the chain's place marked), winding without stopping (a Wind button), the balance and hairspring, the spring detent escapement (the plan above, moving with the model) and the motion work and dial; each a view, a camera, the parts it singles out (the rest see-through), Moving parts only or not and a speed. Its text is from this README and the essay. The Hamilton's walkthrough's overhaul (Sec. V and VIII, its own card) has no Wempe source to follow and isn't ported.
- **Downloads** (16 October 2026; the Download section, and Download it as STL on each part's card): `js/blender.js`, lifted from the Hamilton's `js/blender.js`, which keeps its own; the glTF writer (`GLTF`) and the rig (`RIG_PY`, the Hamilton's `blender/rig.py`) are the Hamilton's, loaded by relative path.
  - **STL**: a part or a piece as built (in the sheets' state and with the connector the page shows), or the movement as shown (parts hidden, Moving parts only), as one binary STL in millimetres in the movement's frame (dial side +y, 12 o'clock −z), the case's parts in the box's; no engraving or printed face. Every one closed: `gltf_check.py --model wempe` checks the movement's and six parts' (`checks.py`'s `GLTF['stl']`).
  - **Blender (.glb)**, `wempe-einheitschronometer.glb`, about 75 MB, two minutes to make in SwiftShader: the whole instrument as the Hamilton's (named parts and pieces, the materials, the jewels and glasses transmitting), its copy built as the page shows it (the sheets' state, the connector, the dial, the timing weights) and its clips played through it with `app.js`'s tick's equations: Running, Beat (slow motion), Run down, Winding (in the case as the page winds it: the bowl turned over, the closing plate opened, the key put on and turned from a quarter of an hour short of run down, the maintaining spring driving the train meanwhile, then all back), Setting the hands (the cannon tube slipping an hour), Exploded, Lift out, Lid, At sea (the Wempe's gimbals, `gwSea`, `gwStep`), and the rig's (Rig · beat, explode, lift, lids, and Rig · ratchet: the maintaining detent riding a tooth of its ratchet, 385.7 beats, which Run down would sample every 450 beats, so it is left out of Run down). Left out: Laid out (the page's since 18 October 2026, not yet a clip), and, having no counterpart, Stop and start and Latch.
  - **Blender rig (.py)**: the Hamilton's rig, which makes only the controls the file has (hours, hands set, explode, lift, lids, roll, pitch). `blender_check.py --model wempe` (16 October 2026): every part Running moves where the rig puts it within 0.003 mm and 0°; explode, lift and lids at 1 at their clips' ends; against Winding, in the movement's frame (the rig doesn't turn the bowl over), the fusee 3.2°, the barrel 2.6°, the chain 1.0 mm and the up-and-down wheel 0.3° off, all the fusee's catch on its click wheel, which the rig hasn't (bounded by a click-wheel tooth, 10.3°).
- **Contacts** (18 October 2026; its own section; `contacts=1` or `contacts=sweep` in the link, `ceps=` the band, `ctin=1`): not from the Hamilton's, a debug view `../shared/contacts.js` gives the viewer (`cfg.contacts`), which the Hamilton's page binds too (its own `app.js`, since 18 October 2026: its README's Controls). Every pair of solids in different parts that touch (within 0.006 mm), come close (a gap up to the band, 0.03 mm unless chosen: 0.01 to 1) or pass into each other, found on the built solids as `fine.py` finds overlaps but along all three axes and with the gap too; an overlap `fine.py`'s `EXPECTED` lists is expected (its reason in the list), any other not. Shown on both solids in each one's frame, so a part carries its contacts as it moves or explodes, drawn over everything with every part a faint ghost (no merged copies, Edges or ink while it shows); a part hidden, faded away, isolated out or hidden by Moving parts only takes its contacts with it on both sides, without a new scan. This moment stops the clock and scans the model as it stands (about 6 s on this machine's GPU, 677 solids), and again, only the pairs where something moved, once the model stands still; Over a beat and the wind poses it in `fine.py`'s 68 states through its own update and keeps what each finds, each pair with the states it meets in (about 4½ min). A scan holds the explode at 0, so the Exploded view shows the assembled contacts carried out with their parts. Picking a part shows its contacts alone and lists its pairs (the worst each does: its overlap, else touching, else its least gap), Inside the picked part too adds its own pieces' (a wheel on its arbor, its screws: overlaps there are joints, shown as expected), a pair clicked shows it alone and › picks the other part; the legend's kinds turn on and off. `tools/contacts.py --model wempe` renders and lists it (`--sweep`, `--part`, `--inside`, `--hide`, `--eps`); `smoke.py --model wempe` works it. What it showed first (18 October 2026): the stop bar's spring 1.0 mm into the fusee's body and the chain's links up to 0.08 mm into it in every state, and the maintaining detent 0.006 mm into its ratchet in 61 of the 68, none in `fine.py`'s table (it leaves out pairs in one turning group, and overlaps under 0.0005 mm³); the escape wheel never touches the locking stone: within 0.1 mm of it in the 49 states where a tooth is locked or being unlocked and none while the wheel runs (phase 0.255-0.29), its least gap 0.034 mm (0.048 at one moment at rest), so at the 0.03 band the lock doesn't show (`contacts.py --sweep --eps 0.1 --part escapeWheel`, 5 min). Its depth is the longest overlap along any of its rays, not how far one solid is in the other: the least of the three axes' is (18 October 2026, the rays replayed): the stop bar's spring is 0.0105 into the wall of its slot (1.0 its height along y), the maintaining detent at most 0.007 into a tooth at some train positions (0.89 its thickness; within `maintaining.py`'s 0.012), the chain's links graze the groove's edge, up to 0.09 on x rays only. Its sweep poses the train from the page's own tooth, so runs differ with the time of day. Inside the part's pieces close but never touching found three faults, fixed (RESOLVED.md): the cover plate screw off its sink, the upper balance jewel's settings 0.03 apart, the hairspring 0.033 into the stud's slot as first drawn. Still drawn clear: the compensation masses, 0.02 off the rim all round with their screws' tips 0.01 off it, which hold them in fact.
- **Laid out** (18 October 2026; View, `view=laidout` in the link): the Hamilton's, the textbooks' developed drawing of the train, its arbors set in one line and seen square from the side, barrel left, balance right, hands below. Each builder places its arbors in a group of their own (the part, or a group in it, about the arbor), so the line moves those groups and no builder takes a second place: `js/movement.js`'s `develop(e)` (0 as built, 1 laid out; `buildWempe.DEV` the line), as the Hamilton's. The barrel and fusee stay, with all they carry (the fusee wheel, maintaining work, stop work, chain, the up-and-down pinion and its wheel and hand); the line runs on from the barrel through the fusee, and the centre, intermediate and seconds arbors follow, each at its real centre distance from the one before (the hole tables': 22.65, 18.12, 12.63 and the escape arbor's 11.85), so every pair still meshes. The escape wheel, detent and its block, the balance, rollers, hairspring and stud turn together about the escape arbor, 10.00 from the balance, so the escapement is unchanged and the balance ends the line. The motion work and hands go with the centre arbor, the seconds hand with the seconds arbor. Each driven pinion is turned by the change in its line of centres times (1 + the driver's teeth / its leaves), so its leaves stay in the driver's gaps (the escape pinion less the escapement's turn), on top of its loaded phase.
  - The frame, its jewels and bushes, the dial and the case are hidden (Moving parts only's `cfg.drive.hide`), since their holes no longer meet the arbors; see-through schematic plates stand in (`userData.devPlate`): the pillar plate and top plate along the line at their heights, 48 wide (the barrel's Ø 41.6 inside them), the intermediate bridge on the dial side under its two arbors, a bridge from the barrel's arbor to the set-up click's screw (hole 46, 12.0 off the line, as the real bridge runs), the lower cock hanging from the top plate and the upper cock over the balance on a foot, and three pillars (Ø 8.1) beside the line clear of the wheels, the chain and the detent. Their shapes are schematic, not the movement's: drawn only in this view and going with Moving parts only, they carry no part name (not listed as parts, sectioned, checked or downloaded), but each stands in for its part (`userData.devOf`, 25 October 2026): a click on one opens that part's card and shows it solid, and its line in the parts list stays live. Where a part shows through a stand-in the click goes to the part; where stand-ins overlap, to the smallest (a bridge before the plate it lies on, which shows round it).
  - Kept shown, though their parts are hidden (`userData.devKeep`): the set-up click and its screw (on the barrel bridge) and the stop pin (in the top plate), whose barrel ratchet and fusee stay in place, so the view holds every element a textbook's developed drawing letters ("Modern chronometer movement", Figs. 83 elevation and 84 plan: frame, barrel, arbor, set-up ratchet and click, chain, fusee and arbor, maintaining ratchet and click, stop work, the train's wheels, arbors and pinions, the up-and-down pinion and wheel, the motion work and hands). Its B′, the stud carrying the stop work, has no counterpart (the Wempe's stop bar slides in a slot across the fusee's small end); its S′, the snail the stopping arm meets, is the stop pin the bar meets (the stop work turned round); its s, the arm's spring, is the bar's spring, inside the fusee's small end under its cover plate and out of sight from any side. `tools/laidout.py --model wempe` checks each is shown and opens its own card when clicked (a real click, in the elevation or else the plan) and draws both views on one sheet with the drawing's letters and a key (`r_laidout_sheet.png`).
  - The camera's field narrows to 20° so the elevation looks nearly flat; Spread sets how far the train is laid out. The viewer (`../shared/viewer.js`) eases it as it eases the explode (a view's `dev`), and Contacts holds it at 0 while it scans, as the explode.

## Building it: tolerances, the bench and the Monte Carlo

The Hamilton's build checks for the Wempe (15 October 2026): how closely the parts the drawings don't size must be made, the tests a made Wempe must pass on the bench, and the whole movement made to a maker's tolerances. Guidance for any maker, proved by their own runs: no Wempe is to hand. The tools (no browser), each writing a `tools/*_wempe.json` for the Wempe's verification report (`--write`; without it each exits 1 if its file is stale): `tools/wempe_tolerances.js` (about 40 s, `tolerances_wempe.json`), `tools/keystone.js --model wempe` (about 5 s, `keystone_wempe.json`), `tools/wempe_bench.js` (about 45 s, `bench_wempe.json`, reading the first) and `tools/wempe_montecarlo.js` (about 30 s, `montecarlo_wempe.json`, reading the bench's). The Hamilton's `tolerances.js`, `bench.js` and `montecarlo.js` read its own files throughout, so these are counterparts reading WESC and WPHYS through `wempe_load.js`; `keystone.js` takes `--model wempe`, its Hamilton output byte for byte as before (with and without settings), and `escgolden.js` holds makeEsc bit for bit (unchanged here).

The swing's band: the model's own swing is 188.5-199.5° over the wind on 05 06_20's drive, under the sources' 225-270°, so each range keeps it in the guard band `wempe_escapement.js` uses, **180-270° over the whole wind** (185° until 19 October 2026's physics pass; 193° until TF was taken from the video, 15 October 2026) (the drive from 05 06_20 at every quarter hour, 0.952 of the reference at 56 h to 1.042 fully wound), and the sources' band is reported beside it.

- **The mainspring** (05 06_20's "15 × 0,37", no tolerance): the drive goes as the strip's thickness cubed. The guard band holds from 0.940 to 1.670 of 05 06_20's pull; the strip's stress fully wound at the measured level is 2.45 GPa (`wempe_physics.js`), which a strip of the same set takes to a mainspring's 2.6 GPa at 1.194: so **−6 % to +19 % of the measured pull**, the strip 0.362-0.393 mm if 0.370 gives that pull, 0.362-0.392 on the 0.370 the solved spring wants at the measured level (since 26 October 2026, the free curve at ×0.75; −7 % to +26 %, 0.361-0.400, on the 0.356 the solved spring wanted at ×0.6 until then). Read as 05 06_20 reads it, g on a 262 mm lever on the fusee arbor: its readings' mean 132.7 g (the sheet's own 131.5) within 124.8-158.5 g. The sources' 225° at the wind's lowest would want 1.338 of the measured drive (a 0.408 mm strip, 177.6 g mean), past the stress limit's 1.194, so not by the strip alone: the swing under the sources' band stays open (Unresolved, the swing's figure).
- **The detent's spring** (1436's "0,07", faint): its stiffness hardly moves the swing (the preload is the banking's, left as set): the guard band holds to 10.2 times the drawn stiffness, 0.152 mm, and with none it swings 208.3°. Its 0.09 (1436's other figure) swings 200.2-211.0°. Its limits are the detent's return in time, which `dynamics.js --model wempe` checks from half to twice the drawn stiffness (0.056-0.088 mm), and its ring (below).
- **The preload** (the banking, not drawn; 0.8 g at the stone estimated since 19 October 2026's physics pass): 0.3 g swings 194.2-204.9° over the wind, 0.8 g 188.5-199.5°, 1.0 g 186.2-197.3°, 1.5 g 180.6-191.8°; the guard band's floor (180°) at **1.55 g**. Its floor is the higher of two: the margin's, **0.78 g** (`wempe_tripping.js` with the lock at 0.235: a held tooth's stone rings 49.3 % of the depth of lock at the estimate, under half from 0.98 times it; 55.7 % at 0.8 times, 78 % at 0.5, teeth past at 0.2 times and under), and the knock's, under 0.64 g (a 3 ms blow of 20 g first lets no tooth go somewhere under 0.8 times; 22.6 g there, 25.6 g at the estimate, 28.7 g at 1.2 times). Until the physics pass, at 0.63 g and the lock 0.175: 0.3 g swings 196.0-205.9° over the wind, 0.63 g 192.9-203.0°, 1.0 g 189.7-199.9°, 1.5 g 185.4-195.8°; the guard band's floor (185°) at **1.55 g** (1.43 g in the figures before their rerun of 18 October 2026). Its floor is the higher of two (18 October 2026): the knock's, **0.61 g**, where a 3 ms blow of 20 g across the plate first lets no tooth go (17.9 g at 0.8 times the estimate, 20.4 at it, 22.6 at 1.2 times; Physics: in time; 0.63 g until the horn was traced, 18 October 2026), and the margin's, 0.51 g (0.49 g from 16 October 2026 until the horn was traced): `wempe_tripping.js`'s sweep under and over the estimate has a held tooth's stone ringing 100 % of the depth of lock with none and at 0.1 and 0.2 times the estimate (teeth get past), 73 % at 0.5, 50.4 % at 0.8 and 44 % at the estimate, so under its margin of half from about 0.81 times (0.51 g), and no tooth past from 0.5 times (with the straight horn: 92 % at 0.2 times, 69 %, 49 % and 41 %, the margin from 0.78 times, 0.49 g, no tooth past from 0.2 times). (`wempe_tolerances.js` first took 0.72 g, the frame's figure from before the banking was read on the pipe, then the estimate itself until the sweep ran under it.)
- **The gold spring** (05 04 01-2: 0.15, sure; its 333 gold's modulus 98 GPa, Au/Ag/Cu 333-333: likely, since 19 October 2026): its work on the return is lost, so softer is free for the swing (none: 209.5° at 12 h; half as stiff: 196.5-207.0°) and the guard band holds to 1.51 times its stiffness (0.172 mm at 98 GPa, or 148 GPa at 0.15). Softer costs the lock: half as stiff, a held tooth's stone rings 71 % of the depth (49 % as drawn). Before the physics pass, at 90 GPa: its work on the return is lost, so softer is free for the swing (none: 207.5°; half as stiff: 198.3-208.3°) and the guard band holds to 2.05 times its stiffness (0.191 mm at 90 GPa, or 184 GPa at 0.15). Softer costs the lock instead: half as stiff, a held tooth's stone rings 52 % of the depth (`wempe_tripping.js`).
- **The depth of lock** (05 04_1: 0.235 since 19 October 2026's physics pass; the banking sets it): as drawn the lock 12.9°, let-off 1.5°, the stone lifted 0.257 mm at the release. **It unlocks only to 0.247 mm** (the discharge stone lifts the stone 0.268 at most; there lock 13.4°, let-off 1.0°), and a held tooth's stone rings under half the depth from 0.232 (twice the 116 µm `wempe_tripping.js` finds as drawn, taken the same at every depth): **a window of 15 µm**, the drawn 0.235 3 µm over its floor, so the bench aims the banking at its middle, 0.239, and sets it again until it reads inside. The wheel's Ø 14 +0.03 at the top of its tolerance locks 0.250 deep and wouldn't unlock: the banking is set to the wheel as made. A shallower lock gives swing back (12.7° of lock and 189.2-194.9° at 0.232). Until the pass (05 04_1: 0.175): as drawn the lock 9.9°, let-off 1.9°, the stone lifted 0.196 mm at the release. **It unlocks only to 0.188 mm** (the discharge stone lifts the stone 0.209 at most; there lock 10.4°, let-off 1.3°), and a held tooth's stone rings under half the depth from 0.154 (twice the 77 µm `wempe_tripping.js` finds as drawn, taken the same at every depth; 0.143 from its 72 µm before the horn was traced). **The wheel's own tolerance, Ø 14 +0.03 (05 04_23), takes a fixed banking past it**: at the top of it the tips lock 0.190 deep and the escapement would not unlock, so the banking is set to the wheel as made. A shallower lock gives swing back (8.8° of lock and 196.5-201.8° at 0.154).
- **The hairspring** (05 03_6's 0.285, boxed, no tolerance; the coil Ø 12.8 ±0.05, boxed): as drawn on the weighed balance it is 0.494 s at steel's 206 GPa, **about 18 minutes a day fast** (0.489-0.501 s over 200-210 GPa: Closed, the hairspring's length) (0.497 s, 8.1 minutes, until 18 October 2026; 0.491 s, 25 minutes, before 15 October 2026's shape batch). A µm of the strip moves the rate 455 s a day, 0.01 mm of the coil's Ø 35 (its drawn ±0.05: ±173), a mm of its length 85, a GPa of the steel's modulus 210 (200-210: ±1,049). A turn of both timing weights (0.432 g at r 18.5 on their 0.16 pitch) is 44.2 s a day, and as drawn they stand against their screws' heads 0.30 mm off the rim (their size estimated): they can only go in, 1.9 turns, gaining up to 83 s a day. So the period is matched, not made: 0.5 s wants the strip 0.2839 (0.2821-0.2867 over the modulus), the weights' reach is 0.18 µm of it, and the coil's own tolerance is twice their reach. The spring is vibrated against its balance and chosen or ground to it, as a 1940s maker did.

**Against the 1904 text** (`keystone.js --model wempe`; it reports, never judges): the wheel's 15 teeth, Ø 14.00, and the roller at 0.484 of it agree; the book's planting puts the balance 9.91 from the escape arbor against 05 04_1's 10.00; the rims cross over 21.7° of the wheel (90 % of a pitch) and 45.7° of the roller (the book's 48°). Where the Wempe differs from the book's English chronometer: the impulse runs 35.1° of the balance against 43°, the wheel catching the stone 12.3° after the release (the book has the jewel 5° inside the wheel before it; the Wempe's comes into the teeth's path 3.8° after); the unlocking takes 9.9° of the balance against 5° (the deep lock, 0.175 on a 7 mm wheel) and turns the detent 0.87° against 2°; the stone has no draw (−3.7°, its flat on the wheel's radius) against the book's 12°, the preload holding the tooth; the stone Ø 0.75 against a third of a tooth space (0.97) and half round against 4/10; the teeth's faces lean 25° against 28° and their backs are the Ø 6 cutter's arc, 3.00 against the roller's 3.39; the detent 12.87 from its flexure to the stone against the wheel's diameter, its spring 5.0 long against 2/7 (3.68), 0.070 against 0.051 thick, 1.9 against 2.03 wide, 1.28 times as stiff; the balance Ø 32.3 against 30.5 and swinging 197° against the book's 225°. The drawings decide.

**The bench** (`wempe_bench.js`: fourteen tests since 25 October 2026, each with how and what it must read; the figures as numbers in `bench_wempe.json`'s criteria):
- *The mainspring's torque*, in its barrel set up ¾ turn: 427 to 839 mN·m at the measured level over the fusee's 5.08 barrel turns (473 to 824 with the free curve at ×0.6, until 26 October 2026), each reading within ×0.940-1.194; half a turn to spare to coil-bound (the model's 0.93); unwinding 1-3 % under winding on the solved spring, over 22 % clean it.
- *The pull on the fusee arbor against 05 06_20*, as Herb. Müller read it in 1941 (a 262 mm lever, each half turn running down): each reading within ×0.940-1.194 of the sheet's at the same turn, and its shape, −4.7 to +4.3 % about the mean with the hump, the dip and the rise.
- *The depths*: the hole tables' centres 22.650, 18.120, 12.630 and 11.850 mm within ±25 µm (`gearing_wempe.json`: the seconds mesh's window less its shake; the tables give the places to 0.001 and no tolerance); a hole closer than its window less its shake re-bushed.
- *Endshake from the sheets' fits*: each arbor's bearings' distance and its length between shoulders, every term with its sheet's tolerance or an estimate: the pillar 19.6 ±0.01 less its two seats 0.3 +0.03 (05 08 00-1, 05 09 01, 05 08 02-1), the bushes' depths (WN-302 0.1 +0.02, WN-300 0.7 +0.02), the arbors' lengths (19 +0.05, 23.05 −0.1, 24.1 −0.1, 22.8 −0.02, 19.7 +0.05, 22.2 +0.1). As drawn the centre arbor has 0.07, the intermediate 0.06, the seconds 0.05, the escape 0.06 (its endstones set 0.03 off the arbor's ends for the 1958 book's 0.05-0.07, since 25 October 2026; 0.04 before), the fusee 0.05, the barrel 0.06; the sheets' tolerances allow −0.02 to 0.13, −0.09 to 0.15, −0.11 to 0.15, 0.02 to 0.08, −0.09 to 0.08 and −0.10 to 0.12. The test reads each arbor against the Soviet Navy's 1958 MX repair book (Appendix 4, pp. 128-129; the Wempe's sheets give none) at 20 °C: the fusee 0.08-0.12, the barrel, centre, intermediate and seconds arbors 0.07-0.10, the escape arbor 0.05-0.07, the balance staff 0.04-0.06 at its endstones (the TU's 4 °C takes 3 µm). The drawings' stacks come out at or just under the book's floors, so a maker frees most arbors a little. Until 19 October 2026 one band, 0.01-0.08 (estimated, after the Hamilton manual's 0.003 in); the user chose the book's figures for a navigating build.
- *The balance vibrated*: 7,200 an hour, or slow by up to 83 s a day (the weights only gain); fast, grind the strip or choose a weaker spring.
- *The free run-down*, the train held, choosing the strip: since 19 October 2026's physics pass the model's balance halves its swing in 46 s (TF 111 s; a running 6MX's TF 102-125 s at its swing); at TF 80, 100, 111, 125, 160, 200 s it halves in 37, 43, 46, 49, 57, 63 s, 05 06_20's drive swings it 170, 186, 194, 203, 223, 241°, the guard's 180° at the wind's lowest wants ×1.144, 0.991, 0.931, 0.870, 0.763, 0.687 of the drive, the sources' 225° ×1.662, 1.424, 1.330, 1.233, 1.065, 0.945. Before it, halving in 55 s (TF 108 s; a running 6MX's TF 96-121 s at its swing); at TF 80, 100, 108, 125, 160, 200 s it halves in 44, 52, 55, 61, 72, 83 s, 05 06_20's drive swings it 175, 191, 197, 209, 229, 247°, and the guard's floor at the wind's lowest wants ×1.159, 0.989, 0.939, 0.853, 0.733, 0.647 of the drive, the sources' 225° ×1.649, 1.398, 1.323, 1.196, 1.019, 0.890.
- *The detent rings*, its foot clamped: 80 Hz with the spring as drawn, 57-113 Hz for 0.056-0.088 mm (`wempe_tripping.js`'s frame: the pitch nearly as the thickness to the 1.5; 1436's 0.09 would ring 116 Hz); the gold spring near 620 Hz.
- *The escapement's figures and the banking* (since 19 October 2026's physics pass): the impulse stone's face 28°10' before the line of centres at rest and the locking stone 37.0° on, 7.14 from the wheel's centre; the gold spring met at −10.3°, released at +2.6° (lock 12.9°), off at +4.1° (let-off 1.5°), passed at −18.8° (overall 22.9°), roller shake 0.011, the teeth 0.39 into the roller; the banking set for a lock 0.239 deep, the window's middle (the drawing's 0.235 is 3 µm over its floor), never under 0.232 nor over 0.247, and set again until it reads inside; the preload at least 0.78 g (the margin's floor, over the knock's under 0.64) and under 1.55 g. Before it: 05 04_1's 24° and 36.5°, the gold spring met at −7.3°, released at +2.6° (lock 9.9°), off at +4.5° (let-off 1.9°), passed at −12.6° (overall 17.1°), roller shake 0.046, the teeth 0.39 into the roller; the lock set 0.175 deep by the banking, never under 0.154 nor over 0.188; the preload by riders on the stone's pipe, at least 0.61 g (the knock's floor, over the margin's 0.51) and under 1.55 g, aimed near 0.84 g (a quarter into its window, estimated; each gram more costs about 9° of swing; 0.49-1.43 g aimed near 0.73 until 18 October 2026).
- *The swing*: in the guard band over the whole wind; the sources' band reported.
- *The maintaining spring*: assembled with its eye's pin up into the ratchet (05 06_4 A-B), closed by the running drive until its ends nearly meet (0.27-0.58 mm apart); the key held, the balance keeps going 10.7-13.9 minutes (wempe_sustaining.js); its ends touching in running, it is weak or set; under 5 minutes (estimated): the ring open (its pins the wrong way round), weak or set.
- *The stop takes a strong hand, and the key is one-way* (25 October 2026, `wempe_overwind.js`): the stop bar and pin file-hard; held against the stop at 1.36 N·m (a strong hand on the Ø 34.5 paddle) nothing marks: hardened, the bar yields at 3.63 N·m on the key and the pin at 9.87, soft the bar at 1.31; the key's coil spring wound of spring wire Ø 0.68 or more; the wrong way the key slips.
- *The cannon pinion's friction* (25 October 2026, `wempe_motionwork.js`): a weight hung 30 mm along the level minute hand turns it between 10 and 295 g; set near 55 g.
- *The 6MX TU's trial, the acceptance test*: 35 days at 36/20/4/20/36 °C, wound to "8" daily, its E ±0.35, R ±2.00, D ±2.30, C ±0.10, S ±1.20, period means ±3.50 and N ≤ 30 (`wempe_errorbudget.js` passes it as adjusted); out on C, the masses moved along the rim from their drawn place (−2.34 s a day a °C) toward 20.5° from the cuts' line, 0.235 s a day a °C a degree (`wempe_thermal.js`).

**The Monte Carlo** (`wempe_montecarlo.js`: 2,000 Wempes, every tolerance drawn at once; the sheets' where drawn, each part anywhere within its limits, estimated where not). The swing comes from a response surface on makeEsc (the drive, the two springs, the preload and the lock's depth, each on a grid), checked against makeEsc rebuilt at five points to 0.6° (makeEsc's swing moves in steps of up to 2° as the lock's depth passes its tables' cells near 0.160-0.163 mm). **As made, 2.4 % work since 19 October 2026's physics pass, after the bench 100 %** (the banking set again until it reads inside the lock's 15 µm window, as a maker measures after setting: once set, 7 of 2,000 missed it); each alone: endshake 8.8 %, the banking as first set 48.6 %, the preload by eye (0.8 ±0.3 g against its 0.78 g floor) 57.6 %, the wheel's 14 +0.03 78.2 %, the hairspring timing in 6.4 %, the rest none. Before the pass: **As made, 3.1 % work (2.9 % before the horn was traced, 18 October 2026; 4.7 % while the preload's least was the margin's 0.49 g, 16-18 October 2026; 5.0 % before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles); after the bench's tests and adjustments, 100 %**, and every movement that fails as made is caught by a test. What spoils it as made, each alone: **endshake** (8 % of movements free: an arbor under 0.01 mm in the fusee 75 %, the barrel 51 %, the seconds 39 %, the intermediate 31 %, the escape 4 %, the centre 1 %: the plates' two seats, 0.3 +0.03, and the arbors' lengths leave it to be set at assembly, as the sheets' own notes set the fusee's face a and the bushes' depths); the preload by eye (0.63 ±0.3 g, estimated, against its floor 0.63 g, the knock's: 53 %; 83 % against the margin's 0.49); the wheel's 14 +0.03 with the banking where the drawn wheel puts it (87 %); the banking as first set (±0.02, estimated: 90 %). The holes (±13 µm, estimated), the train's friction, the mainspring's strip (±0.005), the detent's and gold springs (±0.005) take none out; bored to ±25 or ±50 µm the train still never jams (the seconds mesh's close side, 100 µm less its shake, is the tightest), the depths test re-bushing the close ones. As made the swing over the wind runs 186.9-198.9° at its lowest and 196.9-209.2° at its highest (5-95 %), 66 % in the guard band; after the bench 186.3-196.3° at its lowest and all in it; none in the sources' 225-270°. The hairspring as made times in 8 % (the drawn spring on the weighed balance about 18 minutes a day fast, the timing weights reaching 293 s a day, gaining only; 12 % and 8 minutes before the balance's nuts came off, 18 October 2026): every one is vibrated and matched. Temperature apart: at the masses' drawn place every one fails the TU's C; the trial moves them, and over the materials' ranges a place on the free arc nulls it in 28 of the 32 corners (`wempe_thermal.js`): the alloys, not the maker's tolerances, decide it.

**The mechanisms in turn** (25 October 2026; the Hamilton's `wear.js`, `overwind.js`, `motionwork.js` and `estimates.js` for the Wempe; the report's section 18). The Hamilton's `traindyn.js` has no counterpart: `wempe_transmission.js` (Physics: the transmission) already runs every mesh tooth by tooth through a beat on the weighed bodies.
- *Wear* (`wempe_wear.js`): Archard at every sliding contact, `wempe_strength.js`'s loads fully wound, Rabinowicz's coefficients (oiled: 1e-7 well, 1e-6 poorly, 1e-5 dry; the escapement dry, as a chronometer's runs, at the unlubricated non-metal on metal's 1.5e-6), each against the tolerance it eats, judged over a service interval: three years, the 1954 Soviet instruction's cleaning and fresh oil (`6MX-figures.md`), the TU's ten-year life reported. Every other oiled contact lasts 170 years and more well oiled (the escape arbor's pivot in its jewel first: 176 years, 18 poorly, 1.8 dry; the seconds arbor's in WN-300 199, 20, 2). Two figures go within a service interval or so, both restored at an overhaul: **the balance's endstone** wears the endshake band (the 1958 repair book's 0.04-0.06, set to its middle: 10 µm of room) in 2.7 years, the 12.9 g balance on its Ø 0.21 pivot's end (reported, a repairer's band); and **the drawn escapement's lock window** (15 µm, the banking at its middle: 7.6 µm of room) is worn off the gilt brass teeth's tips in 4.2 years dry (1.3 at 5e-6). The 1950s build's 196 µm window lasts 120 years; its gold spring's face, under the discharge stone twice a period, 15 years.
- *The key forced* (`wempe_overwind.js`): a strong hand taken as 1.36 N·m (the handles' data carried on to the Ø 34.5 paddle: an extrapolation; Swain's 1.06 beside it); forward against the stop every hardened part stands 2.7 times or more short of yielding (the stop bar first, 3.63 N·m). The key's coil spring carries the torque as its wire's tension at its driven end: the wire 51 00 11 scales (about 0.6) yields at 1.57 N·m in patented spring wire, so it is a design value: Ø 0.68 carries half as much again as a strong hand and fits the drawn coil's 0.95 pitch and the body's Ø 0.9 hole. The wrong way the 1943 key slips (its drag about 5 mN·m); the plain 1940 key would bend the click wheel's teeth at 0.73 N·m, less than an average hand's twist (reported: what the one-way key prevents).
- *The motion work* (`wempe_motionwork.js`): the three meshes run at their drawn centres; their depthing windows (closer to the tips on the roots, wider to the end of a 1.2-module search) put the change wheel's stud (hole 19) at ±15 µm, the hour wheel's tips only 50 µm off the change pinion's roots, and **the up-and-down wheel's stud (hole 2) at ±7 µm**, its pinion's tips 35 µm off the wheel's roots as `dial.js`'s tables cut them: tighter than the train's ±25 µm, the place a maker must bore most closely. The cannon pinion's window (`wempe_transmission.js`'s) as a bench weight, and the hands set backward: a fit near the window's middle never turns the train back, one near its top (87 mN·m) would push the escape wheel back on its stone with its own running drive near run down, which the 6MX's instruction forbids.
- *The estimates* (`wempe_estimates.js`, as last revised and both builds): the balance's TF over 80-200 s with the strip the run-down test chooses keeps the swing in the guard band over the whole wind, the strip at most ×1.205 of the drive as last revised (under its stress's ×1.262); the pivots' shake to 0.05 still leaves the holes ±25 µm; the hairspring's four readings of 05 03_7 come to 0.5 s with a strip at most 2.6 % longer than the model's; its outer curve formed 1 mm off its table leaves the pivots 0.025 of the couple over R (the drawn 0.018).
  - Since the mainspring's set was fitted with its friction (×0.75, 26 October 2026) the strip takes at most ×1.194 the drive before its stress passes the limit; the bench's strip for a balance damped at the measured 102 s and above stays within it, while at the sweep's 80 s (under a running 6MX's 102-129 s) it would want ×1.205: reported (`~~`), not failed (the owner's choice), since such a balance would want a stronger steel or a lower drive.

## Making it: the frame under load, the order of assembly, each part on the machine (25 October 2026)

Three checks of whether the Wempe can be made and put together, on the page's own solids, each in the report's section 19 (`verification/geometry.py` runs them).

- **The frame under load** (`tools/fea.py --model wempe`, about 5 minutes, in `ci.py --full`): the Hamilton's finite elements on the parts that hold the going train's pivots, the pillar plate, top plate, barrel bridge, fusee bush and intermediate bridge (their largest visible mesh each), in brass bricks (E 100 GPa, ν 0.34) at two sizes; each arbor's two bearings, the dial side's first (the fusee in the pillar plate and its bush, the barrel in the pillar plate and the barrel bridge, the centre and escape arbors in the two plates, the intermediate and seconds arbors in the intermediate bridge and the top plate); held at their screws (the top plate on the three pillars, Ø 8.1 and 19 long as beams; the barrel bridge and fusee bush on the top plate; the intermediate bridge on the pillar plate), the pillar plate at its rim in the movement ring; loaded with the chain's pull on the drums' common tangent (the spring's moment at 05 06_20's level, `wempe_physics.js`'s MupM fully wound, the solved Mdown at that level run down; the fusee's radius and the chain's height at 8.75 turns and at turn 0, `fusee.js`'s RFT and ac) and each mesh's tooth force at its height (the page's toothed meshes) with `gearing_wempe.json`'s pressure angle, split by the lever rule; all in `checks.py`'s `FEA`. Fully wound the chain pulls 37.7 N (21.7 run down); no pivot hole moves more than 0.36 µm (the barrel bridge's), no arbor leans more than 13.4 µrad (the barrel's), no mesh's depth changes by more than 0.031 µm, 0.01 % of the room its window leaves (the fusee wheel on the centre pinion), and the most stressed brass is at 6.8 MPa (the fusee bush) against its 300-450. The frame is far stiffer than the train needs: the depths a maker holds are the holes' drilling (Building it), not the frame's bending. Left out: the arbors' own bending, the jewels' settings (the brass round them), friction at the fixings, the cocks (the balance's alone).
- **The order of assembly** (`tools/assembly.py --model wempe` + `assembly-check.js`, about 15 s, in `ci.py`'s browser steps): the movement taken apart in the order the Soviet Navy's repair book gives for the MX (*Эксплуатация и ремонт измерителей времени*, 1958, pp. 59-60, "Порядок разборки и сборки", `References/wempe/documents/Ekspluatatsiya-i-remont-izmeritelei-vremeni-1958/p059.png`, `p060.png`): the hands, the dial, the motion work and the up-and-down pinion off the dial side; dial down, the balance's cock with the balance, the detent's block ("Снять колодку ходовой пружины", take the detent's block off: the book's "ходовая пружина" is the detent, p. 60), the barrel arbor's ratchet, the barrel bridge, the fusee arbor's cork washer, flange and washer, the top plate on its three screws, "and all the parts"; together the same backwards (the intermediate bridge on the pillar plate first, the lower cock on the top plate before it goes on, the escape pinion in last before the top plate). Estimated where the book is silent: the barrel out after its bridge (the book puts it in after the top plate), the rest as its assembly reversed, then the intermediate bridge. Every part rastered into 0.2 mm columns as assembled, the model frozen in `audit.py`'s state, each step's parts drawn straight off along the arbors, or along a path of straight legs sideways; a pair fails if one passes through the other or is dragged the long way out of a fit (`checks.py`'s `ASSEMBLY`, its `expected` pairs each with a reason). Found: the detent's block (05 04 01-4 as last revised) hangs under the top plate's inner face between the plates, its end at the rim by a pillar, as on Kirov No. 247 (`8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`), and comes off in the book's place without the plate: its fixing screw out of the plate's outer face, the block with its detent dropped 1.5 off its two steady pins (they reach 1.3 into the plate; 2 would meet the lower cock) and slid out sideways under the plate between the pillars, along −x (every heading from 150° to 180° in plan clear), the stone drawn back out of the escape wheel's teeth (Closed: the detent's block against the book's order); the stop work's bar lies in the fusee's small end under its cover plate and comes out with the fusee; the escape wheel stays in under the top plate only because the lower cock's lug and its jewel's setting overlap only its teeth in plan (5.8-6.9 from its arbor; the roots r 6.05) and pass between two of them, the wheel turned to a gap; the cock's arm runs 1.6 under the cork washer on the fusee arbor's end (x -10.3 to -8.7), so with the washer on it is lifted 1.6 off its seat and drawn out sideways (estimated); a timing screw's head (about 21 from the staff) lies under the dust bush and washer at some swings, so the balance is turned clear; the maintaining detent's screw, its head on the top plate's outer face, comes out before the plate; the chain is unhooked and unwound, not lifted. 48 parts, 18 steps, 10 expected pairs, none failing; the hands planted behind the dial fail it, and the lower cock planted 0.8 into the block's way out.
- **Each part on the machine** (`tools/dfm.py --model wempe`, about 8-15 minutes, in `ci.py --full`; fails only on a void sealed inside one piece): every machined piece's solid (screws, jewels, springs, the chain, the dial and hands left out) sampled level by level between its flat faces' heights on a grid 0.01-0.06 mm square, against stated limits: walls in plan under 0.15 mm, slots narrower than an end mill of radius 0.2, the inside corners it leaves rounded, holes over 10 or 20 diameters deep or under Ø 0.2, floors, what is cut from each face, turned or turned-then-cut or milled, undercuts, setups; `checks.py`'s `DFM` notes how a finding is cut where its sections mislead. Of 171 pieces, 56 are turned, 16 turned then cut (the plates' blanks, the wheels before their teeth and crossings), 99 milled (17 of them toothed); 97 need one setup, 73 two, 1 three; about 300 round holes, none deeper than 10 diameters nor under Ø 0.2. No void is sealed inside a piece. The work a maker plans for: the escape wheel's web recess (Ø 11.62, 0.6 deep) lies 0.04 inside its teeth's floor Ø 11.7, leaving a sliver of brass about 0.04 wide between the recess and each gap (turn the recess to Ø 11.7, or under Ø 11.3); the oil sinks of the bushes WN-300 and WN-301 run to 0.13 of their outside at the top plate's outer face; the cannon pinion's crowned end leaves its tube 0.12 thick round the bore at the top; the stop work's bar 050603-3 is 0.10 wide in places; the upper cock's slit (0.11 wide) is a slitting saw's; the stud's cross hole is drilled from the side (its one undercut); the fusee's groove is a fusee engine's (its sections' 0.08 slivers are the helix). The rest is ordinary: about 1,000 inside corners drawn sharp in some 40 pieces (the counts move by a few per cent from run to run, as the moving parts stand) are left with a fillet up to the mill's 0.2, which their mating parts' edges must clear.

## Making it: the maker's sheets and the build book (25 October 2026)

Since 26 October 2026 the **Build book** is a PDF of engineering drawings beside the page, `book/wempe-build-book.pdf` (`tools/drawbook.py`, the owner's choice: the style of the Wempe's own works drawings, DIN first-angle, a sheet and a table sheet a part, each assembly ballooned; CLAUDE.md lists how it is made), and a card's **Drawing** opens it at that part's sheet (`js/bookpages.js`). What follows is the in-page book it replaced, which `book.py --model wempe` still checks.

The page's **Making it** (its own section, after Download) is the Hamilton's (`../chronometer-working-model/js/maker.js`, shared: the Wempe gives it its own words and choices in `MAKER.bind`'s `cfg`; `../shared/viewer.js` passes it a click on the model, `cfg.pick`, and each card as it opens, `cfg.card`). Each part card gets **Maker's sheet** and **Drawing** (the card's own Download it as STL stays): the sheet lists the part's lines of the parts list, with the fit `bom.py` measured on the model, the material and treatment **its own drawing** gives and how it is made; then its size, volume and mass measured on the solids, its holes and screws. **Measure** (two points on the model), **Build book** (every part's sheet and drawing to print, the materials and processes chapter, the bench's tests) and **Data** (the drawings' sizes `WD`, the escapement's settings, the build, the parts list, as JSON). The sheets follow the version shown (the drawings as last revised by default); the book leaves out another version's parts (`cfg.inBook`), and a sheet lists the lines its meshes carry where a line's card is another part's (`cfg.byHn`: the transit lock's wood screws, a line of the box).

- **The data**: `tools/wempe_makers.py` writes `js/makers.js` (`MAKERS`) from `bom.json`, `BOM.md` (written by `bom.py --model wempe --md`: the fits as measured, grouped by drawing group; `bom_md.py`'s `write_drawings`) and the transcriptions: for each line its sheet (its drawing number in a section's heading; an older sheet under another number by its file among the line's `figs` and its heading naming the part; `ALIAS` where neither does: the hairspring's strip on 05 03_6, the detent on 1436, the staff on 05 04 02), and off that sheet the Werkstoff, the hardening and tempering, the surface marks (DIN 140's triangles), the blank and the untoleranced limit. Nothing is filled from practice: of 223 lines as last revised, 60 have their material on their own drawing; the rest say why not (a Wempe-Norm part, its standard sheet not in the set; a part drawn only on an assembly; the Werkstoff left blank, as on the 1946 staff's sheet, which gives only "gehärtet, anlassen 240°"). `--check` (in `ci.py`) fails if `js/makers.js` is stale; `--list` prints the materials and processes list. Run it after changing `bom.json`, a transcription, the build, the bench or the tolerances (and `bom.py --model wempe --md` after a fit changes).
- **What the drawings say the parts are made of** (read off the transcriptions, as sure as they are): the plates "Messingblech 4 / Sonder Ms α-β Gef." (special brass, α-β structure); the wheels Ms 58 sheet, spring-hard (the escape wheel Ms 63); the pinions and arbors "Präz. Rundstahl" (DIN 175); the balance "Nickelstahl Sonderlegierung u. Messing"; the hairspring "Bandstahl 0,52×0,285 poliert", hardened and tempered dark blue; the gold spring "Goldblech 333 federhart"; the detent (1436) "Stahl", where the impulse roller is "Kupfer Beryllium, Saphir"; the maintaining spring and clicks strip steel, the click spring "Bronzeband"; the bowl "Ms-Sandguß (Kokillenguß)"; the outer box "gebleichte Birke". The mainspring has no sheet of its own: its material is the build's (section "The build"), 05 06_20 giving only its section.
- **How each is made** (`MAKERS[].how`, the book's chapter "Materials and how each part is made"): CNC milled or wire-cut from the drawn sheet or strip, CNC turned from the drawn bar, cast and machined, joinery; bought in (the jewels, against standard sizes; the Wempe-Norm screws and pins); by hand or from a special supply: the cut bimetallic rim (no stock supplier: brass fused on the nickel steel, or a balance blank bought), the hairspring wound on a form, hardened on it and its curves bent to 05 03_7 by hand, the 8 ct gold sheet, the detent filed and stoned to its ring, the mainspring (Nivaflex for the 1950s build), the chain. The kinds are the tool's reading of the drawn stock and the part, not a process plan.
- **The deviations** (the 1950s build's, on by default, each set back to the drawing on its card; `orig=gold,mainspring` in the link): the gold spring thinned at its bend (both builds), the mainspring in Nivaflex 45/5 (the 1950s build). The tolerances on the sheets are `tolerances_wempe_build.json`'s, the tests `bench_wempe_build.json`'s.
- **Checked**: `book.py --model wempe` (as the Hamilton's: every line shown on the model on some sheet, each with a material or the reason it has none and a fit, every drawing at its stated scale, legible on A4, no taller than a page), 0 findings in 227 sections; `smoke.py --model wempe` opens a sheet, switches the gold spring to as drawn and back, measures, builds the book. Not in the book: an order of assembly (the Hamilton's is its manual's Sec. VIII reassembly; no assembly instructions for the Einheits-Chronometer or the 6MX are in the sources).

## The Hamilton's model checks for the Wempe: holes, the maintaining work, the arithmetic (16 October 2026)

The checks the Hamilton's model has that the Wempe lacked, each taking `--model wempe` (its tables in `checks.py`), each in `ci.py` and failing on a fault planted with `--eval`.

- **Every hole populated** (`tools/holecheck.py --model wempe`, about 1½ minutes, in `ci.py`'s browser steps): every round hole in a flat face of every part, as last revised and as first drawn, followed along its axis as deep as the metal round it (`HOLE_WALL`), something in it or a reason it is empty on the real clock too (`HOLE_EXPECTED`). 550 holes over the two states, 504 populated, 23 expected empty: the hand setting's 14-18 (drilled, the work not fitted, as Wempe 3955's plate), the auxiliary holes 3, 12 and 40 in both plates and 20 in the upper cock, hole 30 with its seat and two M1.2, the balance holding device's 51-53 (not built: Unresolved), the pillar plate's 23 (Ø 7 under the balance, nothing on the sheets in it), the dial's Ø 1 at 185.7° (nothing under it in the ring), the impulse roller's Ø 1.3, and as first drawn the balance arm's two screw holes (the 1941 collar has none). The upper cock's screw hidden, its holes in the cock and the top plate fail.
- **The maintaining work** (`tools/maintaining.py --model wempe`, about 2 minutes, in `ci.py --full`), in three parts. On the solids, through the movement's own update (503 frames, 322 of them winding): the maintaining ratchet never turns back; running, the fusee turns with it, resting on the nearer click; winding, back; let go, it catches forward 0.0-4.8° (under half a click wheel's tooth, 5.1°: the two clicks half a tooth apart); the clicks stay 0.017-0.018 off the click wheel, the bearing one 83 % into its teeth; the maintaining detent rides the ratchet −0.010 to 0.016 off it (the seat's table of radii cuts the ratchet's sharp tips by up to 0.01), dropping up to 81 % into its gaps; its wire spring bears on it within 0.016. The page's own loop through two winds with the Wind button (18 October 2026, the ratchet ahead of the wheel by the maintaining spring's lead, `s.ms`): the ratchet turns 55.97° an hour of the train (the wheel's 56.00) and back only as the key goes on, falling to its detent (2.5-2.6° in two runs, fusee.js `fall()`, within a tooth's 3°), or with a set of the wind (the slider: 15.64°, run down with the spring spent); running, the drive is WPHYS.drv(n); a wind from 20 h keeps the train going on the spring (0.98 of the drive running, 0.80 as the key goes on, the detent's fall taken, the escape wheel on 4 teeth over the wind); from run down the spring drives nothing and the train stays held through the wind. Until 16 October 2026 (claude/wempe-maintaining) app.js's `wind0()` set `winding` before reading `drvNow()`, so the spring carried nothing into a wind. Then the stop work at full wind (`wempe_stopwork.py --step 0.05`: the pin 0.020 off the bar's far side, the least gap before 0.692). Planted fault: the click wheel 2 % large, the clicks 0.11 into it.
- **The arithmetic** (`tools/invariants.py --model wempe`, about 10 s, in `ci.py`'s browser steps): the seven pairs of wheels and pinions drawn in mesh (the fusee wheel and centre pinion, centre, intermediate and seconds; the cannon pinion and change wheel, the change pinion and hour wheel; the up-and-down pinion and wheel) each turn as their teeth through the movement's own update, their centres m (z1 + z2)/2 apart to 0.002 (22.650, 18.120, 12.630, 11.850, 12.600, 12.600, 18.400); the hands' tips point at the time to 0.1° (each tip's middle read off its outline within 0.03-0.08°; a beat each half second: 120 a minute from the escape wheel's 15 teeth, 24° a beat); setting 10 minutes turns the minute hand 60° and the hour hand 5°, the train and the fusee not at all; the up-and-down hand is at the dial's printed 0 fully wound and its 56 after 8.75 fusee turns (to 0.08°), turning with its wheel; after 56 h of the train it reads 55.77 (the train's 90/14 h a fusee turn against the dial's 3.75 turns in 24 h, 05 06_20: 0.45 % apart), at run down 55.68 for the 55.91 h run; the groove's 8.75 working turns are 56.25 h of the train, the model's wind from the stop 55.91 h; WESC.SET's balance (2,526.2 g·mm²) and escape arbor (9.96) are wempe_inertia.json's; the drawn hairspring beats 0.494 s against the train's 0.5. Reported: the Winding slider's 0.1 h steps stop 0.01 h short of run down (55.91 h), so it can't set the movement run down (the link's `wind=55.91` can). Planted fault: the hour hand turning 1 % fast fails the hour hand and the hour wheel with it.
- **The pawls** (`tools/mechanisms.py --model wempe`, about 15 s, in `ci.py`'s browser steps, writing `tools/mechanisms_wempe.json`; `tools/wempe_ratchets.js`, a second or two, in its quick steps): each pawl off the built solids, its ratchet turned the way its load turns it until it meets the pawl, then judged as the Hamilton's ratchets.js judges its own. The bearing click: 0.17 mm short of its face, 84 % into the teeth, the load drawing it in at 19.6° (16.6° with its end at the face's middle) against the friction angle's 5.7-11.3°: it seats itself; 49 N at the strongest pull, the click wheel's tooth (brass) at a margin of 5.2, its face 9.5, the screw's shoulder 19. The other click stands 0.76 short of its face, half a tooth on. The set-up click: 0.17 short, drawing in at 19.8°, 140 N fully wound, margins 5.3 and up. The maintaining detent: drawing in at 3.7-4.2°, under the friction angle, so friction holds it where it meets the face and its wire seats it; 19.5 N, margins 6 and up. When the key goes on, the maintaining ratchet falls back to its detent by anywhere from nothing to a tooth's 3.0° as it creeps on running, against the maintaining spring's 15.0° of lead over the wheel at 12 h (its drawn ring closed by the drive: The maintaining spring, above), so a wind always keeps 80-100 % of the spring's travel (the page takes the mean half tooth, `WPHYS.MBK`; until 16 October 2026 the spring's travel was read as 05 06_9's 0.27 mm, 1.09°, which about 64 % of winds would have lost). The clicks' springs (bronze 1.5 × 0.4, their free span estimated 12-16) need 0.01-0.03 mm of set to follow the click wheel down when the key lets go (the fusee and barrel weighed: 4,053 and 27,523 g·mm²). Planted fault: the set-up click's pivot mirrored across the load's line, its load pushes it out (exit 1).

## As first drawn (phase E1)

The Variants' second state (`movement.js`'s `setRev`, app.js's Sheets control): every part keeps its key, the pieces only one state has are built in groups tagged with their state and the other state's hidden, as the band is (the tools check what is shown: `audit.py --rev first`, and `fine.py`, `exploded.py` and `bom.py` with `--eval "__mv.userData.setRev('first')"`); the cards of the parts that differ take that state's text (app.js's REV, REVP). Where the earlier issue is the same line (05 04_5 carries "05 04 02-1" in pencil; 05 04_7.1 was renumbered 05 04 05 on 05 04_7.2) it keeps the line, else it has its own (the banking screw, `cone41`, quantity 0 or 1).

Wempe 3955 is made so: its escape wheel lies just under the top plate (`10_Wempe_3955_Feder_plus.jpg`), its detent block is 05 04_1's with the long arm and cone (`11_Wempe_3955_Gangfeder_plus.jpg`; `10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg` the same block beside the 6MX's).

- **Heights** (05 04_1's elevation, 10:1, 24.4 px/mm): the balance's seat 4.1 over the top plate's inner face ("4,1 bis Unruhauflage", sure), 0.3 higher than 05 04 02 puts it, so the rim, arm, hairspring and collet stand 0.3 higher and the spring's top curve 3.69 under the stud's bar (its pin's hole drilled at assembly); the escape wheel's teeth 0.5 under the plate, 1.4 thick ("0,5", "1,4", sure), the web 0.8 on the pinion's side (scaled 0.74); the impulse roller under the 1941 collar's tube, 0.6 under the plate (the collar's boxed 4.7 −0.05 under the seat; 05 04_1 scales 0.42: the figures win), the discharge roller 1.6 under it ("1,6", sure); the detent 2 wide from 1.3 to 3.3 under the plate ("1,3", sure), its blade's 1 flush with its lower edge (scaled 2.34-3.36); the block's arm 3.7 to 6.5 ("2,8", "6,5", sure).
- **The staff and collar** (05 04_5, Unruhwelle 040401-1, 4.4.41): the collar 040401-2, every figure: a neck Ø 2.1 ±0.03 standing 1.3 over the flange, the flange Ø 6.3 × 1.8 straight for 0.4 then undercut 10° (to Ø 5.81), the tube Ø 2.6 ±0.03 to 4.7 −0.05 under the flange's top. The arm is riveted on the neck (drawn flush with the arm's top, its 0.15 spread into the hole, and at the arm's 2 H7); the sheet's collar has no screw holes, so the arm's two (05 03 01-1, 31.7.41) stand empty. The staff is a blank on its sheet: its ends and the collet's seat are 05 04 02's, its seats through the collar and the impulse roller Ø 1.645, the discharge roller's Ø 1.2 (estimated).
- **The escape arbor** (05 04_7.1, Gangrad 050408 bis 10, 4.4.41): the pinion's far face 15.2 from the wheel's plate face (likely), 17.7 from the arbor's top-plate end (sure), 5.3 from its dial end (likely, the figure overwritten), 23 −0.2 overall (sure): the wheel at 05 04_1's height puts the top-plate end 2.0 into the plate and the dial end 1.96 into the pillar plate, and the seconds wheel's rim lies wholly in the pinion. The arbor Ø 1.2 under the pinion; from the wheel's plate face Ø 1.05 for 0.5 and a cone to the pivot Ø 0.24 × 0.5 (scaled). The collet: a cone Ø 3.0 on the wheel to Ø 2.2 (scaled) at 1.8, then a pipe Ø 1.8 to 5 over it (sure). The wheel's bore 1.8 (05 04_1). Its jewels follow the arbor's ends: the upper hole jewel's setting fills the seat down to it, its bore opened to 0.6 for the cone; the lower bush shortened, the endstone in a plug under the cap (estimated, as the settings are).
- **The detent** (1436's first figures, the unbracketed): the foot 0.65, the square and the blade's thicker part 0.55, the blade 0.33, the horn 0.25; the gold spring 0.025 further out on the thicker square. Its plan and lengths are the revised one's (05 04_1's plan agrees: 8.4 pipe to square, the spring 5, the foot 6.6, 18.8 from the stone to the block's screw).
- **The block** (05 04_1, no sheet of its own): the base is 05 04 01-4's (its screw at hole 13, its steady pins and the foot's screw's hole fall on 05 04_1's holes), the foot's face 0.025 further out for the thicker foot; the hole Ø 1.3 to the foot's screw drilled from the plate's side, 3 deep, to meet the screw 2.3 under the plate (Wempe 3955's base shows it on that face, 2.4-2.5 from the edge). In place of the upright an arm along the detent to 2.9 past the stone, 3.56 wide and flush with the base's edge as both photographs have it (3.6 at 22.3 px/mm by the base's 14.6; 05 04_1 sets it back 1.06 and draws it 2.5 wide, its far edge where the photographs' is), a cove r 1.82 and a ledge 0.78 into the base (scaled). Through it on the stone's line the banking screw (WN-16, the coloured section's label), 5.1 under the plate ("1,4" over the arm's face, sure): M1.2 (05 04_1's thread lines); its tail a cone 12° from Ø 1.25, starting 0.85 inside the arm's far face so it stands 2.47 out (Wempe 3955: Ø 0.84 there, 2.4-2.5 out); its gilt domed head Ø 3.3 × 1.5 on the wheel's side of the pipe, which banks on its flat face (Ø 3.29 fitted on 05 04_1's plan, 3.31-3.36 on the coloured section, 3.0-3.45 on Wempe 3955's photographs: likely; the dome R 1.8 and its edge estimated; its slot 0.3 wide and 0.45 deep right across the dome, as Wempe 3955's in the movement shows it, `10_Wempe_3955_Feder_plus.jpg`: a band following the dome, as the 6MX's; until 18 October 2026 a bar standing on the apex). Until 17 October 2026 only the tail was built (Ø 0.85), the head taken for a gilt dish on no sheet. The arm's end is slit along the screw to clamp it, as 05 04_1's elevation draws it: open at the end, 6.1 long, 0.2 wide, through the arm's width at the screw's axis (sure). The arm is built across its width (its elevation the solid's plan, so the slit and the screw's hole can be cut) and made one mesh with the base; until 18 October 2026 it was not cut.
- **The Exploded view**: the arm lies 0.28 over the lower cock's lug, so as first drawn the block, the detent and the escape wheel rise 30.3 and the lower cock 29.9 (31 as last revised): the arm leaves first and the seconds wheel (33) still clears the cock.
- **Checked** (15 October 2026), in both states: solids, the audit (its coplanar faces in `audit-expected.json` under `movement.first`), fine.py (46 states, nothing new), exploded.py, bom.py. The physics (escphys.js and the tools) is the revised escapement's: the 1941 detent's thicker spring and the heights change its figures a little, not worked.
- **The barrel** (phase E2; 0207 Federhaus, 127-556.04 U24, 15.4.41, 5:1, scaled 12.0 px/mm by its 22.3; the drum 020701, U22; its ring 020701-3): three pieces, the ring Ø 41.5 outside, 38.5 inside, 18 long (sure), the lid on the pillar plate's side ("warm einsprengen"; the arbor's square out through the bottom on the bridge's side: sure), its face 3.7 off the plate and the bottom 0.6 under the bridge (22.3 between them, sure; the 3.7 scaled 3.75), each sprung into a seat Ø 39.2 (020701-3, framed) at its end; the lid 1.2 and the bottom 1.0 thick (0207, sure; 020701's 1.2 would leave 15.6 inside against the framed 15.8), 15.8 between them (framed); each with a boss standing 1.9 inward, its face 3.1 from the lid's outside and 2.9 from the bottom's (sure), Ø 9 with a 22½° cone to Ø 12 (020701, likely), bored 4.55 as the later drum (020701's 4.5; 0207 draws the journals Ø 5). The arbor's core is 020704-1's 12 long with its 22½° ends, between those bosses (11.9, 0.05 off each); its hook at the core's middle, and the mainspring in the middle of the 15.8, both about 0.5 lower than as revised. The hook block is the later drum's (0207 draws the same 6 × 0.5 flat at its M2.6 hole); the Steg is 0207's own (part 106, 127-556.04-097: 2 × 0.5, its plan's "0,4" off the bore, so r 18.35-18.85), through slots 2.025 × 0.55 in the 1941 lid and bottom (0207's section draws them closed round it; their size estimated); the chain lies 0.05 off the 1941 drum's Ø 41.5 (its path is the 1948 drum's 41.6). Wempe 3955's photograph (7_) shows the drum with its chain but not which end carries the lid.
- **The maintaining ratchet** (05 06 02-1 before revision (a), 11.3.48): its tips Ø 37.5 ("Maß 37,25∅ war 37,5∅"), drawn 37.45, the teeth as deep, 0.125 further out under the maintaining detent, which seats on them in this version; the struck "25,4∅" was the chamfer's top, the same chamfer the revised "0,3 +0,15" gives (Readings corrected). Built 27 October 2026. `maintaining.py --model wempe --eval "__mv.userData.setRev('first')"` passes (27 October 2026): the detent 0.011-0.024 off the teeth, the wire spring bearing on it 0.001-0.002. It had failed on two faults, neither the ratchet's: the detent was seated by its outline's corners alone against its ratchet's radius table interpolated between rays, so a sharp tip reached up to 0.015 into it between them (now its outline every 0.01 and the higher of the two rays about each angle, fusee.js `polarMax(…, hi)`; as last revised 0.015-0.025 off, -0.010 before), and the check kept the wire's first geometry, measuring the wire as built against the detent as turned (maintaining-wempe.js caches triangles by geometry now).
- **The maintaining spring** (020605, Gegenfeder kompl., 127-556.04-U18, 15.4.41, the older number of the spring 05 06 01-1, drawn the same day): the same ring, eye, holes and pins, its width given round it, 1.8 at 40°, 1.9 at 100°, 2 at 150° and 195°, 2.5 at 240°, 3.5 at 270° from the eye (sure), drawn between those figures and on to 4 at the square end as 05 06 01-1's (estimated).
- **Not built as first drawn: the fusee of 1941** (02 06 13, "Übergangsserie", and its body 020613-2): its large end is dished, a cone into the bore, with a collar (65) and a separate flange plate (66, held by a set screw "Gewindestift 0,7") in it and a piece at its small end (67); none of these has a sheet in the set, and the dish takes a maintaining work drawn only in the 1947-48 issues. Its groove's radii are the later body's; its flange Ø 36 (35.6 struck), its bore tapered 1:70.
- **Checked** (phase E2) in both states: solids, the audit, fine.py (the barrel's pieces built in each state's turning group with its hook block, so they stay fixed to it), exploded.py, bom.py, smoke.py.
- **The key of 1940** (phase E3; Aufzugschlüssel 127-556.02 U 01 = 51 00 11, 12.12.40, 2:1), replaced by the one-way key of 1943 under the same number: 84 overall and the stem 32 out of the body (sure); the rest traced on the sheet row by row at 6.79 px/mm (by its 84): the handle a flat pear-shaped plate, the tang (part 1; the side view draws it 3.2 thick, no bulb), widest Ø 34.8 about 9 from its top, its neck Ø 16.5; the collar Ø 20.5 × 2.9; the body (2) a cone Ø 17.9 to 10.3 over 19.3; the stem (3) Ø 6.0, its socket (drawn round, Ø 3.1 × 28) and the depth it is pressed in (13.0) estimated, the body from the figured 32 (built from the traced 31.7 until 27 October 2026); the screw (5) on the collar beside the tang. The click spring in the body (4) isn't drawn. The sheet gives no materials: the handle and the body with its collar brass, as Wempe No. 6161's key (`antiquorum_208-4_wempe6161.jpg`: the pear-shaped handle and the collar with its slotted screw brass; the body under it not seen: sure for the handle and collar; black, as the 1943 key's, until 18 October 2026). It stands in the box's key holder where the later key does, on its body's cone. The case's pieces of each state follow the movement's (app.js), so `audit.py box --rev first` audits the box with it (its faces under `box.first`).
- **On the cards only** (phase E3): the figures the revisions changed by less than the model can show, or only in their tolerance, each part's card as first drawn giving the earlier: the movement ring before revision a (6.12.46: 10.7 −0.05, 3 ±0.05, 7 ±0.07, 0.7 +0.05), the lower cock's seat (+0.05 before 2.12.46), the click before 25.5.48 (its hole's tolerance), the set-up ratchet (2.63 −0.03 before 24.11.47), the seconds arbor (24 −0.05 between its shoulders and its wheel's seat 1.3 −0.1 before 24.3.48), the intermediate arbor's pivots (0.82 −0.02, 1940), the change wheel's and the hour wheel's printed tips (21.30, 20.0, before the pencilled 21.05 and 19.7) and the hour wheel's 1.0, and the 1941 fusee (not built, above).

## The Soviet 6MX (phase X)

*Status (18 October 2026): complete. X0-X4 (16 October 2026) built the switch, the movement, the dial and finish, the case and boxes, the cards, the essay's paragraphs, the report's section and the physics worked apart; X5 (18 October 2026, below) settled what they left: every one of the 6MX gallery's photographs read for the model, the parts measured on them built (the escapement's collet and detent block, the lower cock, the ring's screws, the bowl, brackets, closing plate and outer box), its electrical contact built, and the page running the 6MX's own escapement. What no source shows stays in "Estimated" (The Soviet 6MX) and "Unresolved"; the asks for the photographs that would settle it in REQUESTS.md.*

The Variants' third version, Soviet 6MX (Kirov No. 247, 1952; `rev=6mx` in the link): the 6MX (6МХ) the 1st Moscow Watch Factory made from these drawings after 1946-47, built as one photographed chronometer is made, as Wempe 3955 stands for the first-drawn sheets. Kirov No. 247 (February 1952, first generation) has Altmeppen's full set (`photos/dg-chrono-6mx/`: its dial, top plate on millimetre paper, dial side, four obliques, detent and box) and his arrowed comparisons with a Wempe. Its electrical contact (`E-Kontakt`) is left out. The plan is `PLAN-wempe-6mx.md` at the root.

**X0, the switch (16 October 2026).** The version control (heading "Version") has three buttons. `userData.rev` now lists the versions a group shows in (`'last 6mx'`: the revised sheets' pieces the 6MX kept; `'first'`; `'6mx'` for its own), `setRev(v)` shows a group if its list holds `v`, and each `onRev` callback gets the version's name. Until its own parts are built, the 6MX shows every revised piece: its views are pixel for pixel the last-revised ones (`views.py --model wempe --eval "__mv.userData.setRev('6mx')"`). Both earlier versions rendered unchanged, every view 0 px. The tools: `audit.py --rev 6mx` (its keys `movement.6mx`, `box.6mx`), `ci.py`'s `wempe-audit-6mx` and `wempe-exploded-6mx`; `bom.py`'s `dev` entries take a list of versions in `rev`; app.js's cards per version in `REVC`.

**How Kirov No. 247 is measured.** Its top plate (`2_Kirow_247_A.jpg`, square-on) is put on the sheets' hole table by a similarity on four arbors seen on it (the fusee's square in the cork ring, the barrel's square, the balance's cap jewel, the escape wheel's endstone) and pillars 11 and 49: 8.98 px/mm, every residual under 0.55 mm; `holemap.py --model wempe` lays it over the model's plan hole by hole (it now takes `--model`). Its dial side (`3_Kirow_247_uZ.jpg`) the same way on seven holes (the centre, seconds, change wheel, intermediate, escape and the intermediate bridge's two screws): 8.63 px/mm, under 0.42. The millimetre paper under the top plate gives the paper's scale (8.8 px/mm), not the plate's, which stands well above it. Screws' centres are circles fitted to their edges.

**X1a, the frame (16 October 2026).** Each piece and how sure:
- *Pillar 39 at 190°* (Altmeppen's list: pillar 39 in another place). No pillar or screw at its drawn 210° on either face; its screw at 190.0° (top) and 189.6° (dial side), where 11 and 49 read 94.9/94.8° and 350.1/349.9°, their drawn bearings: sure to ±0.5°, pillar 49's 350° mirrored across the O-W line. Its radius reads 36.6-37.3 (each reading corrected by 11's and 49's in the same fit). On r 37 the drawn pillar (Ø 8.1) would stand 0.11 into the drawn seconds wheel's tips (Ø 21.9); it clears them from r 37.2, so it is drawn at r 37.3, 0.2 clear (likely; the 6MX's seconds wheel isn't seen). Both plates' holes and seats, the pillar and its two screws move with it (`WD.P39_6MX`).
- *No hole 20 in the upper cock* (Altmeppen: no window in the upper cock; his comparison's arrow points at the Wempe's hole there). Kirov 247's cock is plain where the overlay puts it: sure.
- *No hole 53 in the top plate* (Stift f. Wendelfeder, for the balance holding work): Kirov 247's plate is bare there, in plain view: likely. Holes 51 and 52 lie under its balance, unseen, and are kept.
- *Holes 3, 12 and 40 kept.* Hole 3 is a clean Ø 3.6 hole at 0.3° (its Ø 3.5 at 0°), 12 at 105.0° (105°), 40 partly under the barrel bridge at 224°: so Altmeppen's "three small windows by the pillar screws" are not these Hilfslöcher, as frame.js had read them (unresolved: what he meant isn't found on Kirov 247's photographs).
- *Jewels in chatons* at the intermediate arbor's two pivots and the seconds arbor's upper (Altmeppen; the TU's 15 jewels are the drawings' 12 and these three). Flush gilt settings about Ø 4.1 on both faces of Kirov 247, the pivot in the middle, no screws. Each chaton fills the bush's hole (4.59 in the plate, 4.3 in the bridge), its stone flush with the face the arbor's shoulder bears on, so the endshake stays the drawings' (likely); the stone Ø 2.2 × 0.6 and the sink estimated. `bom.json`'s six lines carry `"rev":"6mx"` (a line with `rev` counts only in those versions), the bushes `"last first"`, and the arbors' `in` relations take either (`WN-300.t38|6MX.jw38u`); `checks.py`'s `JEWELS` is per version (15 for the 6MX).
- *The electrical contact's fixings* (X5, below): the top plate's two Ø 3.5 access holes over the contact's base screws (219.0° r 37.5 and 179.1° r 38.6: the README read the 219° one, with the screw's dark slotted head 19 mm down in it, as "a cheese-head screw in the top plate at S 28.75 W 23.07"; Kirov 1564's, without the contact, are open to the pillar plate); the dial side's two flush screw ends at 180° r 38.2 and 220° r 37.9 (M1.7, open on Kirov 1564). The small hole at S 20.6 O 30.2 is on Kirov 1564's plate too: not the contact's, not built (Unresolved).

**X1b, the escapement (16 October 2026).** The 6MX has the escapement at the heights the sheets first drew, with the revised block's form:
- *The escape wheel just under the top plate, on a long cone collet* (likely). Kirov 247's detent view (`8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`) and Kirov-Poljot 24020's (`9_KirPol_24020_Feder2.jpg`) show it so, as Wempe 3955's (`10_Wempe_3955_Feder_plus.jpg`, the first-drawn version's source); Altmeppen's comparison of Wempe 3955 with a 6MX (`1_3_Vergleich_Wempe-6MX_Feder.jpg`) marks the block, not the wheel. On 24020's, scaled by the block's base (6.5 deep, 26.6 px/mm), the wheel's teeth read 0.35-2.3 under the plate. So the 6MX takes the first-drawn balance, rollers, escape wheel and arbor and their jewels (`'first 6mx'`), and their Exploded offsets. The staff's form (the 1941 collar or the 1946 hub) isn't seen on any 6MX photograph: the first-drawn one, the staff the sheets draw at these heights, is built (estimated).
- *The detent* at the first-drawn heights its block holds it at (the foot 1.3-3.3 under the plate), with 1436's revised (bracketed) sections (likely). It is not shorter than 1436's: the 6.4 from the pipe to the square read on the loose 6MX block until 18 October 2026 divided a foreshortened span by the scale across the block; along the detent (24.5 px/mm) it reads 8.2 (7.8-8.6), the drawn 8.4 (X5).
- *The block, 05 04 01-4 read in first angle*, as the sheet itself reads (DIN's; How the balance and escapement were measured): the upright at the free face, 3.5-6.5 under the plate; on 24020's it reads 4.1-7.0, the detent's blade 2.9-4.1 above it (±0.3 with the camera): sure in kind, the sheet's figures kept. The 6MX settles, for itself, the reading the drawings leave unresolved: its block hangs its upright below the detent, at the first-drawn heights. Its foot a taper (below); the hole Ø 1.3 drilled from the plate's face to the foot's M1.
- *The banking*: the cross screw through the upright, centred, its domed brass head Ø 3.2 × 1.4 on the wheel's side of the pipe, which rests on its flat face (24020's: Ø 3.2 against the upright's 3; the loose block's, `8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg`, reads Ø 3.8 by the upright's 3.0 at 26 px/mm, which would stand 0.13 into the gold spring's path as it bends aside: the mounted reading taken, likely), its thread 0.6 out behind the upright.
- *Not built:* the adjusting slide. The loose block has a gilt slide in a notch across the base's plate face under a slotted cylinder, and a large slotted screw (head about Ø 3.4 on 24020's, 2.9 under the plate) in the base's end beside the detent's foot; how they meet the plate's hole 13 and which way the slide moves aren't seen (Unresolved). Nor is the collet's form: both 6MXs show one long gilt cone, about 5 long, as Wempe 3955 (Unresolved, below); the first-drawn arbor's cone and pipe are built.

**X1c, the barrel (16 October 2026).** *The barrel cap underneath* (Altmeppen's list; Knirim's, against Lange's Kal. 100): the drawings' own barrel with its lid on the pillar plate's side, the 1941 three-piece barrel the first-drawn version builds (0207, 020701; its arbor's core and the mainspring 0.44 lower with it): likely. Kirov 247's side views (`4_`, `6_Kirow_247_N`, `S`) show a drum about Ø 41.5 × 18 under the chain, too fine to tell that ring from the later drum turned over. The maintaining spring stays the revised one. Not built: *the lower balance cock's other shape* (Unresolved).

**X2, the dial (16 October 2026).** Kirov No. 247's dial (`dg-chrono-6mx/1_Kirow_247_Z.jpg`), traced as the two German dials are (`dialtrace.py --dial kirov`, into `js/dialface_kirov.js`, `WDIALFACE_KIROV`; the Variants' dial switch, *1st Moscow Watch Factory*, which choosing the 6MX selects):
- *Its layout is the drawings'*: the six printed circles fitted by one homography to 10-25 µm, the sub-dials' centres on the arbors at (0, ±23), measure Ø 89.96, 84.91 (the minute track's lines), 26.18 (the up-and-down circle), 37.13, 33.01 (the seconds track's), 22.79 (the seconds' inner circle), the sheet's 90, 85, 26, 37, 33, 23. Printed thin, as Lange 5177's (blur sigma 0.031 mm), its up-and-down sub-dial laid out as Lange's: the circle open at the top (338.3-22.6 deg) for СПУСК (run down) and ЗАВОД (wound) along it, the band's end strokes at 0 and 56 running in to r 5.33. Every figure of the geometric printing is `dial.js`'s `KIROV`, from `--dial kirov --geo`.
- *Traced:* the hours, the up-and-down figures 0-56, СПУСК and ЗАВОД, the seconds figures, the factory's mark 1МЧЗ and ИМЕНИ КИРОВА across the centre, and the serial 247, written in red ink: traced from red alone (red over the other channels, each stroke half way to its own core: the pen's strokes 0.40-0.45 wide, the 2's lighter), drawn #ff617f (the photograph's 165, 59, 79 on its paper's 149, 146, 145, scaled to the model's paper). Where the hands cover the printing: ИМЕНИ's last И (its lower corner under the minute hand) is its first, the same glyph; under the seconds hand's head and stem, the 4's upright (it shows at x 1.59 from its top, z 16.85, to the crossbar) is drawn on down to the line's foot (z 19.25) and the 7's bar (it shows at z 15.46 from x 3.95 to its corner) on 0.4 under the head, straight pen strokes 0.42 wide (estimated; until 16 October 2026 they were lost and the serial read 241); the brass hands' boss and their shadows are cut from the ink, not filled in.
- *Its screws at the 19, 39 and 59 minute marks* (Altmeppen's list): 112.5, 233.6 and 353.1 deg on the photograph (±1 for its tilt), drawn at 114, 234 and 354, the German dial's 120 and 240 moved; the movement ring's tapped holes for them move with them (both built per version).

**X2, the finish (16 October 2026).** *Geneva stripes* on the plates, cocks and bridges (Kirov 247's, 1952; the Wempe's are hammered, drawn plain gilt): straight, 3.95 mm apart (an FFT on plain patches of its top plate, 3.73-4.28 at 8.98 px/mm, and its dial side, 3.71-4.35 at 8.63: the median), running at 50 deg in each face's own photograph (49.8-50.3 and 48.5-52.1): in plan along (-0.643, 0.766) over the top plate, cocks and barrel bridge as one sweep (its stripes run on across them), and along (0.643, 0.766) over the pillar plate's dial face and the intermediate bridge (each plate striped from its own face). Across a stripe the photograph's brightness, folded on the pitch over two patches and averaged (each to its brightest), falls slowly from 1 to 0.61 and rises quickly back: the gilt material's map (frame.js's `G6`), on `solidGeo`'s plan UVs. Likely: the profile is the photograph's light; the faces it doesn't show (the plates' inner faces) take the same. Altmeppen's "the finish changed in the late 1950s" is later than Kirov 247, which is already striped. The barrel and intermediate bridges are built per version for it, their pins with them.
