**X3a, the bowl and bezel (16 October 2026).** The 6MX's bowl isn't R-05 01 00-1 outside: no Ø 118 flange, but a threaded rim Ø 110.5 for a knurled bezel screwed onto it (Altmeppen's "the bowl and bezel brass", and Kirow 422's knurled bezel). Inside it is the drawings' (the bore, its round, the bottom, the recess and closing plate, the winding hole's seat): the movement is the drawings' and stands in it as drawn. Each reading and how sure:
- *The rim, Ø 110.5* (sure to ±1.2): on Kirov 247's dial photograph (`1_Kirow_247_Z.jpg`, in its bowl with the bezel off), scaled by `dialtrace.py`'s own fit of its printed circles (8.75 px/mm, 0.01-0.03 mm rms), 24 rays round the dial read the dial's edge at r 51.0-51.2, the movement ring's gold flange to r 53.0 (its 105.95) and the bowl's dark rim outside it to r 55.0-55.6. The rim stands a little under the dial's plane, so ±0.6 for the camera. Kirov 009, 1564, 2973, 22823, 23239 and Poljot 00127's dial photographs, all in their bowls, show the same narrow rim round the flange (by eye).
- *The outside profile* (likely in shape, its heights estimated): Kirow 422's bowl turned over (`dg-chrono-dec/11_Kirow_DEC_422_Gehäuse.jpg`, side on), its silhouette traced every 4 rows and scaled by its band (930 px) as that Ø 110.5 (8.42 px/mm). Body r 51.8 (51.6-52.2: smaller down the photograph, which lies farther from the camera), a cone to the foot, the foot read r 37.5 and taken as the drawn 36.25 (the camera, about 0.33 m off, makes the foot about 4 % larger than the band; the cone's radii are scaled down the same way, 0.962 at the foot to 0.987 at the body), a step and round out to the band (rows 448-472), the band up to the bezel's edge. The heights come from the photograph's rows, put on the movement's frame by two ends: the foot's face at the drawn YB − 65 (the floor where the cork ring's seat needs it) and the bezel's glass 0.65 over the hands. That is 0.1322 mm a row, 6.4 % more than the photograph's own vertical scale (8.05 px/mm at its widths and a 15° view). The close camera leaves that stretch unmeasured: the view tilts from 9° at the foot to over 20° at the bezel. So, from YB: the foot −65 to −62.4, the cone to −51.3, the body to −13.2, the round to the band by −10.0, the band to the bezel's edge at +0.55.
- *The threaded rim round the flange* (likely): Kirov 14917 with its bezel off (rAw2zmulpk8 0:29 and 2:52, 1080p) shows the thread running up to the gold flange round the dial, and its loose bezel threaded inside. Drawn flush with the flange's top (YB + 4.7), its counterbore r 53.05 down to the drawn seat at YB. A notch 1.8 wide at 12 o'clock takes the ring's radial pin (WN-202, its end at r 55.0), down to YB + 0.8, through the thin wall under the bezel (estimated). The thread isn't drawn.
- *The bezel* (likely in shape): traced on the same photograph. Its lower edge r 56.1 swells (a bell) to the knurl's tips, r 58.3 (Ø 116.6). The knurl runs over 42 rows, 5.5 mm by the stretch; it is diamond on the photographs, drawn as 360 straight teeth 0.4 deep. Then a plain lip rounds into the top face at YB + 13.5. The glass is thick and stands proud (`12_Kirow_DEC_422_Kasten_1.jpg`: a green edge over a narrow brass top): Ø 107 × 4.0, its top edge chamfered 0.8, its underside YB + 12.0, 0.65 over the hands' highest point (the minute hand's boss, 17.70 in the movement's frame), its top YB + 16.0, where the drawn bezel's top is. Inside (estimated: no photograph shows it): the glass's seat 1.5 deep, the sight bore r 52.1 over the dial's edge (51.8), and the shoulder at the rim's top bearing on the flange and the rim, which holds the movement down as the drawn bezel's recess does.
- *The brackets*: the drawn case brackets and locking bracket, their plates on the 6MX's narrower body (r 51.8). Kirov 14917's inner pivots (rAw2zmulpk8 0:29: the cone-pointed screw in the gimbal ring's middle, below the bowl's rim) work out about 15 mm under the dial's face (YB − 10 ± 3, the frame tilted 53° by the dial's ellipse), the drawn YB − 11.5. Kirow 422's brackets stand higher (Unresolved).
- *The winding hole's shutter*: Kirov 14917's foot (rAw2zmulpk8 0:12 and 0:36, the bowl turned over) has the turning closing plate in its recess, its centre screw and its round hole away from the winding hole: the drawings' (05 01 00-2), kept. The pin in a slot on Uy8A88_j-cA (5:21-5:25) is a later Polet's, not built.

**X3b, the boxes (16 October 2026).** Kirov No. 247's box and outer box (`dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/leben-des-6mx/13_`, `14_Kirow_247_Kasten_*.jpg`), built in the drawings' box's place (the gimbals, pivots and transit lock as drawn):
- *The box, 190 each way* (the TU's "190x190x190", 1984: the only figure; sure for the later 6MX, likely for 1952): orange lacquered wood (Kirov 247's sampled #dc6914 to #e07c3e, drawn at 0.7 of it as the mahogany is), in three tiers, as Kirov 1564's, 2973's, 22823's and 23239's (Kirov 422's and 479's, German movements finished in Moscow, have one glazed lid). The tiers measured on Kirov 22823's box, nearly square-on (`bilder-zum-6mx/10_Kirow_22823_Kasten.jpg`), as parts of its height at both edges: the body 97 (0.506, 0.514), the glazed lid 68 (0.354, 0.360), the top lid 25 (0.140, 0.127): likely. Kirov 247's own front puts its parting lines 72 apart by the hasp's width.
- *Its fittings* (likely, by Kirov 247's front, `14_`, scaled by the hasp, 53 wide: 22823's 215 of 773 px): two hasps with knurled screws (the head Ø 11 × 5 on the lower leaf, 6.4 under the line), across both parting lines; the factory's diamond badge on the glazed lid, 38.6 × 24, its centre 37.6 under the upper line, lettered "1 МЧЗ им. Кирова"; the number plate on the body, Ø 31, 40 under the lower line, "247"; brass strips 27 × 12 round each vertical corner at the bottom, 27 under the lower line, 25 under the upper and capping the top (22823's); a ring handle on each side (22823's).
- *Estimated*: the walls 10; the glass in the glazed lid's top under a frame; both lids hinged at the back, opened together (the page has one lid; `WCASE`'s `rev(v)` gives the page the version's lids); the bottom 93.5 under the pivots as drawn, the pivots 2.5 longer for the wider walls, the transit lock's bracket reaching the right wall, the key's shelf with legs 65 so its front edge and the key stand where the drawings' do.
- *The outer box* (estimated in size): of the same wood, felt-lined, a half-round notch in the body's front at the parting line into which a half-round tab on the lid's front closes, a turning hook on the tab over a brass catch under the notch, the number plate 247 on the lid, a leather strap on each side (Kirov 247's, `13_`; Poljot 08479's, `14_Poljot_08479_Kasten_komplett.jpg`). No photograph scales it: 250 each way and high round the box (felt 18 under and over, 8 at the sides, walls 12), the parting line at half, the notch r 48 (`13_`: 0.38 of the front's width). The red cloth bag it stands in (the TU's "чехол") isn't drawn.

**X4, the 6MX's physics (16 October 2026).** `tools/wempe_6mx.js` (with `wempe_inertia.py --rev 6mx`, which weighs its escapement's bodies on the page's solids in that version) works out what its differences change; the page keeps the drawings' swing and rate (the user's choice):
- *The jewels in chatons* change nothing the energy budget can see: the model's sources give oiled steel in ruby and in brass the same friction (μ 0.10-0.15, `physics.js`), so the train's efficiency is the drawings'. They tighten the pivots' shake (the jewel holes the pivot's and 0.025, estimated; the drawn bushes 0.035 and 0.05 loose), and so loosen the holes' tolerance a CNC must hold: ±82 µm against ±77 at the centre-intermediate mesh, ±60 against ±47 at the intermediate-seconds, ±35 against ±25 at the seconds-escape (the escape jewels' own shake 0.03).
- *The first-drawn balance staff and collar* (no hub screws): the balance's inertia 0.01 % less, a beat shorter by about 24 µs, 4.2 s a day fast before timing (4.4 until the bodies were weighed again for 3.06.02), which the timing weights take out as they do the drawings' balance's. The detent's inertia 2.6 % less and the escape arbor's 0.1 %: the swing (191.9-202.0° over the wind, since 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles) and the escapement's rate against 12 h are the drawings' to 0.1° and 0.01 s a day.

**X5, completing it (18 October 2026).** Every photograph in the 6MX gallery (`dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/`, thirteen more 6MXs), Kirov 14100's parts laid out (`chasy-ru-9985-kirova-14100/`) and Romanov's *Proektirovanie priborov vremeni* (1975, Figs. 95-101 and their parts lists) read for the model, each part then measured as below (the 1950s Kirovs first, Kirov 247 before the rest; the user's choice, 17 October 2026). Each reading and how sure:
- *The escape wheel's collet*: one straight cone with a square shoulder onto the arbor, no pipe: Ø 2.77 at the wheel to 1.70 at its end, 5.08 long on `8_Kirow_247_F.jpg` (18.3 px/mm by the wheel's Ø 14 tips and the pinion's Ø 2.97), 3.14 to 1.58 over 5.23 on Kirov-Poljot 24020's (26.4 px/mm): likely. Kirov 247's built; the first-drawn version keeps 05 04_7.1's cone and pipe.
- *The detent block's fixing*: not through hole 13 but by a slotted steel stud Ø 3.4 flush in the top plate's outer face, 2.2 S of it, between two steady pins 10.0 apart (`holemap.py --model wempe` on `2_Kirow_247_A.jpg`, six seeds, 8.993 px/mm, every residual under 0.57; `2_Kirow_009_A.jpg` by hand; each corrected by hole 12's 0.4 offset; the two agree to 0.3: sure to ±0.4). In the block's frame its pins stand 1.9 nearer its end than 05 04 01-4 puts them, the stud 2.1 nearer and 0.8 across from hole 13; the block itself stays where the escapement needs it (its holes drilled otherwise: Unresolved). The plate's holes per version (`WESC.HOLES.block6`). The near pin, 0.55 inside the end face, goes 1.2 into the block, clear above the detent foot's pins (estimated).
- *The adjusting slide and the stop screw* (on the loose block, `8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg`, 30.3 px/mm across, 24.5 along, tilted 30-36°): a gilt key 3.2 wide, flush in a notch across the corner of the base's plate face and free side, from the stud to the edge and 2.5 down the side, the stud through it; a large slotted screw in the base's free side 0.9 from its end and 3.3 deep, its head Ø 4.0-4.4 over the end face by 1.2, beside the detent's foot (every 6MX Feder photograph shows it): likely in kind. Kirov 14100's loose detent has a slotted foot, so the screw is most likely a stop for the foot's place along the block, the banking setting the lock; how the slide works isn't seen (Unresolved).
- *The block's upright*: no slit (the loose block's upright, its face toward the plate, is plain to its end with the banking screw's thread straight through it: likely), and a short taper at its root, widening 0.46 over its last 0.9 (about 27°, on that photograph only: estimate), where the model drew r 2 (estimated). The banking screw's head Ø 3.2 × 1.4 fits all three readings (3.3 loose, 3.1-3.4 on 247, 3.4 on 24020; the loose block's 3.8 came from the wrong scale).
- *The lower balance cock*: full depth only over its lug, the rest of the bar toward the screw and pins cut back to 3.3 deep (every 6MX side view of the detent, a stamped number on the deep part; on `8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`, the full depth taken as the drawn 10: 3.3, 3.0-3.9): likely; the step 1.5 past the lug's edge so the lug and the screw's hole stand on the full depth (estimated). Its plan stays the drawings', and its step is in question (the side views fix only a line across the plan; the loose piece once taken for it is the upper cock: Unresolved).
- *The movement ring's screws*: at 342.2°, 102.2° and 246.4° ±0.5 (both of Kirov 247's faces, `holemap.py --model wempe` at 8.89 and 8.57 px/mm; Kirov 009's and Poljot 20027's the same: sure), so 120°, 144° and 96° apart, where the drawings put 340, 100, 220; steel slotted heads Ø 6.1 ±0.2 × 2.0 (drawn 6.2) where the drawings' are brass 6.2 × 3.05. The ring's band and its tapped holes per version.
- *The bowl, re-measured*: its outside with a pinhole camera fitted in the meridian plane, scaled by the rim's Ø 110.5, on Kirov 2973's bowl near side on (residuals under 0.3 px) and Kirov 247's: the thread r 55.25 down 5.55 from the rim's top, a collar r 56.6 to 7.76, the body r 52.4 (until then 51.8) to 46.7 (247: 47.0), a shallow cone to the foot r 36.7 at 55.3, the foot on to the drawn floor (its end isn't seen): likely. A cone that high leaves no room for the drawings' bore: the inside follows the outside, the bore r 49 then a cone 3.4 inside the outer one to the recess's wall (estimated; the movement's lowest parts clear it by 3.4 and more).
- *The brackets, the high form* (Kirov 247's own bowl, `11_`, `12_Kirow_247_E-Kontakt_*.jpg`; Kirov 2973's the same; Kirow 422's too): each case bracket a flat plate 9.7 high (12.8 to 22.4 under the rim's top), its wings 27.5 either side of a block 8.7 wide which rises 1.8 over the rim's top, the pivot's hole 4.8 under its top (3.0 under the rim's top: ±2), the wings' slotted screws 16.8 from the block at 17.4 under, through the bowl's wall (the small holes in the bore of Kirov 247's and Kirov-Poljot 24020's bowls 16-21 under the rim are their ends: likely); the locking bracket an upright 11.7 wide, two screws in a vertical slot at 20.0 and 27.4 under, cranked out past the bezel to an L hook 3.8 over the rim's top, the pad the transit lock stops against (Romanov p. 204). So the 6MX's bowl, with its bezel, closing plate and movement, hangs 13.2 lower in the gimbals than the drawings' (case.js's `bowlS`, `DY6`; app.js adds the movement to it); the later Kirov-Poljot 24020's pivots stand 10.8 under the rim, as the drawings' nearly do.
- *The closing plate*: a flat disc, no boss (Kirov 14917's foot square on, rAw2zmulpk8 0:11.75; Kirow 422's; a modern one, moMvyx8eXfo 7:20: likely), its screw's head about Ø 8 on its face, the domed knob about Ø 6.5 at r 19 ±2 (the drawings' 25); its groove, holes and spring as the drawings' (the spring unseen on any 6MX: Estimated), the spring's end shortened into the thinner plate.
- *The outer box*: its front about 233 wide (±7 %, the inner box's 190 scaled on `13_Kirow_247_Kasten_1.jpg` and Poljot 08479's), the notch a circular segment, chord 102, depth 33 (until then a half-round r 48), the catch 51 under the parting line. The lid's tab reads larger than the notch (chord 105-111, 41-44 high) and may lap over the body's front: drawn filling it (Unresolved). The parting line's height and the straps' places are hidden by the bag (estimated as before).
- *The seconds wheel*: the gilt collet on its arbor under the wheel (`6_Kiro_247_S.jpg`) is the contact wheel's (below). Pillar 39 reads Ø 8.0 ±0.5, the drawn 8.1; its radius stays 37.3 (Estimated).
- *The electrical contact* (Kirov 247's: `leben-des-6mx/10_`-`12_Kirow_247_E-Kontakt_*.jpg`, `6_Kiro_247_S.jpg`, `2_Kirow_247_A.jpg` and `3_Kirow_247_uZ.jpg` against Kirov 1564's without one; Kirov 009's, 2973's, Poljot 08479's and Kirov-Poljot 24020's of the same design; Romanov 1975, p. 105's parts 52-69 and Figs. 100-101, p. 209; the user's choice, 17 October 2026, to build it). On the pillar plate's inner face by pillar 39, from 176.5° to 236°, a gilt base 58 (2 thick, its outer edge an arc r 45.7 past the plate's rim, cut round pillar 39; its inner edge from Fig. 100: estimate) screwed to the plate by two M1.7 screws 56 (above); on it, on black fibre 68 (1.8) and insulated by bushes 67 (their black flanges Ø 4.6 under the heads), two gilt blocks, 52 (+, 177.6-201°) and 57 (−, 209-231°), each held by two steel cheese-head screws Ø 3.1 × 2.6 (182.9°/193.3° and 214.6°/226.0°, r 43: the two steel heads seen under the top plate's edge are 57's); a gilt knurled contact disc 55 (Ø 11.9 × 1.2, 48 teeth) upright in its radial plane at 207.7° on a bracket on block 57, turnable to renew the contact (the user instruction); the lever 60 pressed on a staff 69 (206.2°, r 31.8 on Fig. 100 mapped, ±1.5; taken r 31.0, so the spiral's measured Ø 8.9 clears the disc) jewelled at both ends, a hole jewel and an end jewel in a setting in the base and in an L-shaped gilt cock over it under a cap plate, its stone 61 (a corundum pallet with a bevelled end) on the contact wheel 10.0 from the seconds arbor, its other arm (15.1) carrying the brass contact screw 54 with a lock nut, tangential at r 45.5, its point on the disc's low face; a flat copper-coloured spiral 59 (7 turns, Ø 8.9) from the staff's collet to a stud 53 on block 52, both the lead and the lever's return. The contact wheel 62 on the seconds arbor under its wheel (Romanov: fixed to it), on a gilt collet Ø 2.0 rising 3.0 above it (3.1 and 3.55 until 18 October 2026): 60 ratchet teeth, tips Ø 20.4 (likely), roots 18.6 (estimate), 8 spokes, one tooth left out. In the bowl, two flat brass springs 66 on black insulation, each held by two screws 65 through the wall, three tines bearing on a block's outer end; outside, their slotted brass heads and a brass socket for each lead, + at 200.6° and − at 235.9°, on black fibre washers, all 27.5 under the pillar plate's inner face (`12_Kirow_247_E-Kontakt_3.jpg`, a camera fitted on the band; the springs on `11_` and Fig. 101: likely; their sizes estimated, ±15 %). The heights from Fig. 101 (9.68 px/mm, which gives the seconds wheel's height to 0.06 and the 19 between the plates) and the S views (10.5 and 21 px/mm). Five more jewels, as the TU's 20 against 15 without the contact (`checks.py` JEWELS). It works as Romanov (p. 209) and the user instruction describe it: the seconds arbor turns 3° a beat, half a tooth; on one beat the wheel's sloping flank lifts the stone (the lever turns about 4°) and the screw leaves the disc, on the next the tooth's radial face lets it fall and the spiral closes it: open half a second, closed half a second, and at the missing tooth closed a second and a half, at the minute (train.js `update`, `R.c6`; which half-second is open isn't given: estimated). The parts the photographs show are where they show them; the stone and the staff's place, seen on no photograph, are Romanov's figure put on them (estimate). **Rebuilt to the photographs (18 October 2026)**, `10_Kirow_247_E-Kontakt_1.jpg` through photocompare.py's `m6_contact` camera (0.6 mm at the block screws) and `14_E-Kontakt1.jpg` (another 6MX of the same design, the `m6_contact_front` camera, about 1 mm): a millimetre scale stood at the staff, the seconds arbor and the screw's tip through the first gives the heights, and the two together give places across the view (they look along nearly the same line, 14° apart, so depth stays Fig. 100's). The cock's arm −12.9 to −14.2, 1.3 thick (−11 to −13.2 before), its leg run up to it; its cap plate three lobes, the two screws and the end jewel in a row 2.1 either side (along (−0.97, 0.24) as triangulated), the arm rounded r 1.6 past the far screw (a round plate with its screws 75° either side of the arm's axis before); the spiral at −11.7 to −12.3 just under the arm, its end run out over the stud and bent down onto its top beside the clamp screw (head Ø 2.2) (−8.5 to −9.1 before, from Fig. 101); the lever gilt (steel before), the contact screw Ø 1.0 threaded, at −5.85 (the photograph's −5.15 ±0.6 taken at the top of its range, to pass 0.1 over block 52's top), held in a gilt block under the lever's end with a brass lock nut 1.1 across; the disc's knurl square, its low face recessed Ø 6.0, 0.3 deep (estimated), a slotted steel axle screw (head Ø 2.6) in it, no raised boss. Left as drawn: the cock's leg renders about 2.5 further from the disc across the view than both photographs show it, within Fig. 100's ±2 but at its edge; moving it needs the arm's root and foot, whose depth no photograph fixes (Unresolved). The fine check required: the stone's end narrowed by its bevel (0.3 at its point), the stud's outer face at r 44.0, one block screw 0.15 off pillar 39 and the cock's screw and pins placed clear of its leg (each within its reading). The base overhangs the pillar plate's window for the intermediate wheel (0.23 over it), so in the Exploded view the 6MX's intermediate wheel leaves toward the dial after its bridge, as a watchmaker takes it out, the lever, spiral and cock lift clear of the contact wheel first, and its seconds wheel rises 30.6.
- *The page's physics* (the user's choice, 17 October 2026): the 6MX runs its own escapement, `WPHYS.rev('6mx')` with its weighed bodies (`WESC.SET6`, checked by `tools/wempe_6mx.js` against `wempe_inertia.py --rev 6mx`), its swing table and its 35-day trial (`WEDATA.rev6`, `wempe_essaydata.js`, its children run as the 6MX: `wempe_load.js`'s `MC_WREV`), in the Swing and isochronism and the Rate and trial panels and in the export to Blender. With the contact wheel weighed on its seconds arbor (the escape arbor's body 10.89 g·mm² against the drawings' 9.96), its swing is 1.9° under the drawings' over the whole wind (190.1-200.1° against 191.9-202.0°: `tools/wempe_6mx.json`), its rate against 12 h theirs to 0.01 s a day; the mainspring is the drawings' (the 1958 Navy repair book's 1.01-1.08 N·m fully wound is the first generation's MX, which a strip about 0.42 thick would give, but no 1950s source gives the 6MX's strip: Unresolved).

- *The contact revised (24 October 2026).* The lever's boss sits on the staff just under the spiral's collet (−10.2 to −11.5), not at the screw's height: on Kirov 2973 (`14_E-Kontakt1.jpg`) and Kirov-Poljot 24020 (`12_KirPol_24020_E-Kontakt2.jpg`, 16.7 px/mm by the cock's arm) the boss is 0.1-0.3 under the collet, the staff bare steel under it, and the arm runs down from it to the screw's holder on block 52 (likely; Kirov 247's own lever is hidden behind the stud and holder). Both arms are drawn sloping from the boss to their ends at the old heights (−6.1 to −7.4); the stone arm's slope is estimated, since no photograph shows it. The staff is Ø 0.6 under the boss (8-11 px against the block screws' Ø 3.1 at 23-26 px/mm, a little further off: Ø 0.4-0.6) and Ø 1.0 above it. Until then the boss and both arms were flat at −6.1 to −7.4, 4.5 under the spiral, on a staff Ø 1.0 the whole way. The cock (its foot, leg, arm, cap plate and screws) was compared again on 2973's, 24020's and 247's photographs and left as it is. The block's stud has a real slot, 0.5 wide and 0.9 deep, square across the base (the loose block, `8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg`, 30.3 px/mm: 15-17 px across, running about 12 px down the stud's side; likely), in place of the kit's 0.35 × 0.55 at an arbitrary heading. The 6MX's block has no Ø 1.3 hole between its near steady pin and the stud (25 October 2026, the owner's choice): 05 04 01-4 drills one there from the plate's face to the detent foot's M1, and the German block in `10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg` shows it, but the 6MX's face is plain there on that photograph and on `8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg` (at about 30 px/mm it would be 40 px across: sure). The drawn versions keep it. The 6MX face's dot between the stud and the far pin (about Ø 0.3, a small hole or a punch mark) isn't drawn. The whole contact (on the pillar plate, on the seconds arbor and in the bowl) is hidden on the page unless Variants' Electrical contact is set to Shown (`contact=1` in the link; the owner's choice, 24 October 2026); under `?qa` it is shown, so every tool weighs and checks it as before, and the physics is the same either way.

**The sheet, Soviet 6MX against the drawings (18 October 2026).** The panel's section after Variants lists every difference the 6MX version builds (app.js's `X6`: 22, grouped as frame, escapement, barrel, electrical contact, ring and dial, case and boxes), each with what the drawings have, what the 6MX has and how sure the reading is, as this section gives them. Choosing one turns the model to the 6MX and closes in on it, the rest see-through (`VIEWER.focusOn`, `shared/viewer.js`: the parts' box, or a place where a part's own box would lose the change, as for pillar 39, the hand-setting holes, hole 53 and the moved ring and dial screws); Drawings and Soviet 6MX flip the version where the camera stands; a view chosen, Reset, Back or Whole model ends it.

## The escapement of 1943 as photographed (17 October 2026)

The Variants' fourth version, *Escapement of 1943* (`rev=1943` in the link; `movement.js`'s `setRev('1943')`): the sheets as last revised but for the escapement, which stands as the German movements photographed with the 1943 detent block (05 04 01-4, 8.7.43) are made: Kirow DEC 422 and 479 (Lange rough movements finished in Moscow) and A. Lange & Söhne No. 5177 (`photos/dg-chrono-dec/8_*_Feder.jpg`). Their escape wheels hang just under the top plate, as 05 04_1 draws it, so the 1946 staff's heights (which no photograph shows) give way to the first-drawn balance, rollers, escape wheel and their jewels (`'first 6mx 1943'`), the 6MX's detent at the block's heights with 1436's bracketed sizes, the escape wheel's upper endstone setting gilt with brass screws (`'first 1943'`, `'last first 1943'`), and a block of its own with the banking screw drawn whole, as the user chose on 16-17 October 2026 (the banking screw is the Wempe's "adjusting screw": it sets the depth of lock). The rest is the revised sheets' (`'last first 1943'`, `'last 6mx 1943'`, the revised barrel `'last 1943'`), and the swing and rate are the drawings' (the 6MX alone runs its own escapement: The Soviet 6MX, X5).

Each reading below was taken on the three photographs, scaled by the upright's 3 beside the head (DEC 479 19 px/mm, its detent's blade 0.9 agreeing; 5177 16.5; DEC 422 17.7). Each is a ratio on the upright's face, so the camera, 30-40° off the screw's axis, scarcely changes it:
- *The detent's sizes*: 1436's bracketed ones, as the 6MX's (likely). The 1943 block clamps the foot's face 0.30 off the foot's and spring's centre plane (0.19 off the stone's line, the blade flush toward the block: Readings corrected), the bracketed foot's half (0.60); the unbracketed 0.65 would stand 0.025 into it. So the bracketed sizes came with the block.
- *The upright's slit runs parallel to the plate* (sure: all three). Its dark line, 0.3 (5 px), runs along the face the screw comes out of, with the screw in it, so the slit's two leaves grip the thread from above and below. The sheet draws it the other way: its front view has the slot 0.3 wide from the upright's end and the M1.2 dashed across it, and its side view shows the hole end-on and the slot's floor dashed across the upright. The photographs win; the sheet's 0.3 wide and 7 long are kept (Unresolved: the length).
- *The screw's axis* lies 0.15 under the upright's middle: 0.1-0.25 on DEC 479 and 422, 0 on 5177 (likely). So 5.15 under the plate, the slit with it. Its hole is the M1.2's along the slit's line, drawn r 0.62 round the shank's 0.6.
- *The head*, brass and slotted, is Ø 3.4: 1.19, 1.20 and 1.06 times the upright's 3, so 3.55, 3.6 and 3.15 (likely, ±0.2). Its top is level with the upright's top or up to 0.24 over it, which the drawn one (0.05 over) agrees with.
  - A cylinder 1.1 long: 5177's side band, about 40° off the axis (its face's ellipse 22 by 29 px).
  - A dome rising 0.4 on it, estimated: DEC 479's and 422's light shows a dome, but none shows its rise. So 1.5 in all, as the 6MX's 1.4-1.6.
  - The slot 0.4 wide (0.3-0.45) and 0.5 deep, along the detent as on DEC 422; the other two have it turned.
- *Its underside* bears on the detent's pipe, as the 6MX's does: 2.28 from the upright's near face (Unresolved: the photographs read more).
- *Its length*, 8.2 under the head, is the 6MX's loose block's (`8_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben_Rus_einfach.jpg`, the only screw seen whole). Its threaded end then stands 2.9 past the upright's far face (estimated: no German photograph shows that side). The thread is drawn as rings at the M1.2's 0.25 pitch, as the kit's screws are. Its own line in `bom.json`, `WN-gkA43` (`"rev": "1943"`), is tapped into the block (`tap`), where the other versions' cross screw, drawn without its hole, is `embed`.
- *Built*: the block's base, holes and fillet are the 6MX's. Its slit end, 7.5 long, is extruded through the upright's width with the slit and the hole in its profile, and made one mesh with the rest, so no seam shows. The slot is a dark bar 0.01 over the dome, as the kit's screws' slots stand over their heads.
- *Checked* (17 October 2026):
  - `isolate.py detentBlock,detent,escapeWheel --model wempe --eval "__mv.userData.setRev('1943')"` beside the three photographs. They agree in the head's size against the upright, its slot, its axis on the slit and the slit's line. They differ in the detent's level and the exposed shank (Unresolved).
  - `audit.py --rev 1943`: every coplanar face is one the last-revised or 6MX audit already lists, with the same count, none new from the block; recorded as `movement.1943`.
  - `audit.py box --rev 1943`: the drawings' box's (`box.1943`).
- *Its physics* (27 October 2026; `tools/wempe_1943.js`, the report's section 16, "The escapement of 1943 against the drawings"): its plan is the drawings' (the banking screw sets 05 04_1's depth of lock), so, as for the 6MX, only its bodies change, weighed on the page's solids in that version (`wempe_inertia.py --rev 1943`, `tools/wempe_inertia_1943.json`): the balance 2575.1 g·mm² (2575.4 as last revised: its first-drawn staff and rollers weigh the same within 0.3; the 6MX's 2521.0 is its own balance), the escape arbor with the train behind it 9.54 (9.53), the detent 8.01 (8.23: the 6MX's detent). Through makeEsc: lock, let-off, overall and drop as last revised (12.88°, 1.50°, 23.72°, −4.23°), the swing 193.2-203.9° over the wind (192.4-202.3° as last revised), the rate against 12 h within 0.2 s a day of the drawings', 3.9 s a day fast before timing, the stone 0.01 µm into a tooth at most. `dynamics.js --model wempe` in time on the same bodies (`MC_ESC='{"I":2575.1,"JW":9.54,"ID":8.01}'`): 300 beats without a tooth lost or gained, the swing 198.7° against makeEsc's 197.6°, the escapement's own rate −11.26 s a day against −11.41 (its check that makeEsc's bodies are the last revised weighed ones fails by design). `tripping.js --model wempe` isn't run on it: its frame is built from the last revised detent's pieces, not the 6MX's. The page runs the drawings' escapement in this version, as before.

## Photographs against the model, in the same view (18 October 2026)

`tools/photocompare.py --model wempe SPEC.json` fits a photograph's camera to model points picked on it (screw heads, a plate's rim snapped along its arcs), renders the model through that camera at the photograph's size and lays the two side by side, blended and outlined (each part's outline, from a pass with every part flat in its own colour, drawn on the photograph); `--regions` crops a box of the three, `--what x,y` names the part drawn at a pixel, `"only"` renders some parts alone. The specs are in `chronometer-working-model/tools/photocompare/`: Wempe 3955's seven views (`w3955_A`, `_N`, `_O`, `_S`, `_W`, `_uZ`, `_Z`) and Kirov No. 247's (`k247_*`), most picks fitting to 0.1-0.6 mm, and Kirov 247's detent, contact and loose parts (`m6_*`). The findings, what was changed and what is still open, are in `Review-results.md` ("Photographs against the model"). A photograph is the measurement: every change below names it and its fitted scale. No photograph goes on the page: the finishes are synthesised from measured statistics.

## Wempe 3955 as photographed (18 October 2026)

The Variants' fifth version, *Wempe 3955 (as photographed)* (`rev=3955`; `movement.js`'s `setRev('3955')`): the first-drawn sheets' pieces, but where Wempe No. 3955's photographs (Altmeppen's, `dg-chrono-dec/`) show another form than every sheet, that form; *As first drawn* stays the drawings' own, the fallback (the user's choice, 18 October 2026). A group listed `first` shows in both, `3955` in the photographed one only and `first -3955` in the drawn one only (`revShows`). Its cards are first's (`REV3955`, `REVP3955` in app.js add to them).
- *The dust bush* (`4_Wempe_3955_N.jpg`, 10.6 px/mm on the bush, its silhouette traced on a 5 px grid and at full size beside the model's outline): the sheet's (05 08 01-3, 2.7.42) but turned to a waist: the Ø 13.1 body to a flat shoulder at y -29.0 (±0.3), then Ø 9.0 (±0.4) up to a collar 1.85 thick (±0.2; the sheet's 3.1), its plan with the flat and the neck the sheet's. Kirov No. 247's is waisted too (below).
- *No cork ring*: its N, O and A photographs show the neck bare (photographed out of the bowl: the cork may have perished or stayed in the case's seat).
- *The fusee's square* 4.5 shorter: its point shows inside the dust bush's mouth, its top at y -43.5 (±0.5), not 6 proud of the bush as 05 06 04-1's 28.3 puts it (-48.0); the O, S and W views show nothing above the mouth.
- *The set-up ratchet's face*: a turned recess round the square, Ø 8.7 at the face to Ø 7.3 at its floor (`2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg` square-on, 0.111 mm/px), 0.7 deep, its wall taken at 45° (estimated); 05 07 00-3 (24.11.47) draws it flat.
- *The escape wheel's collet*: one straight brass cone, Ø 2.95 at the wheel to Ø 1.50 at its end 4.9 below it, no step and no pipe (`9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`, the `w3955_hem_1` camera, 12.98 px/mm along the arbor and 14.1 across; both edges at the brass's saturation's mid-level on 47 rows, a line through them 0.04 mm rms with no curvature: sure). 05 04_7.1 (4.4.41) draws a cone Ø 3.0 to 2.2 over 1.8, then a Ø 1.8 pipe to 5, kept in *As first drawn*. The 6MX's (Kirov 247) is a straight cone too, Ø 2.77 to 1.70 over 5.08.
- *The impulse roller* bright and neutral, as the steel arm (`9_Wempe_3955_Hemmung.jpg`: its face's saturation 0.00-0.02 where the drawn beryllium copper renders 0.49-0.81): drawn steel, though a plated roller would look the same (likely). Its layout is the sheet's: with the balance turned the right way (the `w3955_hem_2` spec's note) the impulse stone and the roller's hole stand where the photograph's do, and the brass discharge block on it where the model's is.
- *The masses' tops plain*, no tapped hole (`5_Wempe_3955_O.jpg`, `6_Wempe_3955_S.jpg`; the drawings' versions keep 05 03_2's M1.2), and *poising holes* in two masses, those at 150° and 210° (on one side of the cuts' line), each drilled Ø 0.6 ±0.15 in its roller-side face inside the rim: at 210° 1.45 in from the mass's centre and 1.1 across, at 150° 1.15 in and 0.2 across (±0.4; the holes unprojected on the masses' face through `w3955_hem_2`'s camera); the other two masses plain where such a hole would show (likely). Their depth is unseen, 1.0 (estimated).
- *The maintaining detent's wire stud* brass, head and shank (`2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg` from above, `5_Wempe_3955_O.jpg` from the side: brass coloured and slotted beside the steel pivot in hole 4's sink; likely); steel in the drawings' versions, as the coloured section colours it and Kirow 247's and Lange 5177's are. The sink beside it is concave, as built (its far wall lit and the pivot at the near rim's line on `5_`).
- *The bowl's bottom*: the closing plate the sheet's (05 01 00-2: on `16_Wempe_3955_Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`, its ellipse 0.80 and its Ø 68 scaling it at 6.65 px/mm, the Ø 8 at 23.7 from the centre, the knob at 25.3, 90° apart, the boss Ø 31, the screw's head about Ø 8), but its face a matte neutral grey (saturation 0.03, value 0.49 against the background's 0.82: blackened or lacquered), two small holes through its boss on the knob's line (12.2 on the knob's side, the spring's Ø 1.2 drilled through; Ø 1 at 10.6 opposite), and the bowl's bottom and winding-hole seat Bakelite, dark through the plate's hole (likely). The drum's widest part measures r 52-54 on the same scale (the sheet's 53); its rounded shoulder, which the sheet draws as a 40° cone, can't be read closer than about 2 mm on this one oblique view: the sheet's profile kept.
- *The hand-setting's notch and groove*: the pillar plate's rim notched where the cut's bore leaves it, a flat 0.33 deep between ends 8.3 apart, the bore's mouth in its face; under it a groove across the movement ring's band for the hand-setting's shaft, straight-sided to 2.3 under the plate's inner face and round below (r 4.565 about an axis 1.54 under the face, the bore's 1.67 within the reading), 9.0 wide, its floor 0.25 above the band's underside, so closed in the band; it passes under the lip's drawn cone and opens the lip at its inner edge, 5.0 wide to r 42.25 (`8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg` through `w3955_train`'s camera, refitted on ten of the plate's holes to 0.07-0.27 mm: the edges unprojected on the band's top, the sides on their planes, the profile on the band's outside; `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`: the lip opened 4.4 wide, the plate's rim seen to r 42.17: likely). On no sheet. Until 18 October 2026 read as 4.5 long and 0.9 deep on `3_`, where the ring's lip edge had been taken for the plate's rim, and not built. The bore itself isn't cut.
- *The detent block's arm* (`11_Wempe_3955_Gangfeder_plus.jpg`, `10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg`): its slit shorter than 05 04_1's, open at the end as the sheet's, to 5.1 from it, 0.14 wide (11_ along the near face at 31 px/mm to 5.0, 10_Vergleich to 5.3; likely; the line's start 0.15-0.35 in, read as a bridge at first, is the end's bright edge: the user's reading, 18 October 2026). Its banking screw 0.55 longer, head face to point: loose, its head's face stands 1.4-1.55 off the arm, its point 2.4-2.55 out of the far face and the pipe 0.47 clear of the head, so turned out about two turns; drawn turned in to bank, the pipe on the head, its point 3.02 out (the user's choice, 18 October 2026: the movement running).
- In *As first drawn* as well (the photograph agreeing with an earlier sheet the model hadn't used): the barrel arbor's square end is 020704-1's (1941), 15 past the upper journal's shoulder to the turning centre's cone; Wempe 3955's tip at -37.6 ±0.3, the sheet's -37.32. Until 18 October 2026 every version had the 1943 arbor's 10 and a 0.5 point.
- *Not changed, the balance's rest*: every whole-movement view (A, N, O, S, W) has the arm about 3-10° off where 05 03_1 sets it at rest (6-12); a stopped balance may stand where a hand left it, so the photographs can't give its rest, and the version keeps the sheet's (the user's choice, 18 October 2026).

**Every version** (the photographs of both specimens, where no sheet gives the form or the sheet agrees):
- *Screw slots* cut into the heads (`shared/kit.js`: a closed solid, the chamfer's ring with its own normals); until 18 October 2026 a grey box stood 0.02 proud across each head.
- *The detent block's fixing screw* (WN-gk) a tall cheese head 2.4 (`6_Wempe_3955_S.jpg`; 1.2 read on `11_` until then).
- *The gold spring's screw* (WN-14) bright polished steel, slotted, its head rounded, Ø 1.4 (`11_Wempe_3955_Gangfeder_plus.jpg` through `w3955_detentBlock`'s camera, 1.44; `10_Wempe_3955_Feder_plus.jpg` face-on, 0.7 of the square's 2: likely); until 18 October 2026 blued, plain, Ø 1.2. No sheet of it is in the set. The detent block's fixing screw photographs black on its top face (`11_`, `15_`: value 0.09, hue 26-38°, not blue) and grey on its turned side: a flat mirror polish reflecting the dark, as drawn, not a temper.
- *The maintaining detent and maintaining spring* tempered light blue at 310° (their sheets' "310° hell-blau anlassen"; both specimens photograph pale grey), no longer deep blue.
- *The finishes*: the plates and bridges hammered gilt, synthesised from `2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg`'s plain patches (brightness 8.8-14.1 % sd about the local mean, mean wavelength 1.76-1.84 mm, weakly oriented, bands along x), on their faces only (walls plain, the 6MX's stripes too); the gilt richer (saturation 0.59-0.73 in the photographs against 0.34-0.51 rendered); the page's earlier plain gilt kept as the Variants' *Finish*, Plain brass, the default again since 25 October 2026 at the owner's choice (the hammered gilt `fin=hammered` in the link; the top plate's engraving, Gerhard D. WEMPE Hamburg, left off since then too; `movement.js`'s `M.setFinish`: the core's gilt, 0xcaa45a at 0.34, on every gilt part, the plates and bridges smooth, the 6MX's stripes in that colour; the export to Blender takes the finish shown); the cork ring brown (Kirov 247's, not cream); the Goldin hands 0.35 rough (at 0.18 they mirrored the page's dark sky).

**The 6MX, from Kirov No. 247's photographs** (X6, 18 October 2026): its balance's cap jewel setting polished steel round a ruby (`2_Kirow_247_A.jpg`, the `k247_A` camera: the outline on the model's; likely; the drawings' gilt round Wempe 3955's white stone); its up-and-down wheel's spokes tapering 1.82 at the hub to 1.18 at the rim (`3_3_Auf-_und_Ab_rus.jpg` rectified by the rim's inside, the five spokes read from r 8 to 13.6: likely; the drawings' 1.6 straight); its escape pinion 3.9 long, its foot 1.5 over the pillar plate (`8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`, measured along the arbor from the escape wheel by the wheel's Ø 14, 17.65 px/mm: likely ±0.2; the drawn 3, 3.3 over it), the train's inertia at the escape wheel 0.2 % more (`wempe_inertia_6mx.json`, `WESC.SET6` within it); its banking screw's thread cut and its dome slotted (`8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`: the thread showing past the upright where the pipe rests on the head, 2.4 against the model's 2.28; the slot across the dome; likely); its top plate and barrel bridge screws polished steel with domed heads (2.4 overall, the dome 0.8, estimated ±0.3), its upper cock's, lower cock's (Ø 5.4), cap jewels', dial-side pillar and gold spring screws polished steel, its intermediate bridge's blued, its change pinion steel; its movement ring gilt; its dust bush waisted (the body 4.8, a flare, the waist Ø 9.8, the collar 1.2) under a cork 2.4 thick; its barrel arbor's square longer and pointed (sides to -36.6, tip -38.5, ±0.4) and its set-up ratchet recessed as Wempe 3955's; its detent block's stud screw standing 2.5 (±0.7) proud; its dial screws on r 49.9 (0.07-0.24 mm on `1_Kirow_247_Z.jpg` against 0.61-0.68 at 50.5).

**Each subassembly in its own version (27 October 2026).** Under the Version buttons, the Variants list the movement's subassemblies, each with the versions that show it differently, so one can be changed while the rest stay as they are (the owner's request: the detent alone, not the whole of As first drawn). The subassemblies (`movement.js`'s `SUBS`, the owner's choice of coherent ones): the escapement (balance, hairspring, rollers, escape wheel, detent and its block, their jewels, the cocks and the dust bush, which the sheets draw to each other's heights, so they change together), the fusee and maintaining work, the barrel and mainspring, the going train, the dial and motion work, the plates, bridges and pillars, the case and gimbals, and the key. Each version's group takes its subassembly from where it is built (`userData.sub`) or else from its part (`PSUB`); each builder's `onRev` its own (`f.sub`). `setRev` takes a version (a preset: the Version buttons, every tool's states) or a mix (`{esc: 'first'}`, the others as last revised); `mv.userData.rev` is the version they all show, or `'mix'`, `mv.userData.mix` each one's. The link keeps a mix as `rev=esc:first,dial:6mx`. A row's buttons are the versions that differ for it, in its pieces or in its parts' cards (`subOpts`): versions shown alike share a button, titled with them all ("Drawings" where both states of the sheets are alike). The cards follow each part's subassembly. `TIES` holds the 6MX's escapement and its dial to its plates (the top plate is drilled for its detent block and carries its horn; `fine.py` found the 6MX's dial and the drawings' ring, either way round, 1.0 mm³ and 2.0 deep into each other at the dial's fixing): the subassembly just chosen settles its ties first, each subassembly once. The 6MX's electrical contact shows only on its own pillar plate. Checked by `fine.py --model wempe` on 31 mixes, each one subassembly away from As last revised, As first drawn, Wempe 3955 or the 6MX (every one clean after the dial's tie), and `audit.py` (movement and box) on nine, which found nothing a whole version doesn't (27 October 2026).

## Estimated, not from the drawings

- **The navigator's voyage** (Navigating by it, `wempe_navigation.js`): the route, from 45° N in port to 15° N by day 20 where the box reaches 85 °F, is estimated (no source gives one; the latitude only scales miles of longitude by its cosine, and the equator's figures are given beside it). Bowditch's probable error of a fix (±2 miles) is taken as a circular normal whose median radius is 2 miles (σ 1.70 a side); Bowditch gives the figure, not its distribution.
- **The detent's knock margin**: since 19 October 2026's physics pass, with the estimated 0.8 g preload, a 3 ms blow across the plate of 25.6 g lets a tooth go on the frame as drawn (22.6 g at 0.8 times, 28.7 g at 1.2 times; the knock's floor under 0.64 g, below the margin's 0.78 g, which the bench takes). Before it (18 October 2026), with the estimated 0.63 g preload, a 3 ms blow across the plate of 20.4 g lets a tooth go on the frame as drawn (20.0-20.1 g before the horn was traced) (`tripping.js --model wempe`, its least-knock search refined from six halvings to ten: the coarse one read 22 g). The check asks 20 g or more, so the margin is a hair at the estimate. The model keeps the estimate; the bench takes the knock's floor, 0.61 g, as a second least beside the margin's 0.51 (the user's choice), its window 0.61-1.55 g aimed near 0.84 g, where the 3 ms blow wants about 25 g (the line through the frame's runs carried on past 1.2 times: estimated).
- **The key holder** (case.js, `Schluesselhalter`, `Schluesselhalter.F`; 18 October 2026): the box's plan, 127-556. A-1 (51-00_1, 1:2; scaled 1.24-1.25 px/mm by the box's 185 and the gimbal ring's Ø 146.6), draws it: a triangle in the back right corner, its legs 22.3 along the two lines it is drawn against (2-4 mm inside the box's inner faces as the box's centre places them, taken as the walls), and on its long edge a round fitting, the circle fitted on 8 rim points (0.4 px rms): Ø 19.5 ±1, its centre 10.35 from each of those lines (13.4-14.5 from the inner faces by the box's centre) and 1.1 inside the long edge, with two dots on the box's diagonal 11.9 apart (about Ø 2.4). The shelf's front edge (the owner, 24 October 2026) is a concave arc following the gimbal ring, as Wempe 6161's (`antiquorum_208-4_wempe6161`) and Wempe 3044's (`milivault_529241_wempe3044_screen`) boxes show it, drawn about the box's centre at r 82.5 so the Ø 19.5 cup stands wholly on the wood, 1 inside it (legs 59.8 along the walls), the key's tube through a hole r 6, 10 thick; until then the plan's straight edge 1.1 beyond the key, which left the cup half over nothing and the key's tube in an open notch. **Estimated:** the cup's height 5 and black finish (Lange 2285's; the drawing gives neither), its Ø 11 bore (the key's cone seats on its top edge, 65 over the floor, so the 1943 key's paddle stands 20.7 over the box's top as before: on the wartime Wempe, `rmg_L5393_wempe_wartime_001`, 24 ±6, 20 ±8 read apart); the screws 14 apart with heads Ø 2.2 on Ø 1.2 shanks, both into the shelf (the dots' 11.9 and Ø 2.4 would put the heads over the key's bore, so they are built as far out as the reading's ±1 allows), the shelf's thickness, its front arc's radius and centre (3044's shelf scales about 45-50 along the walls, ±15 %, against the model's 59.8); that the drawn corner lines are the walls. The 6MX's shelf (legs 65, its hole at the same place) stands 15-30 under the rim with the paddle above (moMvyx8eXfo 3:06, a 2022 box, empty; 12_Kirow_DEC_422_Kasten_1); Wempe 5377's, in a later case, under it. Of the box's wood.

- **The threads' form** (26 October 2026): the sheets give each thread's size (M2.6, M2, M1.7, M1.4, M1.2), not its form or fit, and no photograph in the set shows a loose screw's thread. Every screw's shank is drawn as a right-hand ISO metric thread at its size's coarse pitch (ISO 261/262, DIN 13; M1.7 0.35, M2.6 0.45: `KIT.isoP`), ISO 68-1's basic profile (60°, the depth 0.541 p, flats p/8 and p/4), at 6g's maximum material (its major diameter less es = 15 + 11p µm: an allowance of 7.5 + 5.5p µm on the radius), run out to its root over the pitch before its 45° tip. A size the sources leave estimated keeps its estimate (the case's M3-M6, the 6MX's contact's). The tapped holes of the frame (the plates', bridges', cocks' and bushes' tables, the pillars' ends, the detent block's M1.7 and the wire spring's stud's hole 5) carry the inside thread at 6H's minimum (the basic profile), running out over a pitch to the drilled hole at each face, so screw and hole are the allowance apart radially all round; each screw is turned on its axis to lie on its hole's helix (`KIT.matchThreads`: 43 screws in all the versions). Other tapped holes (the movement ring's, the dial screws' in its flange, the fusee's, the balance's masses', the case's and the 6MX's contact's) stay plain bores at the thread's size and 0.02, the screws in them threaded. Standard practice, not read off a Wempe: sure of the sizes, estimated in form and fit.

  - the chain's pin's angle along the flange (0.17 rad behind the groove's start; its radius, 1 under the ribs' tops, is the sheet's) and the hooks' throats round it;

Anything from 0.9 (its pin's Ø 0.5 hole through it, 0.2 of strip either side) to 1.5 (under the recess's 1.6, 0.1 off its floor) works: its stress is the same at any height, 750 MPa wound against hardened spring steel's 1,200-1,800, and its push, linear in the height, is 0.054-0.12 N half way over that range, 37 times or more the 0.5 g bar's sliding on the cover plate (wempe_strength.js); the bench's stop work test (wempe_bench.js) has a maker check the bar springs back and the stop holds full wind. Its thickness, 0.10, worked from its bending (the stop work), and its shape between the two drawn; its pin's height, mid-spring; the bar 0.02 off the chain where it presses it; the stop pin's fit and its outer end flush with the plate (its length is scaled off the coloured section, 1.85 ±0.1: the stop work; anything from 1.24, to bear on the bar's whole thin end, to about 6, short of the chain on the fusee's fourth turn, would work: a strong hand's 1.06 N·m on the key, Swain 1970 as overwind.js takes it, puts 76 N on it 0.7 out, 134 MPa);

- **The escapement (P1d)**:
  - The locking stone's place, 7.14 from the wheel at 37.0° from the line of centres (0.235 deep): scaled on 05 04_1 against the tips' Ø 14.00 (likely, ±0.04 and ±0.3°; 7.2 and 36.5° until 19 October 2026).

  - Which face of the block lies on the plate. The front view of 05 04 01-4 is taken as the face on the plate, seen from above as the frame's plans are: drawn the other way round, the base's end that the detent's foot is clamped to would face away from the detent. Its side view is read in first angle, as the sheet reads throughout (DIN's; 15 October 2026, sure): the upright at the free face, 3.5-6.5 under the plate, the banking screw 5.0 under it and the foot 1.3-3.3, as 05 04_1's elevation prints them (the upright 6.5 to its end, 2.8 high, the screw's axis 1.4 from its end) and Wempe 9166 shows them (since 27 October 2026: the escapement of 1943's block; until then read in third angle, the upright flush with the plate and the foot 3.2-5.2, to meet the misread rollers). The block's steady pins' holes in the top plate (not in its table: drilled at assembly) and the stud's screw's tapped hole in the cock (not on 05 08 03-1) are cut in frame.js.

The horn bent toward the block to carry the gold spring (its curve, 1436's "Anschrägen", traced on Wempe 3955's photograph since 18 October 2026: likely, ±0.1; 1436 draws the bend later, its length not to scale there); its length 4.4 is 1436's figure with Wempe 3955's photograph (likely; 3.5 until 15 October 2026).

The openings' arcs' centres 9.43 out (traced, not figured), the floor's step slanting 0.6° (traced), the recess Ø 11.62.

  - The balance: the masses' diameter 6.3 (scaled on a poor copy; 05 03_2's side view 6.4-6.5, Wempe 3955 6.3 by the scale it gives), their height and place read on the sheet and photographs (likely: How the balance and escapement were measured), the chamfer at their top (about 0.4) left out; their screws (WN-12: the head Ø 1.6 sunk flush, from Wempe 3955; threaded through the outer jaw to the rim, as the 6MX's book's Fig. 5.62 cuts a mass) and the tapped hole in each top for WN-12a (M1.2 2.0 deep, countersunk Ø 1.85: 05 03_2's plan and the coloured section; left empty; WN-12a was a nut sunk in the inner face until 18 October 2026), the timing weights' size (Ø 3.8 × 3.3: the sheet and photographs part by ±0.4) and their cuts' depths and width, the timing screws' heads (Ø 1.65 × 1.3, scaled on a poor copy), the hub's screws WN-10 (from under the flange, as the coloured section draws them).

The stud's upright, leaning 8° (05 03 02-3), brings its slot 0.56 nearer the axis at the spring's height than an upright square to the bar would: with the slot in the upright's middle the spring and its pin lie side by side in it with the spring 3.55 out, where 05 03_7's curve pins the inner end in the collet, so the drawings likely mean both curves the same (adopted 15 October 2026; until then the outer was drawn in proportion to end 3.87 out). The slot's depth, 2.0. As first drawn (and in the 1943, Wempe 3955 and 6MX versions, whose balance stands 0.3 higher) the spring meets the slot 0.3 higher, where the leaning upright's inner wall stands 0.3 tan 8° = 0.042 further out: the drawn spring's outer curve is formed out by that over its last stretch (from 150° to 200° of the curve, before the slot), and the stud's pin with it, so both sit as in the last state (the spring 0.01 off the wall, the pin against the outer one); the physics keeps 05 03_7's curve (likely: the maker forms the end to the slot; until 18 October 2026 the spring stood 0.033 into the wall in those versions).

The escape arbor's ends likewise, 0.02 off.

0.06 and 0.03, the endshake 0.04, until then), settings to their seats, each holding its stone by a lip (the section draws them burnished over it); the escape wheel's upper hole jewel as last revised thinned to 0.4 and its lower to Ø 1.6 × 0.45 to fit what the sheets leave; as first drawn the escape wheel's lower endstone Ø 1.6 in its plug, the form scaled; the 6MX's chaton jewels and its contact's are left plain discs with olive holes (no section or photograph shows their insides); stones Ø 2.0 (1.8 and 1.6 for the escape wheel's) × 0.35, plain discs, and the escape wheel's lower hole jewel 0.2 off its endstone (a floor of its bush between them as first drawn), until 18 October 2026; the escape wheel's lower jewel in a bush in hole 26; the escape wheel's endstones in round gilt settings as Wempe 3955 shows them (the upper's spigot Ø 4.5 through the lip, flush outside, two brass screws from outside with heads Ø 2.6 × 0.3 under the balance's rim; the lower's Ø 4.6 flush in a seat 0.25 deep in the dial face with a boss 0.36 proud round its stone, Ø 2.2, two brass screws' heads Ø 2.6 lapping it: 2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg and 3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg on millimetre paper; a stadium cap 9.4 × 3 × 0.6 over the lower and a closed setting under the lip above until 16 October 2026); the cock's endstone seen through its setting, colourless (Wempe 3955's and Kirov 247's: sure; a Wempe's described as diamond, Kirov 247's faceted, drawn domed), its screws brass; the cock's hole jewel under it a ruby as the others, showing pink through the endstone (the user's choice, 18 October 2026; 3955's photograph reads grey there, so it may be colourless: unsure); the other screws' heads Ø 2 × 0.6 and their WN numbers where the coloured section's labels couldn't be matched (the block's two, the escape settings', the detent foot's).

- **The energy budget (phase 2b)**:
  - the mainspring's free curve: the Hamilton's measured spring (KLUwI2UUCMQ 32:18) scaled to the length, its turns ×0.75 (the set fitted with the length to 05 06_20's shape with the coils' friction counted, where the level also closes with the drawn strip: Physics: energy; ×0.6, the frictionless fit, until 26 October 2026); its eye the Hamilton's (2.2-9.4 mm along the strip from its inner end, 5.2 high; cut in the drawn strip since 17 October 2026, the hook's face 0.05 off its end toward the tip; the hook's ramp, 0.475, stands 0.1 into the next coil, which a real spring bends over it and the drawn spiral doesn't); its modulus 207 GPa (200-210); no film between packed coils;
  - the level: the solved strip at 0.37 gives 25 % more torque than 05 06_20 measures; the budget and the strengths take the solved shape at the measured level, the losses at their middle (×0.883);
  - the losses to the fusee arbor: the spring's own up to what mainsprings measure (ICMFF9, as the Hamilton's), the coils' mu 0.08-0.16, the pivots' and the chain's rivets' mu 0.10-0.15, the fusee arbor's pivots counted or not (05 06_20's rig isn't drawn);
  - the meshes' friction, mu 0.10-0.20 (steel leaves on brass teeth, run dry), and the pivots' shake for the holes' tolerance (0.02-0.05, the drawn bush bores; the WN sheets aren't in the set);
  - the strengths' ranges (strength.js's: brass, hardened steel, a mainspring's limit), Lewis's Y 0.2, each pivot carrying its arbor's whole load, the chain's plates 0.26 thick (drawn: three plates in its 0.82), its hooks 0.80 (the strip 0.9 finished to the chain's width), the band 0.78 wide, the stop pin's and stop bar's steel (no sheet).

- **The escapement in time (phase 2a)**:
  - the balance's free run-down TF 111 s, a running 6MX's measured on video at the model's own swing (111.1 s, 102-125, at 194.3° since 19 October 2026's physics pass; 108 s, 107.8 s, 96-121, at 199.4° before: References/wempe/VIDEOS.md, "Measured"; a 6MX's balance, not the Wempe's; taken since 15 October 2026, the user's choice), inside its parts' damping (81-208 s: the air's drag coefficients, the endstone's effective friction taken from the Hamilton's measured, the spring's own Q; their middle, 125 s, was taken until then); so the swing, 188.5-199.5° over the wind;
  - the train's efficiency 0.780, the geometric mean of 0.730-0.833 from its pivots at published friction coefficients and its meshes worked tooth by tooth (gearing.js --model wempe at mu 0.10-0.20; 0.749 from the meshes' rule of thumb until 19 October 2026);
  - the discharge stone's place on the staff (`DSB`, 16 October 2026): leaving the gold spring's tip 5° after the balance passes rest, the release at +2.6°. 05 04_1 draws the stone dashed under its roller, about 10 px across, too small to place against the gold spring's tip; with the impulse face where the sheet draws it the tooth lands on the face only from 4° (the release +1.6°), and from 8° the escapement doesn't run (Closed, 05 04_1's angles). Until 16 October 2026 taken as leaving the tip at rest;
  - the detent's preload, 0.8 g at the stone (just over the margin's floor with the deeper lock, at the 0.80 g cap above which the stone, landing with brass's restitution, lands on the escaping tooth's back during the impulse (Closed, 25 October 2026; The physics pass; 0.63 g, the Hamilton's after Op. 78, until 19 October 2026: no Wempe source gives one; a banking sets the lock, the 1954 instruction's п. 11), and its restitution on the banking (0.3); the steel detent spring's modulus 205 GPa; the gold spring's, 98 GPa (96-101: Au/Ag/Cu 333-333, the alloy Poljot 20027's passport gives the 6MX's; 05 04 01-2 says only "Goldblech 333": likely; 90 until 19 October 2026); the brass tooth on the sapphire stone mu 0.15;
  - the hairspring's modulus 206 GPa (200-210) and its working length as read (Physics: hairspring and temperature): the period 0.468 s against 0.5;
  - the densities of the rim's nickel steel (8.1) and the gold spring (11.6, 333-333 as above; 11.0 until 19 October 2026);
  - in makeEsc, the discharge stone's bevelled end as a straight bevel from the acting corner to the back corner, 0.3 (scaled), and the gold spring's tip as its strip's square end;
  - the maintaining spring's working (wempe_sustaining.js): its end at the wheel's pin held by the recess's wall as clamped (the ring's 32.3 -0.1 in the recess's 32.3 +0.05: no figure says it bears), the modulus 207 GPa (200-210), the detent taking the ratchet half a tooth back on average; so its ends' 0.27-0.58 mm in running and its 11-14 minutes; the twist's swing, about 130°;
  - 05 06_20's force scaled off its curve (±0.7 g), by the chain's turn on the groove from the large end: the page's turns from full wind taken off the stop's turn, 8.638 (escphys.js MF; fusee.js MW, the page warning if they part), its run down 8.70 turns on, 0.06 before the curve's start, taking its first value.

- **The escapement in time (phase 2d)**:
  - the stone's friction on the tooth it has let go (μ 0.15, as on the lock: 0.10-0.20 move the landing −9.5° to −9.1° and the swing 214.5-213.5°), its landing on it plastic, and makeEsc's quasi-static ride (`RW`);
  - the detent's own damping (Q 100) and its restitution on its banking (0.3), the Hamilton's;
  - the twist's swing, 0.6 of the running swing (128°);
  - in the frame (tripping.js --model wempe): the banking a single contact, the pipe's side on the banking screw's head at the stone (05 04 01-4's cross screw and the 6MX's photographs: likely; its head not drawn), its stiffness tripping.js's; the contacts' penalty stiffnesses and restitutions, tripping.js's (the return contact's matters: Physics: in time); the gold spring's tip square and unturning in its contact; the gold spring's fixed part rigid with the square up to its bending point (05 04_1); the screw WN-14's mass weighed, at its centre; the stone's lower half's height (to 0.06 under the teeth);
  - the train beyond the escape and seconds arbors in makeEsc's JW left out (0.03 g·mm², −0.08° of swing).

- **The transmission (16 October 2026)**, `tools/wempe_transmission.js`:
  - each mesh's stiffness, the teeth as cantilevers (the brass wheel's tooth and the steel leaf in series, halved for the contact: 1,620 N/mm a millimetre of face; traindyn.js's), and its damping (3 % of critical, oil);
  - the chain's stiffness, 120-700 N/mm (its plates' and rivets' give over the free span and the turns on each drum the tension reaches, μ 0.15 on the groove), 290 taken;
  - the click's face on the click wheel's tooth 10 kN/mm, the detent's 5 kN/mm, the escape tooth on the locking stone 2 kN/mm, the stop bar on its pin 6.7 kN/mm (its sizes, wempe_strength.js's), the maintaining spring banked 40 times its running stiffness; the contacts' damping 5 %, the springs' 2 % (the let-go's figures over the ranges each is varied in);
  - the pawls' springs' set, 0.3 mm (the clicks') and 0.5 mm (the detent's), and the click wheel's backs a straight rise over a pitch;
  - the coils' stick: the friction taken up over 0.05° of the drum;
  - the chain's mass, 8.6 g (2.9 mm³ of steel a pitch), the up-and-down wheel and hand's inertia (560 g·mm²) and the key's (2,200), none weighed;
  - the hand: its grip 1 N·m a radian, winding at a turn a second in half-turn strokes, a strong hand 1.06 N·m (Swain 1970), easing back over 0.15 s; the key's coil spring's drag slipping, 5 mN·m;
  - the cannon pinion's fit, 16.9 mN·m (the middle of its window: no sheet gives it).

- **The hairspring and temperature (phase 2c)**:
  - the reading of 05 03_7's "13 Umgänge −3,5 mm" as the whole strip, and its sheet as this chronometer's (likely: Physics: hairspring and temperature); the strip's ends past the pins (1.35 and 0.9 mm);
  - the drawn spring's isochronism (`WESC.SET.HS` −0.543 s a day per 10° about 207°) from its curves as drawn, both 05 03_7's table; the strip's own mass and the curves' rise left out;
  - the rim's nickel steel as Guillaume's 42-44 % ("Sonderlegierung" isn't named further): 7-8.5 × 10⁻⁶ a °C, E 145-160 GPa; brass 18.5-20 × 10⁻⁶, E 95-110; the carbon steel spring's thermoelastic coefficient −240 to −270 × 10⁻⁶ and expansion 11-12.5 × 10⁻⁶ (published ranges, named in the tool);
  - the masses clamping the rim over their slot (Ø 6.3) so it doesn't curl under them; the arm rigid; the quarters' roots at the arm's edge;
  - the secondary error: the steel spring's share from Dent's error (2-3 s a day over 0-30 °C), and the integral balance's taken as Guillaume's balances' fifth of it at Neuchâtel (1902): the nickel steel's quadratic coefficient isn't to hand.

- **The dial, motion work and hands (P1e)**:
  - the hour wheel's collar, 3.585 over its wheel's lower face, fitted to end 0.3 under the dial with the wheel on the cannon pinion: its sheet (05 02 03, 1940) gives 5.2 −0.1, which with 05 05 03's tube (1943) would put the Ø 8.0 collar 1.4 into the dial's Ø 6 hole; its overall 8.2 kept;
  - the hands' heights over the dial (above: from the 1946 seconds post; 05 02_1 and the coloured section draw them 2 mm lower, on the 1940 post), and the hour hand pushed on its taper 0.385 over its seat's top; the minute hand 0.5 over the hour hand's boss (05 02_1, 1941, scaled);
  - the pinions' tips and roots (above), the change pinion's leaf thickness 0.55 on the pitch circle, the up-and-down pinion of steel (the coloured section colours it yellow; Wempe 3955's is steel);
  - the cannon tube's square's corners turned to Ø 3.45 (Wempe 3955's, `1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg` at 9.14 px/mm: sides 2.60-2.67, half-diagonals 1.72-1.77; `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg` the same: likely, ±0.1; Ø 3.2 until 18 October 2026), its crown so starting 0.6 under the top, and its diameter 3.6 (scaled 3.65); the change wheel's collar Ø 5.5 and the up-and-down pipe's Ø 3.4, 7.7 (its flange: Wempe 3955's, `3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg` at 8.45 px/mm, 7.71 ±0.15, likely; 7.4 scaled until 18 October 2026) and 2.8, the hour wheel's collar Ø 8.0 (05 02 03 scales 7.95-8.2, Wempe 3955's 8.01, rms 0.03: likely; 7.9 until 18 October 2026) and bore 3.62 (scaled at 5:1); the up-and-down wheel's hub Ø 10.4 (the sheet's 10.5 −0.2; Wempe 3955's measures 10.77-10.92, recorded, not adopted: the drawn figure's tolerance excludes it, and Wempe 3955's version is claude/wempe-photo-inner's); the pipes' bores Ø 1.52, 0.01 clear of the studs (Ø 1.5, train.js; 05 02 02 and 05 02 04 scale them 1.50-1.56);
  - the change and hour wheels' riveting sinks (27 October 2026): 040204-1's "0,4" and "30°" at the hour wheel's hole, on its lower face (05 02 03's section draws the chamfer there, the pipe's foot flared into it), the 30° read to the axis (its leader on a chamfer about 0.2 across at 5:1: likely), Ø 6.46 at the face; the change wheel's, drawn the same on 05 02 02 (enlarged 6×, about 0.4-0.5 deep and 0.2 across) but not figured, made the hour wheel's (likely). Their bores, and the change pinion's and up-and-down wheel's, are polygons on their pipes' lathe angles (48, 64), the pipes lying on their flats exactly (until then 24-gons the pipes stood 0.02-0.03 into)
  - the hands' outlines between the sheets' stations (traced), the up-and-down hand's length 10.2 (measured on Wempe 3955's dial: its sheet isn't drawn to scale there) and its pipe Ø 3.0 (scaled), the hour hand's flange bevel (Ø 8.2 to the plan's inner circle 6.8), the minute hand's flange's round (r 0.4, scaled);
  - the seconds hand's collet (05 02 01-2, no sheet): Ø 1.5, 4.6 long, scaled on the coloured section, its end Ø 1.2 through the hand;
  - the dial's screws (WN-10 by the coloured section's label, likely: M1, heads Ø 1.7 × 0.8) and the ring's tapped holes for them; the dial's stepped pin (Ø 1.1 in the ring, 3.7 deep as the coloured section draws it, Ø 1.0 in the dial, whose hole scales 1.0; the step estimated); the hole at 185.7°; both small holes under the printed face, which no photographed dial shows pierced (Wempe 3955's `1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg` at 9.14 px/mm, where a Ø 1 would be 9 px and the screws' sinks show; Lange 5177's; Kirov No. 247's: sure; through the printing until 18 October 2026);
  - the dial's printing: traced on one photograph (Wempe 3955's, 9.2 px/mm), so its edges are good to about 0.03 mm and its line widths include the photograph's blur (sigma 0.046 mm) where under it; the 20's 0 and the 8's top, under hands there (above);
  - the Lange dial's printing (Lange 5177's, 8.9 px/mm, blur sigma 0.024): the same, and where its rust lies on the printing (wear, left out by its colour: brown where the ink is grey) the glyphs' edges taken a quarter of the way from the ink to the paper (0.016 mm in from half way, under this blur), so a few edges stay rough by 0.05-0.1 mm (the S of SÖHNE, the R and N of DRESDEN, the seconds 20, 40 and 50); estimated outright: the 16's 1, lost under rust, which is the seconds 10's 1 scaled to the up-and-down figures' height (0.695) and set by the 6 as the 10's 1 stands by its 0; the serial's last 7 (its stem broken by rust), the first 7 fitted on what shows; the 8's top under the minute hand, an ellipse through its upper loop (as Wempe's); its divisions under the hands or moved by rust (the seconds' 6) at the nominal angle, and the outer corners of the up-and-down band's ends sharp (not measured); the ground white, not the photograph's aged cream (above);
  - the glass's underside 17.0 over the plate's dial face (05 02_1's pencil, unsure), until the case was built (P1f: 16.7, above);
  - a fill light from below in the page (app.js), so the dial, which faces down in the page's views, reads white.

- **The case and box (P1f)**:
  - the bowl's machining: the recess Ø 68.2, the winding hole Ø 8.2, the screw's M3; its inner corner r 7 (scaled); its material: brass as the sheet, though the sheet's "Gewicht 350 gr." is a quarter of the 1.34 kg this bowl weighs in brass (158.4 cm³ with its brackets, weighed on the solid, phase 2d: 350 g is 2.2 g/cm³, a filled moulding's or, machined, aluminium's; a bowl that light wouldn't hang on the drawn pivots: Physics: case, gimbals and the trial);
  - the movement ring's seat 5.3 over the pivots (the bowl's top face until 24 October 2026; 05 01 against its 11,5 reads 4.6 and 4.75, ±0.5, for the 1940 bowl; 5.3 so the cast bowl's foot clears the ring as it turns to be wound; the ring's top level with it until 21 October 2026, the pivots then 11.5 under it);
  - every line of the bezel's section traced on 05 01 01 (no figures, ±0.2); its thread (05 01 01 marks it; its size and pitch: The bowl's thread, rebate and notch, above; until 24 October 2026 its lower bore was opened 0.35 for the ring's pin and plain); the glass's Ø 112 × 2.3 (scaled on the 1941 bezel) and its seat flush with the top; the notch for the ring's pin; the radial pin's 2.0 out and 9.4 long, its hole not cut;
  - the gimbal ring's material (brass; Wempe 3955's is Bakelite; its sizes measured on 05 01 since 25 October 2026, How the case and box were measured); its bracket's length, 57, and its screws, 35.5 apart (Wempe 3955's ring, `16_Wempe_3955_Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`, 35.5 ±0.8: likely; 37.2 until 18 October 2026); the ring bracket's screws round holes where 05 01 says they slide in slots ("Kloben verstellen"), M2.5; the pivots' shoulders 0.1 off what they bear against (endshake), the tapped holes' fits (the ring bracket's screws tapped through the ring, as Schnitt A-B draws them, since 27 October 2026; 2.75 into it before); the brackets' five screws' length (8 and 7.5, 3.3 into the bowl's wall; 6 until 27 October 2026), their tapped holes in the bowl not cut (bom.py's embed measures their threads in its metal); the outer pivots' heads 3.8 thick (51 00 10's side view, rough; 05 01's elevation 4.1); the knurls of the lock nuts and the bearing discs; the outer pivot's tip at 9 o'clock ending at the ring's inside (05 01 draws it 4.7 long, ±0.5: no longer, so the 1942 bowl clears it turned over);
  - the case brackets' foot stepped to the 1942 casting's band and body (r 54 over 13.5 under the pivots, 53 under: 05 01's 1940 bowl has no step), the foot's 3.7 thickness carried along its length and its slots not drawn, the boss's sides radial; the WN-78 screws' M4 (their heads Ø 7.0 as DIN 84's M4); the locking bracket's back stepping out under the bowl's band (its face, crank and slot measured on 05 01, 24 October 2026), the transit lock's lever (28 × 8 in plan) and knob (Ø 19.6), brass with its hub and pin on the drawings' brass case as on Wempe No. 6161's (`antiquorum_208-4_wempe6161.jpg`, one photograph: likely; black knob on steel until 18 October 2026; the 6MX's black on steel, as Wempe 5377's and 4585's): scaled on 05 01 at 2.45 px/mm, their forms after Kirow 422's bowl and the sheet's small views; its heights, which no sheet draws, read on Wempe 5377 (`bonhams_26816_327`) and Greenwich's wartime Wempe against the knob's Ø 19.6 (likely, ±1.5): the lever 2.5 thick (at the pivots' height, in the locking bracket's slot: above, the locking bracket and the transit lock), a hub Ø 9.8 standing 10.8 under the knob, the knob 4.5 thick, its top 4.6 over the rim, the pin Ø 4 standing 16.7 over the lever (16 to 24 October 2026 the lever lay at the box's rim over the ring's top, the knob's top 18 over the rim; until 16 October at the pivots' height, the knob 10 thick on a Ø 4 stem); its bracket's form estimated;
  - the closing plate's screw and knob (WN-71 head Ø 7.2 × 2.5, M3; WN-72 Ø 5.2 × 2.4); its spring (Verschlußfeder 05 01 00-7: Closed, below): the steel wire Ø 1.0, its two turns from r 12.35 to 6.5, the bends r 0.8, the hole Ø 1.2 × 1.5 for its inner end in the bowl's bottom and its stiffness, all estimated; its turn shut, 25° from open (its Ø 8.1 clears the Ø 8.2 hole from 20.8° on; the 6MX's shutter is a pin in a slot, Uy8A88_j-cA 5:21-5:25, so no measure for this one);
  - winding in the case: the bowl turned a full 180° (the videos show its bottom facing up; the instruction says "onto its side"), the way the dial goes (toward 3 o'clock: DIxob2Rl4dc 7:07, likely), and 0.8 s for each step (the turn, the plate, the key);
  - the pins over the outer pivots (05 01's elevation; How the case and box were measured, The box): their size and place scaled on the sheet's pixel profiles (Ø 2.2-2.9 between the lines' centres and outsides, built 2.5; 2.3 proud of the parting face and 3.3 into the wall, ±0.4; likely), their purpose (a lid-locating pin: inferred), their brass, their foot set in the wood without a bored hole (as the handles' screws), and the closed lid's hole for each, Ø 2.7 × 2.6 (no sheet draws the lid; inferred from the pin standing proud) (25 October 2026)
  - the handles' finger recesses' rounding (radius 10, as deep as the hollow: the section's curve fits it at one more point, 2.5 over the foot) and their place along the wall (centred under the strap); the box's floor 12.5, its boards butted, the lid's top a frame 20 wide round a glass 3 thick, the keeper on the front, the handles' screws: as the photographed boxes show them; the hinge one piano hinge 148 long across the back, its leaves 6.5 × 0.6 let into the parting faces and its knuckle Ø 5 in seven lengths (its length measured on Lange 1947's box from above, `drouot_33810142_lange1947_2.jpg`, 664 px at 4.48 px/mm by the box's 185; every photographed box has one, Wempe 6161's, 5377's and Greenwich's too: two hinges 40 long until 16 October 2026, which this line then said the photographs show); the lid's stay, a brass bar 91 × 6 × 1.5 with a slot 2.4 wide, pivoted 5 over the parting line and 40 from the back on the lid's left wall and riding on a pin 20 under it and 45 from the back on the box's (Wempe 6161's form, `antiquorum_208-4_wempe6161.jpg`, on the left as Greenwich's; Lange 1947's on the right), its places fitted to the lid's travel so it clears the back wall at every angle (it leans about 45° open, where 6161's stands nearly upright: its pin and pivot aren't measurable on the photographs); the knurls (the bezel's lowest 2.5 of its Ø 125, 0.4 deep, Wempe 6161 and 5377; the bearing discs' edges, 6161 and Lange 1947; the transit lock's knob, every photographed box; the inner pivots' lock nuts, knurled brass discs Ø 19 × 2.5 inside the ring against its face, Wempe 3955's bowl, where they were hex steel nuts outside it); mahogany, as Wempe No. 6161's round its brass bowl and gimbals (Antiquorum 208-4; its colours sampled on the photograph), where the wartime boxes photographed with Bakelite bowls are oak (Greenwich's, Lange 1947's; the Überkasten's sheet says bleached birch);
  - the outer box (51 00_5): its cushions' cover (felt-grey) and form between their figured middles (a lens (1 − u²)(1 − v²), 1 at the rim), the ends' and floor's 20 (scaled 23, 21), the rim strip's 5 and 4, the strap's leather 3 and its loose ends (not drawn), the eyes' form (a disc Ø 23, 2 thick, its screw's round head the Ø 8.5 centre), the hook and eye (commercial: a ring Ø 9 of 1.6 wire, a hook of the same), the hinges' leaves 12 × 1, which numbers of its parts list are which; the catch (19) inside the front not drawn, its form unread; the wooden box's latch and keeper press 2-3 mm into the front cushion
  - the maker's plate's place on the lid (Greenwich's wartime Wempe), its four pins; the tongue's place as the lid's latch, its pivot screw (M6, in its Ø 6.2) and knob (in its M3.5), its thinned end not drawn;
  - the key's tube 8.0 under the body's top (scaled on 51 00 11's section, ±0.15; its free length then 33.0, the sheet drawing the tube 48.1 against its own 50.1), the spring's wire 0.6, pitch 0.95 and about 12 coils (scaled), where its end stands round the axis (free on the sheet), the tube end's relief to Ø 4.6, the joint of its body's four solids 0.12 under the drill point's foot (no figure: where they join is the build's, not the part's), the square's 60° sink left out; the pin's length (15.3, scaled on 51 00 11's section); the paddle's colour (black, as the body: no sheet for it); where it lies in the box.

- **The case, gimbals and the trial (phase 2d)**:
  - the densities (as wempe_inertia.py's; the dial and the hands' Goldin brass 8.45), and for comparison only Bakelite 1.37, hard Buna rubber 1.18, aluminium 2.7 (published typical values);
  - gimbals.js's: the pivots' damping (0.05 of critical), the stops' wood (1e6 N/m, restitution 0.3), the seas (but for their periods and NORDFORSK's limit), the box's place on the ship; the box turned a quarter as the roll and pitch swapped (small angles, the box on the centreline);
  - the balance's pivots' side friction (mu 0.15 on the Ø 0.21 journal) and the beat error's quasi-static offset; the detent's trip threshold from its preload (0.63 g, the Hamilton's after Op. 78) and weight, static;
  - the masses' setting by the adjuster to ±0.45° of their compensating place (the rim's graduation read as eight punch marks of a 60-division; the spread the 6MX passports' adjusted C gives, sd 0.060; ±0.25° until 18 October 2026); the regulation at 20 °C ±0.5 s a day (the cards' largest period means, 0.81-1.90, come from C and S with it; ±2.5 would fail a fifth of the trials);
  - the scatter and drift (calibrated on the 6MX's eight test cards' E and R: a later Soviet chronometer's, not a Wempe's), the barometer (1 % of the air's density day to day) and the oil (the drive lost 0-10 % a year): the Hamilton's estimates; the air's share of the damping from phase 2a's parts (the middle of its range).
