- **The build checks' figures** (Building it): the guard band, 180-270° over the wind (185° until 19 October 2026's physics pass) (wempe_escapement.js's, 14° under the reference's swing: no source's; 193° until 15 October 2026); the mainspring's strip ±0.005, the detent's and gold springs' ±0.005, the hairspring's strip ±0.003 (boxed, no tolerance written) and its cut length ±0.3, the holes' places ±13 µm (the tables give none), the banking as first set ±0.02 and the preload by eye 0.8 ±0.3 g (0.63 before the physics pass), and as the bench leaves them ±0.005 and ±0.05 g, the endshake set again within 0.01 of its drawn figure; the endshake stacks' undrawn terms (the top plate ±0.02, the seconds jewel's depth 1.75 ±0.02, the escape arbor's endstones 22.84 ±0.02 (22.82 until 25 October 2026), the fusee bush's 0.75 ±0.02, the barrel's bearings 3.29 ±0.02); the endshake band 0.01-0.08 (free in the cold with an oil film, to the Hamilton manual's 0.003 in); the lock's shallow end, 0.232, from the frame's 116 µm taken the same at every depth (0.154 from its 77 µm before the physics pass); the preload's aim a quarter into its window; the banking's aim the lock window's middle, set again until it reads inside; the maintaining spring's 5-minute floor; the timing weights' reach (their size estimated, drawn against their heads); a drawn tolerance taken as made anywhere within its limits.

- **The escapement of 1943** (the Variants' fourth version; The escapement of 1943 above): the banking screw's dome rising 0.4 on its 1.1 cylinder (no photograph shows the rise), its length 8.2 under the head (the 6MX's loose block's), so its threaded end 2.9 past the upright's far face (no German photograph shows that side), its underside where the pipe rests (as the 6MX's).
- **The Soviet 6MX** (the Variants' third version, Kirov No. 247; The Soviet 6MX above). Measured on Altmeppen's photographs, each fitted on the sheets' hole table; estimated or chosen where they don't show:
  - *pillar 39's radius*, 37.3: its bearing, 190°, is measured to ±0.5° on both faces, its radius reads 36.6-37.3; on r 37 the drawn pillar would stand 0.11 into the drawn seconds wheel's tips, so the radius is the least that clears them by 0.2 (the 6MX's seconds wheel and its pillar's diameter aren't seen);
  - *the chatons*: each filling the bush's hole (4.59 in the plate, 4.3 in the bridge; about Ø 4.1 shows), the stone flush with the face the arbor's shoulder bears on (so the drawings' endshake), Ø 2.2 × 0.6, its hole the pivot's and 0.025, the sink a cone Ø 3.4 to 1.2: the photographs show the settings' faces only;
  - *the balance staff*: the first-drawn one with its brass collar (no 6MX photograph shows the hub), taken because the escapement stands at the first-drawn heights;
  - *the barrel*: the drawings' 1941 three-piece barrel, its cap underneath (Altmeppen, Knirim); the photographs can't tell it from the later drum turned over;
  - *the detent*: 1436's revised (bracketed) sections and lengths at the first-drawn heights (the 6MX's detent is not shorter: X5); Kirov 14100's slotted foot not drawn, as the drawings' foot's holes aren't;
  - *the detent block*: 05 04 01-4's figures, read in first angle (the upright 3.5-6.5 under the plate; Kirov-Poljot 24020's reads 4.3-6.6, Kirov 247's 3.9-6.6); the taper at the upright's root (one photograph); the stud's thread and depth in the block, the near pin's 1.2, the slide's depth, the stop screw's thread and head height; the banking screw centred in the upright, its domed head Ø 3.2 × 1.4;
  - *the Geneva stripes*: their profile is the photograph's light; the faces the photographs don't show (the plates' inner faces) take the same;
  - *the dial*: its screws' and pin's sizes the German dial's; the serial's two strokes under the seconds hand (the 4's upright below its crossbar, the 7's bar 0.4 under the head), drawn straight on from what shows;
  - *the bowl*: its foot below 8.3 under the cone (unseen, run to the drawn floor), its inside (the bore and a cone 3.4 inside the outer one), the rim's top flush with the flange and its notch for the ring's pin, the thread not drawn; the brackets' plates' and web's thicknesses, the block's depth taken 4.6 with its outer face at the drawings' r 66.5 for the ring's pivot screw, the locking bracket's crank; the closing plate's spring and screw's head height;
  - *the bezel's inside and its glass*: the glass 4.0 thick, standing 2.5 proud, its underside 0.65 over the minute hand's boss; the seat, sight bore and shoulder not seen; the knurl drawn straight, not diamond;
  - *the boxes*: the walls, the glazed lid's glass and frame, the hinges, the lids opening together; the outer box's height, parting line, walls, felt and straps (its width, notch and catch measured: X5); the lid's tab drawn filling the notch;
  - *the physics*: the 6MX's own escapement, from its bodies weighed on the page's solids (X5); its mainspring the drawings'; the gimbals' periods the drawings' (its bowl, hanging 13.2 lower, isn't weighed apart: Unresolved);
  - *the electrical contact*: the lever, staff, stone, cock and spiral's places from Romanov's Fig. 100 mapped onto the photographed parts (±1.5), the base's inner edge (±2), the blocks' outlines, the contact wheel's tooth depth and form, the springs' sizes (±15 %), the jewels' sizes and the staff's pivots (each 0.02 off its cap jewel, 0.04 endshake: no photograph shows them inside their settings; the upper ended 0.1 into its stone until 24 October 2026), every screw's thread; which half-second the contact is open; the stud's outer face kept at r 44.0 and the stone's bevel to 0.3, so the lever swings clear.
**From the photographs, estimated (photocompare.py, 18 October 2026)**:
- Wempe 3955's set-up ratchet recess: 0.7 deep, its wall at 45° (only its two edges, Ø 8.7 and 7.3, are measured).
- The 6MX's domed screw heads: 2.4 overall with a 0.8 dome (±0.3), read on oblique views against the model's head of the same width; their thread and shank the drawings'.
- The 6MX's cork ring 2.4 thick (its top 1.6 under a 3.86 one's; ±0.3) and its waisted dust bush's flare taken as a cone; the 6MX's detent block stud screw 2.5 (±0.7) proud; its barrel arbor's square end ±0.4.
- Wempe 3955's dust bush waist Ø 9.0 (±0.4) and shoulder height (±0.3), its collar 1.85 (±0.2); its fusee square's tip ±0.5.
- The hammered finish's tile: its statistics measured (scale, spread, orientation), its pattern synthesised.
- The hairspring's collet bored on its sheet's 1:70 to the 1946 staff's seat, Ø 1.647 at the arm's face to 1.604 (25 October 2026 the size, straight Ø 1.648; tapered 27 October 2026): its sheet, 05 03 02-2 (24.2.43), gives 1.6 H7 on 1:70, tapering to 1.55, the earlier staff's; on the 1946 staff's seat (05 04 02: 1.58 to 1.65 s6) such a bore would seat 3.15 higher, where 05 03_5's 13,4 and 0,75 put the collet on the arm. The collet is bored to the seat, as the impulse roller's 1.627-1.65 bore is, and sits 0.05 over the arm; a maker opens the collet to the staff (no effect on the rate).
- The top plate's bushes for the intermediate and seconds arbors (WN-300 at 38 and 35, no WN sheet): bored 0.845 and 0.83, each 0.02 over its pivot, for 05 05_1's "Lagerstellen: Enger Laufsitz" (the centre arbor's WN-301 1.21 on its 1.19); 0.86 both until 25 October 2026 (the per-part audit). The change pinion's root Ø 5.4 (Wempe 3955, ±0.1) leaves 0.05 under the hour wheel's tips, and the up-and-down pinion's 0.035 under its wheel's (the sheets' own figures): depths a maker must watch; no check measures the motion work's depthing (gearing.js takes the going train).

- The mechanisms in turn (25 October 2026, `wempe_wear.js`, `wempe_overwind.js`, `wempe_motionwork.js`, `wempe_estimates.js`): the wear coefficients (Rabinowicz's classes, the escapement's as dry non-metal on metal), the hardnesses' soft ends (333 gold hard rolled 150 HV), each contact's area, slide and the tolerance taken for it (the barrel drum's line 0.10, the chain's 1 % of its pitch, the fusee wheel's teeth 20 µm, the escape tooth's tip land 0.02 and its wedge 0.5, the balance pivot's flat end), the gold spring's loads (about 10 and 2 mN); a hand's torque carried on past the data to the Ø 34.5 paddle; the key's coil wire (a design value: Ø 0.68 or more, spring wire EN 10270-1 DH), its drag back 5 mN·m; the studs' shake 0.02; the cannon pinion's bench weight 30 mm along the hand; a hairspring strip drawn 6 % long; the TF range 80-200 s.

## Superseded, left out or for later

- Superseded escapement sheets, built as first drawn (phase E1, above): 05 04_1's heights of the rollers and wheel, the 1941 staff 05 04_5, 05 04_1's detent block (a plate with a long arm, as on Wempe 3955) and detent, 05 04_7.1's escape arbor, 1436's unbracketed sizes. The staff 05 03_3.2 (1965) is later than the set's time and not built. Built as first drawn (phase E2): the three-piece barrel 0207 (020701, 020701-3) and the maintaining spring 020605; the fusee 02 06 13 (020613-2) not (above). Built as first drawn (phase E3): the 1940 key. On the cards only: the tolerance-only revisions (the movement ring's revision a, the lower cock's seat, the click of 25.5.48) change no shape, for the cards.
- Not built: the lever chronometer's escapement and balance (05 04_20, _21, 05 03_1).
- Superseded figures (history, not built): the movement ring's revision a (0.7 +0.05, 3 ±0.05, 7 ±0.07, 10.7 −0.05 before 6.12.46); the lower cock's seat tolerance (+0.05 before 2.12.46); the top plate's thickness 3.3 (the lever plate's revision b); the click before 25.5.48.
- Not built: the hand setting itself (05 33); its holes 14-18 and window are cut in the pillar plate as Wempe 3955's (How each frame part was measured; Readings corrected). By the sheets: 56, 57 (the seconds contact's screws, "nur bei Bedarf", not in Wempe 3955's plate); the lever's 27, 31-34 and its pocket. Hole 30 (Ankerrad) is built, in the drawn versions its seat 5.2/4.59 as sheet b's Schnitt G-H draws it (the table reams it 2.50), in Wempe 3955's the table's plain 2.50 as its plate shows (27 October 2026), with its two M1.2 holes, and left empty: it is in the detent pillar plate's own table and Wempe 3955's plate has it drilled (the transcription's WN-302 at 30 is the centre arbor's bush at hole 1: Readings corrected).
- Built with the fusee (P1c): the stop pin 05 08 01-5 in hole 7 (no sheet). The cork ring 05 08 01-4 on the dust bush was built on 15 October 2026 (How each frame part was measured), with the bowl's seat it meets (Estimated). Built with the motion work (P1e): the up-and-down pinion on the fusee arbor's stub (05 02 00-5). Not built: the barrel's Steg 05 07 00-1 (coloured section, no sheet): its leader ends on a thin steel strip, about 0.5 thick, standing just inside the drum's wall for the drum's height, where 05 07 01-1's revision (a) cuts the 2 × 0.5 slot through the drum's bottom at 9 o'clock (likely; what it does is unsure: Review-results.md). The "WN-203" beside it is the barrel bridge's steady pin, built (the leader read again 17 October 2026). The stop bar's pin 05 06 03-2 is built (17 October 2026): Ø 0.5 pressed across the bar at u −6.0, standing 0.9 out toward the arbor, the bar's spring's end bearing on it, as 05 06_4's C-D draws it in both positions (likely; the section's "foot" under the bar's end is the bar itself in the groove's opening). The spring's pin in the body, which the model numbered 05 06 03-2 until 17 October 2026, is 05 06 04-4 (the coloured section's label and 05 06 04's parts list: likely).
- Built with the train (P1b): the brass bushes WN-300, -301, -302 (not jewel settings: the coloured section names them Messinglager), the seconds arbor's jewel and setting (at 35, not 38), the stud bushes 05 08 02-2 and the two studs.
- Built in their phases: the upper cock's endstone and its screws (the balance), the cannon tube 05 05 03-3 on the centre arbor (the motion work), 05 08 01-4 on the dust bush (15 October 2026) and the movement ring's radial pin to the case (P1f).
- Built later (P1f): the Überkasten (outer box, 52 00 16 / 127-556.01, 15 October 2026: How the case and box were measured) and the 1940 key (127-556.02 U 01, 51 00 11, a pear-shaped handle; as first drawn, phase E3), superseded by the 1943 one-way key of the same number. Not built: the Bunagehäuse (127-556.03 on 51 00_1, 1942) has no sheet: the bowl is R-05 01 00-1's brass casting; the anti-magnetic liner ("evtl. Einlage gegen magnetische Einflüsse", 001, 002), optional; the hand setting's knob through the case (05 33) and the balance holding device's (05 36), as before.
- Not built (P1e): the hand setting (Zeigerstellung 05 33, 5.11.41): a knob and shaft through the case's wall, a crank, a spring-loaded slide and a pinion on a block on the pillar plate: four figures on the sheet (23.15, 8.5, 10, 3), its parts in the case (05 01, a later phase) and its plate holes 14-18 struck through on the pillar plate's sheet ("nur wenn Zeigerstellung eingebaut wird"), its setting pinion struck through on 05 02_1: not enough to build, and optional by the sheets. Wempe 3955's plate is cut and drilled for it, which the model now is (How each frame part was measured), but nothing is in its holes but the pinion's empty bush: the block, its pins and screws aren't fitted (the knurled brass disc by the gimbal bracket on Wempe 3955's bowl, `16_Wempe_3955_Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`, lies flat on the ring's inner face on the axis of the brass pivot head outside it: the inner pivot's lock nut, not a knob: fairly sure, 18 October 2026). The balance holding device (Unruhhaltevorrichtung 127-556.06 / 36 00, 26.2.42, and its tongs 36 00 01-1, 29.8.41): read and measured (dial.js keeps the record) but it doesn't fit the balance and detent as last revised (Unresolved). The seconds contact (05 38): an option ("nur bei Bedarf"). Searched again on 18 October 2026 (the user's decision then: left unbuilt): 127-556 is the Luftwaffe's equipment number for the ground chronometer BC 1010, Fl. 23881, a lever chronometer on the Einheits frame "mit externer Zeigerverstellung und Unruh-Arretierung" (Wempe No. 3509 of 1943: Knirim, knirim.de/a11mond.htm and the RLM list rlm1.pdf, p. 5), so the hand setting, like 05 36 and 05 38, is that lever chronometer's option. 05 33 (5:1, about 12.2 px/mm, the three figures agreeing to 1.5 %): a radial shaft (35) in the pillar plate's bore (05 08_3a's C-D: Ø 2 at 1.68 from the dial face) meeting the setting pinion's arbor in hole 18, a wheel about Ø 7.8 on its outer end, an intermediate about Ø 10 on a screwed stud (36) in the case wall and a wheel about Ø 10.5 on the knob's shaft in a bush through the wall (8.5 and 10 the two centre distances, 23.15 ±0.1 from the bowl's step to the knob's axis), the knob conical and knurled, about Ø 14; a bent strip on a pin in a spring-loaded slide (the engaging safety: unsure). The setting pinion's 16 leaves follow from 05 02_1 (12.96 to the change wheel, its module 0.36 and 56 teeth). Fitted ones photographed: Wempe 2965 (U-boat lever version, `auctions-museums/muser96-249_wempe2965_4.jpg`: a steel wheel about Ø 8, 14-16 teeth, on a radial shaft in a notch of the movement ring, from above only) and Wempe No. 2900 (lever, Henry's 04-03029, 18 April 2026: archiv.henrys.de, a steel wheel of 15-16 teeth on the ring's edge at about 7:30-8 o'clock from the dial side, two brass wheels inside the bowl's wall, out of focus; no scale). None shows the block, the slide or the knob in place, and no source gives the plan shapes or tooth counts; the plates of Wempe 3955, Lange 5177, Kirow DEC 422 and 479 are cut for it with nothing fitted.

- The Soviet 6MX, left out: the later Polets' pin-in-slot shutter (Uy8A88_j-cA 5:21-5:25; Kirov 14917 has the drawings' closing plate); the red cloth bag the outer box stands in (the TU's "чехол"); the + and − marked on the bowl by its terminals; the contact's lead and plug (the TU's kit, "Контакт выводной АКУ6.622.003": no photograph of it); the later movements' window in the top plate at 202° (Kirov 2973, Poljot 20027: not Kirov 247's or 009's) and their vertical stripes. Kirova 14100's (`photos/chasy-ru-9985-kirova-14100/`, a later 6MX) detent foot slotted between its steady pins (about 2.5:1) and its intermediate bridge plain, not striped (Review-results.md, Wempe 3955 and the 6MX against photographs, item 21): the model builds Kirov 247's (1952), the foot plain and the bridge striped. Built since 18 October 2026 (X5): its electrical contact and its fixings, the adjusting slide, the cards, the essay and report's sections, the page's own physics for it.
- **How the band's hooks lie** (27 October 2026): no sheet draws them in place. The round hook lies level in the fusee's flange slot with its seat on the pin, the band leaving its mouth along its run; the drum hook lies with its left wall 0.01 off the drum, its barb in the drum's slot (cut where it falls), the band rising 0.6 off the drum over its last 1.5 into the hook's mouth; the notches where the band leaves each hook broken 0.1 ("Kante brechen", the size not given). The arbor hook's head 2 along the axis (the side view's 2.09) and its land falling evenly (the end view's line, the drawing not to scale).

## Readings corrected against the sheets

Checked on the sheets while building (the transcription `References/wempe/transcriptions/05-08-frame.md` is left as written):

- **The 1946 staff's rollers, and with them the escapement's heights as last revised** (27 October 2026, from Unresolved, the owner's question on the banking screw). 05 04 02 (29.5.46) dimensions from the hub's flange top: the hub's neck to 4.1 +0.05, then a step to 5.6, then the lower arm. Measured on the sheet at 17.6 px/mm (its Ø 1.65 at the top, 29 px), the step is 28 px wide (Ø 1.6-1.65) and the arm under it 21 px (Ø 1.2); a Ø 2.14 would be 38 px. The step is therefore the "1,65 s6" seat, its leader drawn to it, and the "2,14 +0.01" is the hub's bore at its foot. The impulse roller (02 04 02: 1.6 thick, bored 1.627/1.65 on 1:70) sits on that seat from the hub's end, and the discharge roller under it on the Ø 1.2 r6. Four sources agree:
  - 05 03_3.2, the production staff of 13.3.65: its Ø 1.65 −0.02 seat 1.6 long, from 4.1 to 5.7.
  - The coloured section, which numbers its staff and hub 05 04 02-1 and -2: the impulse roller directly under the hub's end, about 4.2 under the flange top, the discharge roller under it, at 9.4 px/mm.
  - 05 04_1's "4,1 bis Unruhauflage".
  - Wempe No. 9166 of 1972 (Uhren-Hidding's archive, `uhren-hidding.de/326-xxlarge/marinechronometer-chronometerwerke-wempe.jpg`, a side view under the balance): the escape wheel's teeth right under the top plate, the detent along the plate, the block's upright under it with the slotted head at the stone (sure in layout); its balance rim close over the plate, about 0.3-0.5 by the rim's 3.8, as the model's 0.45 (likely). Wempe 9411 (Auktionshaus Stahl 113478) looks the same at low resolution.

  The model had read a collar Ø 2.14 from 4.1 to 5.6 with the impulse roller under it, which put the rollers 1.5 lower than every other source. To meet them it put the escape wheel 1.5 lower (its arbor's ends 0.49 and 0.06 inside the plates), read the block's side view in third angle (the upright flush with the plate, the detent's foot 3.2-5.2), left the banking screw a shank bearing on nothing, and listed the conflict as Unresolved. Now the rollers stand 0.32-3.52 under the plate and the escape wheel's teeth 0.4-1.8, its arbor's ends 1.98 and 1.55 in. The block is 05 04 01-4 read in first angle as the sheet reads, built as the escapement of 1943's, its banking screw whole and the pipe on its head. The detent's foot is at 1.3-3.2, its pipe 1.9-3.8, the lower endstone 1.5 down a window in its setting. The balance, its staff's other figures and every plan stay; makeEsc and the physics are planar, so the escapement's timing doesn't move with the heights (the bodies' inertias do, slightly: `wempe_inertia.json`). The owner chose not to keep the old reading as a version (27 October 2026).
- **The detent's blade flush toward its block** (27 October 2026, the owner's choice "Adopt and refit"; .1436's left view and 10-2's "linke Seite": sure): the foot, spring, square and the blade's thicker part are centred 0.11 ((0,50 − 0,28)/2) off the blade's line toward the wheel, the blade, pipe and stone flush with their face toward the block (the blade centred until then). The block's offset 0.30 → 0.19 (ACR 7.49), so the stone lands 18.77 from the block's screw row (18.73), the detent line turns 0.15°, its flexure lies 0.11 off the stone's line, the gold spring's bending point 0.11 over. The discharge stone's reach (estimated, set so the stone is lifted 0.07 past the lock) takes the gold spring's tip's slide out along the blade as the detent turns about the offset flexure, DOFF times the turn (2.6 µm): RD 1.578 (1.5754 without it, which lifted the tip 12 µm less, let the stone fall back 0.6° sooner onto the tooth's tip with a weak drive, makeEsc's swing 78° at 90 %, and held the swing on the stone's blow's step). The detent weighed about the offset flexure: ID 8.23 (8.16), the 6MX's 8.01 (7.94). The swing 197.1° at 12 h (195.1°), 192.4-202.3° over the wind; HS −0.483 at it (−0.470). Two cross-checks widened, the owner's choice (The physics pass).
- **The drawings check's open items on the balance and escapement, settled** (27 October 2026, from Unresolved; each sheet read again, enlarged):
  - *The collet's bore* (05 03 02-2): its 1,6 H7 on 1:70 to (1,55), the 1943 collet's, against the 1946 staff's seat (1.58 to 1.65 s6): the taper built, the size the seat's (Ø 1.647 to 1.604; straight 1.648 until then), since 05 03_5's 13,4 and 0,75 put the collet on the arm, where a 1.6 bore would stand 3.15 higher.
  - *The staff's lower pivot* (05 03_3.2): 12,6 −0,02 below the seat built (12.59; 12.83 until then, the lug's endstone placed 0.95 inside its face by estimate): the only finished figure for it, as its 13,0 is for the upper, and the coloured section's endstone 10 px (1.06 ±0.11) inside the lug's face lies between the two. Its cones hollow: the detail draws both flanks concave with a "6" on a radius's leader; built R 6 (0.09 deep).
  - *The block's upright foot* (05 04 01-4): read convex in the drawings check; its arc, 35 rows traced on the front view at 17.1 px/mm, fits a circle r 2.96 whose centre lies in the air (rms 0.3 px), a cove, so "3 r" is its radius: from a step 0.35 out 2.5 over the base (the "15") to 2.0 out and 0.4 over it (the "0,4"), then straight down, flush with the 6.5-deep part's end (the hidden line under it). Built so as last revised and in 1943 (a cove r 3 tangent to both, and r 2, until then); the CWW's block (10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg, left) shows the cove. The 1943 block's end face bored as the others (its M1 on 20 sides, which fusee.js's bore closes there).
  - *The block screw 23,8 from the wheel* (05 04_1, 3.2.40) against the top plate's table's hole 13 at 23.41: Wempe 3955, which carries the 1941 block, has its fixing screw's head 23.36 ±0.15 from the escape wheel's endstone setting (2_Wempe_3955_A.jpg: 205.8 px at 8.81 px/mm, the head's centre put back 2.4 to the plate's face): the table is the plate as drilled; kept.
  - *The stud's round point and pin hole* (05 03 02-3): both kept. The point's "0,8" (a diameter, drawn about Ø 0.55) is the blank's: the facets ground to the section's knife edge leave no metal for it, and Wempe 3955's point shows under Ø 0.3. The pin's hole is drilled "bei Montage der Spirale": the end view's circle (0.7 from the end, its centre 0.56 from the upright's face toward the axis) would put the spring against the slot's outer wall, 4.4 from the staff, against 05 03_7's pinning radius and the collet's 3.5, which the model keeps; the pin lies in the slot beside the spring, so no metal is drilled.

- **The fusee body's place on its arbor, and its large face re-turned** (25 October 2026, the per-part audit; from Unresolved). 05 06 04 as revised 2.3.48 (05 06_3.2), measured on the sheet (line centres on profiles across each line, 12.02 px/mm along the axis by the arbor's 19.7 and its collar and step, 7.9 + 3.1; the scan turned 0.0035 rad): "13,7 −0,1" runs from the large face (its extension line down from the flange's outer corner) to the end of the arbor's Ø 6.1 step (its extension line at x 601.7), drawn 13.61; "0,5 +0,05" from the face to the click wheel's face (its extension line down past the wheel), drawn 0.60; the bracketed (12,3) from the face to the small end, drawn 12.17, and (1,6) to the second rib's outer face, drawn 1.79, both "in Klammern gesetzt" by the revision, which made 0,5 and 13,7 the governing figures; "33∅ +0,1" between the 45° chamfer's mouth (Ø 29.8) and the flange's rim, and "5°" an angle at the flange's outer corner with a leg on the 33's extension line; "(a) Fläche muß schlagfrei zur Welle laufen". The face line is one straight line (no step at Ø 33: a 0.25 step would be 3 px). So the large face is re-turned true on the arbor at assembly, all of it, 0.2 deep (the click wheel, 0.7 under the blank's face, 0.5 under the re-turned one), with a 5° fall outside Ø 33 (0.114 at the rim), and the body pressed on so that face lands 13.6 from the step's end; the groove and its end N, cut on the part before, keep their places from the small end, the first rib 0.56 and the ribs 1.8 over both. Where the sheet draws the rest: the small end 1.29 from the step's end, the recess floor 11.24, the arbor's collar starting 0.25 inside the body's bore (the model now 1.25, 11.1 and 0.1 inside; until then 0.91, 10.76 and 0.24 outside). The stack under it comes down to meet it by re-turning the Vorreiber's face a, its own sheet's assembly note (2.76, the fusee wheel 2.385 off the plate as the coloured section's 2.4), and the cover plate lies under an empty 0.425 of the J-K recess as the coloured section draws it (0.5); the stop pin's 1.85 off the coloured section then reaches just past the stop bar's thin end, as the section draws it. Rejected: the face inside Ø 33 re-turned with the flange left standing (the flange 0.2-0.39 into the maintaining ratchet's plate, which runs under the whole face to Ø 37.2, and a step the sheet doesn't draw); the groove measured from the re-turned face (it is cut before). Until 25 October 2026 the face 13.46 from the step, not re-turned, the click wheel 0.7 under it (Unresolved's 0.75 was a slip), the body 0.34 nearer the top plate. Likely; a sharper scan of 05 06 04 would settle the figures to better than the scan's ±0.1-0.2.

- **The case's last open readings of the drawings check** (27 October 2026, batch 10's): 51 00 10's section figures the handle's ends "6" and its middle "12,5" off the side panel and the finger recess "10" deep (the handles built 10 off with 3 thick ends, the recess at its scaled 10.5); 51 00 11's 1940 key figures its stem "32" out of the body (built at the traced 31.74); 05 01 Schnitt A-B draws the ring bracket's screws through the ring to its inside (built 2.75 into it); 05 01 02-3's tube end is sunk 60° "1 +0,2" (left out) and P. 51 00 02-2's tongue has its hole sunk 0,5 × 45° and its far end thinned 0,2 over 7 (left out). Each built as drawn. The tube's relief, read as Ø 4.2 at 21° in the check, measures Ø 4.8 at 9.72 px/mm (47 px across the end): the model's estimated Ø 4.6 at 15° kept. The bowl's floor, whose bottom and turned wall stood 0.01 apart (a dotted ring in Edges), is one closed solid.

- **The bezel screws onto the bowl** (24 October 2026, at the owner's word, with Kirov-Poljot 24020's and a 6MX's photographs of the threaded rim): 05 01 01 marks a thread in the bezel's skirt (its hidden zig-zag), which the model had left plain because nothing drawn on the bowl took it, the bezel lying loose over a plain flange. The thread is cut in both, the bowl's on a rim turned from its flange round the movement ring's flange, with a notch for the ring's pin; which puts the bowl's bottom 49 from the ring's seat, as 05 06_01 gives it, where it had been 53 with an estimated raised seat for the cork. The transcription (`05-01-51-00-case-box-overview.md`: "no thread, holes or glass groove drawn" on R-05 01 01-2) is right for the casting's sheet and left as written.

- Readings settled in the audit of 3.00.00 (16 October 2026), the figures first, then the photographs, then what a maker would do:
  - 05 04_1's "25,68 von Unruhmitte" and "23,8 v. Gangradmitte" put the 1941 block's screw 1.22 from hole 13, the block turned 3.4° about the stone (both points 20.2 from it: the layout's 18.8 along the detent and 7.6 across give 20.28). The layout (3.2.40) comes before the top plate's table; the 1941 block screws into the same plate as the 1943 one, at the table's hole 13 (Wempe 3955 carries the 1941 block on it), so the block stays on 13 in both states.
  - 05 04 02's 14.03 −0.06 runs from the hub's flange to the lower centre's apex on the Ø 1.1 stock, the pivots not drawn: a finished staff that long would put its pivot 0.23 past the lower cock's underside (the lug's far face 10 from the top plate, the endstone 0.95 inside it), so it is the blank's, as built.
  - The dashed "7ʳ" arc about hole 26 on the top plate's plan (05 08_1b) is the escape wheel's tip circle (Ø 14) drawn under the plate, not an edge of the balance opening's tongue.
  - The upper cock's table gives hole 23 "Körner 4,4, kalibr. 4,59" and its Schnitt A-B the seat 5.2 H7 down to a floor 0.5 thick in the boss under the arm, the floor bored through at the table's 4.59 (read again 17 October 2026 on U30's stack and the coloured section: until then read as a seat through, opening into a sink Ø 6 × 0.5 underneath); the top plate's table gives hole 26 2.3 drilled and 2.5 reamed and Schnitt P-Q its jewel's seat. The tables are the coordinate jig's operations (punched, then reamed to locate), the sections the holes as finished: the sections are built.
- 05 06 04-2's Schnitt A-B and 05 07 04 (16 October 2026, the audit of 3.00.00): the flange's "0,87 +0,01" is the slot, "0,8" the first rib and "0,37" the second, summing to the "2 ±0,02" over both (the model had the slot 0.8, the ribs 0.6 and 0.14); "1,2 ±0,01" is the pitch, a land and its rib, so the lands are 0.87, the slot's width (the model had 02-1's pencilled 1.15 and lands 0.82, the chain's own width). 05 07 04's side views draw each hook one solid bar the chain's full width, from the 10 × 0.9 strip: the hooks were the middle layer's 0.26.
- The hand-setting (16 October 2026): Wempe 3955 was said to have it (`6_Wempe_3955_S.jpg`); its plate is cut and drilled for it, but the holes, the window and the bush's bore are empty on both faces (`3_Wempe_3955_uZ.jpg`, `8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg`), and nothing of it is on its dial side. 05 08_3a's "Schnitt C-D (the hand-setting slot)", read as a slot 12 long with a recess Ø 14 and a step 2, is the window along its length: 12 +0.2 across its inner face, the ends Ø 14 arcs, and the circle in it the bore across the plate (1.68 from the dial face, the "2" its Ø); the "cut at right" on 05 08_3b (6.5, 6.5, 8.3 −0.1) is the same window, and its 37° the line of hole 26's screws, along which two of its figures are drawn.

- The stop work, 05 06_4's Schnitt C-D and 05 06 04-2 (15 October 2026): C-D was used unmirrored, though A-B on the same sheet is seen from the fusee's small end and mirrored here; so the bar met the stop pin with its near side, where C-D draws the pin against its far side. Mirrored, with the pin on the far side. The body's "1,1+0,05 tief" (read as a second slot in the transcription) is the same slot's depth toward the bar's upper end: fine.py found the bar, at full thickness there, 0.26 into the chain on the turn below. The cover plate's screw is dimensioned on 02-2 ("6 r", "1"), not 90° from the slot. The slot was not cut and the bar's spring and its pin not drawn; all three are now. And the slot is dimensioned from the line through the chain's pin A ("4", no angle): along it; the model had the bar's turn set by the stop with full wind at the groove's end, 40° off it. Full wind is now the stop's, 0.112 turn short of the groove's end.
- The escape pinion (05 04 05-2, merge of P1b and P1d): first drawn on core.js's default depth (root r 0.97), so the seconds wheel's tips (11.85 − 10.95 = 0.90 from its centre) cut into its root; now on the sheet's tip Ø 2.97 and root Ø 1.6 with the train's tooth forms (WTRAIN.teeth), and phased against the seconds wheel by gearLash (movement.js).
- 05 08_1b: the "2,65" by the fusee's M1.4 holes is 22,65, hole 1 to hole 10 (its leader runs to hole 1); the dashed circle round hole 10 is J-K's recess Ø 22 × 0.7 in the inner face (not a 19 mm clearance); Schnitt J-K runs through hole 10, not the plate's edge.
- 05 08_1b: Schnitt N-O is not a lip at the barrel cut: it runs from the balance opening to hole 51 along the balance holding work's pocket, and its "1,35 −0,05" is the pocket's depth. The radius round 51 reads "4ʳ" (r 4: the "7" is its arrowhead), as it scales (4.0) and Wempe 3955's pocket measures (4.0-4.2).
- 05 08_11: the pillar's body is Ø 8.1 −0.2 (the ∅ written high, as on its 4,3∅ and 4,5∅; 8.06 scaled), not 8.14 (P0's).
- 05 08_7: the "104 +0,1" is the dial's recess in the flange's face, 0.7 +0.1 deep, not the ring's seat (85 H9); "4,7" is the flange's height; the plan's 20° is the first screw hole's angle from 12, 6° the dial's pin hole's.
- 05 08_7 (15 October 2026): the plan's "1,6" is across the radial hole at 12 o'clock (a dashed pair across the ring), the section's "1,1 Ø" across the dial's pin hole (its dashed pair 4.7 off the axis, as 50.5 sin 6°): read until then the other way round, with the radial pin at 6 o'clock. The radial pin is moved and made Ø 1.6; the dial's pin hole and the dial's pin made Ø 1.1 with it.
- 05 08_8 (15 October 2026): the "8 Ø T6 +0,028/+0,019" is the tapered part above the flange (its dimension line runs across it, under the "Kegel 1:70" leader), the "8 Ø g6" the spigot below the flange (its extension lines run down the spigot's walls): the spigot slides into the plate's 8.0 +0.015 and the bush is held by its three screws; the dust bush's 8 H7 is pressed on the T6 taper. Read until then the other way round (the spigot pressed in).
- 05 08_13 (15 October 2026): the "5" is hole 48's distance from the short edge (its extension lines run parallel to the short edge) and the "7,7" hole 42's distance from the chord (its extension lines run parallel to the chord); read until then as the corner's offset from the chord and the distance along the chord to pin 41.
- 05 08_1b (15 October 2026): the pocket N-O's dashed edges converge toward 51: the common tangents of the opening's r 5 and 51's r 4, not a constant 8.
- 05 08_10 (15 October 2026): the section draws no counterbores at 38 and 35: a 45° mouth down to the bush at 38, and the setting at 35 filling the bridge's hole.
- 05 01_3 (15 October 2026): the closing plate's Ø 1.2 is drawn with its drill point inside the boss (blind), and the rim's underside meets the boss in a round.
- 05 08_14: the click's back is an arc r 12.5, as figured (fitted 12.4-12.5); the "85" by it isn't its radius.
- P0's `dims.js` said the Unterplatte's plan is drawn from the dial side with O to the left: it is drawn from its inner face with O to the right, as every plan of the group.

- 05 08_10 (Zwischenbrücke kompl., P1b): the WN-300 leader goes to the bush at 38 (the intermediate arbor's, its face 0.7 +0.02 in); the bearing at 35 is a jewel in a setting, its face 1.7 in (05 05 04-1, -2 in the coloured section). The transcription and P1a's comments had them the other way round (a jewel at 38, a WN-300 setting at 35).
- 05 08_32.2 (P1b): WN-302 is the bush at hole 1 in Schnitt A-B (the centre arbor's; the coloured section's Messinglager WN-302 there), its "0,1 +0,02" its set-down under the plate's inner face, its sink on the dial side; the transcription put it at hole 30. The long stud's 12.3 runs from its end to the plate's inner face (it is the stud's length); the short stud's 6.8 to the dial face. The short stud is at hole 19 (S 6.3: Schnitt A-B is a projection).
- 05 05_2, _5, _8 (P1b): the "6∅", "7∅" and "10∅" beside the wheels, which the transcription read as riveting collars, are the hubs, and the "0,2" each hub's standing proud of the rim; the block under the centre wheel on 05 05_8 is its pinion.
- 05 05_10 (P1b): the sheet is of 7.6.40 (checked 20.4.43), not revised in 1946 as the drawing list gives it; the journal "C" is e8 on 05 05_8 (05 05_10's letters unreadable, read "r6?" in the transcription). Both sheets write "Kegel 1:30" along the journal: a taper, built straight until 15 October 2026; the riveting collar is drawn flared over a groove in the seat, not a plain Ø 3.0 (built so until then).
- 05 04_1's elevation: the figure between the top plate and the escape wheel's teeth reads "0,5" (the transcription's "0,9"); with "1,4" it gives the wheel's teeth 1.4 thick, standing 0.6 over the web, 0.5 under the plate.
- 05 04 02: its 6.1 runs up from the hub's flange top, not from the staff's top end, and 14.03 down from it: the transcription's overall 20.13 (6.1 + 14.03) is not the staff's length (the blank scales about 28 long; the finished staff is 25.86 between its pivots here). Its 1.1 is the arm's seat's length over the flange, 1.8 the flange, 4.1 the hub's length, 5.6 the collar's bottom.
- 05 04_1's pointed piece standing on the pipe, 7.6 long with a 12° point, dashed where it passes under the block's arm: it is a pin through the earlier block's arm at the stone (Wempe 3955's block has a brass cone there, `11_Wempe_3955_Gangfeder_plus.jpg`), not part of the detent.
- 05 04 01-4: the "1,4 +0,1" is the upright's left edge in from the base's end; the base is 7.5 high at its end (25 − 17.5) and its top 15-15.4 from the upright's end beside the fillet.
- 05 03_2 and the coloured section: the timing screw is 05 03 01-2 and its weight -3 (the transcription has them the other way round).
- 05 03_1 (phase 2c): the lever chronometer's balance scales 33.4 across its rim against its masses' boxed Ø 6.35 (12 px/mm), cut at 3 and 9 with the masses 30° from the cuts' line: this balance, not the transcription's 28.7 mm one. Its "unterer" and "oberer Ansteckungspunkt" lead to 15° and 20°: the two pins nearly in line in plan.
- 05 03_7 (phase 2c): the two small circles inside the curve (0.25, −0.15 and −1.31, +0.16 mm from the coil's centre) are the compass's points for its two arcs (0-90°, r 5.94; 110-220°, r 4.35), not an origin for the radii, which are from the coil's centre.
- 05 03_2 (15 October 2026): the side view's two outlines of the masses aren't two heights. The upper half is a view, its near pair whole from the rim's lower face to 6.40; the lower half is a section through the arm, whose cut (1.15, hatched) hides the far pair's foot, so their outline starts 1.24 up. P1d built that outline (5.4 from 1.1).
- 05 06_20 (15 October 2026): "Zugfeder 15 × 0,37" is not overwritten; the 7 is the sheet's stencil 7 (with a bar across and a tick at the top left), as in its "12,17" and "9,71". The transcription's "the 7 slightly overwritten" is left as written.
- 05 04_1's pointed piece on the pipe and Wempe 3955's brass cone (15 October 2026): the tail of the earlier block's banking screw, whose domed head lies beside the stone on the detent's other side (`10_Vergleich_CWW-6MX_Gangfeder_mit_Kloben.jpg`), not a pin. 05 04 01-4's M1.2 across its slit upright is the 1943 block's banking screw.
- 05 03_5: "bottom coil to collet bottom 0,75" matches the collet's hole 0.75 from its face (05 03 02-2): the spring's inner end lies 0.75 over the collet's underside.

- 05 06_2.2, 05 06_15, 05 08_3a, 05 08_8 (P1c): the fusee arbor's pivot (Ø 2.8 ±0.02 to 2.7) and journal (4.5 ±0.02 to 4.39) can't run in the pillar plate's hole 10 (2.70 ±0.009) or the fusee bush's bore (4.1 +0.2): both are drawn opened to the arbor and 0.02, as reamed to it at assembly (frame.js; the coloured section draws the journal Ø 4.4 in the bush). The bush's body below its flange is drawn 2.6, not 2.7: at 2.7 it stood 0.05 proud of the J-K recess's floor, 19.65 from the pillar plate, under the arbor's 19.7 +0.05.
- 05 08_1b's J-K recess: Ø 22 at the face, its 45° wall to the floor Ø 20.6 (27 October 2026, as P1a read it: 05 08_1a's section drops the 22's extension line from the wall's corner on the face line). P1c read the 22 at the floor, for the fusee's cover plate, Ø 21.1, which stands only 0.275 into the 0.7 recess, where the wall is Ø 21.45.
- 05 06 00-3 (P1c): the bore's "4 +0,1" (scaled 4.0) can't pass the arbor's seat (4.38-4.5), and the tube's figure is overwritten ("4,6" or "6,6"); the 1941 assembly's fits (4.45 H7/g6 for the wheel on the arbor, 6.5 H7/g6 for the ratchet on the wheel) are taken.
- 05 07 00-3 (P1c; 15 October 2026): the "12,5" is a diameter, its line running across the circle through its centre (scaled 12.7), and that centre lies on the "6,4" circle (scaled 3.24 from the axis): each tooth's back is an arc r 6.25 centred about 3.2 from the axis, not r 12.5; the faces lie along lines 1.2 off the radius (10°), as built before. The transcription's "tooth back arc radius 12.5" is left as written.
- Fusee and maintaining work (15 October 2026, a survey against the sheets; each built so): 05 06_16's click wheel's faces are undercut, each tip overhanging its gap ahead of its root (the 8° line from a root runs inward past the tooth's own side of the centre), not leaning back. 05 06_12's maintaining ratchet is 1.3 −0.1 thick inside its cup and 1.02 at its teeth (the section draws the floor inside the cup higher), so "2,1" is the cup's rim over the plate outside it; revision (a)'s 0.3 +0.15 × 45° is on the cup's outer top edge. 05 06_14's click: the nose's face at 85° leans back toward the pivot; the base line is the r 3 hollow's chord, 1.52 under the hole, ending at the nose's foot 5.2 from it. 05 05_12's maintaining detent: its tip's end is straight, 2.6 over the line tangent to the boss's underside, at 80° to it, leaning back toward the hole, its lower corner on r 12 (not an arc on r 12). 05 06_7's fusee wheel: its underside recess's mouth is chamfered 60° from Ø 17 +0.2 (the angle from the axis: from the face it would run deeper than the recess). 05 06_8's cover plate: "0,4 −0,1" is the bore's straight land under the hole's 60° chamfer, which opens to the outer face, not a recess in the face on the body; its screw lies 11° from the stop bar's length toward its stop-pin end (05 06_4 C-D: the hole drawn 1.15 across, 5.87 along), on the side away from the bar; Wempe 3955's (8_…Räderwerk_plus.jpg) agrees within perspective (about 19°). 05 06_6's Vorreiber: the 0.3 × 45° is on the flange's hub-side face; face a carries only the Ø 9.9 recess. 05 07_8's hook: the pin's end is a ramp, flush to 0.5 at a radial face (15°), not a flat 0.5 proud. 05 07_14's chain hooks: the top hook is 5 to its crown and 3.5 wide with its nose turned back toward the chain (the model's 3.95 × 2.8 contradicted its own figures); the bottom hook's barb root is 3.5 from its last rivet and its tip points back toward the rivet, 1.4 past the shank, into the drum (it hooked forward, outward). 05 06_10's maintaining spring widens from 272° to 3.65 at its hole (295°) and 4 at its end, traced (3.4 at 288° estimated before).
- The transcription's maintaining spring angles (P1c): 05 06 01-1's 65° is measured from 0° the other way round from the widths' table (its square end lies at the lower left): its pin at 295° on the table's sense.
- The maintaining spring's pins (16 October 2026): the model pinned its eye to the fusee wheel and its other end to the ratchet, the ratchet's sheet turned 65° to meet it. 05 06_4's Schnitt A-B draws the eye's pin whole and labels it "Stift nach oben" (up: into the ratchet, which lies above the spring), the pin at 65° hidden (under the ratchet's plate: down, into the wheel), and the eye at 6 o'clock between the clicks at 9 and 3, where the ratchet's own sheet (05 06 02-1) puts its pin hole: the eye's pin is the ratchet's (sure). So the drive closes the ring onto its banking rather than opening it against the recess's wall (Physics: energy, The maintaining spring).
- The maintaining spring's eye's hole (16 October 2026): on its 0° line, as 05 06 01-1 draws it to scale, not 0.8 off it toward the square end (the transcription's reading of the sheet's "0,8": Unresolved); the ratchet's pin hole, at 6 o'clock on its own sheet, then lies under it with no turn of the ratchet's sheet.
- The maintaining spring's eye (16 October 2026): 05 06 01-1 gives "0,8" by its eye's hole, which the model took (P1c) as the hole's offset from the 0° line toward the square end; but the sheet, to scale there (the eye's r 1.8 scales 1.83), draws the hole on that line (0.06 off, measured on the scan), and the 0.8's leaders run to a line about 2 mm off it, so the hole is built on the line (fusee.js `EYO`). On it the running drive leaves the ring's ends 0.27-0.58 mm apart (Gould's "ends nearly meeting"); with the hole 0.8 toward the square end they would meet at 13.5° and the ring run closed onto them; 0.8 the other way, 1.1-1.4 mm apart (wempe_sustaining.js reports each). What the 0.8 measures isn't known. Its working, 11-14 minutes with the key held, is about the same in each.
- 05 06_9's handwritten "x = 0,27 mm" (020605): not the spring's deflection under the drive (its section gives 14-16°, 3.3-3.7 mm at its pins); perhaps a set or a gap to be kept; its other notes ("angespannt ... bis 4,5") are illegible.
- 05 05 05 (P1c): the detent's thin tip is flush with the face away from its lower shoulder (the kompl. sheet draws it so), not the lower face as transcribed from 05 05 05-1's section, which is drawn the other way up.
- 05 05_8 (P1e): the block on the cannon tube's foot is its pinion (14 leaves, Ø 5.86 at 5:1), 2.4 high (25.4 − 23), not a collar; the tube is steel (blue in the coloured section); its 14.3, 10.8 and 6.3 run from the C shoulder.
- 05 02_6 (P1e): the change pinion is 2.6 long (2.5 on 05 02_1) and Ø 7.3 across at 5:1 (67.4 px/mm by the 4.3 and 7), not the transcription's 2.0 and 5.2; the 4.3 +0.1 runs from the pipe's top to the pinion's lower face, with an undimensioned collar 0.6 over the wheel.
- 05 02_8 (P1e): the hour wheel's collar is Ø 7.9 (plan and section, 17.1 px/mm) and its bore 3.5, not the transcription's 6.1 and 3.2.
- 05 02_10 (P1e): the 5.2 runs from the wheel's lower face to the pipe's foot and the 2.8 from its upper face to the top.
- 05 02_1 (15 October 2026): its "1,2" over the hands is the minute hand's own (its top to its flange's underside, as 05 02 00-1 gives it), not the gap to the hour hand, which scales 0.49 (5.8 px at 11.87 px/mm); the model had the minute hand 1.2 over the hour hand until then. The studs scale Ø 1.52 there, both ends domed.
- 05 02_3 (15 October 2026): the hour hand's blade lies with its underside flush with the flange's, its top 0.3 under the flange's top (the side view), not flush with the top as built until then.
- 05 02_1 (P1e): the struck "15,8" (pencil "17,0") runs from the plate's dial face to the glass's underside, and the "3,5" (pencil "8,5" or "4,5") from the minute hand to the glass; the hour wheel there runs 4.0 from its lower face to its collar's top, 0.3 under the dial.
- 05 36 (P1e): the plan is the top plate's inner face seen from below, S up (a reflected similarity puts it on holes 23, 51 and 52 to 0.08 mm); part 27 (127-556.06 U 02) is the lever drawn dashed from the tongs to its pivot at 52, not the tongs; the dotted ring round the staff in Schnitt A-B is Ø 5.85, the closed tongs' opening; Schnitt A-B's cross-hatched ring under the collar's flange is the patent's brake ring of Preßstoff (18 October 2026), not the collar.
- 05 02_12 (P1e): the hole at 4 o'clock is Ø 1.1 under a sink Ø 1.8 H7 1.0 deep.

- 05 01 01 and R-05 01 01-2 (P1f): the transcription's 4.78 px/mm for Glasrand kompl. is right, but its ring then measures Ø 121 and 13.2 high: the 1941 finished bezel, not the 1942 casting's 125 and 16; the zigzag dashed in its lower bore at the left (a thread?) is not read.
- 05 01_7 (P1f): the key's handle is a flat round paddle Ø 34.5, 3 thick in the body's slot (the section draws it as a bar), not a ball; its "set into the body 6.7" is the paddle's edge in the slot.
- 05 01_8 (P1f): the "35 ∅" is an arc in the side view, not a spherical seat in the body's top face; what its "6,3 +0,1" measures (the slot's depth, scaled 6.6-7.2, or the cross hole's place) isn't settled.
- 51 00_2 (P1f): the 105 runs from the bottom to the line across the side, and the M6 is 11.5 under that line (93.5 over the bottom), not 105 over it.
- 05 01_1 (P1f): the ring's circles fitted on Schnitt A-B give Ø 139.2 and 146.8 to the lines' edges, so about Ø 140 inside and 146.6 outside (the transcription's 138 and 145.4).
- 05 04_23 (15 October 2026, the escape wheel's tooth, its lines traced and circles fitted at 82.8 px/mm, rms 0.25 px): the "11,7" is the gaps' floor, which the locking face runs straight down to, not a recess's edge; the "12,1" is the top of a 0.2 step at the back's foot, 15° (the sheet's "15°") behind the tip, not the root; the face leans 25° from the radius through the tip (the sheet's "25°", fitted 25.2), not 15°. The crossing's flanks are each one arc r 7.9 (fitted 7.93, 7.92) centred 9.43 out on the openings' middle lines: an opening is bounded by one such arc, not two meeting at a point.
- 05 04_7.2 (15 October 2026): the "2" at the top-plate end runs to the arbor's shoulder, 0.4 under the wheel; the wheel is placed by the "19" from the pinion end, its face 2.38 from the top-plate end. Its collet 05 04 10 is two steps, Ø 2.8 for 1.6 and Ø 1.7 on to 4.7, not a cone.
- 05 03_4 (15 October 2026): the "6,5" is the arm's width at the hub, where its two hollow arcs r 20 meet in a point (the arcs' centres outside the arm, the sheet marking one); it is 4 wide where they leave its straight flanks, about 6.1 from the centre.
- 05 03_8 (15 October 2026): the collet's ends are arcs about the bore, and the bore lies in the middle of its length; its slit stops 0.1 short of the bore.
- 02 04 02 (15 October 2026): the impulse stone's end is bevelled 45°, its back 1.3 long against the face's 1.8.
- 05 04 01-4 (15 October 2026): the view under the front view draws the base 6.5 deep only for 6.4 from its end and 3 deep beyond. The sheet reads as first-angle projection throughout (its side view shows the M1 hole in the end face as seen, its lower view the upright as seen from above, and only so does the Ø 1.3, 3 deep from the front face, meet the M1 2.3 from it): Unresolved.
- 05 08 05-2 (16 October 2026): the click's "85" is its beak's end face at 85° to the base, and the "5,7" the thin circle about the hole, tangent to the base, which so lies 2.85 under the hole (scaled 2.81): Closed.
- 05 05 03-2 (16 October 2026): the "1,6 −0,1" is the centre wheel's seat alone, not seat and riveting collar together: Closed.
- 05 05 05-1 (16 October 2026): the maintaining detent's step is a straight hidden edge from the back at 8.6 to the hollow, not an arc at 8.6 about the hole: Closed.
- 05 07 02 (16 October 2026): the barrel arbor's end view is seen from the square's end (first-angle), and so read the hook holds the spring with the arbor pulled as the crossed chain pulls it, its face on the other side from where it was built: Closed.
- 05 04_1 (16 October 2026): its "24°" is the escape wheel's pitch and its 1°, 21° and 2° the wheel's angles, not the balance's; the impulse face is drawn 28°10' before the line of centres (6° + 22°10'), where the model had it 24°: Closed.
- Wempe 3955's seconds numerals are radial, as the sheet draws them, not upright (16 October 2026): Closed.
- The coloured section read again against the model, cut through each pair of arbors at its scale (17 October 2026; the transcription corrected beside it):
  - The balance's hub screws (WN-10) go in from the arm's top, their countersunk heads flush in it, through the arm into the flange's M1 (px 1467-1524 × 314-341); built until then from under the flange, as the section was misread.
  - The dust plate's recess is in its face toward the key (the section; the sheet's 1:100 hole narrows toward it): built until then on the face toward the plates.
  - "Schrb. WN-16" is the banking screw's label (its leader to the slotted head on the 1941 block's arm); the balance's lower setting screws have their own, "Schraube WN-10" (px 1503-1525 × 488-506, missing from the transcription), which the model had given WN-16.
  - "Stellstift WN-205" is the detent foot's steady pins (the Ø 0.53 circles in the foot), "Stellstift 2 WN-201" the block's pins (the dashed Ø 1.0 under the foot, up through the top plate): the model had them the other way round.
  - #88's "WN-58 (or 38)" reads WN-52, the intermediate bridge's screw (built so); #20's "Stift 05 06 04-4" is the stop bar spring's pin in the fusee's body (05 06 04's parts list, piece 5, a 0.5 pin), not a small-end piece.
- The coloured section's open readings settled (18 October 2026), each against the dimensioned sheets and photographs:
  - The 1941 banking (WN-16) is a whole screw: its gilt domed head Ø 3.3 on the wheel's side of the pipe, which banks on it, and its pointed tail past the arm (05 04_1's plan, the section, Wempe 3955's 11_ and 10_ photographs): until then only the tail was built and the head taken for a dish on no sheet.
  - The upper cock has a boss round its setting, its face 3.2 from the top (05 08 03-1's "3,2 ±0,02"), the seat 2.7 deep to a floor bored 4.59, both settings in from the top (U30, the section).
  - WN-12a is the tapped hole in a mass's top (05 03_2's plan's double circle, the section's screw on one mass), not a nut; WN-12 threads through the outer jaw to the rim.
  - The stop bar's pin 05 06 03-2 is pressed across the bar, its spring bearing on it (05 06_4's C-D); the gold spring's screw head Ø 1.4 (photographs); WN-34 3.2 long (the section).
  - Read and found right as built: the hairspring collet (05 03_8: the section's is pictorial), the band's hook near the lid (the band's place fully wound), the 2.3 high steel at the maintaining spring's eye (its pin), the dashed "slot" in the drum's wall (the barrel bridge screw's end).
- 05 06 04-2's Schnitt C-D and 51 00 10 (25 October 2026, the per-part audit's batch 3): the body's three M2 holes stood 47° from where C-D draws them (an estimate, PHI0 + 0.3, never read off the view: cut so, the nearest would have broken into the cover plate's M1.7 6 deep beside it); and the handle's oval 70 × 30 was built as an aluminium pressing hung from the strap, where the section draws it as an unhatched hollow 10.5 deep in the hatched side wall, a finger recess behind the strap (both rebuilt).
- 05 01's three disagreements in the gimbals (25 October 2026; each read on the scan's pixel profiles, line centres, 2.45 px/mm, ±0.4 a reading; Unresolved until then):
  - The case brackets' boss: 9.0 wide (likely; 12.4 from Schnitt A-B until then). The elevation draws the 6 o'clock bracket face on (the pivots a left and right, the locking bracket at 50° standing where 46.7 off the axis puts it): its edge at 288.2-289.0 px from 4.7 over the pivots to 20.1 under, the boss and the crank one width, the axis 277.1-277.5 px by the bowl's two sides at that height (148.4-149.0, 405.8-406.0; the dash-dot C line leans 1.4 px over 105 px, which put it about 1 px right and gave 8.0 before): 10.8-11.4 px, 9.0 ±0.5. Schnitt A-B draws the boss 12.0 (its sides 260.4/289.7 px at 12 o'clock, 257.3/286.7 at 6; not 12.6) and the crank under it 12.1. A face-on outline can't be narrower than a level section, so no crank, chamfer or boss on a wider foot reconciles them: one view is drawn wrong. Kirow DEC 422's bracket face on (`11_Kirow_DEC_422_Gehäuse.jpg`: block 100 px, screws 332.5 px apart, heads 82.5) has the elevation's proportions (the block 0.30 of the screws' spacing and the head 0.83 of the block; the elevation 0.29 and 0.81, A-B 0.38 and 0.58), and Kirov 247's block is 8.7: the elevation's taken. The foot's screws 15.0 either side kept: the elevation's head, a circle fitted to it, 38.1 px off the axis at its face (r about 60), A-B's slots 37.6-37.9 px at r 58.5, both 15.0 at the foot's face (r 57.7) where the screws stand radially.
  - The "11,5": it is the box's, from the pivots to its parting line, not the ring's (sure). Its upper extension line (y 114.9 px) is the box wall's top in the elevation's section (115.1 px; 116.3 on the right), 0.9 under the ring's top (112.7); 51 00 10 puts the same M6 11.5 under the line 105 over the box's bottom. The ring is drawn centred on its pivots in both views (the elevation's top, axis and bottom 112.7, 142.0, 171.0 px at 9 o'clock and 113.9, 143.9, 173.0 at 3: 12.0/11.8 and 12.2/11.9): built ±12.0, its top 0.5 over the parting line (05 01 draws it about 1 over) and 6.7 over the bowl's (11.5 over the pivots and 12.5 under until then); the 6MX's lock lever, laid over the ring, rises with it (its underside 0.5 over the ring's top, estimated).
  - The outer pivots (WN-80): 05 01's form kept (likely). Its elevation and Schnitt A-B agree: the M6 (13.2-13.8 px, 5.4-5.6; A-B 6.1) from the wall through the bearing disc to the ring's outside, a tip Ø 3.25 (8 px; A-B 3.3) through the ring to its inside. 51 00 10 (2.40 px/mm) draws the M6 (6.2 in the panel) standing 4.1 past the wall's inside at 6.8, then a pin Ø 2.7 × 6.0, the head Ø 12.4 × 4.3: its pin reaches the ring's inside too (the 2.0 left past the disc and the ring's 4.0), so the two differ only in where the M6 stops and by the pin's 0.3-0.5. 51 00 10 is the handle's sheet, the screw only context (its M6 and the 4.8 drill dimensioned); 05 01 names the screw, draws it alike in two views and was reissued in 1946 and 1947; and 2 stood off either side would let the ring slide 4 between its pivots, against its "Lagerstellen a u. b leicht spielend". What would settle it: WN-80's own sheet or the ring's (05 01 00-5), not in the set.
- 0207's chain slot (25 October 2026; Unresolved until then, read as 45° from 6 o'clock and 180° from the rivet hole). On its plan ("Federhausdeckel abgenommen", 12.27 px/mm by the drum's outside and bore fitted as circles, rms 0.24 px, centre (154.7, 284.9) px) the Steg's notch is on the 12 o'clock centre line (0.3°), the M2.6 hole is "45°" from it, and the "Schlitz für Kette" lies on the same line at 6 o'clock: its walls 176.5° and 184.8° from 12 at the bore, 174.3° and 181.3° at the rim, so 180° ±2 (sure). The "45°" beside it is its lean through the wall: that dimension's arc is centred on the slot's wall where it meets the rim (radii 58 and 60 px about (150, 537)), not on the drum's centre (192 and 215 px), its arms the 6 o'clock line and the wall produced. The section, cut along that line, draws the Steg (106) under the wall at the top and the slot through the wall at the bottom, 10.4 px (0.87: the "0,85") along the axis, its middle 4.66 from the bottom's outer face. So the slot stands 180° from the Steg and 135° from the rivet hole; the hole 45° from the Steg on the side the strip's end needs (the strip comes back to the block from the Steg, the drum running rotation.y falling; 05 07 01-1 the same), so the slot is the hole + 135° (rotation.y rising). The slot is the chain's (cut at the fusee's assembly where the laid chain's barb comes, -19.57°, the drum standing at 0 fully wound), so the drum's insides were turned to it: the rivet hole at BLK 3.585 (the slot + 5π/4; 1.2, an estimate, until then, the slot 88° from it), the hook block, the Steg and the bottom's and lid's notches for it, the 1941 drum's slots, the mainspring and the arbor's hook, all together (+136.6°). Nothing re-solved: the solve holds the strip's end and the Steg relative to each other (tools/wempe_mainspring.py's BLOCK, STEG; mainspring.py's bounds() reckons from the strip's end), and the page draws the spring from the arbor's hook (HKA), which follows BLK; fusee.js logs an error if the slot moves off BLK − 225°. The slot is cut at that lean (25 October 2026; radial until then): 45° to the radius, leaning back toward the chain's run going in as the bottom hook's barb does (its 50° flank), its walls 2.5 apart round the drum at the rim (1.77 across), 0.85 along the axis, its middle line through the wall's middle at the barb's angle, in both drums for both connectors (fusee.js, DRUM); 0207's walls are drawn freehand (48° and about 30° on the plan), the dimension decides. 0207 is the drum of 1941; the later sheets don't draw the slot (likely for them: the lid's 3 o'clock notch of 05 07 01-2, opposite the Steg, then stands at its angle). The band's slot (no sheet) stands 162° from the Steg.
- 05 06_4's Schnitt C-D, the stop work's figures (27 October 2026; Unresolved since the drawings check, which read "2" from the stop pin's R 14 and "12" and "17,6" as radii, so the bar's ends on r 12 against the 22,5 bar). On the sheet at 7-12× (the scan 12.2 px/mm by the 22,5): the "2" runs from the thick circle, the hub Ø 21, out to a thin arc through the bar's upper far corner, so that corner stands on r 12.5 (the model's 12.51); the "12" is "1,2", from the hub's circle in to a thin arc at r 9.3, the top land's floor, where the bar's lower end lies at full wind (the model's 9.32 there); the "176" is "1,76", from the hub out to the thin arc through the lower point running, r 12.26 (the model's 12.36, set by the 22,5 and the "2" together: the sheet's own 0.1). The "3,2" is the lower end's way between C-D's two positions, running and "im aufgezogenen Zustand": drawn 3.6 (the model's limit, where the spring's wound shape is traced), and the slide the chain gives at full wind is 3.36 (`wempe_stopwork.py`), 3.26 with the lower point at the 1,76: the figure is the stop's own slide, the same 0.1 again. So the figures agree with the bar as drawn to the sheet's 0.1, and the model keeps the drawn bar (sure of the readings: the arrows end on the hub's line).
- 05 06 01-1's maintaining spring near its eye and in its swell (27 October 2026; Unresolved since the drawings check: the eye's join to the band not drawn, the swell's r 6 sampled at 4° and joined straight). Traced along rays from the ring's centre (`tools/wempe_maintspring.py`, 17.26 px/mm by the ring's two circles, rms 0.016 and 0.035): the band is not 2 wide up to the eye but widens toward it, its inner edge an arc r 10.40 (rms 0.005 over 11-40° from the eye) tangent to the ring's inner circle about 50° from the eye, meeting the eye's round in a shallow corner (0.08 into it); the swell is the "6" about the small circle drawn on the 9 o'clock line (8.125 from the axis, tangent to the inner circle at 270°; the trace within 0.02 of it over 278-293.5°); from its corner (293.9°) to the square end an arc r 13.24 (rms 0.004 over 295-335°) through the "4". The outer edge runs into the eye's round with no step on both sheets, its line coming in over the 60° before the eye (0.17 inside its own radius at 10°, 0.07 at 60°, the drawn ring wandering ±0.08); built as an arc tangent to the ring 60° from the eye and to the eye's round (estimated: the angle). The spring's stiffness is unchanged to the check's tolerance (`wempe_sustaining.js`: 1.2204 ψ + 0.2960 ψ² N·m, escphys.js's 1.2204 and 0.2956), its eye and square end, so its banking, as before.
- 05 06 02-2's click springs have two holes (27 October 2026): the "0,6∅" across the end, between two dashed lines 18° from the figured hole (5 from the end), is a second hole Ø 0.6, on the cup's 42° pin, which 05 06 02 draws through the spring as the 60° pin is (the transcription had it "the end's width or a second hole"). Both cut, on the strip's middle (the "0,8 −0,05" from its edge at its lower limit).
- 05 06 02-1's revision (a) (27 October 2026): "Maß 25,4∅ gestr.; 0,3 +0,15 neu" replaced the chamfer's top diameter by its size, 26.1 − 2 × 0.35 = 25.4: the chamfer is the same in both issues (built so, its top Ø 25.4 on the drawn cup), and the first issue differs only in its tips, Ø 37.5 for 37.25 (the drawings check had it "no chamfer"). Built as first drawn (its tolerance not kept: the revised −0.1 taken, drawn 37.45), the maintaining detent seated on its teeth in that version.

- **The barrel's open items from the drawings check** (27 October 2026; Unresolved until then):
  - The drum's seat 1.2 deep, the lid 1.2 (its 1,3 −0,1 at its least) and flush. 05 07 01-1's revision a (11.3.48) put the seat's "1,2 −0,1" in brackets ("Maß 1,2-0,1 in Klammern"): the chain 18,1 − 13,8 − 3,1 fixes it, and its 13,8 −0,05 from the seat's floor to the boss holds only on a 1.2 seat (13.78; 13.68 on the 1.3 seat cut since 26 October). The lid then lies on the rim at the bottom of its tolerance, as 05 07 01 draws it; the spring's space 15.63, its middle 0.015 off the hook block's (sure for the seat, likely for the lid's thickness: a 1.3 lid would stand 0.1 proud, which no view shows at 2:1).
  - The 1943 arbor 40.2 over all (05 07 02's 40,2 ±0,1 with its 5 and 10): the bridge pivot 2.93 (3.38 until now, 40.65 over all), the square starting 0.42 inside the barrel bridge's hole, the set-up ratchet pressed down its 1:100 taper to 0.05 off the bridge as before (likely: the sheet scales 40.4, not to scale).
  - The arbor's hook 05 07 02-2: a head on the Ø 2 pin, its radial face 15° off the pin's centre line (the end view unwrapped about the core: the face at 15.5°, the land falling evenly to flush 4-5° past the line), 0,5 −0,05 high (drawn 0.475), 2 along the axis; wider than the pin as the 1941 hook's 3-wide head (0207). The mainspring's eye bears on the face, the strip unchanged (its tip moves with the face, the hook turns 6.4° to it). Until now the pin's own end ramped across its 2 (the face its flat side, 8.6° off, 0.545 proud at its edges). The head's sheet (05 07 02-2) isn't in the set: its 20° span and the land's evenness are the end view's (likely).
  - The band's hooks 05 07 03-2 and -3, from their sheets (above, The band): placeholders about half the size until now. Read on the sheets: the "7r" at each one's bottom is "1r" (the 2,5 and 1,4 / 1,0 put its centre, the throat's wall and the left wall meet it tangent, the traced arcs 1.01 ±0.002); 05 07 03-2's bracketed crown "(1,5r)" is drawn r 2.08 and its "2,8" below the back's centre is drawn 1.70 (the drawing kept for both, its "6r" and "3,55" agreeing with it); its "30°" is 31.7 between the nose and the seat its figures place; 05 07 03-3's back, tangent to its tip at the figures (r 10 about 1,2 below), lies 0.10-0.15 inside the drawn line (the figures kept).

## Unresolved

- **makeEsc's work stepped by its own ride steps** (27 October 2026, the owner's request to improve the isochronism; `tools/wempe_cliff.js`): the 0.29 s a day dynamics.js's cross-check found over the wind was makeEsc's, not the chronometer's. Its ride through the impulse timed each landing of the stone on the escaping tooth's back at the end of a quarter table step, which stepped the work 1-3 % about a degree apart and held the full-wind swing at 200.9° where dynamics.js runs at 204°; refined to 32-128 steps it converges on 203.9°. Each landing is now timed inside its step, the ride stepped eight times a table step (`RSR`) and the swing solved on the work averaged over ±0.5° (`SMW`): half the 0.98° the impulse raises the swing within each beat, since a real dip half a degree wide (201.5-202° at full wind) remains, which the balance crosses every beat. The swing over the wind is now 192.5-203.9° (197.4° at 12 h), the escapement's change over it +0.93 s a day against dynamics.js's +0.84 (−0.08 apart, the check back to 0.2), and with the drawn hairspring (HS −0.485) the chronometer's rate over the wind −0.14 to +0.24 s a day. What remains is the chronometer's own: the escapement gains about 0.8 s a day for each 10° and the drawn spring loses 0.49. Averaging changed nothing at 4 steps (the steps there are deeper than the window). The page reads the swing off `WBANK.amp` (`tools/wempe_banking.js`), a solve taking about a second.
- **The let-off in heat** (27 October 2026; `tools/wempe_thermal_frame.js`, the report's section 9, "The frame, train and escapement in heat and cold"). Over the TU's 4-36 °C the train's depths, the endshakes, the depth of lock and the chain move under 4 µm, but the steel detent grows less than the brass frame that holds its foot, so the gold spring's tip draws 1.8-2.3 µm away from the staff at 36 °C (the published constants' middle and the corner that moves it most), taking 0.3-0.45° of the drawn 1.50° let-off. At about 1.0-1.1° the escapement loses its impulse (the tooth runs a pitch before it lands on the impulse stone: makeEsc, and `dynamics.js --model wempe` in time on the same settings, the swing 91-97°): the same edge that tops `wempe_tolerances.js`'s lock window (its deepest lock lets off at 1.12°). The drawn lock (235 µm) holds at the constants' middle and not at the corner; the bench's (the window's middle, 238 µm) fails at 36 °C at both. The window that holds over 4-36 °C is 231.7-237.9 µm at the middle (6.2 µm wide against 12.7 at 20 °C), none at the corner. Left for the owner's choice: the bench's aim moved toward the floor, the window narrowed in `wempe_tolerances.js`, or more let-off (the discharge stone's reach or the gold spring's tip, both estimated where the sheets don't size them). The escapement's and drive's share of the rate is small (C +0.008 s a day a °C, S +0.09 s at the middle).
- **The stone's blow on the escaping tooth's back, on a cliff** (26 October 2026; the owner's choice: recorded, the transmission check's bracket reported). With the escape wheel as drawn (its web 0.7: the arbor 8.28 g·mm², JW 9.53), the locking stone, flung off the discharge stone and falling back, catches the escaping tooth's back 0.6 µm above its banking just before the back's end passes (13.47° of the balance, the back running away at about 0.29 m/s); its plastic landing pushes the wheel and gives the balance 0.0024 k a beat, 3.4 % of the impulse, 2.4° of swing. Whether it lands at all turns on a microsecond: with 0.25 g·mm² more at the escape wheel (8.82 and up) the tooth has passed first and the blow is gone. makeEsc counts it since then (`RWD`, Wempe only: the Hamilton's figures bit for bit), and agrees with `dynamics.js` within 1.2° and 0.3 s a day over 8.0-8.9; the meshed train in `wempe_transmission.js` lands as a rigid train of about 10.2, past the step, so its impulse work falls outside the two rigid trains it is bracketed by (reported, `~~`). Until then makeEsc and the time model agreed only because at the old inertia (8.73) the blow had nearly vanished. The landing's plasticity and the back's end are estimates; a made Wempe's swing could sit either side of the step. Since 27 October 2026 (`tools/wempe_cliff.js`, which maps the work's steps about the swing at each drive and the beat's closure over the wind): with the blade centred the swing was held on that step at every drive (the step within 0.2-0.4° of the solved swing at 90-105 %, the beat closing 0.955-1.058 over the wind; wempe_escapement.js checks 12 h alone, 1.013), a stable pinning (under the step the blow adds work and the swing rises, over it the swing falls). With the blade built and the discharge stone's reach taking the gold spring's tip's slide about the offset flexure (Readings corrected) the step lies 3.5-5° under the swing at every drive from 85 to 105 % and the beat closes 0.97-1.01 over the wind (1.000 at 12 h); what is left is the stone's hops on the back through the impulse, a roughness of about 3 % (the swing at full wind held on one at 200.9°). The 6MX's heavier escape wheel (JW 10.45) puts its swing on such a hop's step at every drive (its beat 0.95-1.06; unchecked by the tools; clean with the blade centred).
- **Left open by the drawings check** (26 October 2026, PLAN-wempe-drawing-check.md; each batch's report in `drawing-check/`, the ledger WEMPE-DRAWING-LEDGER.md). Corrected meanwhile are listed in RESOLVED.md; these are the readings no sheet settles, the sheets' own conflicts, and what the model can't yet build:
  - *Kept, the sheets' own conflicts* (27 October 2026, the open items settled): the set-up click's 17,4 overall against 17.53 from its 9,7, 7,5, r 1 and the base line (05 08 05-2: the figures drawn); the stop work's C-D (05 06_4) read from the hub Ø 21, its "1,76" and "3,2" 0.1 off the bar the 22,5 and the "2" fix (the drawn bar kept); the block screw on the plate table's hole 13 (23.41, Wempe 3955 23.36) against 05 04_1's 23,8; the Lange dial's own disc (1453/A) not built, the variant the printing only; the stud's round point and pin hole (the blank's, drilled at assembly); the bowl's tapped holes for the brackets' screws (embed). Each sheet's reading in the ledger WEMPE-DRAWING-LEDGER.md.
  - *Readings not settled:* the lid plan's Ø 6.4 circle (05 07 01-2).
  - *Built short of the sheet, the tools' limits:* the 1943 barrel arbor's hook hole right through its core (05 07 02; drawn 6 deep, as the 1941 one: a hole through a turned core's axis comes out of bore() open); the radial seat for the masses' screws WN-12 (their heads' faces follow the mass's round side instead); the blind pin holes' drill-point ends in the cocks and bridges (solidGeo can't close a blind cone from that side); the upper cock's setting screws' holes plain where they pass through the boss (they leave through its side); the first-drawn block (05 04_1's) not built apart; seams where fused pieces meet face to face (the fusee bush, the ratchet's cup, the detent's pieces).
- **The escape pinion's peak load at the wheel's landing is the time step's, not a load** (27 October 2026, with the detent blade as drawn): `wempe_transmission.js`'s seconds wheel → escape pinion peak, as a multiple of its running force, over the 8 starts: 43-139× at dynamics.js's step, 41-2,061× at half of it and 55-126,000× (8 kN) at a quarter (`MC_TR_H`), where the other three meshes give the same at every step (1.0-2.7×). It grows as the step shrinks and scatters by orders between starts: dynamics.js lands the escape wheel on its stone dead (its speed reset in one step), and the seconds wheel's tooth, 170 µm of backlash behind it through a stiff mesh, strikes within that step or not. The impact itself, v·√(k·m) (the wheel at the lock about 37 rad/s, 0.049 m/s at the pinion's pitch radius 1.32; the seconds arbor reflected 0.72 g; the mesh 1,624 N/mm), is about 1.7 N, 25× running, about 51 MPa fully wound against brass's 120-170 MPa endurance (estimated, not a tool's output). The check judges start 0 at the default step (passes). Left for a later change: judge an impact from the mesh's stiffness and the relative speed, or the force averaged over its contact (π√(m/k), about 66 µs), require a peak to agree at half the step before it is judged as a load, and report all 8 starts.
- **The rigid train against the train on its meshes** (19 October 2026, the physics pass; traced 25 October 2026): `wempe_transmission.js`, the whole transmission as bodies on its meshes, gives an impulse 5.1 % more work than the rigid train makeEsc, dynamics.js and the page take (3.8 % before the per-part audit's first batch reweighed the bodies; 2.1 % with the lock at 0.175, the preload 0.63 g and TF 108 s); as a drive it keeps the swing 6.2° higher than the rigid train's at 12 h. **The cause is the train's backlash, not its meshes' give**: stiffer meshes don't close the gap (×100 at a quarter of dynamics.js's step, which stiff meshes need: +4.5 %; ×0.5 +6.2 %; as drawn +5.1 % at either step), while the last mesh, the seconds wheel on the escape pinion, lets go about 1 % of the time: the escape wheel lands dead on its stone, the seconds wheel runs on into the backlash (170 µm), and at the release the escape wheel starts free of the train behind it. The tool now runs both rigid bounds, the whole train's inertia at the escape wheel (10.01 g·mm², makeEsc's JW 9.98) and the escape arbor's alone (8.73): 11.655 and 13.560 µJ, the meshes' 12.254 between them, 31.5 % of the way (a check), as a rigid train of about 9.68 g·mm² (9.61 interpolated linearly; a rigid twin at 9.61 comes within 1.1 %). Left for the owner's choice: makeEsc's Wempe JW at that equivalent inertia would raise the model's swing by about 6° (escgolden.js untouched: a Wempe setting); the drawn backlash is the teeth's own, so the meshed train is the better model of it. The check stays at 8° and 0.5 s a day until then.

- Wempe 3955's closing plate (`16_…Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`) shows two holes about Ø 1 in the boss's face, at r 11.2 and 11.9 and about 160° apart about its screw (not opposite: 16 October 2026), where 05 01 00-2 draws one Ø 1.2 at 12.4, blind from the other side (built as the sheet). They look added on that chronometer (a pin spanner's, perhaps: moderately sure). The 6MX has no such plate (a sliding shutter; Kirow 422's bottom, `11_Kirow_DEC_422_Gehäuse.jpg`, a flat disc); GUB 11307's (Rwr93vdA8cw 9:34, 9:48, about 9 px/mm) has a plain boss face round its screw, as the sheet's blind hole would leave it. Another wartime bowl's back would settle it.
- As first drawn: 05 04_7.1 draws the escape wheel's recess Ø 11.9 (altered to 12.4 in pencil), 0.7 deep, on the plate's side, as 05 04_1's elevation has it (its dashed walls run from the floor to the wheel's face toward the "2" end, the plate's: 16 October 2026; read as the pinion's side until then), the walls scaling Ø 11.8; against the 1942 tooth form's floor Ø 11.7, which 11.8-12.4 would cut: the wheel is drawn as 05 04_1 and 05 04_23 have it. The collar's boxed 4.7 puts the impulse roller 0.6 under the plate where 05 04_1 scales 0.39-0.42 (the figures built). The pipe on the stone (1.95-3.65 under the plate, estimated): 05 04_1's elevation draws it dashed from 2.26 to 4.35, 1.04 below the blade, the stone hatched from 0.33 down inside it (24.4 px/mm; likely), as 1436's right view does (2 (1.9) long, past the blade's pillar-plate side); built so, its lower end would run 0.65 into the block's arm as built (3.7-6.5 under the plate: `fine.py`), so the arm's height or its near face's place in plan is also in question: kept as estimated. The 1941 banking screw banks the pipe's lower end (likely: its slotted head, Ø 2.28 at 5.17 under the plate on the arm's slit, covers the pipe's last 0.3 in the elevation; Wempe 3955's domed end just under the blade). Wempe 3955's escape wheel collet (10_Wempe_3955_Feder_plus.jpg, 11.8 px/mm by the wheel's Ø 14) is one gilt cone about 6.4 long, Ø 3.4 to 1.3, like the later 05 04_7.2's, where 05 04_7.1 draws a cone Ø 3.0 over 1.8 and a pipe Ø 1.8 to 5: built as the sheet (moderately sure of the photograph's reading).
- The pillar plate's thickness (struck, above): the replacement figure on 05 08_03a stays illegible enhanced (16 October 2026); every photograph has the plate in its ring and the GUB videos show it from above only. A photograph of its edge out of the ring would settle it.
- Whether the movement ring's screws bear on the plate (above): on Wempe 3955 (4_N, 6_S, 7_W, 8_Räderwerk, 8-11 px/mm) the brass heads sit at the plate's rim, 6_S's far half inside its top edge (consistent with bearing), but the 0.35 gap and a flat on the head aren't resolved; the 6MX's steel heads stand on another ring (6_Kirow_DEC_422_S). A close side view at the rim would settle it.
