- Wempe 3955's seconds wheel (`8_…Räderwerk_plus.jpg`) shows a raised ring Ø 6.4 ±0.3 on its train-side face (54 px against the tips' 188 for Ø 22.45, 8.37 px/mm: 16 October 2026), the sheet's hub Ø 6, so likely the hub proud on the face away from its pinion, where 05 05 01 draws it proud on the pinion's side (built as drawn: proud on the train side it would meet the intermediate pinion's end). The 6MX's wheel (6_Kirow_DEC_422_S) has its spokes running into a small boss about Ø 3.7 and no such ring. The wheel's other face or a side view would settle it.
- The escapement of 1943's block against its sheet (17 October 2026; The escapement of 1943 above). Kirow DEC 422, DEC 479 and Lange 5177 all show the upright's slit parallel to the plate, along the banking screw, where 05 04 01-4 draws it upright and across the screw. The slit shows 2.4-3.5 past the screw on them (the arm's length scales 16.5, 15.6 and 13.1 px/mm along it), against the sheet's 4.55 (7 from the end, the screw 2.45 in); the slit's end may fade before it stops, so the sheet's 7 is kept. A side view of a loose 1943 block, or one from its end, would settle both.
- The detent's level against the upright (17 October 2026). On the three German photographs the detent's blade and gold spring stand 0.7, 1.07 and 1.2 over the upright's top (DEC 479, DEC 422, Lange 5177; the blade's 0.9 checks DEC 479's scale). The head's top stands 0.6-1.1 under them. The sheets put the detent's lower face (3.3 under the plate, 05 04_1's 1.3-3.3) 0.2 over the upright (3.5, 05 04 01-4 in first angle), and the escapement of 1943 is built so, its head's top 0.15 under the detent. Either the photographed detent hangs about 0.5-1.0 higher than 05 04_1's "1,3" puts it, or the upright lower; DEC 479's base (6.5 deep, flush with the upright as drawn) reads the upright 3.8-6.7 under the plate (±0.3 with the camera). The screw's underside reads about 2.8-3.8 from the upright's near face on 5177 and DEC 479, the 6MX's loose block 2.8; drawn 2.28, where the model's pipe is. A photograph of the detent from its end, the plate's face in view, would settle the levels.
- The detent's banking and the detent's level: closed 27 October 2026 (Readings corrected: the 1946 staff). The block's side view is first-angle, as its sheet reads and every side photograph of the block in place has it (the 6MX's Kirow 92, KirPol 24020, Wempe 3955's 1941 block, Kirow DEC 422 and 479, Lange 5177; and Wempe No. 9166 of 1972: below), the upright 3.5-6.5 under the plate, the M1.2 banking screw 5.0 and the foot 1.35-3.25; the 1946 staff's rollers were misread, so nothing stands against those heights. Its setting, the detent's preload (Physics: in time): the 6MX's detent rests about 2° out of line with its foot toward its banking (a set spring), but no photograph shows its free line; the estimate is kept (Building it).
- 05 04_1's figures not identified: its 5° (beside the 6°, to about 17° before the line of centres), 20°, the 1° by the gold spring's tip, "1° – 1/8°", the elevation's 2.7 and 2.1, and its 2.3, 1.3 and 6.5 (the plate to the strip's lower edge, scaling 3.30, 1.31 and 6.54). The "0.9" is the "0,5" already corrected (Readings corrected). The sheet's impulse ends at the wheel's +10.5°, the stone's acting end at about r 3.365, where the model's SIMP 3.33 (05 04_6's 1.55 + 1.8) ends it about 9.9° and runs 2.6° on to the lock against the sheet's 2°.
- 1436's bracketed figures (above: taken as the revised sizes): the square's "(0,09)" bracketed alone, the "0,07" with faint brackets whose contents can't be read; the faint "0,18 u.d.f." beside the spring reads "v.d.f." or "v.d.h." (the third letter tall with a descender loop: f or a Kurrent h), its meaning unsettled. Its "4,4" (the horn to the pipe's centre, from the top line) is taken against the 5.2 its drawing scales: Wempe 3955's detent measures 4.4-4.55.
- 05 04_7.2's wheel seat: its callout reads 1.8 H7/h6, its fit table 1.7 (1.7 built). The callout's extension lines, drawn from the wheel's dashed bore, scale 2.0 apart (17.3-17.6 px/mm) and the table's +0.009/−0.007 fall in the same ISA range for both; the 1941 sheets give 1.8 (05 04_1's bore, 05 04_7.1's collet). The collet 05 04 10's own sheet would settle it.
- The swing's figure for the Einheits-Chronometer: none in the set; the model's, 188.5-199.5° (Physics; 191.9-202.0° before 19 October 2026's physics pass, 194.1-204.2° before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles, 201.5-212.1° before 15 October 2026's shape batch and TF 108 s, 208-219° until the masses' height was read), rests on the damping a running 6MX's video gives and the detent's preload (phase 2d: unpreloaded it would swing 6° more), 14 % under the 1954 instruction's 225-270°. It lies under the sources' band, 225-270° (the 1954 Soviet instruction, for chronometers in general), by 25.5-36.5° (23-33° before the physics pass; the train on its meshes would take about 4.6° of it back: above): with the damping now measured on a running 6MX (102-125 s at the model's swing, the model set to it), the drive's level is the likelier cause (05 06_20's measured pull is 0.80 of the solved 0.37 strip's: Physics, energy). tools/wempe_escapement.js reports it (~~); its own 180-270° check is only a guard against the model's swing falling. A 6MX on video swings 110-185° part wound, out of its bowl (References/wempe/VIDEOS.md, "Measured"): its damping bears out the model's TF, its swing says nothing of a wound Wempe's. The clicks of a running Lange VEB (gnIP3NqNbEU) and a Kirova (KxInG08LWXg), read with this escapement in time (`acoustic.py --model wempe`), leave the swing between readings 80-120° apart (which click is which isn't settled).
- **The Soviet design and repair figures against the model** (16 October 2026, recorded, nothing changed; `References/wempe/photos/catalogue-2026-10-16.md`, each read again off the scans). Belyakov's *Часовые механизмы* (1957), Table 3, gives the 1st Moscow factory's MX-2 with "Lange's MX" in brackets (balance Ø 32.25, plate Ø 85); the Navy's *Эксплуатация и ремонт измерителей времени* (1958) gives the MX's assembly checks and plays. Which Lange the brackets describe (wartime or post-war) isn't said, and the drawings decide for the drawn chronometer; they bear on the readings below, and on the 6MX.
  - *Agreeing*: the wheel's 15 teeth, Ø 14 and 25° undercut; the balance's lift 42°18' (42° here); the detent spring 5.0 long and 2.0 wide (1436's 2, bracketed 1.9, built); the discharge circle r 1.55 (1.54-1.56 here); the hairspring's calculated length 502.35 mm in 12.78 working turns (505.4 pin to pin here, 05 03_7's 13 turns less 3.5 mm); the barrel's torque falling 1.84:1 over two days (1.81:1 here).
  - *The depth of lock*: 05 04_1's 0.235 built since 19 October 2026 (the sheet re-scaled on the tips' Ø 14.00: The physics pass; 0.175 before), inside the 1958 book's check, the tooth onto the stone 0.20-0.25, and near Belyakov's linear lock 0.22 (Lange), 0.210 (MX-2); it unlocks to 0.247 (Building it; until then it unlocked only to 0.188, under them). But Belyakov's radii don't close with the centre distance for either chronometer as the models build it (the Wempe's wheel r 7 and impulse circle r 3.8 less the teeth's 0.39 into it make 10.41 against 05 04_1's 10.00; the Hamilton's Table 1, 6.575 + 3.70 − 0.3 = 9.98 against 9.40), so his linear figures may be measured another way (his "lock δ 1°" at the detent's locking arm, 14.25, is an arc of 0.25): settle how before using them.
  - *The detent spring's thickness*: 1436's 0.07 (0.09 at its ends) built; Belyakov 0.05 at the bend, for both. Its stiffness hardly moves the swing (Building it); it moves the detent's ring and return.
  - *The gold spring's thickness*: 05 04 01-2's 0.15 built (as the spring's section in the physics); Belyakov 0.06 (Lange), 0.05 (MX-2), at its bend; his Hamilton's 0.045. 0.06 would make it about 15 times softer, where half as stiff already lets a held tooth's stone ring 64 % of the depth of lock (`wempe_tripping.js`; 51 % with the straight horn). The sheet's 0.15 may be the blank, thinned at the bend as a passing spring usually is: the most consequential of these.
  - *The gold spring's reach*: the 1958 book's check puts its end 0.35-0.40 past the detent's end, and at rest 0.10-0.15 short of the discharge stone; here the tip stands 0.056 past the horn's end radially (the horn 4.4 long), and on both photographed detents just past it along the horn (under 0.3). Different measures; not a clear disagreement.
  - *The impulse radius*: 05 04_1's 3.35 built; Belyakov 3.8 (Lange), 3.435 (MX-2): see the depth of lock on his radii.
  - *Endshake*: the 1958 book's MX: the fusee 0.08-0.12, the barrel, centre, intermediate and seconds 0.07-0.10, the escape arbor 0.05-0.07 (built 0.06, its middle, in every version since 25 October 2026, the 6MX's the book's own: 0.04 before), the balance 0.04-0.06 (side shake 0.005-0.03); the bench takes them since 19 October 2026 (0.01-0.08 estimated before; see Closed, Endshake).
  - *The mainspring's torque*: the 1958 book's MX, 1.01-1.08 N·m fully wound and 0.55-0.60 two days on, against 839 and 427 mN·m here (824 and 473 with the free curve at ×0.6, until 26 October 2026) (the solved shape at 05 06_20's level); the later 6MX's strip is 0.42 × 14.8 × 1400 (Tarasov 1976) against 05 06_20's 0.37 × 15, so not the same spring.

- The stop work (P1c; full wind settled 15 October 2026, Readings corrected): N's undimensioned angle on 02-2 (only a leader to the groove's end) lies 0.07 turn past the model's groove end (0.08 along the axis); searched without result: Wempe 3955's train photograph (about 7 px/mm on the cover plate: the top land runs on unbroken along the front arc between the bar's two ends, the rest hidden by the arbor and the plate) and the GUB No. 12255 restoration, ZEc91vwppVk 3:19-3:55 (the fusee out in the hands; its small end only obliquely and blurred, at 3:34). Nothing works in the groove's last tenth of a turn, so it stays as built. The stop pin's length was scaled off the coloured section (1.85, the stop work). A fusee's small end opened would settle N (REQUESTS.md).
- The mainspring's length to better than ±50 mm (P1c, 2b), and the barrel's 15.8 framed spring space (0207, 1941) against the 1948 drum's 14.95 (its level against 05 06_20 closed 26 October 2026: Closed). No 6MX document gives the spring's sizes or torque (6MX-figures.md), the gallery's "Feder" photographs are of the detent's spring, and Wempe 3955's laid-out spring (`14_Wempe_3955_Band_Kette.jpg`) has no scale for its thickness; UhrenJust's GUB service (pbL64JZ-LGM 9:05-10:48, UG44DSWMv3o 0:00-6:46, 2160p, the spring out) or a measured 6MX spring might. For a made Wempe the free run-down test chooses the strip (Building it).
- The barrel arbor as last revised (05 07 02-1) isn't in the set (P1c): its diameters and core are fitted to the plates and drum; no photograph or video shows the arbor out.
- The hour wheel's collar (05 02 03's 5.2 against the 1943 cannon tube: above), and the hands' heights with the 1946 seconds post: every dial photograph (Wempe 3955, Lange 5177, the Kirows) and GUB aQmO0UtXs24 0:00-2:10 is taken from above; a side view of the hands would settle it.
- The long stud's end (05 08 02's Schnitt A-B at 2:1 draws it about 0.4 short of the plate's inner face, 2 px; 05 02_1 at 5:1, of the 1941 plate, to the face in a 45° sink, and the up-and-down pipe's heights agree with 12.3 from the face to 0.05): built to the face. No photograph shows the dial side in profile.
- The balance holding device (05 36), not built (the user's decision, 18 October 2026). How it works is Wempe's patent DE 742 143 (John Schwarzer, filed 26 November 1941, `References/wempe/documents/`, figs. 1-4): two brass tongs (36 00 01-1, every figure) on one screw at hole 51 in the top plate's pocket, pressed shut by a leaf spring round them (the dark band 05 36's plan draws round their backs and bosses), close concentrically on a brake ring on the staff ("Bremsring aus Preßstoff", moulded phenolic: 05 36's Schnitt A-B draws it cross-hatched under the collar's flange, Ø 6.1 scaled, bore 2.6, its foot 0.2 under the plate's inner face; the plan's dotted ring Ø 5.85), so the balance stops without side load on its pivots; a bell crank (27, U 02) under the plate on a screw at 52, held by a coil spring to a pin at 53 against the flattened eccentric end of a knob's shaft in the case (28, U 03; the knob 1, turned 180° between "frei" and "fest", held by a spring and a detent pin), carries an upright pin in the tongs' notch, which spreads them open at "frei". Built in the version as first drawn on 18 October 2026 and taken out again the same day: drawn open, the tongs clear the impulse roller (the pocket, tapered 10 to 8 wide since 15 October 2026, lets each turn 3.8°, where the 8-wide pocket read until then allowed 0.8°), but the lever, traced on 05 36's plan put on holes 23, 51 and 52 (12.31 px/mm, 0.06 mm), runs 0.9 deep through the 1941 escape wheel (its teeth 0.5-1.9 under the plate, the lever 1.35 thick just under it: fine.py, 2.4 mm³) and grazes the 1941 detent (0.1), as it runs through the detent block of 05 04 01-4 (8.7.43) as last revised; and the 1946 hub has no ring where the tongs close (its neck Ø 2.63). 05 36 is drawn on the lever plate (its spring pin lands by the lever plate's hole 53, O 13.0, not the detent plate's, O 15.0) and 127-556 is the Luftwaffe's lever chronometer BC 1010 ("Unruh-Arretierung": Knirim; the hand setting, above): the device is that chronometer's, whose escapement (05 04_20, _21, 05 03_1) isn't built. No photograph shows one on a detent Wempe: Wempe 3955's plate has holes 51-53 empty (`13_Wempe_3955_obere_Platte_unten.jpg`), the 6MX uses transit dampers; Wempe 2965 (lever, "Anhaltevorrichtung") and 2900 (lever, "Arretierung des Werkes") were sold with one, not photographed in a way that shows it. The 1943 plate keeps the pocket and holes 51-53, so a detent chronometer may have had one, its form not in the set.
- The case (P1f; the sources read again 16 October 2026, the 6MX gallery among them):
  - The bowl's "Gewicht 350 gr.": the casting as drawn is about 152 cm³ (05 01_2's section: the wall 4, the bottom 6.5), 1.34 kg in brass (`gimbals_wempe.json`); 350 g is 2.3 g/cm³, which no moulded case reaches (Buna or Bakelite 180-210 g, a mineral-filled phenolic about 300) and an aluminium casting only nearly (about 380 machined), and "Ms-Sandguß (Kokillenguß)" names a metal casting. Wempe 3955's Bakelite bowl (`16_Wempe_3955_Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`) is beaded and rounded, not R-05 01 00-1's 40° cone: an undrawn bowl. The 6MX's TU gives the whole chronometer ≤ 2.2 kg (6MX-figures.md, TU 1.2.8), where the model's bowl and ring alone weigh 3.26 kg: if the TU's mass leaves out its box, the bowl would be about 280 g, near the sheet's 350 (moderately sure the brass bowl is too heavy; whether the TU's "масса" counts the box isn't said). The machined bowl's sheet (05 01 00-1 without the R-) would settle it.
  - The bezel: screwed onto the bowl (likely): Wempe 5377's bezel is knurled at its lower edge (`bonhams_26816_327_wempe5377_type05.jpg`, sure; its case later), Wempe 3955's edge serrated (moderately), Wempe 8711's "threaded glazed bezel" (Antiquorum's text, catalogue-de-en.md), and 05 01 01 draws a thread's zigzag hidden in the lower bore (about 3 teeth over 4.4: likely); the 6MX's bowls (KirPol 24020, `leben-des-6mx/13_`, `14_KirPol_24020_E-Kontakt*.jpg`; Kirow 247 `12_Kirow_247_E-Kontakt_3.jpg`) have a thin threaded collar at the top, about 5 turns at about 1 pitch, the ring's flange on a step inside it. The thread isn't sized on any sheet, and the drawn sizes (the bezel's bore Ø 106, the flange's Ø 105.95) leave no metal for a collar: not built.
  - The ring's pin: no notch (05 01 01 draws the bezel without one; a notch in the bezel at 12, estimated, was built until 21 October 2026). A screwed bezel can't hold a notch to the pin, and KirPol 24020's bowl has the notch cut down through its threaded collar at 12 o'clock, about 1.6 wide and 4-5 deep (sure for the 6MX); so the Wempe's is likelier in the bowl, but 05 01 01 lays the flange in the bezel's recess, above the bowl's top face, where only the bezel meets the pin. With the bezel built to 05 01 01, which also marks its lower bore threaded, the pin stands clear: its seat, likely in the machined bowl, isn't drawn.
  - The Feststeller: on Wempes 4585 (`rmg_L5393_wempe_wartime_001.jpg`) and 5377 a knurled knob about Ø 20-21 on a short post, on a flat lever on a block on the right wall, with an upright pin about Ø 4 on the lever between the knob and the ring, where Schnitt A-B draws a circle Ø 8.6 under the lever (built Ø 8, as the sheet); the knob's top about 10 (5377) and 17-19 (4585) over the lever against the model's 15.3 (rough readings in perspective; the "17" from the front wall agrees). A side-on photograph of a Wempe's lock with the ring in place would settle the heights. Wempe 3044 (`milivault_529241_wempe3044_screen.png`, from above, rough) has the pin's round head, Ø 8.7 ±1, slotted, built Ø 8.7 × 2.5 on the drawings' case (its height estimated; the 6MX's pin kept plain), its lock's bracket a black block about 20 wide (the model's seat 18, kept), and its lever about 45 long along the front wall against 05 01's 28 (kept: the sheet decides; it may be another lever).
  - The Zunge (P. 51 00 02-2): on Wempes 4585 (aluminium) and 5377 (brass) the box's latch is an angle plate at the front's middle on the body, its top flange slotted about 6.5 long, with a knob about Ø 7 beside the slot, and an angle plate on the lid with a C-shaped hook into that slot; the model's tongue swinging on the lid's front over a keeper doesn't match. The Zunge (iron 10 × 4, 45 long, the M3.5 for a knob, a thinned round end) fits as the bolt behind the body's plate, which the knob moves to catch the hook (inferred); how it moves isn't seen. The 6MX's boxes have escutcheon hasps instead.
  - The box's height (25 October 2026, decided, nothing changed): 05 01's note gives "Holzgehäuse 185 × 185 × 180 mm außen" (sure); 51 00 10 ("Änderung von 51 00 10", gez. 14.7.42) draws the side panel square, 185 wide (±0.5) and 185 high (both figured; the outline 436 × 437 px at 2.38 px/mm), with the pivot's M6 105 over its bottom and the parting line 11.5 over that, so the same box. 05 01 was drawn 30.10.40 and reissued unchanged as working copy 4 (28.6.46, stamped 31.3.47), so its 180 is both the earlier figure and the latest one issued; the handle's sheet is a change to the handle, its panel drawn round it. Kept 180 in every drawn version: the 185 would only make the lid 5 taller (68.5 over the parting line against 63.5), the parting line, the pivots and the gimbals where they are. A measured height of a 1940s Wempe box would settle it (Lange 5499's auction gives 183 × 172 × 180, another maker's box, its axes not named).
  - The pins over the outer pivots (25 October 2026; built, Estimated): 05 01's elevation alone draws them, without a number or part line, inside drawn in crossing coils (a thread or a spring); no photograph shows one (Greenwich's wartime Wempe's side walls' tops over the pivots plain at about 2.5 px/mm, Wempe 6161's box later, with a rebated lip). Whether they locate the lid, catch it (a ball catch) or are something else isn't settled; a photograph of a 1940s box's parting face, or the box's own sheet (127-556.02), would settle it.
  - The Holzkasten's own sheet (127-556.02) isn't in the set; the 1943 key is its assembly's (127-556.02 U 01, parts -01 to -05 on 05 01_6), which fits its holder in the box.
- The escape wheel's web recess against its teeth's floor (25 October 2026, `dfm.py`; Making it): the recess Ø 11.62 (05 04_7.2 scales 11.65) lies 0.04 inside the 1942 tooth form's floor Ø 11.7, leaving a sliver of brass about 0.04 wide and 0.6 high in each gap, which no maker would leave; built as the sheets give it. A sheet giving the recess's diameter as a figure would settle it.

## Closed

Items Unresolved held, each with how it closed: by a new reading of the sources, by the model's own analysis, or by a bench test that settles it for a Wempe made to the model while the original's figure stays unknown.

- **The frame's open readings from the drawings check** (26 October 2026; closed 27 October 2026, on the sheets' pixels and Wempe 3955's photographs):
  - *The pillars' countersinks* (05 08 00-1): drawn at 5:1 from Ø 2.59 at each face (44 px against the spigot's 4.5 at 77) down to the tap drill. Now built so, 0.24 deep to the thread's crests, the thread cut through the cone (KIT's threadWall `th.cs`: the wall the larger of the thread's and the cone's, no run-out), as a maker taps through a countersunk hole; until then Ø 2.7 to 0.03 deep, since a thread run out to its crests lay under the screw's (fine.py).
  - *05 09 01's revision a*: read "∅ 4,5 in 4,59" (not "6,15"), its circled a on P-Q's "4,5∅ H7": the escape wheel's upper setting's lip opened to 4.59 H7, the reamer of holes 1, 35, 38 and of every 5.2 seat's lip on the other sheets (05 08 02-1's G-H, both cocks' 23). Built 4.596, the setting's spigot 4.59 with it (4.506 and 4.5 until then).
  - *J-K's "22 Ø"*: at the face (Readings corrected).
  - *Sheet b's hidden "Ø 5.8" round hole 30*: the plan's two circles there fit Ø 4.78 (solid) and 5.35 (dashed) at 9.71 px/mm (hole 1 to 30's 34.10; the sheet's other holes drawn 2.39-3.45 for 2.50-3.50): G-H's 4.59 and 5.2, no third feature. The table reams 30 to 2.50 instead, and Wempe 3955's plate has that, a plain hole both sides (Ø 2.4, 2.9 over its chamfer, on the dial face; 2.4-2.8 on the inner face, no seat): its version so, the drawn ones G-H's.
  - *The "9,5" across the hand-setting cut* (05 08 02-1 sheet a; kept): the plan fitted on holes 11, 12, 14, 17 (0.07 mm rms) puts its arrow on the dial-face window's side (across −3.1 drawn, the model's 3.25), the line running across the cut's axis to the scan's edge, where its other end is lost (sheet b's matching strip shows the window's 6,5 and 8,3, not it). Ending at the inner face's far edge it would make that face's width 12.5 against C-D's figured 12 +0,2; the model keeps C-D's 12.1 (Wempe 3955's window from inside, 8_Wempe_3955_Räderwerk_plus.jpg through photocompare.py's w3955_train camera, agrees within its ±0.4).
  - *The click's 17,4* (05 08 05-2; kept): its 9,7, 7,5, r 1, 3,5 and 85° fix the working faces against the pivot and give 17.53, 0.03 past the sheet's general ±0,1 on its overall 17,4; the view can't arbitrate (its own figures scale 15.0-17.9 px/mm), nor can Wempe 3955's plan (0.13 mm is a pixel at 8.81 px/mm). The figures that place the beak and nose are kept.
  - *The upper cock's seam* (05 08 03-1): the boss's foot is set 0.01 into the arm, so the fused mesh has no face at TT−12.6 between the two solids' walls (the seat's wall showed cracks there). The setting screws' holes still change from the arm's thread to the boss's plain bore at that level (the boss's cells cut the holes where they leave through its flank, which threadWall can't).

- **The detent's block against the 1958 repair book's order** (Unresolved 25 October 2026, `assembly.py`'s first run; closed the same day by the book's wording, two Kirov photographs and the model's own path). The book takes the block off after the balance and before the barrel's ratchet (p. 59: "Отвернуть винт балансового моста, снять балансовый мост и баланс. Снять колодку ходовой пружины, затем храповое колесо вала барабана": unscrew the balance bridge's screw, take off the balance bridge and the balance; take off the detent's block, then the barrel arbor's ratchet) and puts it on last, after the balance (p. 60: "Установить и привернуть колодку ходовой пружины к платине": fit the detent's block and screw it to the plate), with the lower cock on the top plate throughout (p. 59: "К верхней платине привернуть мост баланса нижний", the first step of the assembly). The model's block hangs under the top plate between the plates, which `assembly.py` took to mean it could leave only with the plate, since it drew every part straight off along the arbors. Kirov No. 247 (`dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/leben-des-6mx/8_Kirow_247_F.jpg`, side on between the plates) shows the 6MX's block just so, hanging under the top plate with its end at the rim beside a pillar, its fixing screw down from the plate's outer face (sure); Kirov No. 14100's top plate off (chasy.ru topic 9985, `96890BF4…` its inner face, `9A7ACAC8…` its outer face) carries no block on either face, its balance opening a keyhole about Ø 9 against the block's 28 × 16 (scaled by eye on the plate's Ø 84.6, the model's, ±10 %). So the block can't come out through the plate; it comes out sideways under it, as the frame is open between the pillars. In the model (25 October 2026: `assembly.py`'s path steps and `split`) its fixing screw comes out of the plate's outer face, and the block with its detent drops 1.5 off its two steady pins (they reach 1.3 into the plate; at 2 the detent meets the lower cock) and slides out along −x, its stone drawing back out of the escape wheel's teeth, 45 mm clear of the rim: every heading in plan from 150° to 180° is clear of every part still in, the lower cock and the escape wheel among them (135° grazes the wheel by 0.06, 105-120° meet it by 1.4). Nothing in the model's geometry changed.

- The stone catching the tooth's back during the impulse (19 October 2026; closed 25 October 2026, by the published restitution and a bench limit): dynamics.js landed the stone plastically (estimated), the tripping frame elastically. Brass's restitution on a hard surface is 0.80-0.95 at 1-10 cm/s (King, White, Maxwell and Menon, *EPL* 93, 14002, 2011, brass spheres on steel; the escape wheel's gilt brass is the side that yields); dynamics.js takes it as `eR` (plastic by default, which leaves every other result as it was) and the frame's sweep as `eD`. At the model's 0.8 g no landing falls in the impulse at 0, 0.80 or 0.95 in dynamics.js, and the frame at 0.80 and 0.95 lets no tooth by (the stone at most 53 % of the lock, the swing within 0.1°): the models agree there. Above it they part: with 0.80 the stone lands during the impulse from 0.85 g (12 in 28 beats; 28 at 0.9 g), with 0.95 from 1.0 g, the swing 0.5-2.5° higher and the rate within 0.19 s a day, no beat lost; plastic, none to 1.0 g (since the audit's first batch; 7 in 12 at 0.9 g before). So the preload's window is capped at 0.80 g (`wempe_tolerances.js`'s `back`, all three versions: as drawn 0.78-0.80 g, the builds 0.64-0.80), the bench's aim inside it (0.785 and 0.68 g) and its test says why; the Monte Carlo's Wempes all work after the bench (100 % in each).
- The gold spring on the return pass (closed 25 October 2026, by the alloy's range and a bench test): 333 gold's modulus has no published figure; its neighbours run 83 GPa (Ag-Cu eutectic, ASTM B628) to 87 (AuAg20Cu10) and 100 (Au-Cu, ASTM B596), copper 110-130. Over 83-110 GPa (`wempe_tripping.js`'s sweep, `tsK`) at the model's contact the frame lets no tooth by (the stone at most 58 % of the lock); the spring's free shape is the one traced on Wempe 3955 (WESC.GOLD). What decides it is the stone's contact on the gold spring as it returns: 4 times stiffer, 6 teeth in 12 get past at either end of the modulus's range, which only a made spring can show. So `wempe_bench.js`'s escapement test turns the balance back through the discharge by hand under power and watches the locking stone (it must not lift), with the 1958 Navy repair book's checks of the spring's reach (p. 60: its tip 0.10-0.15 short of the discharge stone at rest, 0.35-0.40 past the detent's end). Its tip square, the horn 4.4 long and the two springs' colours stand as read above in this entry's first form.
- The hairspring's length against its period (25 October 2026, the per-part audit; closed the same day by the 1958 Navy repair book and the modulus's range): which of the balance's inertia, the strip's section and its modulus leaves the drawn spring at 0.494 s. None is shown off: the period goes as 1/√E, and over hardened carbon spring steel's 200-210 GPa (escphys.js) the drawn spring on the weighed balance is 0.489-0.501 s, 0.5 s at 200.9 GPa (2.5 % under the model's 206). The 1958 book's MX (p. 58: "the hairspring's calculated length 502.35 mm, 12.78 working turns", for 0.5 s) with the drawn 0.285 × 0.52 and the weighed balance asks 199.7 GPa, or at 206 GPa a balance 3.2 % heavier: the same place. Its length is the model's whole-strip reading's (05 03_7's 13 turns less 3.5 mm, 505.4 mm) to 0.6 %, against 548.8 mm for the 13 turns of helix (`h13`), which settles the reading (502.35 / 12.78 = 39.31 mm a turn, the coil's Ø 12.51: the book counts the whole strip in turns of the coil, as 05 03_7 does). 05 03_5's stack (re-read at 3×, 26 October 2026) doesn't count against it: 3.15 + 9.5 + 0.75 = 13.4 puts its 9.5 between the two terminal curves' strip levels, about 13 pitches at the coiled 0.73 (05 03_6: 15 turns 11.5 high, the blank's pitch), with 12 coil ends drawn each side (about 12-12.5 turns, drawn schematically); but 05 03_5 and 05 03_1 carry the lever chronometer's numbers, so its stud's height is that chronometer's fitting, and 05 03_1 boxes the stud's height "6≈", "Höhe übereinstimmend mit 2400 Schwingungen pro Std." (read at 4×; the 2400 not explained): fitted at timing, so the stack's height follows the spring as timed and counts no turns (the whole strip's 11.81 turns opened to 9.5 would be a pitch of 0.80, the period unchanged). Wempe 3955's spring, side on, rises 8.1 ± 0.4 mm (How the balance and escapement were measured), against the whole strip's 8.62 at the coiled pitch and 9.43 for the 13 turns of helix; the model keeps the coiled pitch. The rest is taken up at the bench as the spring is vibrated to its balance (`wempe_hairspring.js` checks the bracket): not by the timing weights, a turn of both 44.2 s a day and as drawn only in (gaining), where the drawn spring is about 18 minutes a day fast.
- The mainspring's thickness against its measured torque (Physics: energy; closed 26 October 2026 by the model's own analysis): the solved 0.37 strip gave 23 % more than 05 06_20 measures, its "0,37" the sheet's own stencil 7, so not a misread thickness. The gap was the free curve's set, which no sheet gives: fitted on 05 06_20's shape without friction it came out ×0.6, but the force was measured running down, and the coils' friction (`mainspring_friction.py` on each spring's own solve) takes most near full wind, where a spring with less set nears coil-bound; counted, the shape fits best at ×0.75 (1.83 % rms at mu 0.10, against ×0.6's 3.24 % and ×0.8's 2.31 %; solved 26 October 2026, about 2 hours), and there the measured force is 0.913 of the solved frictionless torque: the losses to the fusee arbor at their middle take it up with the strip at 0.370, and the strip as solved is stressed 2.46 GPa fully wound, at a mainspring's working limit (2.70 GPa at ×0.6, past it). About ×1.10 of the 23 % was the losses, about ×1.13 the set; nothing is left for the modulus or the thickness. `tools/wempe_mainspring.py` FINAL (1240, 0.6) → (1240, 0.75). The swing and rate don't take the solved spring (the escapement is driven by 05 06_20's measured force), so the page moved only in the essay's words; the 1950s build's drive turn by turn (`wempe_build_drive.json`, its strip over the drawn strip's solved at the measured level) became 1.253-1.281 times the measured from 1.13-1.31, its swing 226.0-237.2° over the wind from 214.8-239.5°.
- The closing spring (05 01 00-7; by the plate's sheet, 17 October 2026): drawn, as an estimate. It has no sheet, and no source shows it: it lies under the plate on Wempe 3955's bowl (`16_…Gehäusetopf_Kardan.jpg`) and in moMvyx8eXfo 7:10-7:45; the 6MX's shutter is another (a pin in a slot, Uy8A88_j-cA 5:21-5:25); the Soviet books here are scans without text, and a search in German, Russian and English (dg-chrono.de, uhrforum.de, pikabu's 6MX repair, NAWCC) found no case taken apart. The plate's own sheet (05 01 00-2) gives its seat: the groove Ø 10 to Ø 26, 1.5 deep, in the face that lies on the bowl's bottom, a chamber closed between them, and the Ø 1.2 drilled blind from the groove's floor at 12.4, against its outer wall, for one end. So: a steel wire Ø 1.0 (the hole's), a flat spiral of two turns across the chamber, r 12.35 to 6.5, at its mid depth (0.25 clear of the floor and the bowl), its outer end turned down 2 into the plate's hole, its inner end up 1.2 into a hole Ø 1.2 × 1.5 in the bowl's bottom (the casting's sheet draws nothing machined). Unloaded with the plate shut, so nothing need stop it there, as the model has nothing; opened 25° it winds up: about 82 N·mm a radian (E 200 GPa, 119 mm of wire), 36 N·mm open, 1.4 N at the knob, 370 MPa in the wire, well within spring steel's. The same chamber would take a flat strip's spiral pinned in the Ø 1.2, or a single open ring at its outer wall (the Hamilton's 42126 is one, Fig. 107); the groove's 8 mm width suggests turns across it, and the Ø 1.2 a wire's end. A photograph of a Wempe or Lange bowl with its closing plate off would settle it.
- The preload's floor (Building it; analysis), since 19 October 2026's physics pass (the lock 0.235, the estimate 0.8 g): teeth get past at 0.2 times the estimate and under, none from 0.5 times, and a held tooth's stone rings under half the depth of lock from 0.98 times, **0.78 g**, the bench's least (over the knock's, under 0.64 g); the estimate was raised to 0.8 g because at 0.63 g the deeper lock's longer discharge reach left it ringing 56 %. Before the pass: `wempe_tripping.js` now runs the frame under the estimate as well as over it (0.1, 0.2, 0.5 and 0.8 times 0.63 g): teeth get past at 0.2 times and under, none from 0.5 times, and a held tooth's stone rings under half the depth of lock from about 0.81 times, **0.51 g** (since the horn was traced, 18 October 2026: before, teeth got past only at 0.1 times and with none, and the margin held from 0.78 times, 0.49 g), the bench's least until 18 October 2026 (0.48 g before 16 October 2026's reading of 05 04_1's angles); since then the bench takes the higher knock's floor, 0.63 g, where a 3 ms blow of 20 g first lets no tooth go (`wempe_tolerances.js`; the window 0.63-1.55 g, aimed near 0.86). What the 1941 shop set it to stays unknown: the banking isn't drawn.
- Endshake (Building it; by the bench): the sheets' own tolerances leave it to assembly (an arbor under 0.01 mm in 92 % of movements made anywhere within them, the fusee's in 75 %), as their own notes leave the fusee's face a and the bushes' depths; the bench's endshake test (each arbor's band from the 1958 MX repair book at 20 °C since 19 October 2026, 0.01-0.08 mm before; each arbor's stack with its terms) catches every one in `wempe_montecarlo.js` and sets it to its band's middle, and after it all are free. Whether the 1941 shop held the plates' seats at 0.3 or fitted each arbor at assembly, the sheets don't say beyond face a's note: for a made Wempe it is fitted at assembly.
- The hairspring's length (Physics: hairspring and temperature; by the bench): read as the whole strip 13 turns less 3.5 mm (05 03_7, the lever chronometer's number), 0.497 s with the weighed balance (0.491 s before 15 October 2026's shape batch, 0.468 s until the masses' height was read); the drawn coil counts (05 03_5 about 12.4 turns of helix between the curves, the coloured section 13, Wempe 3955 about 12, 11.5-12.8) lean toward a turn more, and 0.5 s wants 1.1 % more moment of inertia, a working length about 6 mm longer or the strip 0.4 % thinner: inside the modulus's and the strip's ranges. The drawings' own tolerances move the rate far more than any of these readings (the coil's Ø ±0.05 alone ±173 s a day, the steel's modulus ±1,049), so no drawn length times a made Wempe: the bench vibrates the spring against its balance and chooses or grinds it to 0.5 s, as a 1940s maker did (`wempe_bench.js`, *The balance vibrated*; every movement in `wempe_montecarlo.js` matched). The detent chronometer's own curve sheet isn't in the set, so which length Wempe drew stays unknown.
- The masses' place for temperature (by the trial): drawn 30° from the cuts' line (Wempe 3955's, the 6MX's book; 05 03_2 scales 32.5), where the model's middle constants put the compensating place at 19.5° (63-75° from the arm over their ranges; 20.5° before the bodies were weighed again). 30° is an adjusted chronometer's place, as Wempe 3955's is, so it says the constants differ from the model's middle ones (the rim's nickel steel isn't named beyond "Sonderlegierung"; the spring's thermoelastic coefficient), not that the masses are misplaced; a made Wempe finds its own place in the TU's trial (`wempe_bench.js`: out on C, the masses moved 0.235 s a day a °C a degree), and a place on the free arc nulls it in 28 of the 32 corners of the materials' ranges (`wempe_thermal.js`).
- The chain's run (P1c; a 6MX's photograph): crossed, the inner tangent between the drums (likely to sure). On Kirov 2973 fully wound (`dg-chrono-6mx-gallery/bilder-zum-6mx/4_Kirow_2973_N.jpg`: the chain on the fusee to its small end) the top view `2_Kirow_2973_A.jpg` shows the barrel's short tail ending in 05 07 04's bright curled hook about 197° anticlockwise from O about the barrel's arbor (17° south of west), the chain running from it north up the barrel's west side and under the plate, the barrel's edge bare below the hook: the chain is laid on the barrel the way it turns, so the barrel turns anticlockwise seen from above, against the fusee, which only a crossed chain does. Run down, Kirov 247 (`dg-chrono-6mx/4_Kirow_247_N.jpg`, `6_Kiro_247_S.jpg`) shows no chain leaving the fusee's north or south face at its flange, where an open run would. The crossing itself is never seen: the inner tangent meets each drum 19° off the line of centres and crosses a gap of about 2.4 mm between the pitch circles, behind pillar 49 from the north and the train from the south (LfhRSqN6SZU 1:02-1:19, KxInG08LWXg 0:00-0:37, 3AaPhV8j8bs 0:36-2:21 and WCguXfdZ38E 7:15-17:03 searched).
- The set-up ratchet's teeth (P1c; follows from the chain): on 2_Kirow_2973_A the click pivots above its tip, which meets the ratchet at about 10-11 o'clock; the barrel's turn drives that tooth toward the pivot and locks the click as a strut, holding the arbor against turning anticlockwise from the dial, as built (likely). Wempe 3955, Lange 5177 and Kirov 422 and 479 have the same click and ratchet.
- The barrel arbor's hook (P1c; changed): 05 07 02 draws its end view left of the front view, the ball end left, and the square solid: in first angle (DIN) the view from the square's end, from above. Read so, the 0.5 corner and square face stand on the side the arbor turns from and the ramp falls the way it turns; the spring's eye, pulled along the strip as the coil winds outward, catches the face with its trailing edge and the strip lies over the ramp, so it holds with the arbor pulled anticlockwise from the dial, as the crossed chain and the click have it (likely). The earlier reading took the eye as lying beside the pin, and the model had the face on the other side with the strip's tip abutting it from the side the pull leaves it on, which holds nothing: the face moved and the spring's inner end laid over the pin, its tip 0.18 behind it (`fusee.js`, 16 October 2026). On 17 October 2026 the eye was cut in the drawn strip (the Hamilton's, 2.2-9.4 mm from the tip: Estimated) and the hook turned into it, the tip 0.48 rad behind the hook's centre line.
- The maintaining detent's step (P1c; changed): 05 05 05-1 (05 05_12) draws the step as a dashed straight edge, from the back where the 8.6's extension line meets it (8.6 from the hole, 1.40 above it) obliquely down to the hollow about 5.6 from the hole (±0.3), section A-B's step where it crosses (about 5.9). Built so, the thick part is 0.52-0.61 clear of the fusee wheel's tips (likely); taken as an arc at 8.6 about the hole it was 0.19 into them, and was cut back to 7.55.
- The fusee's arbor (P1c): 05 08_8 doesn't dimension the bush's body below its flange: its 2.7 is only the nominal of 7.1 −0.2 − 2.9 +0.2 − 1.5 −0.1, whose limits are 2.3-2.8 (drawn 2.57 at 33 px/mm by the 7.1), and the assembly 05 08 01's Schnitt A-B draws the bush's end at the J-K recess's floor. The built 2.6 lies inside, leaving the arbor 0-0.05 endshake: the conflict was only at the nominals (likely).
- The drum's revision (a) "0,8 +0,05" (05 07 01-1, was 0.5 +0.03): an opening through the bottom, 2 wide along the wall and 0.8 radially, placed by the 0.95 +0.15, just inside the wall at 9 o'clock: the section's bottom hatch is broken by two lines through the whole 1.25, the plan draws a small rectangle there inside the wall's circle under the "2 +0,05", and the assembly 05 07 01 draws the same cut (likely). Its purpose isn't drawn; not cut (the drum's slots aren't).
- The slot's depth under the chain's pin (P1c): the "29 +0,1" on 02-2 (its comma lost) runs from the line tangent to the flange's and ribs' tops at A (r 17.8) to the one tangent to the Ø 13.5 cutter's lowest point, scaling 2.98 against the sheet's own "1" (48 px/mm): the depth, 2.9 +0.1, the floor at r 14.9 on A's radius, as built (likely). A faint pencil note beside it reads like "3,0 +0,1" (not certain).
- The barrel bridge's chord: the sheet's, as built. Re-measured on Wempe 3955 by its screw heads 42 and 48 (circles fitted along rays) and its chord's edge (about 36 points), it lies at −1.3° to −1.6° against the sheet's −1.07° and the line square to hole 45's −2.39° (about 0.3° for 2 px on screw 48): nearer the sheet. Every 6MX on millimetre paper (eight clean fits of the thirteen A photographs) has its chord parallel to the 42-48 line, 0.0 ±0.2°: a Soviet change, no evidence for the Wempe.
- The click's "85" and "5,7" (changed; Readings corrected): an angle and a diameter. The "85°" bears a degree sign, its arc about the beak's foot from the end face's extension to the base: the end face at 85° to the base, leaning toward the hole as it rises (82.4° built before). The "5,7" is the thin circle about the hole, its dimension line through the centre with both arrows, tangent to the base: the base 2.85 under the hole (2.81 scaled), and the r 6 hollow then meets it 9.69 from the hole, the sheet's "9,7" (`frame.js`, 16 October 2026).
- Hole 53: O 15.0, the detent plate's, as built (sure): Wempe 3955's top plate from inside (`13_Wempe_3955_obere_Platte_unten.jpg`, a similarity on holes 1, 11, 39, 49, 51 and 52 at 11.42 px/mm, residuals 0.03-0.34) has a hole at O 15.12, S 37.18 and none at O 13.0, the lever plate's.
- The upper cock's "3,2 ±0,02": the arm relieved to U30's 2.4 within r 12 of the setting (`7_Wempe_3955_W.jpg` shows the 45° step under the arm, the arm's side face 24 px thick before it and 18 after, 0.75, U30's 2.4/3.2), with a boss round the setting whose face stands 0.8 below the relief, so 3.2 again there: the 3,2 ±0,02 is the boss face's thickness (05 08 03-1's Schnitt A-B, U30 and the coloured section agree: sure; built 17 October 2026).
- The centre arbor (P1b; changed): 05 05 03-2's "1,6 −0,1" spans the wheel's seat alone, from the pinion's face to the collar's foot (05 05_10 at 17.1 px/mm: drawn 1.52; the 17.2 ends at the pinion's face, the framed 19 at the collar's top, C's root), so the riveting collar is the 0.2-0.3 the figures leave (19 − 17.2 − 1.5…1.6). The seat built 1.55, the collar 0.255, the wheel's hub the sheet's 1.5 (`train.js`, 16 October 2026; the seat 1.45 and collar 0.355 before). Its 25.4 on 05 05_8 is used (the cannon pinion's block, 25.4 − 23: dial.js).
- Where the tooth falls on the impulse stone (Physics): explained. The sheet's "21°" is the escape wheel's arc of impulse, 10.5° either side of the line of centres, not the balance's angle where the tooth lands; the sheet's own balance figure for where the impulse begins is 22°10', a static layout with a 1° drop, and the page's layout now takes it (PHI_C, 21° until 16 October 2026). The solved landing is the wheel's chase from rest, which the layout doesn't model: no contradiction.
- The seconds hand's collet (05 02 01-2, no sheet): `1_Wempe_3955_Z.jpg` (9.14 px/mm) shows its brass end, Ø about 1.3, flush inside the hand's blued boss (Ø about 3.2), the post's end (Ø about 0.6) dark inside it: the model's collet, its end Ø 1.2 through the hand flush with its top, on the post's Ø 0.65-0.76 (likely). Its length isn't seen.
- The seconds numerals: radial, as the sheet (05 02 00-4) draws them, on Wempe 3955's dial too (its 50, 10, 40 and 20 turned, 60 and 30 upright), which the page traces, and on Lange 5177's: the earlier reading of 3955's as upright was wrong. The sheet prints "Made in Germany" along the track at 7-5; Wempe 3955's and Lange 5177's dials don't, and the page draws the dial as made, without it (whether only export dials carried it isn't known).
- The up-and-down figures' height: about 1.8-2.1 on Wempe 3955 (its 0, 24, 32), the sheet's "2"; traced at the dial's size.
- 05 04_1's angles (changed, 16 October 2026; the scan's pixels at 24.4 px/mm, the balance at (157.5, 453), the wheel at (303.5, 650.5)). The "24°" is the escape wheel's pitch, 360/15, not the impulse face's angle at rest, which the model and its essay took it for (sure: its arc lies 377-381 px from the wheel's centre and 132-149 from the balance's, and its ends span 23.2° of the wheel and 62° of the balance); 1°, 21° and 2° are the wheel's too, on one arc about it (likely): 1° of drop from the lock onto the impulse stone, 21° of impulse from 10.5° before the line of centres to 10.5° after, 2° on to the lock, so the stone's 36.5° follows from the sheet's own figures (24 + 2 + 10.5); the pencil "11½" is 1° + 10.5°, the impulsing tooth before the line with the wheel locked (the model's 11.58°). The "22°10'" is the balance's, its arrows 7.6 from the balance's centre: from the line of centres to where the impulse begins (the tip circle meets the stone's r 3.365 there exactly; likely); the "6°" (7.0 from it) runs on from there to the drawn impulse face, whose radial edge scales 27.7-28.5° before the line, so the face stands at rest 28°10' before it (6° + 22°10'). The "86°" is centred on the locking stone (4.2 from the pipe's centre), from the wheel's radius through the stone to the line toward the detent's bending point (the model's geometry 85.4°: likely). Built: PHI0 −28°10' (−24° before), the impulse face that much further round its roller, and the layout's PHI_C −22°10' (`escapement.js`). The stone then comes into the teeth's path 6.1° after the balance passes rest, and with the discharge stone where the model had it (leaving the gold spring at rest, the wheel let go at −2.4°) a tooth chased from rest got to the stone's path first: makeEsc and dynamics.js put it onto the stone's corner where it came in, a stand-in for a tooth that, ahead of the stone, gets no impulse. So the discharge stone's place, which the scan can't give (Estimated), moved with it: 5° later, the release at +2.6°, the tooth landing on the face 7.6° after the stone comes in (4° is the least that lands cleanly, 8° doesn't run). The swing at 12 h 197.2° with TF re-fitted to the 6MX's video at the model's own swing: 108 s, as before (199.4° before the change).
- As first drawn (and in the 6MX at those heights) the hairspring's pin at the stud (050302-4.s) rides 0.3 higher with the spring's top curve and stands 0.05 off the stud, which stays: the 1941 stud's seat for it isn't drawn. Recorded as a known deviation in `bom.json` (found 16 October 2026; already so on main at 3.00.01, unseen because CI runs the BOM as last revised only).
- The 6MX's pillar 39 and seconds wheel: the pillar reads Ø 8.0 ±0.5 (`6_Kiro_247_S.jpg`, the drawn 8.1), so a thinner pillar doesn't explain its radius; on r 37 it would meet the drawn seconds wheel's tips, and the wheel's rim passes behind the pillar in the only view of it. Built on r 37.3 (Estimated); a square-on view of the seconds wheel by pillar 39 would settle it.
- The 6MX's contact cock's leg (18 October 2026): both contact photographs put it about 2.5 nearer the disc across the view than Fig. 100's place (the model's leg x 22-25, the photographs' outer corner at x 22.5 ±0.5); its depth and the arm's root and foot can't be read from views 14° apart, so the leg is left at Fig. 100's (±2). A photograph from the plate's centre outward, or square on from above with the top plate off, would settle it.
- The 6MX's lower balance cock's plan and step (18 October 2026). No photograph shows its plan: the loose cranked piece on chasy-ru (`chasy-ru-9985-kirova-14100/F20D7D36…jpeg`), taken for it until now (its setting's screws 6.9 apart, its round end r 6.6 off the setting), is the upper cock, about 55 long at the intermediate bridge's 7.4 px/mm beside it and 65 by the pillars' Ø 8.1 (the drawn lower cock spans 17.2 by 20), its foot with the screw's hole, the riser at 0.58 of its length from the jewel end, the arm with the endstone's setting and its two screws. Its step: every side view (`8_Kirow_247_F.jpg` and the gallery's 13 Feder photographs) looks along the cock's plane from the same side, and through `photocompare.py`'s `m6_detent` camera a point's place along the bar and across it both fall along the image's x (about −8.7 and +10 px a mm), so the deep part's edge (x 339) fixes only a line: a full-width step at u about −6 (the earlier reading's −4.2 took the full width) or one at the lug's edge (u 0 to +2) not spanning the bar; where the model's riser stands (x 259-335) the photograph shows the barrel behind, so the drawn step at +1.5 with the full width is not what 247 has. Kirova 14100's `1E7A2D09…jpeg` shows the cock from another side (the lug 2.5 thick at the far face, a stamped deep face), but its phone camera would need its own fit. What would settle it: that fit, or a photograph of the loose lower cock in plan. The model keeps the drawn plan and its step (X5).
- The 6MX's adjusting slide and stud: how they work isn't seen (no section or underside view). The stud may be an eccentric and the slide its key, or the slide a shim the stud clamps; and the loose block's holes, measured from its end, sit 1.5-2 from where Kirov 247's plate puts them, so either 247's block is drilled otherwise (built so) or the loose block is another issue. A block photographed off the plate on edge, or Romanov's Fig. 99 at a finer scan, would settle it.
- The 6MX's gimbals: its bowl hangs 13.2 lower on its high brackets (X5), so its case's periods on the pivots are shorter than the drawings' 0.96 and 1.05 s; the page's gimbals keep the drawings' (its bowl and ring aren't weighed apart, and its gimbal ring, photographed only in its box, isn't measured: its pivot screws are drawn to reach the drawings' bracket at r 66.5). Kirov-Poljot 24020's brackets stand lower again (10.8 under the rim), as the drawings' nearly do.
- The 6MX's mainspring: the 1958 Navy repair book gives the first generation's MX 1.01-1.08 N·m fully wound and 0.55-0.60 two days on, against 839 and 427 mN·m here (824 and 473 with the free curve at ×0.6, until 26 October 2026), which a strip about 0.42 thick (Tarasov's for the later 6MX, 1976) would give; no 1950s source gives the strip, so the drawings' 0.37 is kept (the user's choice, 17 October 2026). The solver the model's spring comes from (`mainspring.py`), run on Tarasov's spring, gives his published torques: 714 N·mm run down against his 715, 1185 at his wound count against his 1143.5, which is 3.6 % over and within his formula's loss factor of 0.95 (`References/wempe/documents/6MX-figures.md`, section 9; `tools/mainspring_tarasov1976.js`).
- Hole 40 on Kirov 247: filled flush with gilt on both faces, under the contact cock's foot (on Kirov 1564, without the contact, open through both plates): perhaps the cock's fixing from the dial side; built open, as the drawings' auxiliary hole. And the small hole at S 20.6 O 30.2 (124°) in Kirov 247's and 1564's top plates, on no sheet: not built.
- The 6MX's closing spring and the outer box's tab: no 6MX photograph shows the spring (built as the drawings'); the outer box's lid tab reads larger than its notch (chord 105-111, 41-44 high against 102 and 33), so it may lap over the body's front (drawn filling the notch).
- What Altmeppen's "three small windows by the pillar screws", which he says the 6MX lacks, are: Kirov 247 has the Hilfslöcher 3, 12 and 40, read until 16 October 2026 as those windows. Two candidates (18 October 2026): his comparison of a Wempe's top plate (`1_1_Vergleich_Wempe-6MX_A.jpg`) puts arrows on a rectangular notch in the Wempe's movement ring's inner rim by its 340° screw, which the 6MX's ring lacks ("by the pillar screws" perhaps the ring's); or the window in the later 6MXs' top plate at 202° over the seconds wheel (Kirov 2973, Poljot 20027), in the other sense. The German wording would settle it (REQUESTS.md).
